| ENG-057 | Define MCP client safety model | Shipped | P2 | 3 | Before adding a `mcp_tool` node, define the safety model for Janusly as an MCP client. Scope includes org-level `mcp_connections`, credential/env refs, tool discovery caching, per-tool allow/deny lists, timeout/response-size limits, audit events, and prompt-injection boundaries when discovered tools are shown to LLM planners. AC: architecture doc exists; schema/API shape is defined; tests cover disabled connection, unknown tool, timeout, and audit metadata. Expands the MCP-client portion of ENG-025. **Status note (2026-05-14):** v1 of the safety model landed inline with ENG-094 — `mcp_connections` + `mcp_tool_descriptors` schema, command allowlist, env-whitelist spawn, SSRF-guarded SSE, two-flag write consent, per-tool rate-limit, audit + usage telemetry, dry-run gate, and the operator-only Inspector exposure invariant are all live and covered by tests. **Status note (2026-05-17):** Structural fix-class extension for `mcp_tool` failures shipped — `isSensitiveAction` (`packages/engine/src/workflow-readiness.ts`) gains an `mcp_tool` branch that returns `true` unconditionally (the workflow JSON has no visibility into the per-tool `mcp_tool_descriptors.writeSide` flag at suggestion time, so the safe default mirrors the descriptor table's `writeSide: true` default); the `/ai/patch-workflow` dispatcher gate in `apps/api/src/routes/ai-routes.ts` opens from `failingNode.data.type === "http"` to `=== "http" || === "mcp_tool"`; `STRUCTURAL_PATCH_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`packages/ai/src/patch-workflow.ts`) gains one sentence clarifying the action applies to both HTTP write-side AND `mcp_tool`; recovery-matrix grows from 15 → 16 fixtures with a new `mcp_tool_without_approval` case. The merger (`applyStructuralPatchToWorkflow`), the schema (`AiPatchStructuralEnvelope`), and the audit metadata field (`patchStyle: "structural"`) are all already type-agnostic so no schema / merger / audit-wiring changes needed. Classic `tool` nodes (against `SENSITIVE_TOOL_NAMES` / `SENSITIVE_TOOL_SUFFIXES`) intentionally stay on the config envelope — operator UX for "insert approval upstream of `slack.post` / `email.send`" hasn't been validated. **Status note (2026-05-18):** Per-tool rate-limit overrides shipped. New nullable `rate_limit_per_min` column on `mcp_tool_descriptors` (migration `20260518024938_fearless_slyde`); `setToolFlags` in `packages/data/src/mcpConnectionsRepo.ts` now returns `{ before, after }` so the existing admin route can audit on-change. `executeMcpTool` (`packages/engine/src/mcp-tool-executor.ts`) computes `effectiveRateLimit = descriptor.rateLimitPerMin ?? input.rateLimitPerMin` before the limiter call — descriptor override (when set) wins over the org default, NULL keeps today's behaviour. Admin route `POST /mcp/connections/:alias/tools/:toolName` body schema extended with `rateLimitPerMin?: number | null` (integer in `[1, 10_000]` or `null` to clear); new audit action `mcp.tool.rate_limit_set` fires only on actual change with `metadata: { alias, toolName, before, after }`. AGENTS.md MCP client bullet updated to document the override + drop "per-tool rate-limit overrides" from the v2 carve-out + bump the audit-actions count from 6 to 7. 2 new executor tests (descriptor override wins; null falls back to caller default) + 3 new route tests (positive int persists + audits, null clears + audits, 10_001 rejected with 400). No web change (v1 API-only). **Status note (2026-05-18b):** `exposeToAi` per-connection flag with prompt-injection sanitisation shipped. New nullable `expose_to_ai boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` column on `mcp_connections` (migration `20260518173101_odd_proudstar`); `updateConnection` in `packages/data/src/mcpConnectionsRepo.ts` now returns `{ before, after }` so the existing admin route can audit on-change; new `listExposedMcpToolsForAi(orgId)` helper joins connections × descriptors scoped to `(enabled, exposeToAi, enabled)` and caps at 60 tools / 20 KB total with a `(N more truncated)` footer. New `sanitizeMcpToolDescription` in `packages/shared/src/error-signature.ts` strips ASCII control chars → reuses `scrubSecretShapes` → length-caps at 300 chars. New `composeGenerationSystemPrompt(base, exposedTools)` in `apps/api/src/ai-prompts.ts` returns `base` unchanged when no tools are exposed (zero behavior change for non-opt-in orgs) and otherwise appends a data-framed section with each tool prefixed by `- <alias>.<toolName>:` so injection attempts read as list items not commands. Admin route `POST /mcp/connections/:alias` gains `exposeToAi?: boolean` body field; new audit action `mcp.connection.expose_to_ai_set` fires only on actual change with `before`/`after` metadata. Awareness-only in v1 — the LLM is told to emit a `noop` placeholder with id `mcp_<alias>_<toolName>` (mirror of the `wait_*` / `schedule_*` convention) which the operator promotes manually in the Inspector. Automatic Pass-2 promotion (noop → `mcp_tool`) is a future ticket; the grammar cap of 11 branches stays intact. 5 new shared-package sanitiser tests + 3 new route tests (exposeToAi true persists+audits, reverse audits, omitted field stays silent) + 3 new prompt-composer tests (empty list = base unchanged, non-empty appends data-framed section, anti-pattern guidance present). AGENTS.md MCP client bullet rewritten: the "No LLM exposure in v1" carve-out is replaced with the new "Opt-in LLM exposure" paragraph; v2 carve-out collapses to just "HTTP transport". Audit-actions count bumped from 7 to 8. No web change (v1 API-only). **Status note (2026-05-18c):** Final closeout — HTTP (Streamable HTTP per MCP spec 2025-06-18) transport + standalone architecture doc shipped. New `"http"` value on the closed `McpTransport` union (`packages/data/src/mcpConnectionsRepo.ts`); `transport` column is text so no migration needed. New `createHttpMcpClient` factory in `packages/engine/src/mcp-client.ts` mirrors `createSseMcpClient`: validates URL via `validateHttpTarget` BEFORE constructing `StreamableHTTPClientTransport`, passes headers as `requestInit.headers`. New http branch in `buildClientForConnection` (`packages/engine/src/mcp-tool-executor.ts`); `McpToolEnvelope.transport` widened to `"stdio" \| "sse" \| "http"`. Admin route accepts `transport: "http"` with `url` required; existing `:alias` update route allows URL updates on http connections too. Same DNS-rebinding gap from sse v1 applies (SDK fetch path doesn't share the pinned `undici.Agent` dispatcher) — documented in both the AGENTS.md bullet and the new architecture doc. New `docs/architecture/mcp-client.md` consolidates the AGENTS.md MCP client bullet into an 11-section maintainer doc (overview, three transports, two-flag write consent, per-tool opt-in, four sanitisation layers, per-tool rate limit, env-ref resolution, dry-run gate, audit + usage telemetry, permission catalog, multi-tenant scope). AGENTS.md MCP client bullet rewritten: "Two transports" → "Three transports" with the http description inline, the now-closed v1 carve-out line dropped, doc cross-reference added. 3 new mcp-client tests (http SSRF rejection, headers pass-through, no requestInit when no headers) + 1 new executor dispatch test + 3 new route tests (http create + run discovery, empty-url rejection, url update on http). No web change (v1 API-only — Inspector dropdown for http appears in a future ticket). Closes ENG-057 entirely. |
| ENG-058 | Build Workflow Review Agent | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Add a second-pass AI reviewer that grades generated or edited workflows before save/run. The reviewer checks retries, timeouts, HTTP response caps, secret handling, missing approvals, unknown tools, malformed routers, missing outputs, ambiguous prompts, and PII/sensitive-action risk. AC: `/ai/review-workflow` returns structured findings with severity, affected node/edge, rationale, and suggested patch; Save/Run can surface blocking P0/P1 findings; tests cover safe workflow, missing timeout, secret risk, dangerous action without approval, and malformed router. **Summary:** New `POST /ai/review-workflow` route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` mirrors the existing `/ai/generate-workflow` and `/ai/explain-workflow` posture (rate-limited via the shared `"ai"` bucket; wraps `llm.generateObject` in try/catch; audits both AI-mode and fallback paths as `ai.workflow.reviewed`). The structured-output schema `ReviewFindingsSchema` is a flat `{ status, issues: ReviewFindingSchema[] }` shape capped at 50 issues per response — concrete-typed throughout (no `z.unknown()`, no recursion) so it works on Anthropic + OpenAI strict mode. The new `REVIEW_WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT` gives the model a severity rubric (info/warn/fail), a checklist tied to the AC (retries, bounds, hardcoded secrets, approval upstream of write-side, unknown tools, malformed routers, missing outputs, ambiguous prompts, PII risk), and per-finding format (code/severity/message/nodeId?/edgeId?/rationale/suggestion). New `packages/engine/src/workflow-review-fallback.ts` provides `buildReviewFallback(workflow)` that combines `validateWorkflow` structural issues with ENG-059's `checkWorkflowReadiness` and reshapes both into the review format with deterministic rationale strings, `mergeReviewFindings(primary, fallback)` that appends omitted deterministic blockers after AI sanitization, plus `sanitizeAiReview(review, workflow)` that drops findings whose `nodeId`/`edgeId` doesn't resolve (LLM hallucination guard) and re-rolls the status from the surviving issues — same defense-in-depth pattern as `sanitizeAiWorkflow` for the generation route. The fallback path fires when the LLM is unavailable (no key, quota, network) so the response shape stays consistent and `mode: "fallback"` flags the source per the AGENTS.md AI-fallback contract. AI Copilot panel (`apps/web/src/components/AiCopilotPanel.tsx`) gets a third "Review this flow" button alongside Generate and Explain; findings render inline using the same `we-readiness-issue--*` CSS tokens as the production-readiness badge so severity colors are consistent. 7 new engine tests cover the fallback (clean/missing-retry/raw-secret/malformed-router), deterministic merge, and the sanitizer (drops hallucinated nodeIds/edgeIds, re-rolls status). Save/Run is NOT gated on AI review — the AC's "surface blocking findings" is interpreted as UI-level surfacing; gating `/start` would add multi-second LLM latency to every run plus fallback-path risk. ENG-059's deterministic readiness gate already handles production-mode blocking. **Reviewer fixes (collateral):** (a) all three fallback paths in the route now write an audit row (`ai.workflow.reviewed` with `mode: "fallback"` + reason) — the original implementation skipped audit on the shape-invalid and no-LLM paths, which was consistent with the sibling routes' existing behavior but inconsistent with the documented "AI mutations write audit on success AND fallback" invariant; the route now records every AI surface call regardless of which fallback path fires; (b) `sanitizeAiReview`'s edge-id resolution no longer synthesizes `edge_${index}` placeholders for edges without an explicit `id` — the previous implementation would let an AI-hallucinated `edgeId: "edge_3"` survive sanitization because the index happened to land on a real but unnamed edge, surfacing a finding the operator couldn't locate in the canvas; only edges that carry a real `id` string contribute to the resolution set now; (c) the fallback and AI-success paths now append structural `validateWorkflow` blockers so a schema-valid but graph-invalid workflow (for example, a router candidate pointing at a missing node) cannot pass review just because the LLM omitted the finding. |
| ENG-059 | Add Production Readiness Check | Shipped | P1 | 2 | Add a deterministic readiness gate for workflows, separate from AI review. Checks include retries on external calls, timeout and response caps, declared outputs, approval/human gate for sensitive actions, credential references instead of raw secrets, eval coverage, and rollback availability. AC: `/workflows/readiness` returns pass/warn/fail with issue list; UI shows a "Production Ready" badge; workflows can still run in dev but production mode can block fail-level issues. **Summary:** New pure `checkWorkflowReadiness(workflow)` in `packages/engine/src/workflow-readiness.ts` runs 6 deterministic checks: `http_missing_bounds` (warn — http nodes without explicit `timeoutMs`/`maxResponseBytes`/`maxRedirects`), `external_node_missing_retry` (fail — http/tool/ai/agent without `config.retry.maxAttempts >= 2`), `workflow_missing_outputs` (warn), `sensitive_action_missing_approval` (warn — POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE http or write-side tool name without an `approval` ancestor in the DAG), `raw_secret_in_config` (fail — sensitive-keyed field values that aren't `{{secret.X}}` / `{{env.X}}` templates), `workflow_missing_evals` (warn — placeholder until eval tracking lands). Reuses `SENSITIVE_KEY_PATTERN` from `safe-persist.ts` so the secret-key list stays in lockstep across the persistence chokepoint and the readiness gate. The status rolls up to `pass`/`warn`/`fail` (worst severity wins). New `POST /workflows/readiness` route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` (next to `/validate`, same `role: "editor"` posture) returns the result, layering on a DB-aware `checkRollbackAvailability` helper that emits `workflow_missing_rollback_version` (warn) when `count(workflow_versions) < 2` for the saved id. Production-mode gate: when env `JANUSLY_PRODUCTION_MODE=true`, `POST /start` runs the readiness check before delegating to `startRun` and rejects fail-level workflows with HTTP 422 + the issue list. Dev mode (env unset) keeps the existing behavior unchanged. New `WorkflowReadinessBadge` component (`apps/web/src/components/WorkflowReadinessBadge.tsx`) renders in the App header next to the brand lockup — colored dot + label (`Production Ready` / `N warnings` / `N blockers`); click expands an inline list with `code`, message, suggestion. Refetches on the `platformVersion` tick (cross-panel reactivity hook). 13 new engine tests covering each check + the rollup logic. AGENTS.md note pins the new env flag so future maintainers know to extend the readiness check (not bypass it). Sister to (not overlapping with) ENG-058's future AI Workflow Review Agent — this is rules-only and never falls back. **Reviewer fixes (collateral):** (a) the `workflow_missing_evals` placeholder is now opt-in via env `JANUSLY_REQUIRE_EVAL_COVERAGE=true` so a clean workflow can actually reach `status: "pass"` and the badge can show a green "Production Ready" — without this, the always-on warn made the green state unreachable for any workflow today; (b) `WorkflowReadinessBadge` now selects `getWorkflowJson` from the Zustand store directly (`useWorkflowStore((s) => s.getWorkflowJson)`) instead of taking it as a prop — App.tsx's destructuring of the whole store produces a fresh local binding on every re-render (every node drag, every polling tick), which would have made the badge re-fetch on every tick. The selector returns the stable function reference. |
| ENG-060 | Build Replay Lab | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Create a run replay sandbox for failed and successful runs. Operators can replay with the same captured input, replay with a proposed patch, compare node outputs/cost/latency, and approve a new version only after replay passes. AC: UI exposes “Replay in Lab” from run details and DLQ; replay runs are tagged as sandbox/eval; production side effects are either disabled or require explicit tool-level replay policy; comparison report shows changed nodes, cost, latency, and pass/fail. **Summary:** Standalone surface assembled on top of ENG-077's sandbox infra. New `replayRunAsValidation` adapter in `packages/engine/src/adapters/replay-lab.ts` writes a fresh `runs.replayMode="validation"` row + seeds all nodes pending + enqueues root nodes in one transaction; mirrors `startRun`'s atomicity contract. Trigger input from the source run flows into the new run's `inputJson.input` so `{{input.*}}` references resolve to the same values the source saw. New `POST /runs/replay-lab` route (`editor`, `"ai"` rate-limit bucket) rejects nested labs (source `replayMode` already set), resolves the workflow snapshot from `workflow_versions.dagJson` → `runs.inputJson.workflow` (ad-hoc fallback) → caller-supplied `suggestedWorkflow` (validated through `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow`), and audits `replay_lab.started`. New `GET /runs/compare` route (`viewer`) delegates to `getRunComparison` in `packages/data/src/runComparisonRepo.ts`, which queries `runs` + `run_nodes` + `usage_events` (multi-tenant scoped) and aggregates per-node `{ base, replay }` bundles with status/latency/cost/tokens/output. The route registry's `GET /runs` prefix matcher excludes `/runs/compare` so first-match-wins routing works. Reuses `replayMode="validation"` (no new replay-mode value) so the existing dryRun gating, write-side skips, and `isNull(runs.replayMode)` rollup exclusions apply uniformly; audit action (`replay_lab.started` vs `recovery.validation_started`) distinguishes lab vs recovery intent. Web side: new `<ReplayLabDialog />` overlay modal with state machine `idle → starting → replaying → comparing → done | error` (polls `/run` at 1500ms, mirrors `RecoveryDialog`'s polling shape with `aliveRef` for unmount safety), new `<RunComparisonView />` per-node table with status pills + `Equal/Changed/Only base/Only replay` chips + latency/cost deltas (stable-stringify for key-order-tolerant output diff). Entry buttons in three surfaces: DLQ selected-entry action bar, RunsPanel active-run card (only for terminal non-validation runs), per-history-row compact Lab button. ESC + backdrop + close button are all blocked during `starting/replaying/comparing` so the operator can't accidentally toss an in-flight lab. The history-row layout switched from a single `<button>` to a `<div role="group">` with two interactive children; `.list-card-row:focus-visible` restores the focus ring lost to `all: unset`. New tests: 7 engine adapter cases pinning runs/runNodes/runEvents inserts + root-node discovery + trigger-input propagation; 7 RunComparisonView cases pinning equality classification + delta formatting + summary; 5 ReplayLabDialog cases pinning the state machine + error envelope. Web workspace tests +12 (131 → 143), engine +1 (471 → 472). |
| ENG-061 | Cluster failure patterns | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Group failures by normalized error signature, node type, tool, provider, and workflow. AC: Recovery Queue shows clusters such as “Missing secret: GITHUB_TOKEN”, “HTTP 401 on GitHub node”, and “OpenAI timeout on ai node”; each cluster shows frequency, affected workflows, first/last seen, impact, and suggested owner; clustering runs without leaking secret values. **Summary:** New pure normalizer `normalizeErrorSignature(error, context)` in `packages/engine/src/error-signature.ts` runs 7 first-match-wins rules in priority order: `secret_missing` (regex on message + explicit `code: "E_SECRET_MISSING"`), `http_error` (`error.statusCode` 4xx/5xx, then `HTTP 4xx` regex), `network_timeout` (`ECONNRESET`/`ETIMEDOUT`/`timeout`), `ai_provider` (`error.aiError` envelope + reason matchers — `quota exceeded` / `rate limit` / `context too long` / `model not found` — plus provider inference), `parse_error` (JSON / unexpected token), `tool_input` (invalid tool input + tool not found), `unknown` (fallback that scrubs + truncates to 80 chars). Defense-in-depth: every produced signature passes through `scrubSecretShapes` before return — closed regex set covers `sk-…` / `ghp_…` / `xox[baprs]-…` / `AKIA…` / `Bearer …` / 3-segment JWT `eyJ…` patterns and replaces matches with `[redacted]`. The persistence chokepoint already key-redacts the JSON we read from, but error MESSAGES are free-form strings; this scrub is the last line of defence. New pure aggregator `clusterFailureSamples(samples)` in `packages/engine/src/cluster-failures.ts` deduplicates by `(runId, nodeId)` preferring the DLQ-source sample (so a failed run that landed in DLQ counts once), groups by signature, aggregates affected-workflow counts via inner `Map<workflowId, ClusterWorkflow>`, tracks min/max `createdAt`, keeps up to 5 representative sample refs, sorts descending by frequency. New repo `collectFailureSamples(orgId, windowDays = 30, limit = 500)` in `packages/data/src/failureClusterRepo.ts` runs two parallel queries — one over `dead_letters` scoped by `dead_letters.org_id`, one over failed `run_nodes` scoped by `runs.org_id`; saved workflows left-join through `workflow_versions` for names and DAG metadata, while ad-hoc runs fall back to the persisted workflow snapshot. Pulls `nodeType` from the persisted DAG/`nodeJson` so the signatures get useful suffixes (`HTTP 401 on http node`). The DLQ writer now carries run `orgId` into `dead_letters` so non-default tenants show up in both DLQ lists and cluster rollups. New `GET /dlq/clusters?windowDays=…` route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` (`role: "viewer"`) registered BEFORE the wildcard `/dlq` dispatcher (first-match-wins in the route registry — wildcard would otherwise swallow it). Web side: new `FailureClustersCard.tsx` mounted at the top of the Recovery panel inside `DeadLettersPanel.tsx`. Each cluster row shows the signature, category pill (`Secret`/`HTTP`/`Network`/`AI provider`/`Parse`/`Tool`/`Other`), frequency, affected-workflow count, last-seen relative time, and suggested-owner chip (`Ops`/`Workflow author`/`Platform`); click expands to show per-workflow occurrence counts + recent run ids. Severity bar on the left edge: `secret_missing`/`ai_provider` → red, everything else → amber. Reuses the cross-panel `platformVersion` selector for refetch and the existing `panel-card`/`field-label`/`mode-pill` design tokens. 24 new engine tests pin every category, the secret-shape scrubs (Bearer/sk-/ghp_/JWT), the non-empty unknown fallback, DLQ org propagation, the deduplication contract, and the descending-frequency sort. 3 web component tests cover loaded, empty, and error states for the cluster card. Engine workspace tests +24 (240 → 264). |
| ENG-062 | Add Workflow Health Score | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Score each workflow across reliability, safety, cost, latency, maintainability, and AI risk. Inputs include success rate, DLQ count, retries, approvals, secret hygiene, token/cost usage, p95 latency, missing outputs, and readiness/review findings. AC: workflow list and detail page show score + breakdown; score recalculates from recent runs and static checks; tests cover score changes after failed runs, missing timeouts, and added approval gates. **Summary:** New pure aggregator `computeWorkflowHealth({ workflow, readiness, signals })` in `packages/engine/src/workflow-health.ts` produces a 0–100 rollup with six per-category sub-scores: reliability (success rate − DLQ penalty − retry penalty, capped), safety (readiness fail/warn penalties), cost (band $0.01/run → 100, $1.00/run → 30), latency (band p95 5s → 100, 60s → 30), maintainability (declared outputs + multiple versions + approval node), AI risk (AI-node count + token volume + raw-secret findings). Weighted overall: 0.30 reliability + 0.25 safety + 0.15 latency + 0.10 cost + 0.10 maintainability + 0.10 aiRisk; status thresholds `≥80 healthy / ≥60 warn / <60 unhealthy`. Constants exported (`BREAKDOWN_WEIGHTS`, `NEUTRAL_DEFAULT`, `STATUS_THRESHOLDS`, per-category bands) so future tuning lives in one place. Neutral default 80 covers brand-new workflows with insufficient data (totalRuns < 5 → reliability/cost neutral; p95 null → latency neutral). New repo `collectHealthSignals(orgId, workflowId, windowDays = 30)` in `packages/data/src/workflowHealthRepo.ts` runs four batched queries across `runs`, `run_events` (retry count + p95 latency), `dead_letters` (open count), `usage_events` (cost + tokens), and `workflow_versions` (distinct version count). Multi-tenant scope via `eq(workflowVersions.orgId, orgId)` AND `eq(workflowVersions.workflowId, workflowId)` on the run query; subsequent queries gated on the `runIds` set. The `HealthSignals` type is duplicated structurally between `packages/data` and `packages/engine` to keep the dependency direction clean (engine → data, never the reverse). New `GET /workflows/health?workflowId=…` route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` (`role: "viewer"` since health is post-save reporting, distinct from `/workflows/readiness` which is `editor`) confirms workflow ownership, loads the latest `workflow_versions` row, runs `checkWorkflowReadiness` on the parsed DSL, then composes `collectHealthSignals` + `computeWorkflowHealth` into the typed response. New `WorkflowHealthBadge` component (`apps/web/src/components/WorkflowHealthBadge.tsx`) renders a colored dot + "Health: 87 / 100"; click expands the six per-category sub-scores with rationale text. Refetches on the `platformVersion` tick (cross-panel reactivity). Mounted in two surfaces: App header next to `WorkflowReadinessBadge` (live workflow's score), and per-row in `WorkflowsDashboard` (compact mode without label). Skipped (`null`) when `workflowId` is undefined (ad-hoc unsaved workflows). Reuses `we-readiness-badge--*` and `we-readiness-issue--*` CSS tokens — same visual language as the readiness badge. 17 new engine tests covering each category band, the rollup math, and the AC's three assertions ("score changes after failed runs / missing timeouts / added approval gates"). Engine workspace lands at 240 (was 221). |
| ENG-063 | Export Run Explain Report | Shipped | P2 | 3 | Let operators export a failure/recovery report to Markdown/JSON and optionally send it to Slack/Linear/GitHub. Report includes timeline, root cause, failed node, attempts, redacted inputs/outputs, suggested fix, owner, and next action. AC: export never includes raw secrets; report is accessible from run detail and Recovery Queue; fixture test snapshots validate shape. **Summary:** Shipped Markdown + JSON download via a new `GET /reports/run-explain?runId=…&format=markdown\|json` route (`role: "viewer"`, multi-tenant scoped via `eq(runs.orgId, auth.orgId)`). New pure builder `buildRunExplainReport` in `packages/engine/src/run-explain-report.ts` consumes `{ run, runNodes, runEvents, recoveryAudit? }` snapshots and produces both formats from the same structured envelope: summary (id / status / workflow version / replayMode), root cause (via the shared `normalizeErrorSignature` taxonomy — category + `suggestedOwner` of `ops`/`workflow_author`/`platform`), failed node (id / status / attempts / redacted error summary), timeline (capped at 50 most-recent events with a truncation notice), Suggested fix (reads from the most recent `ai.workflow.patch_suggested` audit row — mode / topApproachLabel / envelopeKind / patchStyle / suggestionsCount), and next action (operator-actionable string keyed off the suggested owner). The route writes `report.run_explain.exported` audit rows tagged `{ format, recoveryAuditFound }` so there's a trail of who exported what. Defense-in-depth: every rendered free-form string passes through `scrubSecretShapes` even though source rows are already redacted at write time by `safePersistPayload` — Bearer tokens, `sk-…`/`ghp_…`/`xox[baprs]-…` shapes, and JWT segments are scrubbed. Web side: new `downloadFromApi(path, filename?)` helper in `apps/web/src/api.ts` mirrors `api()`'s auth resolution (Supabase JWT OR dev headers) but reads `res.blob()` + clicks a synthetic anchor so the browser downloads as a file rather than rendering text inline. Two Export entry points: the DLQ panel's selected-entry action bar next to "Suggest fix" / "Retry" / "Resolve" / "Replay in Lab", and per-row on RunsPanel history (next to the existing Lab button). Both buttons use the standard `small-command` token and a `lucide-react` `Download` icon. **v1 scope explicitly narrow:** download only — external delivery (Slack / Linear / GitHub) is documented as a clear future follow-up (would add a `POST /reports/run-explain/deliver` route + reuse the existing `slack.post` / `webhook.send` / `github.create_issue` integration tools). New tests: 10 builder cases (clean succeeded run skips root-cause / failed-node sections; failed run renders both with the correct suggested owner; multi-failure run picks the latest failed node; recovery-audit row renders the Suggested fix block; legacy audit without patchStyle surfaces "unknown"; secret-shape values in error messages are scrubbed to `[redacted]`; timeline truncation kicks in at the cap with a Markdown notice; empty timeline shows "No events recorded yet"; validation runs flag `replayMode` in the summary), and 6 route cases (Markdown happy path + Content-Disposition header + audit row, JSON variant, recovery audit auto-discovery, missing runId 400, unknown format 400, cross-org / missing run 404 with no enumeration leak). No AGENTS.md change — the report is read-only and reuses existing redaction. |
| ENG-064 | Add recovery metrics dashboard | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Add business-value metrics for selling and operating Janusly: workflow success rate, mean time to recovery, failed runs by pattern, cost by model/provider, p95 latency, approvals pending, auto-fixes suggested/applied, rollbacks triggered, and replay pass/fail rate. AC: dashboard reads bounded/preaggregated queries; web shows a Recovery/Operations overview; metrics can be used in demos without manual DB queries. **Summary:** New repo `collectRecoveryMetricsSignals(orgId, windowDays = 30)` in `packages/data/src/recoveryMetricsRepo.ts` runs six parallel windowed queries — run status counts, MTTR samples (replayed-DLQ `replayedAt - createdAt`), human approvals pending (`run_nodes.status='waiting'` with the approval waiting reason), cost by provider/model (grouped in JS), p95 latency (nearest-rank), replay outcomes from rows with `replayedAt` stamped — with multi-tenant scope on every query. New pure rollup `composeRecoveryMetrics` in `packages/engine/src/recovery-metrics.ts` produces six `RecoveryMetric` cards with severity bands exported as constants: success rate ≥95% healthy / ≥85% warn; MTTR ≤2m healthy / ≤15m warn; p95 latency ≤5s healthy / ≤30s warn; approvals 0 healthy / ≤5 neutral / >5 warn; replay rate ≥90% healthy / ≥70% warn; cost is `neutral`. New `GET /recovery/metrics?windowDays=N` route (`role: "viewer"`). Web side: new `'operations'` tab added to `ActiveTab`; `BuilderSidebar.tsx` gets a new "Operations" nav button with a `Gauge` lucide icon; `RightPanel.tsx` routes the tab to `OperationsPanel.tsx` which renders six metric cards in a responsive grid with severity-tinted left borders, a thin progress bar on the success-rate card, a per-provider cost breakdown table below the grid, and the existing `<FailureClustersCard />` embedded so operators get clusters in the same tab. Refetches on `platformVersion` tick. 14 new engine tests pin every severity band, MTTR formatting, cost rollup with provider canonicalisation, duration formatter, and null handling; 3 web tests cover the Operations panel loading/success/error states. **Out of scope:** "auto-fixes suggested/applied" + "rollbacks triggered" counters from the AC — both need new audit-log actions that don't exist yet; surface stays absent in v1, will plug in when the Recovery MVP ships the apply path. **Collateral fix from manual smoke:** `failureClusterRepo.queryFailedRunNodes` was passing the `since` Date inside a `sql` template literal — postgres-js renders Dates inside `sql` parameters via `String()` which yields the JS `Date.toString()` form ("Sat Apr 04 2026 …") instead of an ISO timestamp; postgres rejected the bind and the Operations panel + Runs tab cluster card both surfaced "Failed query: …" errors. Fixed by passing `since.toISOString()` with an explicit `::timestamptz` cast inside the same `sql` template. The `eq(workflowVersions.orgId, …)` predicate was also dropped to `eq(runs.orgId, …)` so org scope flows through `runs.orgId` (canonical) rather than the optional `workflow_versions` join (ad-hoc workflows have no version row). |

New tests: tools.test.ts catalog assertion bumped to 15 + 12 dispatcher cases (3 per tool: happy path / missing-or-empty-required-arg / out-of-shape optional). v1 carve-outs: `POST /dlq/validate-fix` (sandbox replay writes a `runs` row), bulk `workflows.health.all` (existing `workflows.list` + per-workflow `workflows.health` covers it), MCP resources surface (different SDK shape) all deferred to future tickets. |
| ENG-072 | Add visual workflow diff | Shipped | P1 | 2 | Make workflow version changes understandable to operators, not just JSON diffs. Show nodes added/removed, edge changes, config changes, secretRef changes, approval/retry/timeout additions, and AI patch rationale. AC: diff UI supports old vs new workflow versions and proposed patches; used by Recovery MVP before applying a fix; tests cover node/edge/config diff. Expands ENG-039. **Summary:** New pure aggregator `computeWorkflowDiff(before, after)` in `packages/shared/src/workflow-diff.ts` produces `WorkflowDiff` with workflow-level field changes, node-level changes (added/removed/changed), and edge-level changes (added/removed/condition-changed). Lives in `@janusly/shared` (zero runtime deps) so the web bundle imports it via `@janusly/shared/src/workflow-diff` subpath without pulling the engine graph. Tag classifier marks AC-required signals: `secret_ref` (path's last segment matches `SENSITIVE_KEY_PATTERN` OR value uses `{{secret.X}}` / `{{env.X}}` template), `retry` (`config.retry` / `config.retry.*`), `timeout` (`config.timeoutMs`), `bounds` (`config.maxResponseBytes` / `config.maxRedirects`), `approval` (added/removed `approval` node), `rationale` (workflow-level cosmetic — `name`, `metadata.*`). Deterministic sort: nodes by id within each kind bucket, edges by key, fields by path. Reruns produce deep-equal output so the UI never flickers. Also moved `SENSITIVE_KEY_PATTERN` from `packages/engine/src/safe-persist.ts` → `packages/shared/src/sensitive-keys.ts` so the diff classifier and the persistence chokepoint share a single source of truth — adding a new key shape there improves redaction at write time AND secret-ref tagging at diff time without one layer depending on the other. New `WorkflowDiffView.tsx` (~280 lines) renders the diff in three sections (workflow / nodes / edges) with severity-tinted rows: green (added), red (removed), amber (changed). Per-field tag pills (`Retry` amber, `Secret-ref` red with `ShieldAlert` lucide icon, `Approval` red, `Timeout`/`Bounds` amber, `Metadata` info-blue). Optional `aiPatchRationale` prop renders below the diff sections inside an info-toned helper-text block — Recovery MVP hookup point. `VersionHistoryPanel.tsx` got a "Compare" toggle: when off, click-a-version-to-hydrate works as before; when on, each row gets a checkbox (max 2 selectable; oldest selection drops on third click) and the inline `<WorkflowDiffView>` renders below the list with the older version always on the left regardless of click order. 17 new shared-workspace tests cover empty/identity, add/remove/change for nodes + edges, every tag-classifier branch (including the path-vs-template secret_ref distinction), workflow-level field changes, determinism. 6 new web jsdom tests cover the renderer's summary line, empty state, secret redaction, AI-rationale rendering, Compare-mode selection, and clearing stale compare state when the active workflow changes. No new API route — the existing `GET /workflows/versions` already returns `dagJson` per version, the diff is computed in the browser. |

| ENG-074 | Stream HTTP response bodies | Shipped | P2 | 3 | Today `fetchHttpTarget` returns `HttpResult.body: string` — the body cap (default 1 MB, configurable via `init.maxResponseBytes`) prevents OOM but limits use cases that legitimately need large fetches (multi-MB CSV, PDF, image processing). Add an opt-in streaming mode so consumers can iterate the body chunk-by-chunk without buffering the whole payload. AC: `fetchHttpTarget(url, { bodyMode: "stream" })` returns `HttpResult & { body: ReadableStream<Uint8Array> }`; the existing byte-counter still aborts the stream when the cap is exceeded; `transform`, `condition` evaluator, and persistence path (`run_nodes.state_json.output.body`) gain explicit handling for stream-typed bodies (truncate to a configurable preview for audit, full stream available downstream); recipes for "fetch a 5 MB CSV and process row-by-row" added to docs. Tracked as a follow-up to ENG-048 — the current bound-execution chokepoint is the right place for the new mode to plug in. **Summary:** `fetchHttpTarget` gains TypeScript overloads on a new `bodyMode?: "buffer" \| "stream"` option (default `"buffer"`, zero breaking change). Streaming returns `{ body: ReadableStream<Uint8Array>, ... }` backed by a TransformStream-style reader that shares the existing `AbortController` — `maxResponseBytes` aborts mid-flight with the same descriptive error string the buffered path emits, and the `Content-Length` pre-check rejects oversized declared bodies before opening the stream. The pinned `undici.Agent` + DNS-rebinding guard are untouched. New `consumeStreamToPreview(stream, previewBytes)` helper drains the stream into a UTF-8 preview + total byte count, propagating the cap-exceeded error if it fires during preview read. The `http` node executor and `http.request` tool both gain `bodyMode` + `streamPreviewBytes` knobs and auto-consume to a JSON-safe `{ body: <preview>, streamed: true, streamedBytes, streamTruncated }` envelope before returning so persistence stays clean. Defense-in-depth: `safePersistPayload` substitutes any leaked `ReadableStream` with `{ __stream: true, note }`; `template.getByPath` throws the new `StreamValueInTemplateError` (carrying the offending dotted path) when a `{{...}}` reference resolves to a stream; `expression.getBySafePath` throws an analogous error so `condition` nodes can't silently coerce a stream to a boolean. New `http.streamPreviewBytes` org-config key (range 1024..1_048_576, default 65536, env fallback `JANUSLY_HTTP_STREAM_PREVIEW_BYTES`) controls per-tenant preview size. New `docs/nodes.md` "Streaming HTTP responses (5 MB CSV recipe)" section. v1 carve-out: streams must be consumed within the same executor invocation; cross-node live streaming stays out of scope. **Status note (2026-05-18d.1):** Remaining work closed by ENG-105 — `csv.fetch` tool ships the row-by-row processor that consumes the streaming body through the shared RFC 4180 parser, bounded sample + counts persisted instead of the full payload. ENG-074 is now Shipped end-to-end. |
| ENG-075 | Fix AI workflow generation schema for OpenAI strict mode and Anthropic | Shipped | P0 | — | `/ai/generate-workflow` was broken on every provider in five stacked layers, all of which had to be peeled before AI Studio actually worked. (1) OpenAI strict mode rejected `propertyNames` emitted by every `z.record(z.string(), X)`. (2) Anthropic via the AI SDK rejected the circular `$ref` from `WorkflowInputSchema`'s `z.lazy(() => ...)` self-reference. (3) Once schema rejection was bypassed, the model itself returned semantically broken workflows (router with empty `candidates`, http with no `url`) because `NodeSchema.config` is `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())` and gave zero per-node-type guidance. (4) Anthropic structured output rejected `z.unknown()` / `z.any()` fields with "Empty schema that accepts any JSON value is not supported"; every free-form field had to be typed concretely or dropped. (5) Anthropic's grammar compiler caps schema size — empirically the limit is identical across Haiku/Sonnet/Opus 4.5+ (all three accept up to 11 discriminated-union branches with the full envelope; 12+ rejects with "compiled grammar is too large"), so the AI side covers a deliberate 11-type subset rather than the then-current engine's full 16; ENG-033 later kept the cap but changed the selected 11 after the engine grew to 20. Bonus: a stale `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=https://api.anthropic.com` shell var (set by Claude Desktop) silently routed to `/messages` instead of `/v1/messages` and 404ed every call — the SDK respects the env override as-is. Pre-existing since ENG-028 introduced the recursive `inputs` schema. AC: `/ai/generate-workflow` produces a real AI-authored workflow for representative prompts; the fix preserves `WorkflowSchema` as the engine's strict parse target. **Summary:** Replaced the omit-based slim schema with a typed `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema` in `apps/api/src/index.ts` — a `z.discriminatedUnion("type", [...])` over 11 node types (`noop`, `http`, `transform`, `condition`, `ai`, `tool`, `agent`, `router`, `approval`, `multi_agent`, `loop`), each with its required config fields explicit and free-form `z.unknown()` either typed (`z.record(z.string(), z.string())` for mappings) or omitted (headers, body, tool input — operator fills in Inspector). At the time, the remaining 5 types (`wait_until`, `webhook`, `agent_reflection`, `router_llm`, `subworkflow`) stayed operator-only — AI emitted a `noop` placeholder named after the requested step and the operator promoted it. **Updated by ENG-033:** the current 11-type set is `noop`, `http`, `transform`, `condition`, `ai`, `tool`, `agent`, `router`, `approval`, `human_form`, `loop`; `multi_agent` is now operator-only/noop-promotion. The system prompt gained a SUPPORTED-TYPES rule, a PLACEHOLDER rule, a ROUTER rule, and a worked-example JSON of a smart-router workflow. Defensive `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` handling in `packages/ai/src/llm-client.ts` auto-appends `/v1` when the env var points at the canonical host without it — fixes Claude Desktop's stale shell injection without disturbing intentional proxy overrides. `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` default updated to `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` (the dated suffix; the bare `claude-haiku-4-5` doesn't resolve). The post-generation `sanitizeAiWorkflow` step still re-validates against the strict engine `WorkflowSchema`, so the engine's parse contract is unchanged. Cross-provider unblock without weakening any per-provider strict mode. End-to-end smoke verified 5 representative prompts (smart router, approval gate, multi-agent crew, loop batch, wait-until placeholder) all return `mode: "ai"` with sensibly-shaped workflows. |
| ENG-076 | Expand AI workflow generation to all node types | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Today `/ai/generate-workflow` is capped at 11 of the engine's 20 node types because Anthropic's structured-output grammar compiler rejects schemas with 12+ discriminated-union branches under the full envelope ("The compiled grammar is too large"). The 9 operator-only types (`multi_agent`, `wait_until`, `webhook`, `agent_reflection`, `router_llm`, `subworkflow`, `parallel_fork`, `join`, `schedule`) currently land as `noop` placeholders that the operator promotes in the Inspector. ENG-033 intentionally reorganized the 11 direct-generation slots to include `human_form` and demote `multi_agent`: structured human input is common enough and schema-light enough to generate directly, while multi-agent crews have a large nested shape and usually need operator intent. Empirically the Anthropic limit is identical across Haiku/Sonnet/Opus 4.5+ (it's an API-level grammar cap, not per-model), so just upgrading the default model doesn't help. Investigate paths to recover the missing operator-only types where direct generation is actually worth the complexity: (a) wait for Anthropic to raise the grammar cap and re-test; (b) two-pass generation — first pass scaffolds with the 11-type union, second pass promotes specific noop placeholders to a tighter single-type schema; (c) intent-specific generation surfaces such as `/ai/generate-multi-agent` or `/ai/generate-scheduled-flow`; (d) provider-specific expansion only after OpenAI or another provider is re-opened with a current verification plan. AC: prompts that explicitly mention wait/schedule/subworkflow/fork-join or multi-agent intent get a high-quality promoted workflow path without degrading Anthropic `mode: "ai"` reliability; the engine's strict `WorkflowSchema` remains the runtime source of truth; tests cover at least one recovered type per strategy. Depends on ENG-075. **Summary:** Shipped strategy (b) — two-pass generation — with `wait_until` as the first recovered type. New `promoteNoopPlaceholders` helper in `packages/ai/src/promote-noop.ts` scans Pass-1's slim-schema output for noop nodes whose id starts with `wait_`, `sleep_`, `pause_`, or `delay_` (case-insensitive), then calls Pass-2 against `AiWaitUntilConfigSchema` (single-shape, provider-strict-safe — no `oneOf`, no `propertyNames`, no open `additionalProperties`) with the operator's verbatim prompt as the hint. The LLM extracts the ISO 8601 duration relevant to the detected noop's id and the helper flips the noop's `type` + `config` in place. **Detection is id-prefix, not LLM-emitted metadata:** an initial design asked the model to set `config.promote: "wait_until"` + `config.hint` on placeholders; live-LLM smoke against Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5 showed the model consistently ignored the instruction and emitted empty configs (the path of least resistance). Operators consistently see descriptive ids for time-pause placeholders, the existing prompt already encourages snake_case descriptive ids, so the id-prefix heuristic is reliable in practice. Per-noop failures (LLM throws, schema rejects, whitespace-only duration, empty operator prompt) degrade silently — the offending noop stays unpromoted and the rest of the workflow is preserved, so the response stays `mode: "ai"` with `promotionsSucceeded < promotionAttempts` surfaced in the audit row. `GENERATE_WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT` gains a "WAIT-INTENT NAMING" paragraph and a worked wait-intent example pinning the id convention. Audit metadata on `ai.workflow.generated` extends with `promotionAttempts` + `promotionsSucceeded`; existing readers unaffected. Rate-limit posture documented in the helper's file header: the route's single `enforceRateLimit("ai", ...)` gate fires at entry only; Pass-2 calls bill against the same Redis bucket window but aren't individually capped. AI-fallback contract preserved: Pass-1 failure → existing fallback envelope; Pass-2 catastrophic throw → existing fallback; Pass-2 per-noop failure → `mode: "ai"` with partial-degrade signal. **Strategy-coverage scope:** v1 ships strategy (b) only; the other 3 strategies (a/c/d) stay unimplemented and `wait_until` is the only recovered type. New tests: 11 helper cases (happy path on `wait_3_days`, every documented prefix family recognised case-insensitively with non-alphanumeric separators, edges/workflow-level preservation, no-op passthrough on workflows without wait-intent noops, non-noop with wait-prefixed id ignored, ids that merely contain `wait` mid-string correctly skipped, per-noop LLM-throws isolation, empty operator prompt short-circuit, whitespace-only duration rejection, context + Pass-2 prompt plumbing); 1 prompt-pin case asserting the new "WAIT-INTENT NAMING" paragraph + worked example are in the system prompt body. AGENTS.md AI integration entry extended with the two-pass + partial-degrade contract. **Live-LLM smoke verified:** Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5 against `pnpm dev`: prompt "wait 3 days then call https://example.com/webhook" lands a `wait_until` node with `config.duration: "P3D"` and audit row `promotionAttempts: 1, promotionsSucceeded: 1`. |
| ENG-077 | Sandbox replay before commit (Recovery v2) | Shipped | P1 | 3 | The Recovery MVP (ENG-053) saves the AI-suggested workflow version BEFORE replaying — if the replay re-fails, the regression is persisted and the operator must rollback manually. Tighten the loop: after the operator approves the patch, the recovery dialog runs the replay in a **sandboxed** mode (the `replayDeadLetter` adapter accepts a new `dryRun: true` flag) that re-executes the failed entry against the suggested workflow WITHOUT writing the version row, gates on terminal status, and only commits the save when the sandbox replay reaches `succeeded`. Sandbox runs are tagged `runs.replayMode: "validation"` (new column) so they're excluded from the cluster + health rollups. Write-side tools (`http POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE`, write-side tool registry entries) are gated in sandbox mode — they emit a `dry-run skipped` event instead of executing. AC: Recovery dialog gains a "Validating fix…" step between Review and Apply; the operator only sees "Apply" enabled after sandbox passes; failed sandbox replays surface the new run's errorJson inline so the operator can iterate. **Subsumes parts of ENG-060 (Replay Lab)** — ENG-060 becomes the standalone surface; ENG-077 is the tight Recovery integration. **Summary:** Schema gains `runs.replay_mode` (text, nullable) + `(orgId, replayMode)` index. New engine helper `getRunReplayMode` plus `NodeContext.dryRun` flag plumbed by `executeNode` so node executors can gate writes per run. HTTP node skips POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE in dryRun and emits `node.dry_run.skipped`; safe methods (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS) still execute. `ToolDefinition` gains optional `writeSide` flag (mark `http.request`); tool node executor checks `isToolWriteSide(name)` + refines on `input.method` for http.request, emitting `tool.dry_run.skipped` when gated. New `DLQReplayAdapter.replayDeadLetterAsValidation` writes a fresh run (synthetic `workflowVersionId`, `replayMode: "validation"`, suggested workflow snapshot in `inputJson`, `parentRunId` linkage) in one transaction, then enqueues only the failing node. New `POST /dlq/validate-fix` (`role: "editor"`, "ai" rate-limit bucket) validates the proposed workflow through the same `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow` chain as `/ai/patch-workflow`, calls the adapter, audits `recovery.validation_started`, returns `{ runId }`. `RecoveryDialog` state machine extended with `validating` (polls `GET /run` at 1.5s until terminal) and `validation-failed` (renders the failed node's `errorJson` plus an "Iterate" button) states; the polling effect claims the terminal-status path before yielding so concurrent in-flight polls don't double-fire save+replay. Apply button labeled "Apply & validate"; production save+replay only runs after sandbox `succeeded`. `workflowHealthRepo` and `failureClusterRepo` queries add `isNull(runs.replayMode)` filters so validation runs don't pollute production signals. New tests: dryRun gating on http + tool executors, validation-adapter shape pin, RecoveryDialog success + failure flows, `isToolWriteSide` helper. |

The dialog still runs the ENG-077 sandbox validation once on the representative before saving; after save it calls cluster apply instead of single replay, surfaces "replayed N of M" plus per-row errors, and warns when the batch is capped. Reviewer fixes keep closed DLQ rows out of the bulk list and fall back to a still-open member when the original representative is stale. |
| ENG-079 | One-click workflow rollback | Shipped | P2 | 1 | Every saved workflow has a complete version history in `workflow_versions` but the only path to "go back to v3" is to manually re-save the older `dagJson`. Add a "Rollback to this version" button per row in `VersionHistoryPanel` (visible to editors) that opens a confirmation dialog rendering the existing `WorkflowDiffView` (current → target) and, on confirm, saves the target row's `dagJson` as a new version (preserves history forwards) plus an audit row tagged `workflow.rolled_back` with the source version id. Returns 404 with the same message regardless of cross-org / wrong-workflow / missing — no enumeration leak. Audits `workflow.rolled_back` with `{ sourceVersionId, sourceVersion, newVersion }`. New `RollbackConfirmDialog` reuses `WorkflowDiffView` (current → target labels), runs an `idle → rolling-back → done | error` machine; on success hydrates the canvas to the rolled-back DAG, calls `bumpPlatformVersion()` so sibling panels refetch, and toasts. Snapshot of `(currentId, targetId)` taken at click time so a sibling save bumping `platformVersion` mid-dialog can't shift the diff under the operator. Cancellation guard via `aliveRef` so a rollback that resolves after dismount can't leak hydrate/bump/toast. |

Extend `/ai/patch-workflow` to return `Array<{ workflow, rationale, approachLabel: "add_retry" \| "raise_timeout" \| "swap_secret_ref" \| "add_approval" \| "fix_url" \| "other", confidence: number }>` with up to 3 suggestions. Helper in `packages/ai/src/patch-workflow.ts` returns `{ mode, suggestions: PatchSuggestionItem[], model?, provider?, aiError? }` with the LLM-emitted array verbatim; `SYSTEM_PROMPT` instructs the model to emit 1-3 distinct alternatives ordered most-likely-fix first with self-rated confidence + `approachLabel`. Route in `apps/api/src/index.ts` fan-out-merges each suggestion's `patchedConfig` onto the failing node, validates each through `WorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow`, drops invalid items, sorts survivors by confidence desc, and degrades to fallback when none survive. Response carries the new `suggestions: Array<{ workflow, rationale, approachLabel, confidence }>` plus legacy `suggestedWorkflow`/`rationale` fields mirroring `suggestions[0]` for back-compat. Audit metadata extended with `suggestionsCount` + `topApproachLabel`. `RecoveryDialog` renders a tab strip when `suggestions.length > 1` (selected drives the diff + rationale + Apply); single-suggestion responses keep the old single-pane UI. Tab strip uses ARIA `role="tablist"` + `role="tab"` + `aria-controls` linkage to the tabpanel wrapping the diff view, with arrow/Home/End keyboard navigation. New test suite: 22 envelope cases (`ai-patch-schema.test.ts`), 32 parametrised matrix cases over a 10-fixture catalog (`ai-patch-multi-suggestion.matrix.test.ts`), 6 helper-boundary cases (`patch-workflow.test.ts` rewritten), 7 dialog test cases including back-compat shim, keyboard tab navigation, and tab-reset on Iterate. New `apps/api/src/recovery-matrix-fixtures.ts` is the single source of truth for failure shapes (HTTP 404 typo / 500 transient / 401 / body cap / redirect / timeout / tool input / missing secret / AI provider transient / write-side without approval); `pnpm seed:recovery-matrix` clears and seeds the 10 fixtures into `dead_letters` for manual smoke. Smoke verified on Anthropic claude-haiku-4-5: 5/10 fixtures returned `mode: "ai"` with 1-3 suggestions (`http_500_transient`: 2 add_retry suggestions; `http_timeout`: 3 with raise_timeout 85% / raise_timeout 55% / add_retry 45%; visual tab-swap verified on `http_500_transient` in real Chrome). The 5 fallback fixtures all hit fundamental schema gaps the per-type envelope can't express (HTTP `headers` missing for 401/missing-secret; tool `input` missing for tool_input_invalid; structural patches needed for write-side approval) — already tracked as ENG-088 (provider-strict envelopes for non-resilience nodes) and ENG-083 (structural multi-node patches). |
