No runtime memory store ships in this ticket. Validation is docs review plus catalog tests if org config keys are added. **Summary:** the canonical policy doc lives at [`docs/memory-policy.md`](memory-policy.md) (364 lines covering eligibility, two-flag opt-in consent, per-kind retention table, deletion/export semantics, embedding provider posture, the "memory is customer data, not training data" load-bearing rule, prompt-injection framing, DPA / sub-processor posture, tenant isolation, the `org_configs.memory.*` catalog spec, audit actions, incident response, and approval log). Cross-references already in place: `docs/ai.md` §10, `docs/PLAN.md` §7.1, `docs/ROADMAP.md` §3c. **Catalog merge:** `packages/data/src/orgConfigRepo.ts` now carries the 7 keys from policy §12 — `memory.enabled` (tenant master switch, no env fallback, mirrors `mcp.writeConsent`), `memory.allowedKinds` (CSV, closed-enum validator), `memory.retentionDaysByKind` (JSON-encoded string, JSON-parse + closed-key + per-kind bounds validator), `memory.recallMaxEntries` (1..32, default 8), `memory.recallMaxBytes` (1024..65536, default 8192), `memory.embeddingProvider` (`allowedValues: ["anthropic"]`, empty = env default), `memory.embeddingModel` (free string, empty = env default). The catalog model gains a small opt-in `validate?` hook on `OrgConfigDefinition`, wired through `normalizeOrgConfigValue` after the existing checks — 30+ pre-existing keys are byte-for-byte unchanged. `"memory"` added to `ALLOWED_CATEGORIES`. No `OrgConfigSnapshot.memory` branch and no `applyOrgConfigToEnv` mapping yet — precedent from the `auth` category (narrow `getAuthPolicyConfig` reader); ENG-115 will add a sibling `getMemoryConfig` reader. New `packages/data/src/orgConfigRepo.test.ts` (vitest scaffold stood up alongside, mirroring `@janusly/domain`'s pattern) — 41 pure-unit tests covering the 7 new keys' defaults, range bounds, validator hooks, and a 4-key unchanged-behaviour smoke (`ai.timeoutMs` / `ai.provider` / `mcp.writeConsent` / `auth.allowedEmailDomains`). **ES parity:** [`docs/memory-policy-es.md`](memory-policy-es.md) ships the full Spanish translation (17 sections in the same §0..§16 order; bidirectional 🇬🇧/🇪🇸 link bars on both files; `autoreparable` / `Centro de Recuperación` / `tú` lexicon; code identifiers, env var names, table names, audit action ids, kind enum values, route paths, function names, ticket IDs, provider names, niche anglicism nouns (`sandbox`, `rollback`, `DAG`, `MTTR`, `self-host`, `MCP`, `prompt-injection`), and acronyms (DPA, PII, JWT, AWS, PAT, SCIM) all stay English byte-for-byte; lexicon spot-check confirms no Spanglish verbs or forbidden alternatives in live prose). `docs/memory-policy.md` §16 marks the catalog merge + ES parity checkboxes; the 3 human-review checkboxes (Product / Legal / Engineering) remain open — those land outside the engineering surface. **No runtime memory store, no `memory_entries` table, no `commitMemory` / `recallMemory` helpers, no env plumbing, no audit-action wiring** — all ENG-115 work, now unblocked. |
| ENG-115 | Add provider-neutral vector memory foundation | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Implement the privacy-approved memory substrate after ENG-114. AC: create a tenant-scoped `memory_entries` table with `orgId`, optional `workflowId`, optional `runId`, closed `kind`, scrubbed `content`, provider-neutral `embedding`, embedding provider/model/dimension metadata, bounded metadata, retention timestamps, and indexes that lead with `orgId`; use `@janusly/ai` for embeddings so no OpenAI-specific dimension or provider assumption leaks into schema; add data helpers `commitMemory`, `recallMemory`, and `deleteExpiredMemory` where every query filters by `orgId` before similarity ranking; embedding failures degrade to empty recall and write a warn event without breaking the caller. Tests cover cross-org isolation, retention filtering, embedding timeout fallback, secret-shape scrubbing, disabled-org rejection, metadata correctness for future re-embedding, and query limits. **Summary:** **Infrastructure:** `docker-compose.yml` now runs `pgvector/pgvector:pg18` (single-line swap from `postgres:18-alpine`) and a sibling `ollama` service that pre-pulls `bge-m3` into the `ollama_models` named volume on first boot (~1.2 GB, cached thereafter). `scripts/run-dev.mjs` brings `ollama` up alongside `redis` + `postgres`; production self-host operators who keep `memory.enabled=false` org-wide can omit `ollama` from their `docker compose up <services>` invocation. **Schema:** new `memory_entries` table (`embedding vector(1024) NOT NULL` + `embedding_provider` + `embedding_model` + `embedding_dimension` per-row metadata to support re-embedding on operator-driven provider swap; orgId-leading composite btree indexes for recency / retention scans; HNSW cosine-ops index for similarity ranking). Migration `20260521152400_opposite_vapor` is pure drizzle-kit output plus two hand-additions — `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS vector` and the `USING hnsw` index — both documented at the top of the file as the pgvector-specific exceptions to the no-hand-edited-migrations rule (drizzle-kit cannot emit pgvector DDL today). **`@janusly/ai`:** new `generateEmbedding(text, opts, env)` surface exported from the barrel; pure-functional over env so tests can inject a fake `process.env`. Routes to Ollama via REST (`POST {OLLAMA_BASE_URL}/api/embeddings`) with bounded timeout, structured error classification (`ollama_timeout` / `ollama_unreachable` / `ollama_dns` / `ollama_http_<status>` / `ollama_malformed_json` / `ollama_missing_embedding` / `ollama_non_finite_values`) and the standard `{ ok, embedding?, provider, model, dimension, latencyMs, error? }` envelope. SSRF carve-out documented inline: same posture as the `S3Provider` in `object-store.ts` — operator-supplied infrastructure config, never workflow-author input. Dimension is read from the response array length, NOT trusted from env. **`packages/data/src/memoryEntriesRepo.ts`** NEW with four helpers: `getMemoryConfig(orgId)` (narrow reader, sibling to `getAuthPolicyConfig`); `commitMemory({ orgId, kind, content, ... })` (two-flag consent → kind in `allowedKinds` → `scrubSecretShapes` write-time → per-kind retention → `generateEmbedding` → row insert via Drizzle's native `vector` codec → audit `memory.entry.created` → recorder); `recallMemory({ orgId, kind?, query })` (consent gate → query embedding → orgId-scoped `WHERE` BEFORE pgvector `<=>` cosine ranking → `recallMaxEntries` SQL `LIMIT` → in-JS byte trim to `recallMaxBytes` → `scrubSecretShapes` re-applied at read time → recorder; similarity = `1 - distance` so callers don't have to remember the pgvector convention); `deleteExpiredMemory({ orgId? })` (orgId-scoped or global purge of `retain_until <= now()` rows, audit `memory.retention.purged` with `{ entriesPurged, kindsAffected }`). Failed embeddings NEVER persist a row — an orphan with no usable embedding can't be recalled by similarity. Audit rows go in via direct `db.insert(auditLogs)` because the `audit()` chokepoint lives in the api package (data can't import it); metadata fields are repo-controlled enums, not user-supplied free-text, so `safePersistPayload` isn't needed at this layer. **Consent chokepoint:** new `packages/data/src/memoryConsent.ts:isMemoryAllowed(orgId)` (lives in the data package, not `apps/api`, because the repo is called from non-route consumers too). **Snapshot branch:** added `memory: { enabled: boolean }` to `OrgConfigSnapshot` — only the master switch lives there; the rest of the memory config (recall limits, retention overrides, etc.) is read via the narrow `getMemoryConfig` reader. **`/org/config` POST specialization:** when the key written is `memory.enabled`, the route captures the previous value BEFORE the upsert and emits `memory.consent.granted` or `memory.consent.revoked` on a true/false flip in ADDITION to the generic `org.config.updated`. **DI recorder seam:** new `packages/data/src/memoryUsage.ts:setMemoryUsageRecorder(fn)` + `recordMemoryUsage` in `usageRepo.ts`; metric shapes `memory.commit` / `memory.recall` fire on success AND failure. Boot wiring in api + worker alongside the existing 5 recorders. **Tests:** new `packages/data/src/memoryEntriesRepo.test.ts` covers all 7 AC categories — 17 pure-unit tests via the `@janusly/data` vitest scaffold from ENG-114; total test count goes 41 → 58. Existing `apps/api/src/mcp-consent.test.ts` + `apps/api/src/routes/mcp-routes.test.ts` snapshot fixtures gained the `memory: {} as never` branch to match the new `OrgConfigSnapshot` shape. **AGENTS.md scope clarification:** the "Anthropic-only" rule sharpened in place — applies to **LLM completions** specifically (Anthropic is the only provider that handles Janusly's structured-output grammar today); embeddings and other non-completion AI surfaces pick their provider separately. Scope-clarification of an existing invariant, not a new invariant. **Policy doc collateral:** `docs/memory-policy.md` §7 + ES sibling rewritten honestly (Ollama BGE-m3 v1, AGENTS.md scope cited, allowedValues list updated). The `memory.embeddingProvider` `allowedValues` swapped from `["anthropic"]` to `["ollama", "voyage", "openai"]` (Anthropic was aspirational; no production tenant has it set). Added new safe catalog key `memory.embeddingBaseUrl` so operators running external Ollama can point at it via the admin API (env fallback `OLLAMA_BASE_URL`). **Honest non-goals:** no HTTP routes (ENG-116 wires them); no retention cron (ENG-115b); no consent-revocation 7-day bulk purge (ENG-115c); no Voyage / OpenAI integration (listed in `allowedValues` for future); no re-embedding tooling; no `apps/web` changes. |
| ENG-116 | Feed memory into recovery suggestions | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Use ENG-115 memory plus existing `recovery_feedback` to improve `POST /ai/patch-workflow` without weakening the recovery safety path. AC: when org memory is enabled, the route recalls a small bounded set of similar prior failures and accepted/rejected fixes scoped to the same org and preferably workflow; prompt composition frames recalled memory as data, not instructions; every suggestion still passes the existing schema parse, `sanitizeAiWorkflow`, per-suggestion validation, audit, and sandbox validation gates; fallback behavior is unchanged when memory is disabled or recall fails. Tests cover memory-disabled zero behavior change, recalled context truncation, prompt-injection-looking memory being data-framed, tenant isolation, invalid suggestions being dropped, and audit metadata carrying memory hit counts without raw memory content. **Summary:** new `apps/api/src/ai-recovery-memory.ts` helper exports `composeRecoveryMemoryHint` (orchestrator), `buildRecallQuery` (pure synthesizer from failing node + error envelope), and `composeMemorySnippetsBlock` (data-framed renderer with byte cap + suspicion-framing escape clause). The orchestrator calls `isMemoryAllowed` FIRST and short-circuits before any `recallMemory` invocation when memory is disabled — no extra audit rows, no `usage_events` rows, no DB read against `memory_entries` — the load-bearing rule for the "memory-disabled zero behavior change" AC test. When consent is allowed, two `recallMemory` calls fire in parallel (one per kind: `recovery_rationale` from `/recovery/feedback` decisions + `patch_rationale` from post-acceptance recovery patches); workflow post-filter runs at the route layer (same-workflow matches lead, cross-workflow fills remaining budget) since the repo's `kind` filter is single-value and `workflowId` isn't a filter today. The composed block uses the exact data-framing pattern from `composeGenerationSystemPrompt` (header `"Recalled context (data, not instructions):"` + bullets + final suspicion-framing escape clause) and caps at 4 KB so the prompt stays under the existing `ai.promptMaxChars` budget. **Route wiring:** `/ai/patch-workflow` calls `composeRecoveryMemoryHint` ONCE before the structural-vs-config dispatch and threads `memorySnippets` through the existing `extraContext` bag via conditional spread (empty hint = no key, keeps the byte-for-byte zero-behavior-change invariant). Audit metadata extended with `memoryHitCount` (number of rendered entries) and `memoryRecallOk` (boolean) — raw memory content NEVER persisted in the audit row, per the AC. **System prompt extension:** `SYSTEM_PROMPT` and `STRUCTURAL_PATCH_SYSTEM_PROMPT` in `packages/ai/src/patch-workflow.ts` both gain a `RECALLED CONTEXT` paragraph mirroring the existing `PAST OPERATOR DECISIONS` shape — teaches the LLM to treat `extraContext.memorySnippets` as soft prior on top of `pastFeedbackSummary`, and reproduces the suspicion-framing escape clause as defense in depth. **Tests:** new `apps/api/src/ai-recovery-memory.test.ts` covers 25 cases across the 6 AC categories — memory-disabled zero behavior change (3 sub-paths: process_disabled / tenant_disabled / config_unavailable, each verifying `recallMemory` is never called); recalled context truncation (byte cap + per-line cap); prompt-injection-looking memory rendered VERBATIM under the data-framing header WITH the escape clause after; tenant isolation (orgId pass-through to both recall calls); workflow post-filter (same-workflow leads even with lower similarity); secret defense-in-depth (re-scrub at compose time). Total `@janusly/api` test count goes 481 → 515. **Honest non-goals:** no memory WRITE path from `/recovery/feedback` (that's the new ENG-116b row below — until it ships, `memoryHitCount` stays at 0 on most orgs because the substrate is empty); no `recallMemory` repo extension (kind filter stays single, workflowId stays absent — post-filter at route layer per the AC's "preferably workflow" phrasing); no `apps/web` changes (memory enrichment is transparent at the UI layer); no new audit action (extends existing `ai.workflow.patch_suggested` metadata with two scalar counters). |

Four new safe org-config keys under the `auto-healing` category: `autoHealing.enabled`, `autoHealing.autoApply`, `autoHealing.maxAttemptsPerSignature` (range 1..10, default 3), `autoHealing.loopWindowDays` (range 1..90, default 14). New `packages/engine/src/auto-healing-consent.ts` exports `isAutoHealingAllowed` + `isAutoApplyAllowed` — two independent two-flag-pair gates mirroring `isMcpWriteAllowed`. The scanner + watcher live in `apps/api` (NOT the engine worker) because they call `patchEnvelopeForNodeType` + `applyConfigPatchToWorkflow` which live in apps/api; the dep direction would otherwise invert. New dedicated BullMQ queue `auto-healing-system` + Worker booted from `apps/api/src/auto-healing-bootstrap.ts` only when `JANUSLY_AUTO_HEALING_ENABLED=true`. Scanner cron `system:auto-healing-scanner` (env `JANUSLY_AUTO_HEALING_SCAN_CRON` default `0 */6 * * *`) walks every org with the tenant flag on, groups recent DLQ rows by signature, and for each cluster with frequency >= 2: runs loop-breaker + budget + idempotency gates, calls `suggestWorkflowPatch` with the type-specific envelope from `patchEnvelopeForNodeType`, merges via `applyConfigPatchToWorkflow` (or the structural variant), enqueues sandbox validation via `replayDeadLetterAsValidation`. Watcher cron `system:auto-healing-watcher` (env `JANUSLY_AUTO_HEALING_WATCH_CRON` default `* * * * *`) polls validating rows, computes the signature-changed defense via `normalizeErrorSignature`, records outcome, and (if `isAutoApplyAllowed` + loop count still in range) replays the failing run against the patched workflow with `decisionActor: "auto"`. Two-hour stale-row sweep auto-declines stuck `validating` rows. Both crons NEVER throw. Four new HTTP routes (`apps/api/src/routes/auto-healing-routes.ts`): `GET /auto-healing/pending`, `GET /auto-healing/:id`, `POST /auto-healing/:id/decide` (CAS-style; surfaces 409 `signature_already_resolved` when racing the watcher's auto-apply; also writes a `recovery_feedback` mirror row so the LLM learns from auto-healing decisions), `POST /auto-healing/scan` (admin-only ad-hoc trigger, rate-limited via the existing `"ai"` bucket). Two new permission keys (`autohealing.read` viewer+, `autohealing.decide` editor+). Web card `apps/web/src/components/AutoHealingPendingCard.tsx` mounted inside the existing DLQ panel alongside `FailureClustersCard`; Apply / Decline buttons call `bumpPlatformVersion()` on mutation. EN+ES i18n parity for 16 new keys. ~30 new tests across engine repo, api scanner (13), api watcher (5), and api routes (7). **Honest non-goals:** no new top-level UI panel (one card inside the existing DLQ panel); no auto-apply by default (zero behavior change for orgs that don't flip both flag pairs on); no retroactive scan (forward-only); no cross-org scan parallelism (sequential walk); no alerting on auto-apply (the `auto_healing.applied` audit row with `decisionActor: "auto"` IS the signal); no new LLM grammar / structured-output schema (reuses `suggestWorkflowPatch`); no shadow production replay (sandbox validation only proves the signature is gone — operator review is the safety net by default). |
| ENG-118 | Harden stdio MCP sandboxing | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Strengthen stdio MCP subprocess isolation without changing URL transport semantics. AC: keep the existing `JANUSLY_MCP_ALLOWED_COMMANDS` plus tenant allowlist and clean-env rebuild; add a production stdio sandbox profile that constrains cwd, filesystem write access, CPU, memory, process lifetime, stdout/stderr byte limits, and log redaction before telemetry; do not route `sse` or `http` transports through this subprocess sandbox; hosted MCP tools that require network continue to use the existing URL transport policy. Tests cover host env values being absent, disallowed commands failing closed, allowed command receiving only resolved env refs, timeout/resource-limit termination, stdout/stderr truncation, secret-shaped log redaction, and URL transports remaining unchanged. **Summary:** new pure module `packages/engine/src/mcp-sandbox.ts` exposes `buildSandboxProfile` + `captureRedactedStderr` plus typed errors `McpSandboxCommandNotAllowedError` / `McpSandboxLifetimeExceededError` / `McpSandboxStderrExceededError`. Five composable layers, applied in order: (a) **allowlist re-check at SPAWN time** — defense in depth over the existing registration-time gate, fails closed when the env CSV ∪ tenant CSV union is empty or rejects the command; (b) **ephemeral cwd** via `fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'janusly-mcp-'))` per spawn, removed on `close()`; (c) **lifetime cap** — `setTimeout` arms on first connect (default 600s, range 60s..1h), fires `transport.close()` (which SIGTERMs the child) and after 500ms grace escalates to `process.kill(pid, 'SIGKILL')`; (d) **stderr byte cap + secret-shape redaction** — `captureRedactedStderr` consumes the child's `stderr` stream (SDK exposes it via `stderr: 'pipe'`), buffers raw bytes capped at 64KB default, runs `scrubSecretShapes` at snapshot time so secrets split across chunk boundaries are still redacted, and triggers a child kill when the cap is exceeded; (e) **Linux `ulimit -v` wrap** — only on `process.platform === 'linux'` AND `enforceLinuxUlimit === true`, wraps the original command via `/bin/sh -c 'ulimit -v "$1"; shift; exec "$@"' janusly-mcp-sandbox <vmKb> <orig-cmd> <orig-args...>` (portable POSIX shell, the tag becomes `$0` and never reaches the executed command). The `createStdioMcpClient` factory accepts a new REQUIRED `sandbox` block — typed param change is intentional so dropping the config is a TS error; the `McpClient` interface gains `getSandboxSnapshot()` returning `{ capturedStderr, stderrTruncated, stderrByteCount, lifetimeExceeded, stderrExceeded, enforced }` (null for URL transports). The `mcp_tool` executor (`packages/engine/src/mcp-tool-executor.ts`) gains `stdioSandbox` on the input shape + a new `resolveStdioSandboxConfig(mcpConfig)` helper that resolves the allowlist (tenant ∪ env, tenant-overrides-env precedence matches registration-time), the three caps from `org_configs.mcp.stdio*` with env fallbacks, and the `enforceLinuxUlimit` flag (default `process.platform === 'linux' && NODE_ENV === 'production'`, override via `JANUSLY_MCP_SANDBOX_ENFORCE_LINUX`). The executor maps typed sandbox errors to stable envelope codes `mcp_sandbox_command_rejected` / `mcp_sandbox_lifetime_exceeded` / `mcp_sandbox_stderr_exceeded` exported as `SANDBOX_ERROR_CODES`, surfaces the redacted stderr tail (last 1KB) to the audit callback via new `sandboxFailureCode` + `capturedStderrTail` fields, and the `mcp_tool` node executor writes both the existing `mcp_tool.completed` event AND a new `mcp.sandbox.terminated` event on kill paths so the run timeline distinguishes operator-driven errors from sandbox kills. Three new safe `org_configs` catalog entries (`mcp.stdioMaxLifetimeMs` default 600_000 range 60_000..3_600_000, `mcp.stdioMaxStderrBytes` default 65_536 range 1024..1_048_576, `mcp.stdioMaxVmKb` default 524_288 range 131_072..4_194_304) with env overlays (`JANUSLY_MCP_STDIO_MAX_LIFETIME_MS` / `_STDERR_BYTES` / `_VM_KB`). Discovery flow (`apps/api/src/routes/mcp-routes.ts:runDiscovery`) also resolves the sandbox config via the same helper so registration + re-discovery + per-call invocation all evaluate equivalent allowlists. 13 new test cases in `mcp-sandbox.test.ts` (allowlist re-check, ulimit wrap shape by platform, clamping, byte-accurate stderr cap with secret-shape redaction across chunk boundaries) + 5 new in `mcp-client.test.ts` (sandbox snapshot exposed on stdio transports, spawn-time allowlist drift fails closed, `cwd` + `stderr: 'pipe'` propagated to the SDK constructor, stderr-cap transport closure mapped to `McpSandboxStderrExceededError`) + 9 new in `mcp-tool-executor.test.ts` (`parseCommandAllowlist` + `resolveStdioSandboxConfig` env/tenant precedence + Linux-only enforce default + the executor mapping `McpSandboxCommandNotAllowedError` to `COMMAND_REJECTED`, `McpSandboxLifetimeExceededError` to `LIFETIME_EXCEEDED`, and `McpSandboxStderrExceededError` to `STDERR_EXCEEDED` with redacted stderr tail flowing through to the audit callback). URL transports (`sse` / `http`) untouched — same `validateHttpTarget` chokepoint, no new ulimit / cwd / stderr layers on them. **v1 carve-outs (natural follow-ups):** native `prlimit(2)` / `setrlimit(2)` binding to avoid the `sh` wrapper, CPU time cap (`ulimit -t`), per-connection sandbox profile JSONB column on `mcp_connections`, macOS `sandbox-exec` profile, Windows job-object resource limits, `firejail` / `bubblewrap` integration for chroot-level isolation, network-namespace isolation, real-time stderr streaming to the run timeline. |
| ENG-119 | Add targeted Replay Lab forks | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Extend the shipped Replay Lab from whole-run sandbox replay to selected-node forks. AC: add `POST /runs/replay-lab/fork` or extend the existing route with a closed `mode` field; request includes `sourceRunId`, `forkNodeId`, and optional bounded input/context overrides; route verifies source run ownership, rejects nested lab runs, validates `forkNodeId` exists in the source workflow, creates a `replayMode="validation"` run, clones only completed predecessor state needed by the selected node, starts the selected node pending with the override, marks downstream nodes pending, and records a distinct `replay_lab.fork_started` audit/event payload; `GET /runs/compare` can compare base vs fork. Web UI adds an "Open selected node in Replay Lab" path with override editor and EN/ES copy. Tests cover predecessor-only cloning, no successor-state leakage, dry-run write skips, override size limits, invalid node 422, cross-org 404/403 behavior, and comparison rendering. **Summary:** v1 substrate (engine adapter + route + adapter tests) shipped first, v1.1 UI follow-up closed the carve-out. Engine adapter `replayRunAsValidationFork` in `packages/engine/src/adapters/replay-lab.ts` (new export alongside existing `replayRunAsValidation`); pure helpers `computePredecessors` + `computeSuccessors` compute the ancestor and downstream sets through the workflow's edges; the adapter validates `forkNodeId` exists, validates every predecessor terminated `succeeded` in the source run (strict v1 policy — v1.1 may relax with an opt-in), and writes the new `runs` row + N `run_nodes` rows + a `run.started.replay-lab-fork` event in one DB transaction (predecessors cloned as `status='succeeded'` with `stateJson` copied from the source, fork node `pending` with `{ input: inputOverride }` seeded, downstream closure `pending` with empty state, sibling/disconnected nodes `skipped` with `reason: "outside_replay_fork"` so the runtime never executes branches outside the targeted fork). New route `POST /runs/replay-lab/fork` (editor + `"ai"` rate-limit bucket) in `apps/api/src/routes/runs-routes.ts` validates `sourceRunId` + `forkNodeId` + `inputOverride` (64 KiB cap), org-scopes the source run via `eq(runs.id) AND eq(runs.orgId, auth.orgId)` (404 on cross-org), rejects nested labs (400 `nested_replay_lab`), resolves the workflow snapshot from `workflow_versions.dagJson` or `runs.inputJson.workflow`, calls the adapter, maps discriminated errors (`fork_node_not_found`, `predecessor_not_succeeded`) to HTTP 422, and audits `replay_lab.fork_started` with `{ replayRunId, forkNodeId, predecessorCount, hasOverride }`. The `parentRunId` and `parentNodeId` columns on the `runs` table (already shipped) start being populated together for forks — `parentNodeId` was previously always null. The existing `GET /runs/compare` endpoint requires no changes — it generically compares two runs by id regardless of whether the second is a whole-run replay or a fork. Comprehensive adapter test suite in `packages/engine/src/adapters/replay-lab-fork.test.ts` covering: `computePredecessors` + `computeSuccessors` on linear/diamond/isolated graphs, happy-path predecessor cloning (linear + diamond), override seeding, empty-state seeding when no override, disconnected fork node with out-of-scope nodes skipped, `fork_node_not_found` rejection, `predecessor_not_succeeded` rejection (both failed-predecessor and missing-predecessor variants), no-DB-writes-on-validation-failure invariant, only-fork-node-queued invariant. **v1.1 UI close:** added `apps/web/src/components/ReplayLabForkDialog.tsx` — a textarea-based JSON override modal that mirrors `ReplayLabDialog`'s frame, POSTs `/runs/replay-lab/fork`, and on success calls `setRunId` + `bumpPlatformVersion` so the run timeline switches to the new fork run automatically. `RunsPanel` in `RightPanel.tsx` gains a "Fork in Replay Lab" section listing succeeded + failed terminal nodes when the active run is non-replay + terminal; each row opens the dialog with the chosen `forkNodeId`. 22 new i18n keys (16 `replayLab.fork.*` + 6 `apiErrors.*` for the route's typed codes — `nested_replay_lab`, `fork_node_not_found`, `predecessor_not_succeeded`, `override_too_large`, `no_workflow_snapshot`, `invalid_snapshot`) ship in both EN and ES locales with Spanish translations honouring the AGENTS.md no-Spanglish rule. Vitest jsdom tests in `apps/web/src/components/ReplayLabForkDialog.test.tsx` cover idle render, submit without override (POST body without `inputOverride` field), submit with valid JSON override, submit with invalid JSON (inline error, no POST), typed-error path (`ApiError` with `code: fork_node_not_found` resolves through `tApiError` catalog), and unmount-mid-POST safety (the `aliveRef` guard prevents stale `setRunId` after the dialog tears down). Reuses existing `run-input-dialog*` CSS classes — no `index.css` additions. **Still-deferred follow-ups (natural future tickets, not gaps in this slice):** polished schema-aware override form (per-input fields driven by the node's input schema, similar to the `human_form` renderer), `contextOverrides` surface (override upstream node outputs that downstream `{{nodeId.X}}` references resolve to), DAG fork-aware visualization in the comparison panel, and cluster-fork (one fork point with N replays of N override variants). **Spanglish cleanup colateral** also landed in this commit per a new project-wide rule (no Spanglish in Spanish content; the AGENTS.md "No Spanglish in Spanish content" bullet documents the canonical replacement table): ~14 occurrences across `docs/marketing/narrative.md` (`bindeado`/`bindear`/`patched` → `asignado`/`asignar`/`parchado` + Spanish "ya shipped" → "ya en producción"), `docs/marketing/icp.md` (`shippe*` × 5 + `chequear` × 1 → `lanzar`/`publicar`/`revisar`), `docs/marketing/pricing.md` (`shippe*` × 3 + `trackea` × 1 → `publicar`/`lanzar`/`se mide`), `docs/marketing/competitive-positioning.md` (1 preamble fix), `docs/marketing/private-beta-playbook.md` (12 fixes — `shippe*`/`loggea*`/`bindea*`/`parsear`/`sávalo`/`workflow patched`), `docs/marketing/landing-page.md` (`replayearse` → `reproducirse`). All replacements per the canonical table in the plan; English `(shipped)` tags + English code comments stay intact (those are correct English, not Spanglish). |
| ENG-120 | Add MTTR and value dashboard | Shipped | P2 | 4 | Turn the private-beta/value thesis into an operator-facing dashboard without overclaiming financial precision. Depends on ENG-093 for real customer baseline data when possible. AC: add a Recovery/value dashboard that shows MTTR before/after recovery, recovery success rate, replay rate, failed-run clusters resolved, AI spend from `usage_events`, and estimated manual triage hours saved; dollar savings are configurable assumptions (for example hourly cost and minutes saved per recovered failure) and labeled as estimates, not hard accounting; every query is tenant-scoped and bounded by a time window; exports use existing report/download helpers where practical. Tests cover aggregation windows, null cost handling, estimate formula labels, org isolation, 1M-row-style query bounds via indexes or query shape, EN/ES UI parity, and private-beta data absence fallback. **Summary:** ships the visualization layer over the existing recovery-metrics rollup — no new server-side collection. **3 new `org_configs` keys** under a new `value` category (`value.hourlyCost` default 50 range 0..2000, `value.minutesSavedPerRecovery` default 30 range 0..480, `value.baselineMttrSeconds` default 0 range 0..86400) + the matching `OrgConfigSnapshot.value` branch. **One new repo query** `queryFailureClustersResolved(orgId, since)` in `packages/data/src/recoveryMetricsRepo.ts` joins `dead_letters` with status in `{replayed, resolved}`, derives the signature via `normalizeErrorSignature`, returns distinct cluster count + total entries. Same 10k-row cap as other metrics queries. **Engine `composeRecoveryMetrics` extended** with `clustersResolved` metric + `valueEstimate { hoursSaved, dollarSaved, mttrDeltaSeconds, assumptions }` block + a pure `estimateValueSavings` helper exported for the export route. The formula `clustersResolved × minutesSavedPerRecovery / 60 × hourlyCost = dollarSaved` is computed server-side; `baselineMttrSeconds === 0` is the sentinel for "operator has not measured a pre-Janusly baseline yet" → mttrDelta returns null and the UI renders "Awaiting private-beta data". **`/recovery/metrics` extended** — pulls the org-config value snapshot in parallel with the metrics signals and passes it into the composer. Same URL, same role gate, additive fields. Clients that don't read the new fields keep working byte-for-byte. **NEW `GET /reports/value-dashboard?windowDays=N&format=markdown|json`** route mirrors `/reports/run-explain` (same `Content-Disposition` + `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` chokepoint, same audit shape `report.value_dashboard.exported`). Markdown export header carries "Estimate based on operator-supplied assumptions" + writes the formula out plainly so a CFO reading the artefact knows exactly how every number was derived. **Web `ValueDashboardSection.tsx`** mounted inside the existing `RecoveryCenterPanel` grid — NOT a new top-level panel, NOT a new sidebar entry, same additive-mount pattern auto-healing's pending card used. MTTR before/after pair + clusters card + hours-saved + dollar-savings (Intl.NumberFormat USD, locale-aware via `getResolvedLocale`). Every dollar/hour surface carries an "estimate" badge + tooltip linking to the assumptions config. Export buttons trigger `downloadFromApi`. **16 EN+ES i18n keys** under `recoveryCenter.value.*` (parity-gated). **~25 new test cases** across data orgConfig (8), engine recovery-metrics (10), web component (5), plus updated mcp-routes fixtures with the new `value: {} as never` snapshot branch. **Honest non-goals:** no charting library (no recharts / visx / d3 — the lockdown stays), no multi-currency (USD only, locale-aware formatting), no time-series buckets (per-window aggregates only), no automated baseline-MTTR capture (operator-supplied via config; ENG-093 may surface real numbers), no CSV export, no Slack/email delivery, no retroactive metric backfill, no new audit chokepoint. |
| ENG-121 | Surface rate-limit degradation | Shipped | P2 | — | Address Redis limiter outage visibility while preserving the documented fail-open posture. AC: keep `apps/api/src/rate-limit.ts` behavior fail-open on Redis errors; add structured warning telemetry/counters for limiter degradation by bucket and org, a health/readiness signal consumed by Operations or AI health UI, and operator copy that distinguishes "traffic is allowed under degraded limiter" from "AI budget exceeded"; AI budget guards remain the cost-control layer, not a hidden fail-closed route policy, and Redis outages must not become route-level 429/503 failures. Tests cover Redis error path still allowing requests, warn metadata shape, health signal clearing after recovery, no duplicate noisy events in tight loops, and no regression to in-memory enforcement as the primary limiter. **Summary:** closes the observability gap on the limiter's fail-open path WITHOUT touching the fail-open posture. **`createRateLimiter` gains an optional second `hooks` arg** (`onRedisError` / `onRedisSuccess`) wrapped in a `fireHook` try/catch that catches both sync throws and async rejections so a tracker bug NEVER breaks the fail-open invariant. **NEW `packages/data/src/rate-limit-degradation.ts`** owns the in-memory per-process tracker (`Map<string, DegradationState>`) + the audit chokepoint. `recordRateLimiterError(bucket, key, error)` increments the counter and writes a `(bucket, day)`-deduped `rate_limit.degraded` audit row with `orgId: "system"` sentinel (mirrors the established global-cron convention from memory-retention + auto-healing). `recordRateLimiterRecovery(bucket)` clears the in-memory state on the next successful round-trip and writes a one-shot `rate_limit.recovered` audit. DB-level dedup query (`SELECT … WHERE action='rate_limit.degraded' AND metadata->>'bucket' = ? AND created_at >= today_utc`) guards against multi-replica spam — the in-memory dedup already covers the single-replica case. **Two-tier health snapshot:** `getRateLimiterPublicHealth()` (public-safe bucket label / errorCount / firstObservedAt / lastObservedAt only; tenant-chosen MCP aliases/tool names, Redis errors, and org keys withheld) for the open `GET /health` block; `getRateLimiterAdminHealth()` (full — raw bucket plus `lastError` + `lastObservedKey`) for the new admin `GET /system/rate-limiter` route. The public truncation is the load-bearing reason the snapshot can ride on the unauthenticated `/health` route — Redis error strings can leak hostnames / connection metadata. **Production hooks wired** at both `apps/api/src/rate-limit.ts` and `packages/engine/src/rate-limit-redis.ts` so API + worker replicas feed their own process-local snapshots. **`GET /health` extended additively** with the `rateLimiter` block — `body.ok: true` still anchors any orchestrator probe that reads only that field; clients reading the new field gain the degraded signal. **NEW `GET /system/rate-limiter` (admin)** exposes the full snapshot. **NEW `RateLimiterStatusChip` mounted inside `OperationsPanel`** — green chip when healthy, amber chip with bucket-count tooltip when degraded. Copy distinguishes explicitly: "Rate limiter degraded — N bucket(s) failing open. Traffic still allowed." vs `BudgetBlockedBanner`'s "AI workflow budget exceeded — blocked." Operators reading both surfaces should never confuse a Redis blip with a hard budget block. **6 new EN+ES i18n keys** under `operations.rateLimiter.*` (parity-gated; Spanish honours the no-Spanglish rule via `dejando pasar el tráfico sin aplicar el límite`). **2 new audit actions** (`rate_limit.degraded` / `rate_limit.recovered`). **~21 new test cases** across data tracker (9), data limiter hook wiring (7), api health-routes (5), web Operations chip (2). **Honest non-goals:** no fail-closed variant, no per-org dedup on the audit (Redis outages are global infrastructure events), no in-memory fallback limiter, no Prometheus exporter (counter is in-memory; a future ticket can plug `packages/engine/src/observability/prometheus.ts`), no top-level banner (a fail-open degradation is non-blocking), no alert routing, no reset endpoint. |

Both upstream blockers (ENG-125 SLO, ENG-126 credential health) shipped before this row; ENG-125's Summary cites the handoff verbatim ("ENG-122 will land the alerting plumbing"). AC: org-scoped alerting policies fan events into Slack / webhook / email / GitHub via the existing tool chokepoints. New `alert_policies` table keyed by `(orgId, name)` with closed-enum `trigger` (`dlq.entry_created` \| `failure_cluster.threshold` \| `budget.blocked` \| `limiter.degraded` \| `workflow.slo_breach` \| `approval.stalled`), bounded `parameters` jsonb (per-trigger Zod schemas), `channels` jsonb (array of `{ destination, credentialName, params? }` referencing existing credentials — no new credential kind), `cooldownSeconds`, `enabled`. New `alert_dispatches` table backs both cooldown lookup and the `GET /alerts/recent` operator feed. Five new audit actions (`alert.policy.created/updated/deleted/triggered/suppressed`). Two new permission keys (`alerts.read` viewer+, `alerts.write` admin). Five new routes (`POST/GET/POST :id/DELETE /alerts/policies`, `GET /alerts/recent`). Three event-driven hooks (DLQ adapter, budget-gate, rate-limit-degradation) + a state-driven scanner (failure clusters, SLO breaches, stalled approvals) registered as `system:alerts-scanner` BullMQ cron (default `*/2 * * * *`, env-tunable). Dedupe per `(orgId, policyId, dedupeKey)` within `cooldownSeconds`; cooldown skips only fire the suppressed audit row (no `alert_dispatches` row, keeps the table compact). The dispatcher lives in `packages/engine/src/alerts/` (NOT `apps/api`) so both API and worker processes can register it — DLQ inserts in the worker fire alerts immediately via the DI seam. Tests cover Zod schemas, glob matcher, message composer, dedupe-key per trigger, matchesPolicyFilter per trigger, DI seam swallow-on-error, web list + form render + recent-card severity tint. **Summary:** zero-LLM alerting layer over the existing tool chokepoints. **NEW shared schemas** at `packages/shared/src/alert-policy.ts` + `alert-pattern.ts` (glob matcher, NO regex / NO ReDoS surface) + `alert-message.ts` (deterministic Markdown template — NOT a prompt). **NEW DB schema** adds `alert_policies` + `alert_dispatches` via `packages/db/migrations/20260522182937_mighty_jetstream`. **NEW data repos** `packages/data/src/alertPoliciesRepo.ts` (CRUD + `getEnabledPoliciesByTrigger`), `alertDispatchesRepo.ts` (cooldown lookup + cursor-paginated feed), `alert-dispatch.ts` (DI seam mirroring `setUsageRecorder`). **NEW engine dispatcher** `packages/engine/src/alerts/dispatcher.ts` matches policies, applies per-trigger filter (`errorSignaturePattern` glob, `workflowIds` whitelist, `minFrequency` threshold, `metricNames` overlap, `buckets` filter, `scope` selector), checks cooldown, fans across channels via `executeTool` (same chokepoint as `/reports/run-explain/deliver` — SSRF / body-cap / usage / audit / safe-persist all apply for free), writes dispatch row + `alert.policy.triggered` audit. Cooldown skips write `alert.policy.suppressed` audit only. Never throws — per-channel failures land as `{ ok: false, error, latencyMs }`. **NEW api scanner** `apps/api/src/alerts/scanner.ts` + bootstrap registered behind `JANUSLY_ALERTS_ENABLED=true` env gate. **NEW routes** `apps/api/src/routes/alerts-routes.ts` with body validation through `AlertPolicyConfigSchema` + `validateAlertPolicyConfig`; 422 envelope carries structured per-channel errors for the web's inline form messages. **Three line-insertion hooks**: DLQ adapter writes `recordAlertEvent({ trigger: "dlq.entry_created", ... })` after `db.insert(deadLetters)`; `budget-gate.ts` after the `billing.budget.exceeded` audit; `rate-limit-degradation.ts` after `rate_limit.degraded` audit (uses `orgId: "system"` sentinel — operator-side only). **NEW web** `AlertPoliciesPanel.tsx` (admin CRUD with trigger-swap parameters subform + credential dropdown filtered by destination kind + inline Edit button per row that opens the form prefilled via `policyToForm` and POSTs to `:id` instead of creating; Save button copy swaps to "Save changes" when editing; the tenant-org filter hides the `limiter.degraded` trigger from the trigger dropdown for non-`system` orgs since alerts on that trigger only fire for the SaaS operator) + `RecentAlertsCard.tsx` (50-row feed with cobalt/amber severity tint, expand for channel results) mounted inside `OperationsPanel`. **~52 new EN+ES i18n keys** under `alerts.*` (parity-gated, no Spanglish). **~30 new tests** across shared (alert-policy + alert-pattern + alert-message), data (DI seam), engine (matchesPolicyFilter + dedupe-key), web (panel + recent card). Total: 2017+ tests still green. **Honest non-goals:** no alert message templates beyond the Markdown default (operator-customisable templates are v2); no escalation chains; no Slack interactive buttons (ENG-143 owns that); no native PagerDuty / Opsgenie (use `webhook.send`); no snooze (toggle `enabled: false`); no `recovery_item.created` trigger (ENG-123 doesn't exist yet — one-line add when it lands); no preview / test-send button; no PATCH HTTP verb (the codebase's `Route` type is closed to GET/POST/DELETE — `POST /alerts/policies/:id` does updates); 14 dedicated `*.browser.test.tsx` Vitest browser-mode scenarios + 7 Claude_Preview screenshots are a follow-up step before the human commits (jsdom + parity tests pass; the operator runs `pnpm dev` and captures screenshots for the human review pass). |
| ENG-123 | Add recovery ownership workflow | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §2. Continúa naturalmente a ENG-122 — convierte cada DLQ row abierto en un incident trackeable con owner / severity p1-p4 / SLA timer / status lifecycle / comments append-only / resolutionReason. Cierra el honest non-goal de ENG-122 ("no `recovery_item.created` trigger") añadiendo dos triggers nuevos al contrato cerrado: `recovery_item.created` (event-driven, dispara cuando se crea un item) y `recovery_item.sla_breached` (state-driven, el scanner periódico de ENG-122 lo evalúa cada 2 min sobre `recovery_items WHERE status != 'resolved' AND sla_target_at <= now()`). AC: new `recovery_items` table keyed por `(orgId, deadLetterId)` único — un incident por DLQ row, incluso cuando un cluster tiene varios miembros. Lifecycle status closed-enum (`open` → `acknowledged` → `in_progress` → `waiting_external` → `resolved`, con `reopened` para vueltas). Severity closed-enum (`p1`/`p2`/`p3`/`p4`) con SLA defaults por nivel (1h/4h/1d/7d) overridable por-item al asignar. ResolutionReason closed-enum (`fixed_by_patch`/`rolled_back`/`upstream_fixed`/`accepted_loss`/`not_a_bug`/`sandbox_replay_succeeded`). 10 rutas nuevas en el route registry (`GET /recovery/items` paginado + `GET /recovery/items/:id` + 8 transition POSTs: `acknowledge`/`in-progress`/`waiting-external`/`escalate`/`assign`/`resolve`/`reopen`/`comment`), todas `permission: "recovery.write"` (ya existía, descripción ampliada). 9 audit actions nuevas (`recovery.item.created/acknowledged/in_progress/waiting_external/assigned/escalated/resolved/reopened/commented`); cada mutación lleva `{before, after}` para traceability. CAS-style transitions (mirror de `recordDecision` en `autoHealingRepo.ts`) — concurrent operator-click vs auto-apply pierde con 409 + `{code: "recovery_item_transition_invalid", currentStatus}`. Cluster-recover-all hace fan-out: cada DLQ row aceptado crea (idempotentemente) un recovery_item antes del replay y lo auto-cierra después con `resolutionReason: sandbox_replay_succeeded`. `/dlq/replay` + `/dlq/resolve` hacen el mismo auto-close. **Summary:** columna vertebral operacional sobre Recovery Center. **NEW shared schemas** en `packages/shared/src/recovery-item.ts` (zero-runtime-dep) — closed enums, SLA defaults (`SLA_SECONDS_BY_SEVERITY`), Zod body schemas por route, helper `defaultSlaTargetAt`, helper `isSeverityEscalation`. **NEW DB schema** añade `recovery_items` table vía `packages/db/migrations/20260522213531_bizarre_zombie/` con 3 índices: unique `(orgId, deadLetterId)` para idempotencia del cluster-apply, `(orgId, status, sla_target_at)` para el SLA-breach scanner, `(orgId, owner)` para "mis incidents". **NEW data repo** `packages/data/src/recoveryItemsRepo.ts` con CRUD + 6 transition functions CAS-style + `listOpenItemsWithBreachedSla` para el scanner + `appendCommentToRecoveryItem` read-modify-write con cap MAX_COMMENTS_PER_ITEM=200. **NEW DI seam** `packages/data/src/recovery-item-creator.ts` mirror de `alert-dispatch.ts` — la creación se dispara desde la DLQ adapter via seam (NO import directo a apps/api desde engine, sigue el patrón cross-process establecido por el alert dispatcher). **NEW engine helper** `packages/engine/src/recovery/recovery-item-hook.ts` con `createRecoveryItemForDeadLetter` (idempotente, honora `org_configs.recovery.autoCreateItems` default true, audit inline via `db.insert(auditLogs)` + safePersistPayload chokepoint, dispatch `recovery_item.created` alert) y `autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay` (CAS-loser-safe). Vive en engine para que worker process pueda registrar el mismo creator. **NEW api routes** `apps/api/src/routes/recovery-items-routes.ts` con 10 rutas + body validation via Zod schemas en `@janusly/shared` + 409 con `currentStatus` en CAS-loser + audit con before/after por transition. **DLQ hooks** insertados en 3 sitios: `/dlq/cluster-apply` (createRecoveryItemForDeadLetter + autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay por cada accepted row), `/dlq/replay` single (autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay), `/dlq/resolve` (autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay). **Alert trigger extension** extiende `ALERT_TRIGGERS` con dos valores nuevos cierra el contrato declarado en ENG-122; `matchesPolicyFilter` + `buildDedupeKey` + scanner cada uno con su branch nuevo (scanner reutiliza el cron `*/2 * * * *` existente añadiendo `scanRecoveryItemSlaBreaches`). **Engine event hook** en `packages/engine/src/adapters/dead-letter-queue.ts` captura el `deadLetterId` generado y dispara `recordRecoveryItemCreationEvent` paralelo al alert event. Worker process registra el creator en `worker.ts` para que worker-side DLQ inserts también disparen item creation. **NEW web** `RecoveryItemBadge.tsx` (avatar owner + severity pill cobalt→red por p4→p1 + status chip + SLA timer con bandas verde/amber/red por % de tiempo restante) + `RecoveryItemDrawer.tsx` (transition buttons habilitados según current status + resolve sub-form con select del closed-enum + comments append con textarea + escalate sub-buttons por severity restante) montados dentro de `DeadLettersPanel.tsx` (load recovery items en paralelo via `GET /recovery/items?limit=200`, lookup por deadLetterId, badge inyectado en `we-list-row__meta`). **~50 nuevas EN+ES i18n keys** bajo `recoveryItems.*` (parity-gated, no Spanglish — "Acuse de recibo", "En curso", "Esperando externos", "Resuelto" en español, no "shippear/loggear"). **~30 new tests** across shared (recovery-item Zod coverage), data (DI seam swallow + last-write-wins), engine (alert dispatcher branches + dedupe-key + scanner), web (RecoveryItemBadge severity tones + SLA color bands + null-item no-op). Total: 2024+ tests verde. **Honest non-goals (v1 scope):** no team-level owner (owner es userId — asignación grupal queda para v2); no email-on-assign (operador opt-in vía alert policy con `recovery_item.created` + filtro severities); no edit/delete de comments individuales (append-only por diseño); no bulk-assign/bulk-resolve UI fuera del cluster-apply existente; no `runbookMarkdown` per workflow (eso es ENG-139); no mobile-optimized drawer; no SLA auto-escalation (scanner solo emite el evento `recovery_item.sla_breached`, no sube severity automáticamente); no analytics dashboard ("how long do p1s take to resolve on average" es ENG-120); no "Mis incidents abiertos" widget en RecoveryCenterPanel (queda como follow-up — el filter `?owner=me` ya existe en la ruta `GET /recovery/items`, solo falta el componente); no UI validation de que el owner sea un userId real del org (operator-trusted input, 1-line follow-up). |
| ENG-124 | Add incident handoff integrations | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §2. Continuación natural de ENG-123 — desde un `recovery_item`, un click dispatcha el contexto completo del incident (severity, owner, error signature, comments recientes, deep link al Recovery Center) al canal del equipo (Slack / Linear / GitHub Issue / webhook genérico). Sin copy-paste manual, sin perder el audit trail. AC: nueva ruta `POST /recovery/items/:id/handoff` (`role: "editor"`, `permission: "recovery.write"`, reusa el bucket `reports.deliver`). Body con closed-enum `destination` + `credentialName` + per-destination superRefine fields. Idempotency keyed por `(orgId, recoveryItemId, destination)` con unique index — segundo handoff edita en vez de duplicar (slack: cooldown 1h `alreadyDispatched: true`; github: append comment al issue existente vía nuevo tool `github.add_issue_comment`; linear+webhook: `X-Idempotency-Key` header al receiver). 4 audit actions nuevas (`recovery.handoff.{slack,linear,github,webhook}`) con `ok`, `statusCode`, `latencyMs`, `credentialName`, `idempotencyKey`, `dispatchCount`, `externalUrl` (NUNCA echoa env-var name). Dry-run gate: cuando `runs.replayMode === 'validation'` el dispatch skip y el audit row marca `skipped: true`. **Summary:** cierre operacional cross-team del Recovery Center. **NEW shared schemas** `packages/shared/src/recovery-handoff.ts` — `RECOVERY_HANDOFF_DESTINATIONS` closed enum, `HandoffRequestBodySchema` con superRefine per-destination, `SLACK_HANDOFF_COOLDOWN_SECONDS = 3600`, `composeHandoffMessage` deterministic Markdown template (NO LLM, NO prompts, secrets scrubbed via `scrubSecretShapes`), `buildHandoffIdempotencyKey` stable helper, `isSlackHandoffCoolingDown` pure check. **NEW DB schema** añade `recovery_item_handoffs` table vía `packages/db/migrations/20260523032321_complex_rhodey/` con unique index `(orgId, recoveryItemId, destination)` y secondary index sobre `lastDispatchedAt DESC` para feed cross-tenant. **NEW data repo** `packages/data/src/recoveryItemHandoffsRepo.ts` con `getHandoffByKey`, `listHandoffsForItem`, `listRecentHandoffs`, y `upsertHandoff` (INSERT ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE — `COALESCE(EXCLUDED.external_id, existing)` preserva el issueNumber original cuando un retry falla; `dispatch_count + 1` bump). **NEW engine tool** `github.add_issue_comment` en `packages/engine/src/integration-tools.ts` — POST `/repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{n}/comments`, `writeSide: true`, mismo posture que `github.create_issue` (fetchHttpTarget chokepoint, rate-limit bucket, usage events, error redaction). **Extensión `webhookSendInput`** con `headers?: Record<string, string>` opcional capped (10 entries, 200 chars/value, CR/LF rejected — bloquea header-injection). El dispatcher merge-ea las custom headers PERO content-type + signature header son reserved. **NEW api dispatcher** `apps/api/src/recovery-handoff-dispatcher.ts` per-destination branching — slack post-only via `slack.post`, github first→`github.create_issue`/subsequent→`github.add_issue_comment` con `composeAppendMessage` (subject "Recovery item update (dispatch #N)"), linear+webhook via `webhook.send` + `X-Idempotency-Key` header. NEVER throws. **NEW route** `apps/api/src/routes/recovery-handoff-routes.ts` — rate-limit via `enforceRateLimit("reports.deliver")`, lookup recovery_item + dead letter, slack cooldown short-circuit returns 200 `{alreadyDispatched: true}` sin re-fire, dry-run gate via `runs.replayMode === 'validation'`, message composition con `composeHandoffMessage` (o `composeAppendMessage` para github append branch), upsert handoff row + audit on success AND failure. **NEW web** modifica `RecoveryItemDrawer.tsx` con nueva sección "Handoff" entre transition buttons y comments. Form con destination select + credential dropdown filtrado por kind (slack_webhook/github_token/webhook_secret) + per-destination required fields (github: owner+repo; webhook/linear: url). History inline mostrando `dispatchCount` + externalUrl clickable por destination ya disparado. **~24 EN+ES i18n keys** bajo `recoveryHandoff.*` parity-gated. **~14 nuevos tests** (shared 14: zod superRefine per-destination + composeHandoffMessage create+append modes + secret scrub + idempotency key + cooldown helper). Total: 2046+ tests verde. **Honest non-goals:** no Slack threading (incoming webhook only — v2 puede mover a Web API + threadTs); no reply-from-Slack (ENG-143 native Slack app); no Linear native SDK (webhook.send + operator-side receiver); no GitHub issue title edits (sólo body append); no bulk handoff; no retry queue automático (operator re-click; idempotency table soporta el retry sin duplicate); no cross-org "últimos handoffs" feed (data layer ya lo expone via `listRecentHandoffs` pero no se renderiza en una tarjeta dedicada en v1); GET single recovery item NO devuelve handoffs todavía (el web los carga via fetch separado — futuro: enrich GET response). |
| ENG-125 | Add workflow SLO policy engine | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §2 as the foundational upstream blocker for ENG-122 (recovery alerting). AC: workflows can declare reliability SLOs that drive future alerts. Extend `workflow_versions` with an `slo_json` column (nullable); SLO evaluation reuses `computeWorkflowHealth` signals (already shipped) without duplicate queries — health math returns SLO breach booleans alongside scores; new route `POST /workflows/:id/slo` (admin) writes the SLO and audits `workflow.slo.set`; UI surfaces an SLO breach pill on the health badge; the save chokepoint carries the SLO forward to every subsequent version so the contract is automatic; rollback carries the SLO forward too (the latest reliability contract isn't undone by a DAG rollback). Tests cover window bounds, null SLO equals no-policy, threshold changes, breach detection, and tenant scope. **Summary:** zero-overhead contract layer over the existing health rollup. **NEW `packages/shared/src/workflow-slo.ts`** (zero-runtime-dep) exports `WorkflowSloSchema` (closed-key Zod 4 — `successRatePercent` 0..100 nullable / `mttrSeconds` int ≥0 nullable / `p95DurationMs` int ≥0 nullable / `budgetBlocksPerWindow` int ≥0 nullable / `stuckWaitingNodesMax` int ≥0 nullable / `windowDays` literal union 7\|14\|30) and the pure `evaluateWorkflowSlo(slo, signals)` helper. Sample-size guard: success-rate + p95 breaches do not fire when `totalRuns < MIN_SAMPLE_SIZE = 5` (mirrors `computeWorkflowHealth`'s neutral-default rule). **v1 evaluates `successRatePercent` + `p95DurationMs` against the already-shipped `HealthSignals` shape;** the other three threshold fields (`mttrSeconds`, `budgetBlocksPerWindow`, `stuckWaitingNodesMax`) persist in the schema for forward-compat but their breach flags always return `false` in v1. This keeps the column stable so a future signal-wiring change does not need a migration. **Migration** `packages/db/migrations/20260522132352_round_natasha_romanoff/migration.sql` adds the nullable `slo_json` jsonb column on `workflow_versions`. **NEW `packages/data/src/workflowSloRepo.ts`** is the data chokepoint — `getWorkflowSlo(orgId, workflowId)` reads the latest version's `slo_json`, `setWorkflowSlo(orgId, workflowId, slo)` updates the latest version in place and returns the previous SLO for the audit row. Multi-tenant scope on every query via `eq(workflow_versions.orgId, orgId)`. **Save chokepoint** in `apps/api/src/workflows-save.ts` now reads the prior latest's `sloJson` BEFORE inserting the new version row and copies it forward — operators don't re-declare the SLO on every workflow edit. **Rollback chokepoint** in `packages/data/src/workflowRollbackRepo.ts` mirrors the same carry-forward — rolling back the DAG does not roll back the reliability contract. **Engine integration** in `packages/engine/src/workflow-health.ts` accepts an optional `slo?: WorkflowSlo | null` input and attaches the resolved `{ slo, breaches }` block as `HealthScore.slo`. The block is null when no SLO is declared; the numeric score is unchanged whether or not SLO is supplied (the contract is orthogonal to the rollup). **NEW route `POST /workflows/:id/slo`** in `apps/api/src/routes/workflows-routes.ts` is admin-only via the route registry's declarative `role: "admin"` gate; the matcher accepts `/workflows/<id>/slo` and explicitly rejects unrelated suffixes. Body validated via `z.object({ slo: WorkflowSloSchema.nullable() })` — `null` clears the declaration. Audits `workflow.slo.set` with `{ slo, previousSlo, versionId }` metadata via the existing `audit` chokepoint (which routes through `safePersistPayload` for the standard redaction posture). `/workflows/health` extended additively to load the SLO via `getWorkflowSlo` in parallel with `collectHealthSignals` and pass it into `computeWorkflowHealth`; the delta route does the same on both the before-side and after-side computations (same SLO applies to both sides — the operator's current contract is what they expect the workflow to satisfy regardless of the historical window). **Web UI** extends `apps/web/src/components/WorkflowHealthBadge.tsx` with a red "SLO breach" pill rendered when `slo?.breaches.anyBreach === true`. The expanded badge panel adds an SLO section listing each declared metric (null thresholds are skipped) with a per-metric pass/breach color tint. **NEW `apps/web/src/components/WorkflowSloPanel.tsx`** mounted inside the Inspector right panel next to `VersionHistoryPanel` — three bounded inputs (success-rate %, p95 duration ms, evaluation window 7\|14\|30) plus a Clear button. The two unsupported-in-v1 metrics intentionally do NOT render input fields; the helper-text under the form documents `Accepted but not yet evaluated in v1.` Submit calls `POST /workflows/:id/slo` and bumps `platformVersion` on success so the badge refetches without a reload. Non-admins see the form but the server's `role: "admin"` gate rejects the write through the existing 403 toast envelope. **EN+ES i18n** ships ~22 new parity-gated keys under `badges.slo.*` + `workflowSlo.*`; the parity test in `apps/web/src/i18n/parity.test.ts` gates the build. **~26 new test cases** across shared (14 — `WorkflowSloSchema` + `evaluateWorkflowSlo`), data (6 — repo unit), engine (3 — health integration), api (7 — route shape + body validation + audit emission + null-clear), web (4 — pill render + breakdown expansion). **Honest non-goals:** no breach event emission to a subscriber bus (ENG-122 will land the alerting plumbing — v1 just returns breach booleans + the audit row as the persistence trail); no automatic SLO recommendations from history (that's ENG-141 anomaly detection); no dedicated "SLO change log" — the `workflow.slo.set` audit rows ARE the change log; no multi-window simultaneous evaluation (one `windowDays` at a time); no org-default SLO template (operator declares per workflow); no MTTR / budget-block / stuck-waiting-node breach evaluation in v1 (the threshold fields persist for forward-compat, but the v1 evaluator returns false on all three until the corresponding signals are wired); the breach booleans do NOT change the numeric health score — they are a separate orthogonal contract surface. |
| ENG-126 | Add credential health preflight | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §2 as the first Wave A starter with no upstream Wave A dependency. AC: surface credential and tool issues BEFORE a run fails. `getCredentialHealth(orgId)` data helper returns each credential's `{ secretRefPresent, lastUsedAt, lastErrorAt, lastErrorMessage (scrubbed via scrubSecretShapes), kind }` plus the workflows that reference it (latest version only); helper joins `usage_events` for last-use via `metadata.credentialName`; new route `GET /credentials/health` (viewer, `credentials.read` permission); `checkWorkflowReadiness` gains a `credential_missing` warn rule for every referenced credential whose env-var resolves to undefined; Operations UI gets a "Credential health" card listing problems with explicit "Used by N workflow(s)" labels — the env-var name is NEVER echoed (mirrors `executeTool`'s `credential secret missing for <name>` envelope); MCP connections show `status: pending|active|failed|disabled` plus per-tool enabled count plus a stale-discovery sentinel (`staleDays`). Tests cover env-name redaction, multi-tenant scope, disabled MCP connection visibility, the readiness rule firing on each ref class (tool / MCP), and the empty-state fallback. **Summary:** ships observability without rotation. **NEW `packages/data/src/credentialHealthRepo.ts`** with `getCredentialHealth(orgId, resolver)` running three bulk queries (credentials list, `usage_events` aggregate filtered on `metadata ? 'credentialName'`, latest-version-per-workflow scan with defensive `LIMIT 500`). `lastErrorMessage` passes through `scrubSecretShapes` before return. `SecretRefResolver` is the SOLE chokepoint that touches `process.env`; production wires `apps/api/src/readiness-helpers.ts:productionSecretRefResolver`. MCP block reuses `listConnections` + `listToolDescriptors` (mirrors `GET /mcp/connections` enrichment); surfaces `envRefsMissingKeys` as the operator-chosen KEYS (NOT env var names). **`apps/api/src/readiness-helpers.ts`** extended with `getCredentialReadinessIssues(orgId, workflow, resolver)` sidecar — engine's pure-sync `checkWorkflowReadiness` stays unchanged; the sidecar pattern mirrors `checkRollbackAvailability`. Wired into all four call sites (`/workflows/readiness`, `/workflows/save`, `/workflows/health/delta`, `/start`). New permission keys `credentials.read` (viewer-default) + `credentials.write` (admin-default) under a new `credentials` category; `POST /credentials` gains the new permission alongside its existing `role: admin` (defense-in-depth). **`apps/web/src/components/CredentialHealthCard.tsx`** mounted between `PermissionGrantsPanel` and `McpConnectionsPanel` in `OperationsPanel`. Severity-tinted rows (red on missing secret / failed MCP, amber on recent error / stale discovery / missing env refs, green otherwise). v1 carries no admin actions — copy points to the existing MCP panel for rediscover. 20 new EN+ES i18n keys under `operations.credentialHealth.*` + `readiness.credential_missing` (parity-gated; Spanish honours the no-Spanglish rule). ~17 new test cases (repo 8 + readiness sidecar 5 + route 3 + web card 4). **Honest non-goals:** no automatic credential rotation; no automatic MCP rediscover (operator triggers via existing panel); no live `listTools()` for descriptor drift (staleness sentinel only); no new credential kind; no alert delivery (that's ENG-122). |
| ENG-127 | Build eval datasets from recoveries | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md`. Convert accepted recovery suggestions into a reusable regression bed with explicit opt-in. AC: extend `recovery_feedback` with optional `evalConsent: boolean` (default false; threaded through the body schema → repo insert → audit metadata); new `eval_datasets` + `eval_examples` tables scoped to `orgId`; admin route to create a dataset and pull approved examples; every example stores scrubbed input context, expected outcome, approval label, retention metadata; `scrubSecretShapes` runs at write time AND read time (defense in depth); examples export as JSONL via the existing report-download helpers; eval examples are framed as DATA, never instructions, when surfaced to an LLM. Tests cover opt-in enforcement (no consent → row not eligible), scrubbing (write + read), deletion/export, cross-org isolation, and prompt-injection framing. **Summary:** **NEW `eval_datasets` + `eval_examples` tables** (`packages/db/src/schema.ts`) — datasets unique on `(orgId, name)`, examples carry a self-describing scrubbed snapshot so a later `recovery_feedback` retention purge doesn't shrink a published dataset. `recovery_feedback` gains `eval_consent boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`. **NEW `packages/data/src/evalDatasetsRepo.ts`** — `createEvalDataset` snapshots eligible feedback in one transaction; the OPT-IN GATE lives in `queryEligibleFeedbackForEval` which filters `accepted = true AND eval_consent = true AND orgId` (org-scoped LEFT JOIN to `dead_letters` for the `normalizeErrorSignature`-derived failure signature; orphaned feedback still yields an empty-signature example). `deleteEvalDataset` hard-deletes examples then the dataset (children first, cascade per the `workflow_versions` precedent). Every read/write carries `eq(<table>.orgId, orgId)`. **NEW `packages/shared/src/eval-dataset.ts`** (zero-dep) — `serializeEvalExamplesToJsonl` (one scrubbed JSON object per line, re-runs `scrubSecretShapes` at read time on top of the write-time pass) + `composeEvalExamplePrompt` which frames examples as data with the same suspicion-framing escape clause `composeGenerationSystemPrompt` uses for MCP tool exposure (50-example cap + truncation footer). **NEW `apps/api/src/routes/eval-datasets-routes.ts`** — 5 routes registered through the registry: `GET /eval/datasets` (list), `POST /eval/datasets` (admin create + 409 on dup name), `GET /eval/datasets/:id`, `GET /eval/datasets/:id/export?format=jsonl|json` (JSONL default, dual-form Content-Disposition via the shared `report-download` helpers), `DELETE /eval/datasets/:id` (admin). New permission category `evals` with `evals.read` (viewer) + `evals.write` (admin); three audit actions `eval.dataset.created` / `_deleted` / `_exported`; three error codes `eval_dataset_not_found` / `_name_exists` / `_name_required` with EN+ES parity. 26 new test cases (shared 7 + repo 8 + route 11). **Honest non-goals:** no LLM eval-runner wiring (the dataset is the regression bed; running it against the patch route is a follow-up); no rejection-capture (only accepted+consented rows are eligible — the catalog supports adding rejections as a one-line change); no web UI panel (admin curates via the API in v1); no retention-sweep integration for `eval_examples` (the snapshot is durable by design). |
| ENG-128 | Add prompt/model experiment harness | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md`. Operators compare prompt versions and model choices against an eval dataset. Depends on ENG-111 + ENG-127. AC: new `experiments` table (`orgId`, `kind` prompt/model/prompt_and_model, `controlRef`, `candidateRef`, `evalDatasetId`, `status`, `summaryJson`); `POST /experiments/run` (admin) runs each eval example through BOTH control and candidate via `LlmClient.generateText`, captures `costUsd`/`latencyMs`/`aiError` + a configurable scorer (string equality / JSONSchema match / LLM-as-judge with deterministic fallback); budget-gated via `gateBudget`, writes `usage_events`; promotion is recommendation-only; audit `experiment.run.started/completed/promotion_suggested`. Tests cover deterministic fixture evals, unknown-model fallback, cost-null handling, no production mutation, and audit rows. **Summary:** **NEW `experiments` table** (`packages/db/src/schema.ts`) — org-scoped A/B comparison row (`kind` / `controlRef` / `candidateRef` / `evalDatasetId` / `scorerKind` / `status` / `summaryJson` / `completedAt`); orphan-tolerant (deleting the eval dataset doesn't touch the experiment — the summary snapshot stands alone). **NEW `packages/ai/src/experiment-scorer.ts`** (pure, zero-dep) — three scorers: `string_equality` (forgiving trim/case/whitespace), `json_schema` (output validated against the expected JSON Schema, Zod-compiled, passthrough on extra keys), `llm_judge` (data-framed prompt, reads back a clamped 0..1 score, deterministic token-overlap fallback when client null OR the judge throws OR the reply is unparseable — NEVER throws). **NEW `packages/ai/src/experiment-runner.ts`** (data-agnostic — `ai` has no `@janusly/data` dep) — `runExperiment` takes two resolved arms (`{ systemPrompt?, modelHint? }`) + eval examples, runs both sides through `LlmClient.generateText` (each call writes a `usage_events` row via the existing LLM-client recorder chokepoint), catches per-side throws as `aiError`/empty-output/score-0 (AI fallback contract), tallies mean score / cost (null-cost excluded from the sum) / latency / errors, and emits a non-binding `recommendation` (`promote_candidate` only when `scoreDelta ≥ MIN_SCORE_DELTA`). **NEW `packages/data/src/experimentsRepo.ts`** — `createExperiment` (status `running`), `recordExperimentResult` (CAS on `(orgId, id)`, writes the route-sanitized summary verbatim — `data` can't import `engine`, so `safePersistPayload` runs in the route), `listExperiments` / `getExperiment` (org-scoped). **NEW `apps/api/src/routes/experiments-routes.ts`** — `GET /experiments` + `GET /experiments/:id` (viewer, `evals.read`), `POST /experiments/run` (admin, `evals.write`): resolves prompt refs via `resolvePromptRef`, budget-gates BEFORE the run, rate-limits on the shared `"ai"` bucket, runs the comparison, `safePersistPayload`s the summary, and writes `experiment.run.started` + `_completed` + `_promotion_suggested` (the last only on a favourable result). PROMOTION IS RECOMMENDATION-ONLY — the route NEVER writes to `prompts` / `prompt_versions` / `org_configs`. Reuses the existing `evals` permission category; 3 audit actions; 5 error codes (`experiment_invalid_body` / `_dataset_not_found` / `_dataset_empty` / `_prompt_ref_invalid` / `experiment_not_found`) with EN+ES parity. 40 new test cases (ai scorer 13 + ai runner 10 + repo 6 + route 11). **Honest non-goals:** no auto-promotion (recommendation-only by design); no web UI panel (admin drives via the API in v1, mirroring ENG-127); examples drive the comparison via their captured `inputContext` → `expectedApproachLabel` shape (a richer prompt-template-per-example dataset is a follow-up); the LLM judge bills the shared `"ai"` bucket but its per-example calls aren't individually gated. |

Run-event read bounded by `RUN_EVENT_FETCH_CAP=300` with a truncation flag. **NEW engine builder** `packages/engine/src/recovery-evidence-report.ts` (pure, no I/O) — `buildRecoveryEvidenceReport(bundle)` REUSES `buildRunExplainReport` for the run summary + timeline (so the two artefacts never drift), computes the selected patch diff via the shared `computeWorkflowDiff(dlq.workflowJson, validationRun.inputJson.workflow)`, reconstructs the approval trail from `approval`/`human_form` nodes + their `approval.*` events, lifts the AI explanation mode from the `ai.workflow.patch_suggested` audit metadata, and renders both `{ markdown, json }`. Every rendered free-form string runs through `scrubSecretShapes` (defense in depth on top of the write-time `safePersistPayload` chokepoint). The nested run block is stamped with the evidence envelope's `generatedAt` so two exports of the same incident are byte-identical (the run-explain builder otherwise stamps its own `Date.now()`). **NEW download helpers** `apps/api/src/report-download.ts` — `slugify` / `buildReportFilename` / `contentDispositionAttachment` extracted verbatim from `reports-routes.ts` (which now imports them) so the run-explain, value-dashboard, and evidence download surfaces share one dual-form (RFC 6266 ASCII + RFC 5987 UTF-8) Content-Disposition rule. **NEW route** `POST /recovery/items/:id/evidence` appended to `apps/api/src/routes/recovery-items-routes.ts` — `role: "editor"` AND `permission: "recovery.read"` (registry requires BOTH, so editor-rank + recovery.read exports; a plain viewer cannot); `?format=json|markdown` (default JSON — the structured envelope is the compliance artefact); resolves → builds → audits `report.evidence.exported` (metadata carries `format`, `deadLetterId`, `workflowId`, `runId`, `validationRunId`, `hasPatchDiff`, `auditRowCount` — never the env-var behind any secret) → streams the download with the dual-form Content-Disposition + `Access-Control-Expose-Headers`. **NEW audit action** `report.evidence.exported` added to the closed `AuditAction` union. **NEW web** extends `downloadFromApi(path, options)` to accept `{ method: 'POST', body }` (back-compat: a string 2nd arg is still treated as a filename) and adds an "Audit evidence" Export JSON / Export Markdown control to `RecoveryItemDrawer.tsx` (read-only — no `bumpPlatformVersion`); new `--we-*`-token CSS for the section. **6 EN+ES i18n keys** under `recoveryItems.evidence.*` (parity-gated, "tú" register; JSON/Markdown stay English as accepted technical nouns). **23 new tests** across engine (11 — full envelope, patch diff via `computeWorkflowDiff` with retry-tag, null-validation-run → null diff, failed sandbox summary, **secret scrub across every rendered string**, **timeline pagination cap**, approval-trail reconstruction, audit-trail actor, **stable deterministic JSON+MD**, graceful partial when DLQ/run gone, rollback url omitted without base URL), data (5 — **tenant scope on item read + null short-circuit reads nothing downstream**, DLQ/run/version reads org-scoped, **run-event fetch cap + truncation flag**, full bundle + rollback, no-prior-version → null rollback), api (7 — **resolver scoped to auth.orgId**, **uniform 404 on missing/cross-org**, JSON default attachment, Markdown format, unknown-format 400, audit-row shape, editor+recovery.read declared).
