| ENG-133 | Add retention and archive policy | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md`. Customers can configure retention within safe bounds. Depends on ENG-114 (memory policy — shipped). AC: new safe `org_configs.retention.*` keys (`runEventsDays` 7..365 default 90, `auditLogsDays` 30..730 default 365, `usageEventsDays` 30..365 default 90, `recoveryFeedbackDays` 30..365 default 180, `memoryEntriesDays` 7..730 default 90); a daily BullMQ-scheduler-managed purge job deletes expired rows scoped to `orgId`, writes a summary event, and audits `retention.purged`; legal-hold bypass via a per-row `holdUntil` timestamp checked by the purge; tests cover catalog validation, retention job bounds, hold bypass, export-before-delete, and cross-org isolation. **Summary:** Built ON TOP of the three existing standalone retention sweeps (`audit-logs` / `scim-events` / `memory`), not duplicating them. **Catalog:** `orgConfigRepo.ts` gains a new `retention` category + the 5 number keys with the AC's bounds/defaults (each `min`/`max`-validated by the existing `normalizeOrgConfigValue` pipeline + env fallbacks `JANUSLY_RETENTION_*_DAYS`), plus a narrow `getRetentionPolicyConfig(orgId)` reader (mirror of `getAuthPolicyConfig` — scoped ≤5-row read, re-clamps a stored out-of-range/non-numeric value back to the catalog default so the sweep never deletes against a nonsense window, fails soft to defaults on a DB blip). **Schema:** additive nullable `hold_until timestamptz` column on all 5 target tables (`run_events`, `usage_events`, `audit_logs`, `recovery_feedback`, `memory_entries`) — the legal-hold bypass; a future-dated value exempts the row from the sweep. **Repo:** new `retentionRepo.ts` with 5 per-org `deleteExpired<Table>ForOrg` helpers (same 10k-row batched-DELETE + `maxBatches` 1k-cap shape as `auditLogsRepo.deleteExpiredAuditLogs`; every DELETE carries `org_id = $orgId` AND the `(hold_until IS NULL OR hold_until <= now())` bypass conjunct; `run_events` — which has no org column — scopes through the parent `runs.org_id` via a join), `listOrgIdsForRetention()` (UNION-distinct across the 5 tables, excludes the `system` sentinel in-query), and the `setRetentionExporter`/`runRetentionExport` DI seam for export-before-delete (operator wires an archiver; never load-bearing — a thrown exporter degrades to a warn and the purge proceeds). Barrel-exported (flat + `retentionRepo` namespace). **Scheduler:** new `retention-scheduler.ts` (engine) mirroring the sibling sweeps — reserved `system:retention` id, `JANUSLY_RETENTION_CRON` default `0 5 * * *` UTC (after the 02:00/03:00/04:00 sweeps), `validateCronExpression` warn-fallback, never-throws. Per ORG (unlike the cross-org siblings): iterates `listOrgIdsForRetention`, reads each org's bounds once, runs the export seam BEFORE purging, purges all 5 tables in series (a per-table failure is isolated, the loop continues), and writes exactly one `retention.purged` audit row per org with the real `orgId` + a per-table breakdown. One org's failure never aborts the rest. Worker wires `registerRetentionScheduler` at boot + `handleRetentionTrigger` on `RETENTION_JOB_NAME`. **Audit:** `retention.purged` added to the closed `AuditAction` union (system-actor write — engine scheduler, real orgId, `userId: null`, via direct `db.insert(auditLogs)` like the sibling sweeps since the `audit()` chokepoint lives in `apps/api`). **45 new tests:** catalog (12 — 5-key presence, category/type, AC defaults+ranges, in-range/floor/out-of-range normalization), `getRetentionPolicyConfig` (5 — defaults, overrides, out-of-range clamp, non-numeric ignore, fail-soft), `retentionRepo` (19 — hold-bypass + orgId-bind on every table, run_events-via-runs join, timestamptz cutoff param, batched-loop bounds incl. cap + short-batch-not-capped, listOrgIds distinct/union/system-exclusion, export seam present/invoked/never-throws), scheduler (11 — cron bounds, per-org config flow-through, export-before-delete ordering, one-audit-per-org breakdown, per-table-failure isolation, cross-org isolation, enumerate-failure skip). Self-typecheck clean on data/engine/api/db; full data (255) + engine (836) + api (685) suites green. **Out of scope (acknowledged):** no admin HTTP route for the export seam (the DI hook is wired but no operator-facing `/retention/export` endpoint ships — a follow-up; the `retention.*` config is already settable via the existing `POST /org/config`); no `run_nodes`/`proactive_observations`/`ai_chat_sessions` tables (the AC + scope note lock the 5 listed tables; those land when their owning features ship); no web panel (config is admin-API-only this ticket); the migration is generated once by the orchestrator from the appended `schema.ts` columns (5× nullable `hold_until`). |
| ENG-137 | Add live run streaming over SSE | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §3 — the last unshipped Wave A (Recovery operationality) item. Replace polling for active runs with a tenant-scoped SSE stream so operators see failures in real time. AC: new route `GET /runs/:runId/stream` (viewer, `runs.read`) fans `run_events` rows + run status transitions to subscribed clients via Redis pub/sub; per-org subscription cap (default 50, configurable via `org_configs.runs.streamMaxSubscriptions`); each event payload reuses the same `safePersistPayload` redaction the polling route uses; stream auto-closes on terminal status + 30s grace; web `RunsPanel` + `RecoveryCenterPanel`/Operations open the stream and fall back to polling when SSE is blocked; Operations shows a "Live" pill while a stream is healthy; never deliver events to a different org's subscriber; SSE is gated by `auth.orgId` at connection time AND at every event publish; reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`. **Summary:** Shipped as a Redis pub/sub bridge with the engine as the publish chokepoint and the API as the subscriber/SSE server. New engine DI seam `packages/engine/src/run-event-stream.ts` (`setRunEventPublisher` / `publishRunEvent`, never throws; `createRedisRunEventPublisher` centralizes the channel name `janusly:run-events:<runId>` + `{orgId,event}` message shape + a bounded immutable `runId→orgId` cache) wired into `persistence.ts` `appendEvent` (every event row) + the `succeeded`/`failed`/`cancelled` run-status flips (ephemeral `run.status` signals). Both the API (`apps/api/src/run-stream.ts`, reusing the request-path Redis client for PUBLISH) and the worker (`packages/engine/src/run-event-redis.ts`, its own PUBLISH client) register the publisher at boot; the worker is the load-bearing writer. The API subscriber hub holds ONE dedicated ioredis subscriber connection, refcounts per-run channel SUBSCRIBE, fans out in-process to every open SSE response, enforces a per-replica per-org cap (in-process Map — connections are process-pinned, so a Redis counter would only leak on crash), and re-checks `msg.orgId === subscriber.orgId` before writing (defense in depth on top of the per-run channel + the route's `run.orgId === auth.orgId` gate). The route (`runs-routes.ts`, registered before the `/runs` list matcher) writes a 25s heartbeat comment (the server destroys idle sockets at 60s), replays the `Last-Event-ID` gap via the existing `(createdAt,id)` keyset, and arms a 30s terminal grace close. The dead DB-polling `/events` route was removed (zero consumers). **Web uses `fetch` + `ReadableStream`, NOT native `EventSource`** — `EventSource` can't send Janusly's header-based auth, so the new `openRunEventStream` helper reuses the exact `api()` headers and the new `useRunEventStream` hook parses SSE frames into the store via the merge path; the existing 1.5s `/status` poll loop stays as a fallback whose tick is a no-op while SSE is live (`streamTransport === 'sse'`) and resumes on any stream fault. New store field `streamTransport` drives the `RunStreamChip` "Live"/"Polling" pill (green/amber, reduced-motion-aware) mounted in `RunsPanel` + the Operations header. New safe `org_configs.runs.streamMaxSubscriptions` (default 50, range 1..1000, env `JANUSLY_STREAM_MAX_SUBSCRIPTIONS`) — no schema migration. EN+ES `runStream.*` i18n keys (parity-gated). Tests: engine seam (8), API hub refcount/cross-org/cap/redaction (7), web hook frame-parse + fallback + terminal-handoff (5); `runs-routes` itself stays untested at module level by precedent (imports `start-run → queue → BullMQ`). Live smoke (real stack): catch-up replay streamed all 8 real `run_events` with correct `id:`/`event: run-event`/`data:` framing; cross-org connect → 403; live Redis fan-out confirmed by connecting to a paused approval run (no `Last-Event-ID`) and observing the post-resume node lifecycle + `run-status: succeeded` signal arrive live; psql confirmed SSE writes zero new rows. Build + test + e2e (real Chromium starts/watches runs incl. human-form pause/resume) green. Net diff: new engine seam + worker publish client, API hub + boot wiring + frame helpers + CORS header allowance (`x-janusly-session`/`Accept-Language`/`Last-Event-ID`, a latent cross-origin SSO gap — collateral fix), new web hook + chip + CSS + store field, org-config key, EN/ES copy, 3 test files. Out of scope (acknowledged): cross-replica global cap (per-replica is correct for connection caps), cookie auth, streaming non-run surfaces. |
| ENG-138 | Add AI suggestion confidence calibration | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md`. Calibrate the LLM's self-rated patch `confidence` against the operator's observed accept/reject history so the Recovery dialog shows a trustworthy number, not the model's raw guess. AC: new `confidence_calibrations` table keyed `(orgId, approachLabel)` storing rolling 30-day accept rate, sample size, last computed, and a linear curve (`calibrated = a*raw + b` solved from observed accept rate per raw-confidence bucket from `recovery_feedback`); a daily BullMQ job per org recomputes; `/ai/patch-workflow` reads the calibration and emits BOTH raw and calibrated confidence; the recovery dialog renders the calibrated value as primary with a "(model self-rated X%)" subtitle when they differ ≥10 pts; <20 samples in the window → calibration skipped, raw shown unchanged; audit `confidence.calibration.computed` daily summary; opt-out via `org_configs.ai.confidenceCalibrationEnabled` (default true). **Summary:** Shipped as a data-collects / engine-computes split mirroring `recoveryMetricsRepo` + `recovery-metrics.ts`. **NEW DB schema** appends `confidence_calibrations` (`acceptRate`, `sampleSize`, `curveSlope`, `curveIntercept`, `lastComputedAt`, unique `(orgId, approachLabel)`) PLUS a nullable `raw_confidence` integer column on `recovery_feedback` — the calibration sweep buckets only rows carrying it, so headless/legacy rows drop out of the fit (never skew it toward 0). **NEW engine math** `packages/engine/src/confidence-calibration.ts` (pure, no I/O) — `fitCalibrationCurve(samples)` buckets decisions by raw confidence (10-pt buckets), takes each bucket's observed accept rate as the y-target, weighted-least-squares fits a line, and returns `null` below `MIN_CALIBRATION_SAMPLES=20` OR when x-variance is too low OR — the **monotonicity guard** — when the slope is non-positive (an inverted fit could re-order suggestions, so it degrades to raw). `applyCalibration(raw, curve)` maps + clamps to [0,100]; a `null` curve returns raw unchanged, and because fitted curves always have a positive slope the mapping is monotonic in raw (suggestion ordering can never invert). `CALIBRATION_SUBTITLE_DELTA=10` exported for the web. **NEW data repo** `packages/data/src/confidenceCalibrationRepo.ts` (barrel-exported flat + namespace) — `listCalibrationSamples` (org+approach-scoped, `raw_confidence IS NOT NULL`, 30-day window, 5k cap), `listCalibratableApproaches`, `listCalibrations` (read by the patch route), `upsertCalibration` (INSERT ON CONFLICT keyed `(orgId, approachLabel)`), `listOrgIdsWithCalibrationEnabled` (feedback orgs MINUS those whose `ai.confidenceCalibrationEnabled` is explicitly `false`). Multi-tenant scope on every query; the org-discovery read is the documented lone un-scoped read. **NEW engine scheduler** `packages/engine/src/confidence-calibration-scheduler.ts` — global `system:confidence-calibration` daily cron (`JANUSLY_CONFIDENCE_CALIBRATION_CRON` default `30 1 * * *` UTC, `validateCronExpression` warn-fallback, never-throws) mirroring `audit-logs-retention-scheduler.ts`; walks every opted-in org, fits each calibratable approach, isolates per-approach + per-org failures, and writes one per-org `confidence.calibration.computed` audit row (real `orgId`, `userId: null`, direct `db.insert(auditLogs)` like the sibling sweeps). Worker wires `registerConfidenceCalibrationScheduler` at boot + `handleConfidenceCalibrationTrigger` on the job name. **NEW org config key** `ai.confidenceCalibrationEnabled` (boolean, default true) added to the catalog + the `OrgConfigSnapshot.ai` type + the snapshot builder. **Patch route** (`apps/api/src/routes/ai-routes.ts:POST /ai/patch-workflow`) reads the curves once per request (gated on the config flag, best-effort — a DB blip degrades to "no calibration"), indexes them by approach, and stamps each suggestion with `calibratedConfidence` alongside `confidence`; the sort stays on raw `confidence` so ordering is preserved; audit metadata gains `calibrationEnabled` + `calibrationCurvesAvailable`. **Feedback write path** threads `rawConfidence` end-to-end: the `RecoveryFeedbackBodySchema` (`z.number().int().min(0).max(100).optional()`), the `recordRecoveryFeedback` repo input, the `POST /recovery/feedback` handler, and all four `recordFeedback` call sites in `RecoveryDialog.tsx` (apply-success ×2, cancel, iterate) now pass `selected.confidence`. **NEW web** `RecoveryDialog.tsx` — `SuggestionTab` gains optional `calibratedConfidence`; the tab renders the calibrated number as the primary `we-recovery-tab__confidence-primary` and a dimmer `we-recovery-tab__confidence-self` subtitle "(model self-rated X%)" only when `|calibrated − raw| ≥ CALIBRATION_SUBTITLE_DELTA` (local literal `10`, mirrored from engine since web can't import it); `normalisePatchSuggestion` mirrors raw onto calibrated for the legacy shape. **3 EN+ES i18n keys** under `recoveryDialog.review.*` (`tabConfidenceTitle` softened to "Confidence", new `tabCalibratedTitle` + `selfRated`; parity-gated, ES "tú" register). **NEW CSS** for the stacked confidence pill (`--we-*`-token, no inline hex). **30 new tests:** engine math (11 — sample-size threshold at/under 20, no-rows → null, degenerate single-bucket → null, **inverted-fit rejected**, raw fallback, clamp, **monotonicity across pairs + descending ranking preserved**, over-confident pull-down, subtitle-delta constant), data repo (8 — null-`raw_confidence` filter, empty → raw upstream, scoped read verbatim, **opt-out subtraction**, default-on, empty discovery, upsert ON CONFLICT shape), scheduler (11 — cron bounds/default/invalid/redis-fail, fit + **threshold skip**, **per-approach failure isolation**, zero-approach no-audit, **per-org tenant scope**, enumerate-failure warn, empty no-op). Self-typecheck clean on db/data/engine/api; `vite build` green (web); full data (277) + engine (858) + api (707) suites + web i18n parity (3) green. **Out of scope (acknowledged):** no admin "recalibrate now" route (the `runOrgCalibration` helper is exported for a future one); no per-bucket calibration display (single linear curve in v1, as the AC specifies); no calibration history/drift table (the daily audit row IS the trail); no confidence calibration on `/ai/suggest-improvement` (the AC scopes it to `/ai/patch-workflow`); the migration (new table + the `recovery_feedback.raw_confidence` column) is generated once by the orchestrator from the appended `schema.ts`; calibration only has data once operators start deciding on suggestions *after* this ships (the `raw_confidence` column is nullable, so pre-existing feedback rows never participate). |

`sourceRef` is the deep-link target: approachLabel / memory entry id / workflow id / run id / signature category / tool name) + three async orchestrators (`assembleRecoveryEvidence` / `assembleExplainRunEvidence` / `assembleSuggestImprovementEvidence`) that fetch the two supplemental sources the routes didn't already hold (workflow runbook via `getWorkflowMetadata`, recent failures via `queryFailureSamples`) and funnel everything through `scrubEvidenceRows`; all three routes attach `evidence` on BOTH the AI and fallback paths and add `evidenceCount` to their audit metadata (the rows never land in the audit row — only the count). **NEW web** `EvidencePanel` in `RecoveryDialog.tsx` — a collapsible `<details>` "Why this suggestion?" surface (one chip per row: kind label + one-line snippet + monospace `sourceRef` deep-link token, memory/runbook tinted cyan vs cobalt decision sources), re-scrubs every row via the shared `scrubEvidenceRow` at render (defense-in-depth), hidden entirely when evidence is empty; mounted inside the review tabpanel below `WorkflowDiffView`; `--we-*`-token CSS, no inline hex. ~9 EN+ES `recoveryDialog.evidence.*` i18n keys incl. a pluralized summary (parity-gated, "tú" register, "runbook"/"Slack" stay English). | ENG-153 | Add recovery cooldown / debounce | Shipped | P1 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md` §13.1.7. One recovery item per failure storm, not one per DLQ row. AC: when a new DLQ entry arrives with the same `(orgId, workflowId, errorSignature)` as an existing still-open `recovery_items` row whose last occurrence is within `org_configs.recovery.debounceWindowSeconds` (default 300, bounds 30..3600, 0 = off), the new DLQ row attaches as a child (`recovery_item_children` keyed by `(recoveryItemId, deadLetterId)`) instead of creating a new item; parent carries `occurrenceCount` / `firstOccurredAt` / `lastOccurredAt`; UI surfaces an "N occurrences" badge + a child-DLQ drawer; ENG-122 alert policies dedupe over the parent (one `recovery_item.created` per storm, not per DLQ); resolving the parent closes the storm; ENG-117 auto-healing is unaffected (it clusters `dead_letters` by signature, which still persist 1:1). **Summary:** Threaded through the existing `createRecoveryItemForDeadLetter` hook (registered at both api + worker boot) — no new chokepoint. The DLQ adapter already computed + passed `errorSignature`; this ticket persists it on `recovery_items` as the debounce match key. Sliding window anchored on `lastOccurredAt` (each occurrence refreshes it), matched against any non-`resolved` item so an in-progress incident still absorbs repeats; `0` disables, non-zero clamps to `[30,3600]` in the engine. New data-layer functions `findOpenRecoveryItemForDebounce` (returns null without a DB read when signature is absent; orders by `lastOccurredAt` desc, limit 1) + `attachDeadLetterToRecoveryItem` (idempotent `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the child unique index; bumps `occurrenceCount` + `lastOccurredAt` ONLY when a row was actually inserted, so replays never double-count) + `listRecoveryItemChildren` / `countRecoveryItemChildren` (tenant-scoped, capped 200). The hook attaches + audits `recovery.item.occurrence_attached` and returns BEFORE `recordAlertEvent`, so a storm pages once; a defensive guard skips self-attaching a parent's own DLQ row; the never-throws posture holds (a debounce read failure degrades to creating the item). New route `GET /recovery/items/:id/children` (viewer, `recovery.read`, org-gated via the parent lookup) returns `{ children, total }`; the existing GET payloads gain the new fields via `hydrate`. Web `RecoveryItemBadge` shows an amber "N occurrences" pill (count > 1); `RecoveryItemDrawer` adds an occurrences section with a lazy-fetched child-DLQ list. New safe `org_configs.recovery.debounceWindowSeconds` (no schema migration for the key); one DB migration adds 4 columns to `recovery_items` + the `recovery_item_children` table + a `(orgId, workflowId, errorSignature)` lookup index (plain in-transaction `CREATE INDEX` — low-write table, no `CONCURRENTLY` runbook needed). EN+ES `recoveryItems.occurrences.*` i18n keys (parity-gated). Tests: data-repo (null-signature short-circuit, idempotent attach no-double-count, parent-vanished → null, child list cap/count) + engine hook (debounce off=0 → always create, attach + no created-alert, no-parent → create, signature-absent → create, self-attach guard, never-throws on lookup failure) + web badge (storm-pill gating) + org-config catalog (key + bounds) + i18n parity. SQL-filter correctness (window boundary, multi-workflow signature collision, tenant scope) verified against real Postgres in the live smoke. Out of scope (acknowledged): auto-resolving child `dead_letters` rows on parent resolve (operators use the existing DLQ + cluster-apply flow); merging the rare race-duplicate parent (documented, operator-resolvable; advisory-lock hardening noted as a follow-up); ENG-152 routing / ENG-149 push debounce. |
| ENG-155 | Add workflow snippets library | Shipped | P1 | 4 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md`. Composable patterns to drop into a workflow. Never mutates inputs. **NEW DB** appends the `snippets` table (unique `(orgId, name)`, `org_updated` index, `builtin` always false for rows — built-ins are code-only). **NEW data repo** `packages/data/src/snippetsRepo.ts` (`listSnippets` / `getSnippetById` / `findSnippetByName` reads + `createSnippet` / `updateSnippet` / `deleteSnippet` writes; every query carries `eq(snippets.orgId, orgId)`). **NEW routes** `apps/api/src/routes/snippets-routes.ts` — `GET /snippets` (viewer/`snippets.read`, concatenates built-ins ahead of custom rows), `POST /snippets` create + `POST /snippets/:id` update (POST-as-update) + `DELETE /snippets/:id` (admin/`snippets.write`, both reject a `builtin:` id with 409 `snippet_builtin_immutable`), and `POST /snippets/:id/inserted` (editor/`snippets.read`) — an audit beacon that persists nothing (insertion itself is client-side; the workflow only persists on the next Save). **NEW permission category** `snippets` (`snippets.read` viewer+, `snippets.write` admin). **NEW audit actions** `snippet.created` / `_updated` / `_deleted` / `_inserted`; **NEW error codes** `snippet_not_found` / `snippet_name_exists` / `snippet_builtin_immutable` (EN+ES `apiErrors.*`). **NEW web** `SnippetInsertMenu.tsx` (cmdk-shell dialog: fetch `/snippets`, search, target-node selector, built-in/custom badge, inserts via the shared `insertSnippet` into the Zustand graph + fires the audit beacon + toast) mounted in `App.tsx`; an "Insert snippet…" button in `InspectorPanel` (selected-node card + empty state) and a Cmd+K palette entry (`action.insertSnippet`). ~40 EN+ES `snippets.*` i18n keys including per-built-in name/description copy (parity-gated, "tú" register, "snippet" kept as accepted technical noun, no Spanglish). Tests (52 new, all green): shared (25 — built-in catalog shape, id-collision resolution, edge stitching to a target / explicit entry / no-target / ghost-target, no-mutation, condition preservation, create/update schema bounds), data (7 — orgId predicate captured on every read/write, builtin:false on insert), api routes (20 — gates, built-in concat ordering, built-in immutability 409 on update+delete, duplicate-name 409, 404s, audit emission, insertion beacon for built-in + custom), web i18n (built-in copy EN+ES presence + category labels). Self-typecheck clean on shared/data/db/api/engine; `vite build` green (web tsc runs through Vite); full shared+data+api+web vitest green. **Out of scope (acknowledged):** no web editor UI to AUTHOR custom snippets (API exists; `POST /snippets` is admin-only — a future ticket adds the form); no node auto-layout beyond a simple lane grid below the existing graph; no snippet versioning / sharing across orgs; no AI-suggested snippet insertion; the `snippet.inserted` beacon is best-effort (a failed beacon never blocks the insertion). |
