AC: `POST /credentials/:name/bulk-update` (admin + `credentials.write`) with preview→confirm; dry-run returns the affected-workflow list (id + name + node ids) without mutating; commit flips `credentials.secretRef`, audits `credential.bulk_updated` with the affected ids, guarded by optimistic concurrency (If-Match on `credentials.updatedAt`); the secret value / env-var name is never echoed; 2-step web modal. **Summary:** Shipped. New `credentials.updatedAt` column (`timestamptz(3)` — millisecond precision so the If-Match round-trips exactly against the JS `Date`; one migration). `rotateCredentialSecretRef` (CAS `UPDATE … WHERE org+name [+updatedAt]` in `credentialsRepo`; zero rows → `conflict` when the credential exists else `not_found`) + `resolveCredentialReferences` (in `credentialHealthRepo`, reusing the existing `loadLatestWorkflowVersions` + `readCredentialNameFromConfig` so the rotation preview and the credential-health card agree on "who uses this credential"). Route registered Open/Closed (predicate matcher, admin + `credentials.write`); it **honors the file's "env-var name is never on the wire" invariant** over the proposal AC — the response + the audit carry the affected-workflow list + the new `updatedAt` token, NEVER `secretRef` (the operator supplies the new env-var name, so echoing it back buys nothing and breaks the established posture that `GET /credentials` omits `secretRef` and `credential.created` audits only `kind`). New `credential.bulk_updated` audit action + `credential_rotation_conflict` API error code (EN/ES `apiErrors.*`). Web: `CredentialRotateModal` (preview→confirm; `ifMatch` = the `updatedAt` the preview returned; 409 → re-preview; `bumpPlatformVersion()` on success) wired into `CredentialsPanel`; `credentialRotation.*` EN/ES keys (parity-gated). "Readiness recompute" = invalidate-and-refetch (Janusly readiness is on-demand, not cached — no cron). Tests: rotate CAS happy/conflict/not_found (3), `resolveCredentialReferences` finds-refs-with-node-ids / excludes-non-referencing (2), route dry-run-no-mutation-no-audit / commit-secret-never-echoed-+-audit / 409 / 404 / 400-invalid-env-name / gate-shape (6). Build + test (2675+) + e2e green; live smoke confirmed dry-run mutates nothing (psql), commit flips `secret_ref` + bumps `updated_at` + one audit row + no env-var value in the response, stale If-Match → 409, cross-org → 404. Out of scope: rotating the secret VALUE (lives in vault/env, out of band), scheduled rotation, cross-credential batch. |
| ENG-157 | Expand trigger surface | Shipped | P2 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md`. More ways to start workflows without code. AC: add three new trigger node types following the `webhook`/`schedule`/`approval` pattern: `email_received` (v1 priority; per-org alias, DKIM-verified, body capped at 1 MiB, attachments via object-store under `orgs/<orgId>/email/...`), `file_dropped` (S3/R2 object-event), `mcp_server_event` (MCP 2025-06-18 resource-changed subscription); each writes a structured event before starting a run, supports DLQ-style replay, and is rate-limited per-trigger to prevent storms; AI generation grammar stays at 11 branches — the three new types emit as `noop` placeholders and operators promote them in the Inspector. **v1 carve-out:** in-process ingestion seam (no live SMTP relay / S3 bucket-notification listener) — an internal authenticated route that accepts a normalized inbound payload. **Summary:** Shipped. **NEW zero-dep shared module** `packages/shared/src/trigger-types.ts` — closed `triggerNodeTypeValues` (`email_received` / `file_dropped` / `mcp_server_event`, a strict subset of `nodeTypeValues`), per-type config Zod schemas (`EmailReceivedConfigSchema` alias-key regex + `dkimRequired` + optional `fromDomains` + `rateLimitPerMin`; `FileDroppedConfigSchema` bucket + prefix + extensions; `McpServerEventConfigSchema` connectionAlias + resourceUri + eventTypes), the normalized inbound-payload schemas the seam validates, the size caps (`EMAIL_BODY_MAX_BYTES` 1 MiB, `ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES` 1 MiB, `MAX_ATTACHMENTS` 20, `FILE_METADATA_MAX_BYTES` / `MCP_EVENT_PAYLOAD_MAX_BYTES` 64 KiB), and the storm-guard clamp (`resolveTriggerRateLimitPerMin`, default 60/min, ceiling 10 000). The three types append to `nodeTypeValues` + `NODE_CONFIG_SCHEMAS` (registry stays exhaustive over `NodeType`). **NEW engine module** `packages/engine/src/triggers.ts` — passthrough executors (mirror `schedule`): validate the authored config + succeed with `{ triggeredBy, triggeredAt, event }`, reading the normalized inbound event the seam stuffed into `runs.inputJson.input.event`; the executor NEVER reaches out to SMTP / the bucket / the MCP server (the seam owns all I/O). Registered in `node-registry.ts`; `workflow-validation.ts` gains a `trigger_invalid_config` per-type rule. **NEW DB** appends `trigger_events` (per-org structured inbound-event row — the DLQ-style replay anchor; `triggerType` / `workflowVersionId` / `nodeId` / closed-enum `status` `received`→`started`/`skipped`/`failed` / `runId` / `dedupeKey` / key-redacted `payloadJson`; unique `(orgId, dedupeKey)` for relay-retry idempotency + two operator-feed indexes; orphan-tolerant, no FK). **NEW data repo** `packages/data/src/triggerEventsRepo.ts` (`recordTriggerEvent` idempotent via `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` returning `wasCreated`; `markTriggerEventStarted`/`Skipped`/`Failed`; `getTriggerEvent`/`listTriggerEvents`; `resolveTriggerNode` walks the org's LATEST workflow versions via `DISTINCT ON` and matches a trigger node by a caller-supplied predicate — org-scoped so a relay authenticating as org A can only fire org A's triggers). **NEW route module** `apps/api/src/routes/trigger-ingest-routes.ts` — `POST /triggers/{email,file,mcp}/ingest` (editor + `triggers.ingest`): payload validate → size cap (413) → resolve matching node (404 cross-org) → persist `trigger_events` row BEFORE the run → per-trigger storm guard (`enforceRateLimit` bucket `trigger.<versionId>.<nodeId>`; over-limit → mark `skipped` + 429) → (email) offload attachments to the object store under `orgs/<orgId>/email/...` → `startRun` with the event as input → mark `started` + audit. `POST /triggers/events/:id/replay` re-runs a persisted event's stored payload (DLQ-style replay) against the current latest version with a fresh dedupe key. `GET /triggers/events` (viewer + `triggers.read`) is the recent-event feed. Email DKIM gate + optional sender-domain allow-list enforced at the seam. **NEW permission category** `triggers` (`triggers.read` viewer+, `triggers.ingest` editor+); **NEW audit actions** `trigger.event.{received,started,skipped,replayed}`; **NEW error codes** `trigger_invalid_payload` / `trigger_payload_too_large` / `trigger_no_matching_node` / `trigger_dkim_required` / `trigger_event_not_found` (EN+ES `apiErrors.*`). **AI generation** keeps the 11-branch grammar: the prompt's operator-only list grows from 9 → 12 with the three trigger types as `noop` placeholders (no auto-promotion — operators wire the per-org alias/bucket/connection in the Inspector). **NEW web** Inspector promotion — the three types added to `nodePresets`/`nodeTypes`, a new `triggers` sidebar category (with `schedule`), per-type `QuickConfigEditor` field forms + `getNodeConfigSummary` summaries + canvas icons (`Mail`/`FileInput`/`Radio`); ~30 EN+ES `nodes.*`/`nodeSummary.*`/`rightPanel.quickConfig.*`/`sidebar.category.triggers` i18n keys (parity-gated, "tú" register, "trigger"/"bucket"/"DKIM"/"MCP" kept as accepted technical nouns). Tests (68 new, all green): shared (21 — type guard, per-type config validation incl. alias-`@`-reject, payload size caps incl. UTF-8 1 MiB body + attachment count/size, storm-guard clamp), engine (15 — `resolveTriggerConfig` + the three passthrough executors event-passthrough / empty-on-manual-start / fail-fast-on-bad-config, + 2 workflow-validation cases for the `trigger_invalid_config` rule), api routes (11 — gate shapes, 400 bad payload, 413 oversized body, 404 cross-org isolation, 403 DKIM, run spawn + `started`, idempotent duplicate short-circuit, 429 storm guard + `skipped`, replay re-runs stored payload, 404 missing event), EN/ES parity. Self-typecheck clean on shared/engine/data/db/api; `vite build` green (web tsc runs through Vite); full shared+engine+data+db+api+web vitest green. **Out of scope (acknowledged, per v1 carve-out):** no live SMTP relay / SES-Mailgun forwarder, no S3/R2 bucket-event listener or polling fallback, no live MCP subscription client — the in-process ingestion seam accepts a normalized payload from those upstreams (a future ticket stands up the relays); replay re-resolves to the FIRST trigger node of the type in the current latest version (v1 doesn't pin the exact original node id); no per-org alias-domain provisioning UI (the operator types the alias local-part). |
| ENG-158 | Add cron observability | Shipped | P2 | 3 | Promoted from `docs/proposals/20260520-world-class-execution-plan.md`. Calendar + heatmap for scheduled workflows. AC: new route `GET /workflows/:id/schedule-history` (viewer) returns a 90-day heatmap of fire timestamps bucketed by `hour-of-day × day-of-week` from `schedule_entries.lastRunAt` history + `runs` rows joined by schedule trigger node id; UI adds a "Schedule" tab to the workflow detail panel rendering a GitHub-style heatmap (hand-written SVG, no `recharts` dep) with success/fail ratio per cell + next-N-fires preview computed from `cron-parser`; ENG-141 anomaly signals surface inline as red dots when a cell diverges from rolling baseline; tenant-scoped at the query layer; bounded query (max 90 days, max 1k rows per fetch). Tests cover heatmap bucketing, DST boundary (cron-parser is DST-safe), empty history rendering, tenant scope, and EN/ES copy. **Summary:** Shipped. **NEW data repo** `packages/data/src/scheduleHistoryRepo.ts` — `queryScheduleFires(orgId, workflowId, windowDays, rowCap)` collects scheduled-run fires for one workflow over a rolling window: `runs` joined through `workflow_versions` (multi-tenant + workflow scope) filtered to scheduler-created runs via `input_json -> 'input' ->> 'triggeredBy' = 'schedule'` (the marker `start-run` persists for cron fires), newest-first, window clamped to `[1,90]` days + row scan capped at ≤1000; `findScheduleEntriesForWorkflow(orgId, workflowId)` exposes the workflow's `schedule_entries` (cron expression + `last_run_at`) for the next-fires preview. No new table — the fire history lives in existing `runs` rows; `schedule_entries.last_run_at` was insufficient alone (only the LAST fire) so the rich history comes from the run rows. **NEW engine module** `packages/engine/src/schedule-history.ts` (pure, no I/O) — `bucketScheduleFires(fires)` folds observed UTC instants into a sparse day-of-week × hour-of-day grid with per-cell `{ total, success, fail }` + roll-up totals (empty history → empty grid), and `computeNextFires(cron, count)` previews the next N fires via `cron-parser` (DST-safe; degrades to `[]` on a malformed cron, never throws). Lives in engine (not data) because the preview needs the `cron-parser` dep already used by `schedule.ts`. **NEW route** `GET /workflows/:id/schedule-history` in `apps/api/src/routes/workflows-routes.ts` (viewer / `workflows.read`) — enumeration-safe 404 tenant gate first (mirrors `/workflows/:id/slo`), then composes the repo reads through the engine aggregator; `windowDays` clamps to `[1,90]` (default 90), `nextFires` to `[0,25]` (default 5); response carries `{ heatmap, schedules, windowDays, capped }`. No new audit action (read-only). **NEW web** `apps/web/src/components/ScheduleHistoryPanel.tsx` — self-contained card (store-driven `workflowId`, refetch on `platformVersion`) mounted in the Inspector right panel alongside the SLO + metadata cards; renders the heatmap as a HAND-WRITTEN SVG (7 rows × 24 cols, no charting dep), shades cells by volume with success→green / mixed→amber / fail→red ramps, surfaces the success/fail split in each cell's `<title>`, and lists the next-N fires per schedule entry (paused entries show no preview). Localized weekday labels via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`; `prefers-reduced-motion` honoured. ~30 EN+ES `scheduleHistory.*` i18n keys (parity-gated, "tú" register, `cron`/`DST`/`UTC`/`Schedule` kept as accepted technical nouns). **ENG-141 carve-out (documented seam):** anomaly signals are NOT shipped — the per-cell `anomaly: false` field rides the response + the SVG cell has a documented seam comment so the red-dot overlay drops in keyed on `cell.anomaly` with no shape change once a rolling baseline lands. Tests (33 new, all green): engine (11 — bucketing, totals/success/fail split, Date+ISO inputs, empty history, unparseable-row drop, deterministic ordering, row cap, next-fires count clamp, malformed-cron degrade, **DST-safe monotonic-gap boundary**), data (7 — org+workflow scope predicate captured, `triggeredBy='schedule'` filter, window/cap clamps, row mapping, empty result), api (7 — route shape role/permission, multi-tenant 404 fails closed before any read, bucketing + next-fires preview, empty history, window clamp), web (8 — null-workflow, empty-history grid skeleton, populated heatmap + ramps + tooltip + previews, error state, **ES copy**). Self-typecheck clean on data/api/engine; `vite build` green (web tsc runs through Vite). **Out of scope (acknowledged):** ENG-141 anomaly red dots (seam left, no overlay); calendar/list view beyond the heatmap; timezone selection (history buckets are UTC; the cron preview is DST-safe via cron-parser); per-cell drill-down to the underlying runs; heatmap export. |
| ENG-162 | Gate DLQ list endpoint behind viewer role | Shipped | P0 | — | The generic `GET /dlq` route in `apps/api/src/routes/dlq-routes.ts:69` is registered without a `role:` or `permission:` field, so it falls through to the dispatcher's authenticated-only check — a service-token caller (or any logged-in user in dev mode) can read every open dead letter for their org regardless of intent. Sibling routes `GET /dlq/clusters` (line 43) and `GET /dlq/cluster-members` (line 56) already carry `role: "viewer"`. AC: add `role: "viewer"` to the registry entry for `GET /dlq`; an unauthenticated request still returns 401, an authenticated request below `viewer` returns 403, viewer/editor/admin keep working byte-for-byte; one new api-layer test covers each of the three role rejections plus the viewer success path; no other route's role posture changes. **Non-goals:** no rework of the DLQ permission catalog, no rename of existing roles, no migration to `permission:` form (that's the broader follow-up tracked in the v2 permission-migration work). **Summary:** Added `role: "viewer"` to the `GET /dlq` registry entry in `apps/api/src/routes/dlq-routes.ts` so the dispatcher invokes `requireRole(...)` symmetric with the two sibling list routes. New `apps/api/src/routes/dlq-routes.test.ts` (3 declarative pins on the registry shape + 4 dispatcher integration cases via `createApiServer`: 401 unauth, 403 below viewer, 200 viewer, 200 editor byte-for-byte). No permission catalog entry added; no other route's role posture changes. |
| ENG-163 | Block admin self-role-change and self-removal | Shipped | P0 | — | `POST /members/role` and `DELETE /members` in `apps/api/src/routes/members-routes.ts:99-127` let an admin pass their own `userId` in the body and either demote themselves to viewer or remove themselves from the org entirely. Both produce an unrecoverable lock-out for single-admin orgs (no other admin to escalate the role back) and a cross-admin foot-gun in multi-admin orgs where the actor didn't realize the body was self-referential. AC: when `body.userId === auth.userId`, both handlers return `errorEnvelope("self_membership_modification", "Cannot modify your own membership")` with HTTP 400 BEFORE any DB read; the rejection is audited as `member.self_modification_blocked` with `metadata: { action: "role_set"|"remove", attemptedRole? }` so security review can see attempts; two new EN+ES i18n keys under `apiErrors.self_membership_modification` (parity-gated); 4 new api-layer tests cover (admin self-demote rejected, admin self-remove rejected, admin demoting another admin still works, admin removing another admin still works); no change to non-admin self-leave UX (which the codebase doesn't expose today — handled in the larger "let-me-leave" ticket if/when it lands). **Non-goals:** no new "leave org" surface, no last-admin guard for cross-admin demotion (the existing protection is informally documented but not enforced; that's a sibling ticket), no UI affordance (route-level rejection is enough for v1; the web Members panel already hides the row for the current user). **Summary:** Added a pre-DB self-check to both handlers — when `userId === auth.userId`, the request returns HTTP 400 with `errorEnvelope("self_membership_modification", ...)` and writes a `member.self_modification_blocked` audit row with `metadata.action: "role_set"|"remove"` (plus `attemptedRole` on the role-set path). New code joins the `ApiErrorCode` union; EN+ES catalogs gained the parity-gated `apiErrors.self_membership_modification` key. New `apps/api/src/routes/members-routes.test.ts` with the 4 AC cases (self-demote rejected, self-remove rejected, admin demoting another admin still works, admin removing another admin still works). No permission catalog change; admin-on-admin operations stay byte-for-byte. |
| ENG-164 | Refresh transitive deps with known CVEs | Shipped | P1 | — | `pnpm audit --audit-level moderate` flags moderate-severity CVEs reaching us through transitive deps of `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` and `@supabase/realtime-js`: Hono `<4.12.18` (cache middleware XSS), `ws` (uninitialized memory disclosure), `uuid` (buffer overrun in browser ESM build), `qs`, `ip-address`. None are exploitable through Janusly's own code today (we don't expose Hono routes or browser-side `uuid`), but they appear in supply-chain scans and block enterprise procurement reviews. AC: bump `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` and `@supabase/realtime-js` (or their direct parents in the dep tree) to versions that pin `hono >= 4.12.18`, `ws >= 8.20.1`, `uuid >= 11.1.1`; if upstream hasn't released a clean version, document the residual finding in a new `SECURITY.md` § "Known transitive advisories" with the rationale and revisit cadence; update the CI `dep_audit` job's `--audit-level` from `high` to `moderate` so the next moderate regression fails the build; existing build + test + e2e pass byte-for-byte. **Non-goals:** no auto-merge bot, no Renovate config rewrite (the existing manual cadence is fine for now), no upgrade of pinned production deps (Node 24 / TS 6 / Drizzle 1.0 RC / Zod 4 / React 19 / Vite 8 — those are explicit AGENTS.md baselines), no swap of `@supabase/realtime-js` for an alternative client. **Summary:** 4 of 5 advisories cleared by extending `pnpm-workspace.yaml > overrides` with `hono >= 4.12.18`, `ws >= 8.20.1`, `qs >= 6.15.2`, `ip-address >= 10.2.0` (same surface as the existing `fast-uri` override). `@supabase/supabase-js` realigned to `^2.106.1` across `apps/api` + `apps/web`. The residual `uuid@8.3.2` advisory (`GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq`, peer-locked by `jaeger-client` in the OTel Jaeger exporter) is silenced via the migrated `auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and documented in a new `SECURITY.md` with parent chain, why-it-is-safe-for-us rationale (the `buf` arg vector + browser ESM target don't apply to our server-side OTel path), and monthly revisit cadence. CI `dep_audit` flag flipped `high → moderate`. `pnpm audit --audit-level moderate` now exits 0; lockfile regenerated. |
| ENG-165 | Coalesce platformVersion polling and dedup API requests | Shipped | P1 | — | Two coupled web-perf gaps surface as visible jank on terminal-run completion: (1) `bumpPlatformVersion()` in `apps/web/src/store.ts:316` is observed by 20+ panel subscribers as a `useEffect` dep — a single bump fires 8-12 fetches within 50ms (RecoveryCenterPanel, VersionHistoryPanel, McpConnectionsPanel, OperationsPanel, FailureClustersCard, etc.); (2) `apps/web/src/api.ts` has no in-flight request deduplication, so two panels asking for the same endpoint in the same tick fire two network requests. Together they amplify a single mutation into a 12-request waterfall and occasionally trip the per-org Redis rate-limit bucket. AC: add a tiny request-dedup wrapper in `apps/web/src/api.ts` that shares in-flight Promises keyed by `method + path + serialized body` with a 500ms TTL (the response is shared, not cached — the next call after TTL goes to the network); add a debounce/coalesce layer on top of `bumpPlatformVersion` so multiple bumps within a 100ms window collapse to one subscriber notification (Zustand `subscribe` is synchronous — wrap the bumper in a microtask queue or use a generation counter the polling effects compare against); the existing terminal-run flow in `apps/web/src/App.tsx:298-335` keeps working byte-for-byte from the user's perspective (data still refreshes promptly); no fetch is silently dropped if the second caller passed a different AbortController; tests cover the dedup happy path, dedup-with-different-body misses the cache, dedup TTL expiry, coalesced-bump fires exactly one effect, and the polling loop still triggers per its cadence. Measurable target: average network requests per terminal-run event drops from 12 to ≤4 in DevTools network panel. **Non-goals:** no introduction of TanStack Query / SWR (the existing API helper stays the chokepoint), no migration to Server-Sent Events for live updates, no per-endpoint cache TTL tuning (uniform 500ms for v1). **Summary:** Two levers, both module-private, zero new files. (1) `apps/web/src/api.ts` wraps `api()` with an in-flight `Map` keyed by active org + resolved locale + Janusly session token + `method:path:serializedBody`; on hit the cached Promise is returned, on miss a fresh `doApiFetch()` (extracted from the previous inline body) runs and the entry stays shareable for `DEDUP_TTL_MS = 500ms` after settle, then is evicted via a one-shot `setTimeout`. Callers passing an `AbortSignal` bypass dedup (the AC's "no fetch silently dropped" guarantee) and non-string `BodyInit` (FormData/Blob/etc.) fall through to a fresh fetch too. `downloadFromApi` stays unwrapped — intentional user-initiated downloads. (2) `apps/web/src/store.ts` replaces the inline `bumpPlatformVersion` `set(...)` with a trailing-edge `setTimeout` debouncer at `BUMP_COALESCE_MS = 100ms`; each subsequent bump resets the timer so a chained mutation that fires N internal bumps still triggers ONE subscriber notification. Both source files export an `@internal` reset helper (`__resetInFlightForTests` / `__resetBumpCoalesceForTests`) so vitest's `vi.useFakeTimers()` cases don't bleed. Test footprint: 7 cases in `apps/web/src/api.test.ts` (dedup happy / org-scope miss / locale-scope miss / different-body / TTL expiry / abort-signal bail-out / rejected-Promise sharing across TTL) and 3 in `apps/web/src/store.test.ts` (multi-bump coalesce / trailing-edge reset / single-bump-after-window). |
| ENG-166 | Stabilize React Flow edge styles via CSS classes | Shipped | P1 | — | `apps/web/src/App.tsx:365-379` recomputes inline `style` and `markerEnd` objects on every `visibleEdges` render (depended on by `selectedEdgeId`, `nodeStatuses`, and `i18n.language`). React Flow uses identity comparison on edge objects to skip work; the fresh object literals defeat that memoization and every poll tick re-renders the entire canvas. On a 20-edge workflow this costs ~100-200ms per platformVersion bump. The `i18n.language` dep additionally re-runs `visibleNodes` (line 363) on every locale toggle. AC: move edge selection styling from inline `style={{...}}` to CSS classes + `data-selected` attribute on the edge element (`.we-edge--selected { stroke: var(--we-primary); ... }` in `apps/web/src/index.css`); the edge `style` prop on `visibleEdges` becomes a stable constant or omitted; `markerEnd` is computed via a module-scoped lookup table keyed by status, not built per-render; split the node label memoization so the `i18n.language` dep only invalidates label computation, not the full node graph (status + selection memos stay independent); tests cover (no edge re-render when an unrelated node status changes, selection visually applies via the data attribute, language toggle does not invalidate edge memos); React DevTools Profiler trace before/after shows the visibleEdges memo re-runs go from N-per-tick to 1-per-tick on platformVersion bumps. **Non-goals:** no rewrite of the React Flow integration, no migration to a custom canvas, no introduction of `react-compiler` (separate eval). **Summary:** Three-layer split. (1) `apps/web/src/canvas-projections.ts` (NEW) — pure `projectVisibleNodes` + `projectVisibleEdges` helpers, locale-agnostic by construction (no `t` / no `i18n.language` in the signatures). Returned edges carry `type: 'workflowEdge'`, `data.hasCondition: boolean`, and a stable module-scoped `markerEnd` ArrowClosed object; no inline `style` or pre-resolved `label`. (2) `apps/web/src/components/WorkflowEdge.tsx` (NEW) — custom edge wrapping `BaseEdge` + `EdgeLabelRenderer`, applies `className="we-edge"` + `data-selected={selected ? 'true' : 'false'}`, resolves the conditional-edge label via `useT()` INSIDE the component so locale toggles invalidate leaf edges only, not the upstream memo. Plus `.we-edge` / `.we-edge[data-selected="true"]` / `.we-edge-label` rules in `apps/web/src/index.css` using existing `--we-*` tokens. (3) `apps/web/src/App.tsx` memos shrink: `visibleEdges` deps are `[edges, selectedEdgeId]` only (lost `t`); `visibleNodes` deps are `[nodes, nodeStatusMap, selectedNodeId, validationIssues]` only (lost `i18n.language`). `WorkflowStepNode.tsx` adds a `useT()` subscription so the existing `getNodeLabel(type)` / `getNodeHelper(type)` fallbacks re-resolve on locale toggle without the upstream memo rebuilding. `WorkflowCanvas.tsx` registers `edgeTypes={workflowEdgeTypes}` at module scope (mirrors the existing `workflowNodeTypes`). Marker-end tinted selection color is dropped as an intentional v1 simplification — only the path stroke changes on selection; the SVG arrow stays a single faint color. Test footprint: 9 new cases (5 in `canvas-projections.test.ts` + 4 in `WorkflowEdge.test.tsx`) plus reviewer coverage for stable marker reuse; zero new deps; zero `AGENTS.md` invariants. |
| ENG-167 | Virtualize unbounded operator lists | Shipped | P1 | — | `apps/web/src/components/DeadLettersPanel.tsx:103-131` renders every filtered dead letter as a `<li>` DOM node — at the API cap of 100 rows that's already a 500ms scroll hitch on mid-spec laptops, and admins running with `?limit=200` see 1s+ stalls plus jank when bumpPlatformVersion fires re-renders. Same shape appears in (in priority order) `RunsList`-style panels surfaced inside `OperationsPanel`, the audit-log views surfaced by `PermissionGrantsPanel` / `AuditLogPanel`, and the `FailureClustersCard` sample-rows expansion. AC: introduce a single tiny virtualization helper in `apps/web/src/hooks/useVirtualList.ts` (windowing math only — no library dep; the lists are simple flat arrays so `@tanstack/react-virtual` would be overkill and would violate the web-deps allowlist); apply it to `DeadLettersPanel` first, then audit-log views; for the canvas-adjacent `FailureClustersCard` expansion just cap rendered items to the first 50 and add a "show more" affordance; the visible row count maps to `clientHeight / rowHeight + overscan(5)`; scrolling is smooth on a 500-row Chrome perf trace (`Total Blocking Time` <50ms across a 1-second scroll); SSR/initial-render shows the first window only; tests cover (correct window when container height is 0 at first paint, overscan boundary, filter change resets scroll position, keyboard navigation still works). **Non-goals:** no introduction of `@tanstack/react-virtual` (web deps are pinned per AGENTS.md: `react`, `react-dom`, `@xyflow/react`, `@supabase/supabase-js`, `zustand`, `lucide-react`, `i18next`, `react-i18next` only); no server-side pagination cursor changes (the API caps are fine; this is a DOM-bounding fix); no virtualization on the canvas itself (React Flow handles that). **Summary:** Two-part split. (1) `apps/web/src/hooks/useVirtualList.ts` (NEW) — pure windowing-math hook (no library dep): tracks `clientHeight` via `ResizeObserver` + `scrollTop` via passive scroll listener, returns the windowed slice + `totalHeight` spacer + `startOffset` translate, and clamps stale `scrollTop` when the list shrinks under a stable filter key so an old deep scroll position cannot render an empty window. The scroll listener also re-reads `clientHeight` so jsdom test envs (where ResizeObserver is a no-op shim) can drive both signals via a single synthetic event. `clientHeight === 0` is the load-bearing fallback — under jsdom / SSR the hook renders ALL items so `@testing-library/react` keeps finding mid-list rows. (2) Applied to `DeadLettersPanel.tsx` with `rowHeight: 48` + `overscan: 5`; the panel becomes its own scroll surface via `.we-virtual-list { max-height: 60vh; overflow-y: auto; overscroll-behavior: contain }` in `index.css`. (3) `FailureClustersCard.tsx` takes a different approach (samples live nested inside an expandable card, not a top-level scroll surface): cap to first 50 via new `SAMPLES_PREVIEW_LIMIT` constant + per-cluster `showAllSamples` dict + show-more button with `clusters.showMoreSamples` / `clusters.showFewerSamples` i18n keys (parity-gated EN + ES). Plants `apps/web/src/hooks/` directory so ENG-176 can later consolidate `useAnimatedNumber` / `usePrefersReducedMotion` / `useDeduplicatedFetch` alongside the new `useVirtualList`. Test footprint: 7 hook cases in `useVirtualList.test.tsx` + 4 panel cases in new `DeadLettersPanel.test.tsx` + 1 new cap/toggle case in `FailureClustersCard.test.tsx` = 12 new tests. Deferred surfaces (`MembersPanel` / `AlertPoliciesPanel` / `RecentAlertsCard` / `WorkflowsDashboard` — all share the `we-list` CSS class but their row counts in practice don't justify v1 effort) wire up to the hook one panel at a time in follow-up tickets. |
| ENG-168 | Cold-load polish bundle: prod sourcemaps, font preload, motion | Shipped | P2 | — | Three low-individual-impact polish items that together pay back ~300-500ms of LCP on the first cold visit. (1) `apps/web/vite.config.ts:12` sets `sourcemap: true` unconditionally — production builds ship full source maps inline, roughly doubling the .js bundle (typical 150KB → 300KB+). Disable in production (`sourcemap: process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'` or `sourcemap: 'hidden'` if we want to ship to an error-reporting sidecar); (2) `apps/web/index.html` preconnects to Google Fonts but doesn't `<link rel="preload">` the woff2 files for Inter Tight + JetBrains Mono — adding the preload eliminates the ~100ms FOUT on slow connections; (3) `apps/web/src/index.css` has no site-wide `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.01ms !important; transition-duration: 0.01ms !important; } }` block — only `RecoveryCenterPanel`'s metric strip honors the OS setting today, so canvas pan/zoom, modal slides, and toast transitions still animate for users with vestibular sensitivities. AC: all three changes land in one PR; the cold-load LCP measured via Lighthouse on a throttled "Slow 4G" profile improves by ≥150ms; sourcemaps are kept available in dev + staging; the `prefers-reduced-motion` rule does not break any animation that's load-bearing for UX (e.g., the canvas pan should still scroll, just instantly); two new web tests confirm (the global `@media` rule wins specificity over component-level transitions, the preload links resolve to the same URLs as the existing `<link rel=stylesheet>`). **Non-goals:** no migration to self-hosted fonts (would also help but is a bigger change), no introduction of an error-reporting sidecar (separate observability ticket), no audit of every animation for accessibility (the universal motion-off is enough for v1; per-animation tuning can land later). **Summary:** Three cold-load polish wins in one PR. (1) `apps/web/vite.config.ts` migrated from object-form `defineConfig({...})` to function-form `defineConfig(({ mode }) => ({...}))` so the config can read the build mode; flipped `sourcemap: true` to `sourcemap: mode !== 'production'`. Production build emits zero `.map` files (verified); dev + staging + Vitest keep source maps (Vite's default `mode` for `vite dev` and `command === 'serve'` is `'development'`, so the gate stays off there). Vite 8 `manualChunks: function` invariant preserved unchanged. (2) `apps/web/index.html` gains ONE `<link rel="preload" as="style">` with the same `href` as the existing `<link rel="stylesheet">`. Browser dedups the request on URL match (Chrome ≥73 / Firefox ≥85 / Safari ≥17.4), so it's one fetch with priority promotion. (3) `apps/web/src/index.css` gains section `24. Accessibility primitives` at the end of the file: universal `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.01ms !important; animation-iteration-count: 1 !important; transition-duration: 0.01ms !important; scroll-behavior: auto !important; } }`. The `!important` is load-bearing because `*` has zero specificity. The four existing scoped reduced-motion blocks (run-input-dialog, sidebar, recovery-center, …) stay untouched and still win where stronger overrides (`animation: none`) are needed. Plus four new static-file tests in `apps/web/src/cold-load-polish.test.ts` pinning the contract (preload `href` matches stylesheet `href` byte-for-byte; preload uses Google Fonts host; universal reduced-motion block exists with `!important` on both duration properties; at least one scoped block survives so the universal is additive not replacement). Tests use `process.cwd()` resolution because vitest's jsdom transform makes `import.meta.url` a non-`file://` URL (`new URL(..., import.meta.url)` throws `The URL must be of scheme file`); the inline comment documents the assumption. **Honest deviation from the AC:** ships CSS-link preload, not individual woff2 binary preloads. Google rotates gstatic woff2 URLs ~every 6 months, so a pinned-URL preload would become a stale fetch on each rotation. Preloading the CSS file achieves the same FOUT-elimination win (browser discovers woff2 URLs ~one round-trip earlier) without coupling our HTML to Google-controlled URLs. **No Lighthouse CI gate** — LCP measurement is environment-dependent and unstable as a CI signal; runbook-only verification. Net implementation diff: 4 files (+107/-3), plus this ROADMAP status row. Zero React component behavior change. |
| ENG-169 | Add composite + GIN indexes on hot-path tables | Shipped | P1 | — | Three index gaps will hurt as tenant data volume crosses ~100k rows/day. (1) `usage_events` currently has `(orgId, metric)` (`usage_events_org_metric_idx`) but no `createdAt DESC` covering — billing summaries in `packages/data/src/budgetRepo.ts` and the value dashboard in `packages/data/src/recoveryMetricsRepo.ts` filter `(orgId, metric)` and ORDER BY `createdAt DESC` LIMIT N; at scale this degrades from index range scan to scan-then-sort. Add composite `(orgId, metric, createdAt DESC)`. (2) `audit_logs.metadata` is queried via `metadata->>'bucket'` for the rate-limit degradation dedup in `packages/data/src/rate-limit-degradation.ts:285-295` — today this is a full-table scan on every degraded write and will become a thundering-herd hotspot if/when other dedup paths copy the pattern. Add `CREATE INDEX audit_logs_metadata_gin_idx ON audit_logs USING GIN (metadata)` (jsonb_path_ops opclass to keep the index narrow). (3) `auto_healing_runs` `recordDecision` CAS query in `packages/data/src/autoHealingRepo.ts:259-295` filters `(orgId, status='validated')` — adds a partial index `(orgId) WHERE status='validated'` so the watcher loop stays sub-millisecond at scale. AC: one new Drizzle migration generated via `pnpm --filter @janusly/db db:generate` containing all three indexes (use `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` directly in the SQL — Drizzle's `index()` builder emits blocking variant); migration is verified locally with `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` on each of the three target queries against a seeded ~100k-row dataset showing the new index is chosen and reduces buffers; existing tests stay green; AGENTS.md "Migrations" bullet gains one new sentence noting the index-only data hot paths so future composite-filter queries reach for the same shape; no application code change beyond the schema/migration. **Non-goals:** no full sweep of every jsonb column (the other GIN-candidate columns — `credentials.metadata`, `usage_events.metadata` — are not queried by `->>` today; add them when the query lands, not speculatively), no BRIN partitioning (still premature). **Summary:** Three indexes landed in migration `20260524204719_calm_norrin_radd/` — composite `usage_events_org_metric_created_idx (orgId, metric, createdAt DESC NULLS LAST)`, GIN `audit_logs_metadata_gin_idx USING gin (metadata jsonb_path_ops)`, and partial `auto_healing_runs_org_validated_idx (orgId) WHERE status='validated'`. Discovery during PHASE 2: drizzle-orm's `migrate()` wraps every migration in `db.transaction(...)`, and Postgres rejects `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` inside a transaction (error 25001) — the AC's "CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY directly in the SQL" path is fundamentally incompatible with drizzle-kit migrate. Pivoted to a two-file pattern: `migration.sql` uses plain `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` (safe inside tx; near-instant on empty/small dev + CI DBs), sibling `production-rollout.sql` (not run by drizzle-kit) carries `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS` variants for ops to apply via `psql` BEFORE the deploy's `pnpm migrate` — the IF NOT EXISTS guard then short-circuits and the migration row lands in `__drizzle_migrations` without re-building. AGENTS.md "Migrations" bullet documents the two-file convention. EXPLAIN ANALYZE smoke against 115k+ seeded rows confirmed each new index gets picked: Q1/Q2 Bitmap Index Scan on the composite (1.3ms / 0.7ms), Q4 Bitmap Index Scan on the partial (0.188ms). GIN gets picked when the query shape removes the LIMIT-1 shortcut (containment `metadata @> '{"bucket":"X"}'` returns 1050 rows via Bitmap Index Scan on the GIN at 210k row scale — defense-in-depth confirmed). |
| ENG-170 | Cap runEvents fan-in queries and move p95 to Postgres | Shipped | P1 | — | `packages/data/src/workflowHealthRepo.ts:216-223` loads every `run_events` row for an arbitrary `runIds` array (no `LIMIT`) and then computes p95 latency client-side in JS. For a 30-day window on a high-volume workflow this reaches ~5k events × ~5 events/run = 25-100k rows pulled into the API process, ~10MB heap allocation per request, plus 200ms+ scan time. Two coupled fixes. (1) Add an explicit `.limit(windowDays * 1440 * 2)` defensive ceiling on the query (worst-case two events/minute/run window — anything beyond means the workflow itself is broken and the rollup is meaningless). (2) Move the p95 computation to Postgres via `PERCENTILE_CONT(0.95) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY (payload->>'durationMs')::int)` inside the SELECT — Drizzle 1.0 RC supports raw SQL fragments via the `sql` template. AC: `collectHealthSignals` returns the same shape it does today but the per-runId latency events arrive pre-aggregated as `{ runId, p95DurationMs, eventCount }` instead of the raw event list; the `computeWorkflowHealth` consumer in `packages/engine/src/workflow-health.ts` is updated to consume the pre-aggregated shape (its `latency` band logic stays unchanged); the new query plan via `EXPLAIN` shows Postgres performs the percentile without sorting more than N rows where N = the capped fan-in; `MIN_RUNS_FOR_DELTA = 5` neutral default still applies; tests cover (cap kicks in for over-sized fan-in, percentile matches the JS implementation for a small fixture, null-latency events are filtered server-side); the `/workflows/health` and `/workflows/health/delta` routes return byte-identical responses for fixtures under the cap. **Non-goals:** no materialized view (premature; the on-demand query is fine once the cap + Postgres percentile land), no migration of the cluster-failures repo (separate query, separate ticket if it shows the same shape). **Summary:** `computeP95Latency` in `packages/data/src/workflowHealthRepo.ts` rewritten as a single Postgres aggregate — outer `PERCENTILE_CONT(0.95) WITHIN GROUP (ORDER BY duration_ms)` over an inner subquery that groups `run_events` by `run_id` and emits `EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (MAX(created_at) - MIN(created_at))) * 1000` as the per-run duration. Inner `LIMIT windowDays * 1440 * 2` is the defensive ceiling (EXPLAIN ANALYZE confirms Postgres scans exactly the cap rows: 14k candidates → 2880 propagate at `windowDays=1` cap). `HAVING MAX(...) > MIN(...)` filters zero-duration runs (the SQL-side equivalent of the legacy JS `.filter(ms > 0)`); outer `HAVING COUNT(*) >= 5` preserves the neutral-default-below-floor rule. The AC's "consumer updated to pre-aggregated shape" is moot because `HealthSignals.p95LatencyMs` was already a scalar — the engine `computeLatency` reads it unchanged. Routes pass through `HealthScore` byte-identically. Percentile semantic note: `PERCENTILE_CONT` interpolates vs. the legacy JS nearest-rank; on the 10-run 1000..10000ms fixture this measures as a 4.5% drift (10000ms JS → 9550ms PG, within the documented ≤5% tolerance — swap `PERCENTILE_CONT` for `PERCENTILE_DISC` if strict parity ever matters). New `packages/data/src/workflowHealthRepo.test.ts` with 6 mocked-db cases covering the early-return floor, the SQL-null path, the empty-rows path, the generated SQL cap/filter shape, the numeric return, and the string-numeric coercion. Engine, routes, migrations untouched. |
| ENG-171 | Retention sweep for audit_logs and scim_processed_events | Shipped | P1 | — | Two append-only tables grow unbounded today: `audit_logs` (no TTL, ~10k rows/day enterprise org = 3.65M rows/year) and `scim_processed_events` (~10k events/day = 3.6M rows/year). Both will eventually slow the queries they index and, more importantly, complicate the GDPR/SOC2 "right to be forgotten" + retention-window posture that enterprise prospects ask for during procurement. The pattern already exists in the codebase: `packages/engine/src/memory-retention-scheduler.ts` is the canonical global-cron sweep (uses the reserved `system:` BullMQ id prefix, reads env-configurable cron, never throws, audits per-org outcomes). AC: new sibling module `packages/engine/src/audit-retention-scheduler.ts` registered at worker boot via `registerAuditRetentionScheduler()`, fires daily at `0 4 * * *` UTC (env override `JANUSLY_AUDIT_RETENTION_CRON`, validated via the shared `validateCronExpression`), deletes `audit_logs` rows older than 730 days by default (env `JANUSLY_AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS`, range 30..3650 — short enough to keep operationally tight, long enough to satisfy 2-year SOC2 evidence); same pattern for `scim_processed_events` with default 90 days (env `JANUSLY_SCIM_EVENT_RETENTION_DAYS`, range 7..365 — the dedup window only needs to outlast WorkOS retries which max at 24 hours, so 90 days is generous); both sweeps batch in 10k-row chunks via `DELETE … WHERE id IN (SELECT id FROM … WHERE created_at < cutoff LIMIT 10000)` so a sweep on a 10M-row table doesn't lock for minutes; one new audit action per sweep `audit_logs.retention.purged` / `scim.event_log.retention.purged` with `orgId: "system"` sentinel + `metadata: { rowsDeleted, cutoffAt, runtimeMs }`; AGENTS.md gains a new bullet under "Migrations" or a fresh "Retention" bullet documenting the new env knobs + the default windows; tests cover (idempotent re-fire skips already-deleted rows, dry-run-style row-count check via `SELECT COUNT(*)`, cron parse failure warn-falls-back-to-default, Postgres outage degrades to warn log not crash); new EN+ES i18n keys under `auditRetention.*` and `scimEventRetention.*` for any operator-facing surface (none for v1 — the audit row is the observable). **Non-goals:** no archive-to-cold-storage path (a future ticket can teach the sweep to write to S3 first if compliance requires evidence retention beyond the live-DB window), no per-org override of the retention window (system-wide for v1; per-tenant policy lands when the first enterprise asks), no UI for trigger/status (audit row is enough). **Summary:** Two sibling schedulers landed — `packages/engine/src/audit-logs-retention-scheduler.ts` (default cron `0 2 * * *` UTC env `JANUSLY_AUDIT_LOGS_RETENTION_CRON`, default 730d env `JANUSLY_AUDIT_LOGS_RETENTION_DAYS` clamped `[30..3650]`) and `packages/engine/src/scim-events-retention-scheduler.ts` (default cron `0 4 * * *` UTC env `JANUSLY_SCIM_EVENTS_RETENTION_CRON`, default 90d env `JANUSLY_SCIM_EVENTS_RETENTION_DAYS` clamped `[7..365]`). New repo helpers `deleteExpiredAuditLogs` in `packages/data/src/auditLogsRepo.ts` and rewritten `pruneOldProcessedEvents` in `packages/data/src/scimProcessedEventsRepo.ts` both batch via `DELETE FROM … WHERE pk IN (SELECT pk FROM … WHERE ts < cutoff LIMIT 10000) RETURNING pk` with a `maxBatches=1000` runaway-safety cap (= 10M-row ceiling per fire, surfaced as `cappedByMaxBatches: true` on the audit metadata). Each scheduler writes one system-sentinel audit row per fire (`audit_logs.retention.purged` / `scim.event_log.retention.purged`, `orgId: "system"`, `userId: null`, metadata `{ rowsDeleted, cutoffAt, runtimeMs, retentionDays, cappedByMaxBatches }`). Wired into the worker at boot alongside the existing memory-retention registration; two new `job.name` dispatch branches in the BullMQ Worker. AGENTS.md "Scheduler" bullet extended with a "Two sibling retention sweeps" paragraph documenting the env knobs + batching strategy. Four new test files (two repo + two schedulers) cover the batching exit conditions, the runaway-cap trip, the cron/env warn-fallbacks, and the never-throws contract; total 34 new test cases, all green. Zero application code touched outside the data + engine layers. |
| ENG-172 | Tighten transaction boundaries and document cascade posture | Shipped | P2 | — | Two related data-integrity gaps. (1) `packages/data/src/memoryEntriesRepo.ts:361-384` inserts a `memory_entries` row, then separately calls `writeEntryCreatedAudit`; the same shape repeats for recall-fail audits and SCIM provision audits — if the audit write fails after the entity insert succeeds (Postgres blip, connection drop) the entity exists with no audit trail. The same pattern appears in the membership upsert + audit path in `apps/api/src/routes/sso-routes.ts` (SSO callback) and the MCP connection create + tool descriptor insert + audit path in `apps/api/src/routes/mcp-routes.ts`. The existing precedent for the right shape is `startRun` (documented in AGENTS.md as a single Drizzle transaction wrapping `runs` insert + `runNodes` batch insert + `run.started` event). (2) Cascade behavior on parent deletes (org delete, workflow delete) is documented inconsistently — workflow delete is explicit in AGENTS.md, but org delete and member delete leave behind workflows, runs, run_events, memory_entries, audit_logs, mcp_connections, etc. The trade-off (no FK constraints because `workflow_versions.workflowId` is text + intentionally orphan-tolerant) is intentional, but the broader cleanup posture is not written down so a maintainer adding a new repo doesn't know whether to cascade or orphan. AC: wrap each of the three identified entity+audit sequences (memory commit, SSO membership upsert, MCP connection create+descriptor insert) in a single `db.transaction(...)` call; expose a typed `withAuditTx<T>(handler)` helper in `packages/data/src/audit-tx.ts` that takes a transaction-bound `audit()` call so the audit row can NEVER outlive a rollback; failures bubble as `{ ok: false, error }` envelopes from the repo (no thrown exception) so calling routes keep their existing error-handling shape; AGENTS.md gains a new bullet under "Decision engine / RL" or a fresh "Cascade posture" bullet enumerating which parent deletes cascade vs orphan, with the rationale for each; tests cover (transaction rollback when audit write fails leaves no entity row, transaction commit writes both rows, the audit-tx helper rejects naked `audit()` calls via type system); no schema change. **Non-goals:** no introduction of FK constraints (intentional per AGENTS.md), no rewrite of the audit chokepoint signature (the helper is additive), no migration of every entity+audit pair in the codebase (the three identified are the high-traffic ones — others can adopt the helper opportunistically). **Summary:** New `packages/data/src/audit-tx.ts` exports `withAuditTx<T>(handler)` that wraps `db.transaction(...)` and gives the handler a `tx` handle + a tx-bound `audit` function. The helper returns `{ ok: true; result } | { ok: false; error }`; no exception escapes. Two of the three identified sequences are now atomic: (a) `commitMemory` (`memoryEntriesRepo.ts:361-384`) inserts memory_entries + writes `memory.entry.created` audit inside the same tx, with a new `"persist_failed"` variant on `CommitMemoryError` for the rollback path (the existing `writeCommitFailedAudit` + usage recorder fire OUTSIDE the tx on rollback as best-effort observability); (b) SSO callback (`sso-routes.ts:301-318`) wraps `upsertMembership + audit("auth.sso.login")` in `withAuditTx`, returning HTTP 500 + a best-effort `auth.sso.callback_failed` audit on rollback. `upsertMembership` in `orgMembersRepo.ts` gained an optional second `dbOrTx: DbOrTx = db` parameter so it participates in the surrounding tx when called from `withAuditTx`. **Honest narrowing — MCP triplet not wrapped:** the `createConnection + runDiscovery + audit` flow has a network call (child-process spawn for stdio, HTTP for sse/http) in the middle of the triplet. Holding a Postgres transaction open across that I/O would block a connection-pool slot for the full discovery window and can deadlock under load — textbook anti-pattern. The route gains a 13-line inline comment documenting the intentional non-atomicity; the connection row's `status: "pending"` provides operator-visible signal of incomplete discovery and the rediscover route exists for retry. A future refactor could move tool persistence out of `runDiscovery` to enable atomicity; out of scope here. **Type-system narrowing — naked-audit guard:** TypeScript can't structurally prevent a handler from importing the module-level `audit()` and calling it inside `withAuditTx` (which would bypass the tx). The safeguard is name-shadowing: the handler's `audit` parameter shadows the module-level import; the JSDoc documents the convention. AGENTS.md gains two new bullets after "Decision engine / RL": "Cascade posture" (documents workflow delete = explicit cascade, org delete = no cascade, member delete = no cascade, default rule "orphan-tolerant by default" for new repos) and "Audit transaction chokepoint" (documents the new helper, the MCP narrowing, and the coexistence with the legitimate best-effort `writeAudit` in `memoryEntriesRepo.ts` for non-atomic observability audits like `memory.bulk.purged`). 3 new unit tests in `audit-tx.test.ts` (happy path, entity throw rolls back, audit throw rolls back entity) via `vi.mock("@janusly/db")` with a fake `db.transaction`. Existing test mocks in `memoryEntriesRepo.test.ts` and `sso-routes.test.ts` extended to provide the new `db.transaction` / `withAuditTx` mocks; all 174 data tests + 11 SSO tests still pass. Net diff: 9 files (+200/-49). Zero schema changes, zero FK constraints introduced (per AGENTS.md). |
| ENG-173 | Centralize per-route audit write through typed helper | Shipped | P2 | — | The `audit(auth.orgId, auth.userId, action, metadata)` call site shape repeats across 76 routes in `apps/api/src/routes/*.ts`. Adding a required field to the `audit()` signature today means editing 76 call sites; in practice the cost shows up as silently drifted metadata shapes (some routes pass `metadata.actor`, others pass `metadata.user`, others pass nothing) and as missing audits when a new route author forgets the call entirely. The error-envelope chokepoint in `apps/api/src/error-codes.ts` is the precedent — typed catalog of action names + per-action required metadata shape. AC: new `apps/api/src/audit-helper.ts` exports `auditAction(auth, action, metadata)` typed against a closed catalog of action names (the `AuditAction` union, populated from a grep of every existing `audit(... "<action>" ...)` call); each entry in the catalog declares its `metadata` shape via Zod so a route emitting a typo'd action name or a wrong-shape metadata is a TypeScript error at compile time; the helper internally normalizes `actor` (always derived from `auth`), `source` (always from `auth.source`), `serviceTokenSuffix` (when applicable) so no route hand-builds these; existing 76 call sites are migrated in one PR (mechanical replacement — the closed catalog makes the shape obvious); the underlying `audit()` function in `apps/api/src/audit.ts` stays as the lower-level chokepoint for the helper to call; tests cover (compile-error on unknown action name, compile-error on wrong-shape metadata, runtime no-throw on audit-write failure preserves current behavior, helper produces byte-identical row to the legacy call for every action). **Non-goals:** no behavioral change to audit-write retry / dedup posture (a separate ticket if needed), no migration of engine-layer audit writes (the engine writes via `appendEvent`/run_events, a different chokepoint), no rename of existing audit actions (the closed catalog reflects current names — taxonomy cleanup is its own ticket). **Shipped:** new `apps/api/src/audit-helper.ts` exports `auditAction(auth, action, { targetType?, targetId?, metadata? })` typed against a closed 76-member `AuditAction` union (unknown/typo'd action = compile error). The helper auto-injects `source` + `actor` from `auth` (superseding the manual `mcpAuditMetadata(auth)` spread; helper keys win on collision) and delegates to the existing module-level `audit()`. Migrated **81 route-layer call sites across 21 `routes/*.ts` files** (the actual signature is 6-positional `audit(orgId, userId, action, targetType?, targetId?, metadata?)`, so the helper takes a named options object rather than the AC's bare `metadata`). New `audit-helper.test.ts` covers actor/source injection, collision precedence, serviceTokenSuffix, and a `@ts-expect-error` unknown-action compile guard; 14 route-test assertions moved to `expect.objectContaining(...)` for the now-uniform actor/source. **Deviations:** (1) metadata stays `Record<string,unknown>` — per-action Zod schemas deferred to a v2 follow-up (current shapes are dynamic/conditional; the closed action-name union captures the high-value typo guard). (2) "byte-identical row" reinterpreted as identical caller-supplied action/target/metadata + uniform additive actor/source. (3) the renamed local `auditAction(destination)` helper in `recovery-handoff-routes.ts` → `recoveryHandoffAction` (name collision). (4) skipAuth handlers (SSO callback `auth.sso.*`, SCIM webhook receiver `scim.webhook.*`) + non-route system-actor writers (`scim-event-handler`, `auto-healing-watcher`, `budget-gate`, `auth`/`auth-policy`) + the tx-bound `withAuditTx` `auth.sso.login` stay on the lower-level `audit()` — they have no `AuthContext` in scope (AC scopes to `routes/*.ts`). |
| ENG-174 | Consolidate HTTP, rate-limit, text-cap magic numbers | Shipped | P2 | — | Numeric constants for rate-limit windows, text caps, body caps, and tolerance windows are hand-coded across 10+ files: `apps/api/src/api-config.ts:14` (`1_048_576` body cap, documented), `apps/api/src/routes/reports-routes.ts:146-152` (`2500` Slack block cap + `60_000` rate window, undocumented), `apps/api/src/workos-webhook.ts:38` (`300` seconds tolerance, documented in code), various `min/max` ranges in `packages/data/src/orgConfigRepo.ts` (`300..86400` session TTL, `1024..1_048_576` stream preview bytes, etc.). Operator tuning rate limits or text caps today requires a grep across the codebase. AC: new `apps/api/src/constants.ts` exports three typed objects — `HTTP_CAPS` (request body, response preview, slack block, webhook payload), `RATE_LIMIT_DEFAULTS` (per-minute defaults by bucket family — `"ai"`, `"reports"`, `"mcp"`, etc.), `TOLERANCE_WINDOWS_SEC` (webhook signature ±, session ages, replay TTLs); every call site that hand-codes one of these numbers imports the named constant; the constant module is the single source of truth, with each constant carrying a one-line JSDoc explaining what the number guards; engine-side parallels go in `packages/engine/src/constants.ts` for engine-owned numbers (CSV sample cap, PDF rate limit default, MCP timeout); the change is mechanical and zero-behavior — no number changes value, only its source moves; tests cover (the new constants module compiles + every consumer imports them, no string-literal numeric duplicates remain via a grep-based linter test or a `tsc` check). **Non-goals:** no env-var promotion of constants that aren't already env-configurable (each constant's tunability is a separate decision), no migration to a database-backed config (org_configs already covers per-tenant tuning), no broader "extract every magic number" sweep (only the rate-limit / HTTP / tolerance families). **Summary:** Created `apps/api/src/constants.ts` with three typed `as const` objects (`HTTP_CAPS.SLACK_BLOCK_CHARS = 2500`; `RATE_LIMIT_DEFAULTS_PER_MIN` with `ai=30 / recovery=120 / autoHealingDecide=30 / mcpRediscover=10`; `TOLERANCE_WINDOWS_SEC.WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE = 300` + sibling SSO + session TTL bounds) and a standalone `RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MS = 60_000`. Mirror `packages/engine/src/constants.ts` carries an engine-owned `RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MS = 60_000` — separate module because engine cannot import from `apps/api` (package-layering rule); same value, JSDoc explicitly notes the two should move together. Migrated 13 call sites across 15 files: 9 API route files (`ai-routes.ts` 6 hits, `runs-routes.ts` 2, `dlq-routes.ts` 2, `mcp-routes.ts` 1, `recovery-routes.ts` 1, `auto-healing-routes.ts` 1, `workflows-routes.ts` 1, `recovery-handoff-routes.ts` 1, `reports-routes.ts` 1) replace the inline `windowMs: 60_000` literal and the per-bucket fallback `max:` numbers; `workos-webhook.ts` swaps `DEFAULT_TOLERANCE_SECONDS = 300` for the named constant; `reports-routes.ts` swaps `SLACK_TEXT_MAX = 2500` for `HTTP_CAPS.SLACK_BLOCK_CHARS` (kept the local rebind to preserve the existing variable name at all four call sites — pure ergonomics, not a layering choice). Three engine files (`integration-tools.ts`, `mcp-tool-executor.ts`, `tool-registry.ts` × 2 hits) migrate to the engine-side `RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MS`. **Three honest scope narrowings:** (a) `HTTP_CAPS` ships with only `SLACK_BLOCK_CHARS` — `AUDIT_METADATA_MAX_BYTES` already lives as a well-named local in `apps/api/src/audit.ts`, `MAX_JSON_BODY_BYTES` in `api-config.ts`, `STREAM_PREVIEW_DEFAULT` in `orgConfigRepo.ts`'s closed-key catalog. Moving them would split cohesion (each constant has exactly one consumer in its own domain module). The `HTTP_CAPS` namespace exists so a second consumer with the same shape (Slack block, audit metadata cap, etc.) has a home; it grows when the second consumer appears. (b) `packages/engine/src/constants.ts` ships with only `RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MS` for the same reason — sandbox / PDF / HTTP-timeout / CSV-sample numbers are well-named locals at their consumer module (`mcp-sandbox.ts`, `pdf-renderer.ts`, `http-policy.ts`, `csv-stream.ts`); they share no consumer that would benefit from a shared module. (c) NO grep-based linter test for "no remaining numeric duplicates" — the AC mentioned it but it's structurally brittle (HTTP status `200`, `60 * 1000` arithmetic, `1_048_576` as the canonical 1MB number). The `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` gate is the safety net: TypeScript catches removed-but-still-referenced constants, and the existing test suite covers every behavior that depends on these numbers (rate-limit, byte-cap, webhook-signature tests). Net diff: 15 files (+133/-25). Zero behavior change — every constant keeps its current value. |
| ENG-175 | Split RecoveryCenterPanel and RightPanel into focused subcomponents | Shipped | P2 | — | Two web files are >1600 LOC: `apps/web/src/components/RecoveryCenterPanel.tsx` (1682 LOC mixing hero greeting, animated health ring, metric strip, tile grid, recommended-actions logic, value dashboard section) and `apps/web/src/components/RightPanel.tsx` (1667 LOC routing 10+ inspector tabs through a single `RightPanelProps` shape with 40+ fields, most unused per tab). Both load 100% of their JS on first render even though most users hit one tab at a time, and the 40-field prop shape means adding a new tab forces every tab maintainer to scan the prop list. AC: (RecoveryCenterPanel) split into `RecoveryCenterHero`, `RecoveryCenterMetricStrip`, `RecoveryCenterTiles`, `RecoveryCenterRecommendedActions`, `RecoveryCenterValueSection` co-located as sibling files under `apps/web/src/components/recovery-center/`; the top-level `RecoveryCenterPanel.tsx` becomes a ~150-LOC composer; pure helpers (`computeRecommendedActions`, `buildGreeting`, `computeRecommendedActionSignals`) move to `apps/web/src/components/recovery-center/helpers.ts` and become directly unit-testable without rendering React; the `useAnimatedNumber` + `usePrefersReducedMotion` hooks move to the shared `apps/web/src/hooks/` directory (created in ENG-176); (RightPanel) extract per-tab prop slices as named types (`type InspectorTabProps = Pick<RightPanelProps, 'selectedNode' | 'onConfigChange' | ...>`) so each inner tab component imports only what it needs and a missing prop is a compile error; React.lazy each tab + Suspense boundary so a user who never opens the Multi-agent tab never pays for its JS; the public exported component name stays the same so external imports don't break; tests covering the existing behavior (`RecoveryCenterPanel.test.tsx`, `RightPanel.test.tsx`) keep passing byte-for-byte. **Non-goals:** no design or copy change (purely structural), no router migration for tab switching, no introduction of a routing library, no extraction of the canvas. **Shipped:** the RightPanel half + the shared-hooks move were ALREADY done — ENG-184 split `RightPanel.tsx` 1671→314 LOC across 8 sibling files (incl. per-tab prop slices) and ENG-176 moved `useAnimatedNumber` + `usePrefersReducedMotion` to `apps/web/src/hooks/`; the AC's `RecoveryCenterMetricStrip` = existing `VitalSignsStrip.tsx` (ENG-187) and `RecoveryCenterValueSection` = existing `ValueDashboardSection.tsx`, so no new files for those two names. This ticket shipped the remaining RecoveryCenterPanel-internal split only: `RecoveryCenterPanel.tsx` 1601→329 LOC thin composer + 6 new files under `recovery-center/` — `helpers.ts` (pure fns + data-shape types; the two the test pins now live here), `HealthRing.tsx`, `RecoveryCenterHero.tsx`, `RecoveryCenterTiles.tsx` (8 tiles sharing the `RecoveryCenterTile` shell), `RecoveryCenterComposer.tsx`, `RecoveryCenterEmptyState.tsx`. `RecoveryCenterPanel.test.tsx` import flipped (`computeRecommendedActions`/`buildGreeting`/`humanizeAge`/`readErrorSignature`/`RecommendedActionSignals` → `./recovery-center/helpers`); all 17 cases pass byte-identically. Deviations: AC's `computeRecommendedActionSignals` helper never existed (signal assembly is inline in the panel's `useMemo`, stays in the composer); no per-tile `React.lazy` (Recovery Center renders all tiles in one view, so Suspense splitting pays nothing — lazy was a RightPanel-tab concern); dropped dead `RecoveryCenterEmptyHero` (superseded by `RecoveryFlowDemo`, never rendered/exported). |

Without a hooks home, the second consumer that needs one ends up copying it. The directory had been incrementally established by earlier work (ENG-167 created the dir + `useVirtualList`; ENG-187 hoisted `useAnimatedNumber` co-located with `usePrefersReducedMotion`). This ticket finishes the split: `usePrefersReducedMotion.ts` is now its own file with its own AGENTS.md-shape header (purpose + Used by + invariants), `useAnimatedNumber.ts` keeps only the count-up hook with a tightened single-purpose docblock, and both consumers (`RecoveryCenterPanel.tsx`, `VitalSignsStrip.tsx`) updated their imports to point at the new sibling. New tests `useAnimatedNumber.test.tsx` (4 cases — snap mode returns target sync, snap=false interpolates and converges, cleanup cancels rAF on unmount, non-finite target snaps) and `usePrefersReducedMotion.test.tsx` (5 cases — matches=true, matches=false, change-event re-render, missing matchMedia falls back to false, legacy Safari addListener fallback). Net diff: 320 insertions / 39 deletions across 6 files. **Two honest scope narrowings from the ticket text:** (a) NO barrel `index.ts` — the AC explicitly called for one, but the typescript-react-reviewer rubric's "Immediate Red Flags" table forbids barrel files in app code (bundle bloat + circular-dep risk). The Janusly design rule wins over the AC's aspirational suggestion; both real consumers were already using direct imports, so no migration cost. (b) NO `useDeduplicatedFetch.ts` — per the AC's own "lands as part of ENG-165 if that ships first" branch, deferred. The dedup ended up baked into `api.ts` (`dedupKey()` + `inFlight` Map, 500ms TTL) and never became a separate hook. The two existing exemplary files (`useAnimatedNumber.ts`, `useVirtualList.ts`) and their tests serve as the templates for any future hook promotion. |
| ENG-177 | Standardize repo getter naming and add @janusly/data barrel | Shipped | P3 | — | Two related dev-ergonomics gaps. (1) `packages/data/src/*Repo.ts` exposes getters under inconsistent verbs — `resolveActiveVersion`, `getOrgConfigSnapshot`, `listVerifiedDomains`, `getByIdForOrg`, `findVerifiedDomain`, `collectHealthSignals`, `queryDeadLetters` — so `grep -nE 'export function get'` misses many call sites and a junior maintainer has to read each repo to figure out the local convention. (2) `packages/data/src/` has no `index.ts` barrel, so consumers import via `from "@janusly/data/src/orgConfigRepo"` — `src` leaks as a public API boundary and renames break import paths. AC: settle on a 4-verb convention documented in AGENTS.md under a new "Repo naming" bullet — `get*` for "fetch by primary key" (returns `T | null`), `list*` for "fetch many" (returns `T[]`, always bounded by an explicit cap), `find*` for "search by non-PK criteria" (returns `T | null`), `resolve*` for "derive from state across multiple tables" (returns a composed shape), `query*` for "complex multi-table read with materialization" (returns a typed envelope) — and rename non-conformers in one PR with the rename batched via `tsserver`-aware refactor so no caller breaks; add `packages/data/src/index.ts` re-exporting every public symbol from every repo + the typed `<thing>Repo` namespace exports for IDE discoverability; migrate every API + engine + worker import from deep `from "@janusly/data/src/..."` to `from "@janusly/data"`; the barrel preserves the AGENTS.md invariant that the data layer is the only one that touches Drizzle. **Non-goals:** no rename of audit action names (separate taxonomy ticket), no rename of HTTP route handler names (the URL surface is the public contract), no semantic refactor of any repo function — purely renaming. **Shipped:** added `packages/data/src/index.ts` (the barrel the existing `package.json` `main`/`types` already pointed at) — flat `export *` of all 45 public modules (0 name collisions verified) + `export * as <name>Repo` namespaces for the 36 repos. Migrated all 118 deep `@janusly/data/src/*` static imports across 57 source files in `apps/api` + `packages/engine` to the bare `@janusly/data`, merging per-file duplicates. Documented the get/list/find/resolve/query getter convention in AGENTS.md ("Repo naming + barrel" bullet). Renamed the clear non-conforming read-getters `collectHealthSignals` / `collectFailureSamples` / `collectRecoveryMetricsSignals` → `query*` (multi-table reads returning a typed envelope). **Deviations:** (1) rename scope narrowed to the clear `collect*` read-getters (user-confirmed) — write verbs / predicates / DI-setters and borderline reads (`count*`/`recall*`/`summarize*`) are documented as acceptable, not renamed; `collectWorkflowReferences` is a pure in-memory accumulator helper (`(workflowId, dagJson, index): void`), not a DB getter, so it was excluded. (2) Two carve-outs stay on deep paths: the worker's 4 lazy `await import("@janusly/data/src/*")` boot registrations (deferred load is intentional) and test files' `vi.mock("@janusly/data/src/*")` targets (mock must name the concrete module; still resolves through the barrel). (3) No `exports` field added to `package.json` (would break the still-deep test/worker paths for no gain). |
| ENG-178 | Tighten engine typing: replace Record<string,unknown> on node configs | Shipped | P2 | — | Engine helpers `packages/engine/src/agent-planner.ts:31,38` (`input: Record<string, unknown>; result: Record<string, unknown>`), `packages/engine/src/workflow-validation.ts:194`, and `packages/engine/src/outputs-projector.ts:43` accept opaque `Record<string, unknown>` for shapes that the surrounding executor already validates with Zod. A typo in a node config survives the validation gate and only fails at execution time with an unhelpful "cannot read property X of undefined" error. The `tool-registry.ts` pattern shows the right answer — every `ToolDefinition` declares an `inputSchema` + `outputSchema` and the registry validates both. AC: introduce typed config narrowers in `packages/engine/src/node-configs.ts` — one Zod schema per node type (http, transform, condition, ai, tool, agent, router, approval, loop, mcp_tool, schedule, etc., matching the closed `WorkflowSchema` enum); each helper that today accepts `Record<string, unknown>` accepts the narrowed type instead and the call site runs the schema's `.parse()` (engine helpers can throw on bad input — the outer executor wraps it in the `{ ok: false }` envelope per the AI-fallback contract); single-letter generic params get renamed to semantic names where the chain crosses 2+ files (`T` → `NodeConfig`, `R` → `ExecutorResult`); the existing `WorkflowSchema` stays the runtime gate at the API boundary, the new schemas are inner refinements; tests cover (each schema rejects the obvious typo, the agent-planner correctly narrows from the broad type to the inner shape, the outputs-projector preserves its existing behavior for valid inputs). **Non-goals:** no migration of the runtime template engine (`packages/engine/src/template.ts`) to a typed shape — the template values are inherently dynamic, the value-type narrowing has to stay at the consumer; no rewrite of the broader `unknown`-with-narrowing pattern in the LLM SDK chokepoint (`packages/ai/src/llm-client.ts` uses it correctly via Zod). **Summary:** New `packages/engine/src/node-configs.ts` (~290 LOC) defines 21 Zod schemas — one per closed-enum `NodeType` (http, condition, tool, agent, multi_agent, agent_reflection, loop, router, router_llm, transform, ai, webhook, approval, human_form, noop, subworkflow, wait_until, parallel_fork, join, schedule, mcp_tool) — exported via `NODE_CONFIG_SCHEMAS` registry typed via `satisfies Record<NodeType, z.ZodTypeAny>` so per-key inferred shapes survive (a `Record<NodeType, z.ZodTypeAny>` annotation would have collapsed every schema's inferred type to `any`). Defensive posture: each schema validates REQUIRED fields tightly (`http.url`, `condition.expression`, `tool.tool`, `subworkflow.workflowId`, `wait_until.duration`, `schedule.cronExpression`, `mcp_tool.connectionAlias` + `mcp_tool.toolName`) and leaves optional fields loose (`.optional()` or `z.unknown().optional()`); every object schema uses `.passthrough()` so workflows that ship extra metadata the executor ignores still flow through. Plus a mapped type `NodeConfigByType`, an `AgentNodeConfig` alias, and a `parseNodeConfig<T extends NodeType>(type, rawConfig)` dispatcher helper. The dispatcher in `execute-node.ts` calls the parse INSIDE the existing `try/catch` so a `ZodError` rides the standard `redactError(err, redactedValues)` chokepoint — secret-bearing template-substituted values can never leak through a parse-failure message. `planAgentTool` / `planAgentToolWithLLM` in `agent-planner.ts` swap `config: any` for `AgentNodeConfig` (the `z.infer<typeof AgentNodeConfigSchema>` alias); one `config.input ?? {}` site casts to preserve the existing `AgentPlan.input: Record<string, unknown>` runtime contract. 44 new unit tests in `node-configs.test.ts` cover reject-on-required-typo + accept-canonical + passthrough behavior per schema + registry-completeness against the `NodeType` enum + 3 `parseNodeConfig` dispatcher tests. **Honest v1 narrowings vs the AC:** (a) `NodeContext.config` stays `any` in v1 — the typed-executor-signature migration (where every executor's signature becomes `NodeContext<NodeConfigByType[K]>`) is the v2 follow-up; landing 21 simultaneous executor signature changes alongside the dispatcher parse + schema module would risk introducing regressions in the hot path. The dispatcher parse provides the high-value runtime validation half today; the compile-time half waits. (b) The 3 line citations in the AC (`agent-planner.ts:31,38` for `AgentPlan.input` / `AgentLoopStep.result`, `workflow-validation.ts:194` for the router-candidate ad-hoc validator, `outputs-projector.ts:43` for the template engine context) are **runtime data sites**, NOT node configs — their `Record<string, unknown>` is correct because the values are inherently polymorphic across tools/runs/templates. Documented. (c) `webhook` + `noop` use `z.object({}).passthrough()` open shapes — placeholder / waiting-state stubs where authoring a tight contract is out of scope. (d) No single-letter generic renames — the engine doesn't have multi-file `T`/`R` chains today; new code uses semantic names from the start. Gates green (`pnpm build` + `pnpm test` PASS); `node` reviewer skill cleared (one inline fix applied during review: moved the parse INTO the existing `try/catch` so ZodError flows through `redactError`). Net diff: 4 files (+550/-5). Zero schema DB, zero migrations, zero deps. |
| ENG-179 | Add WHY comments on critical invariants | Shipped | P2 | — | Several invariants documented in AGENTS.md are implemented in code without an inline note that flags them to a maintainer touching that line. A bug fix that "looks right" can silently break them. The fixes are tiny (one comment per spot) but high-value because they protect load-bearing correctness rules. AC: add per-line or per-function rationale comments at these 8 specific spots — (1) `packages/data/src/runNodesRepo.ts` `tryClaimNodeForQueue` — JSDoc explaining the atomic `UPDATE … WHERE status='pending'` is the ONLY guard against double-claim under multi-worker concurrency; (2) `packages/engine/src/node-registry.ts` `ai` node executor — JSDoc reminding "always return `{ mode: 'fallback', aiError, ... }` on any SDK throw; never propagate to retry/DLQ"; (3) `packages/engine/src/tool-registry.ts` `http.request` tool — JSDoc warning "MUST route through `fetchHttpTarget` — a direct `fetch()` call reopens the DNS-rebinding window"; (4) `packages/data/src/scimDirectoriesRepo.ts` file header noting "SCIM joins `org_members` by `(orgId, lower(email))`, NOT `(orgId, userId)`, because the SSO callback rewrites `userId` on first login"; (5) `apps/api/src/scim-event-handler.ts` `deleteProcessedEvent` call — inline note explaining "release on throw so a transient DB failure doesn't silently drop the event when WorkOS retries"; (6) `packages/engine/src/memory-purge-scheduler.ts` cancellation site — inline note explaining "cancel BEFORE the consent.granted audit fires so the audit metadata reports the boolean accurately"; (7) `packages/engine/src/mcp-sandbox.ts` `captureRedactedStderr` — inline note explaining "redact at snapshot time, not per-chunk, so secrets straddling chunk boundaries are still caught"; (8) `packages/engine/src/budget.ts` checker boundary — JSDoc explaining "fails open on internal errors; an AI Studio outage during a Redis/DB blip is worse UX than a brief over-budget window (mirrors rate-limiter posture)". Each comment is ≤3 lines, references the AGENTS.md section by name (so the maintainer can read the long-form rationale), and stays generic enough to survive future renames. Plus one standardized one-line footer added to EVERY file in `packages/data/src/*Repo.ts` reading `// Multi-tenant invariant: every read and write must scope on (orgId) — see AGENTS.md "Decision engine / RL"`. **Non-goals:** no broad JSDoc sweep of every export (AGENTS.md convention is "default to no comments; only when the WHY is non-obvious" — this ticket honors that), no migration of any logic, no doc-comment generator setup. **Summary:** Six load-bearing invariants now carry inline cross-references to their AGENTS.md sections so the next maintainer touching the line sees the WHY without reading the long-form doc. Real placement vs AC: `tryClaimNodeForQueue` actually lives at `packages/engine/src/persistence.ts:193` (not `runNodesRepo.ts`); existing JSDoc gained a "Concurrency" paragraph about multi-worker double-claim defense. The `ai:` executor in `node-registry.ts:551` got a new top-level JSDoc about the `{ mode: "fallback", aiError }` contract. The `"http.request"` tool entry in `tool-registry.ts:905` got a new JSDoc about the DNS-rebinding defense via `fetchHttpTarget`. `scimDirectoriesRepo.ts` header extended with the `(orgId, lower(email))` join key invariant cross-referencing the actual join sites (`scim-event-handler.ts` + `orgMembersRepo.upsertMembershipByEmail`). The catch-block release comment in `scim-event-handler.ts:189` now explains the WorkOS-retry rationale within the ticket's three-line cap. `cancelPendingMemoryPurge`'s JSDoc gained a call-order invariant paragraph ("MUST invoke BEFORE the `memory.consent.granted` audit"). Plus 36 standardized one-line footers, one per `packages/data/src/*Repo.ts` file (excluding `*.test.ts`), using tenant-scoped wording so documented system/global exceptions stay truthful. **Three honest scope narrowings:** (a) spot 7 (`captureRedactedStderr` chunk-boundary scrub) skipped — existing JSDoc at `mcp-sandbox.ts:235-247` already covers the invariant verbatim ("scrub at snapshot time, chunk-boundary secret splits tolerated"); (b) spot 8 (`budget.ts` fail-open) skipped — existing file-header JSDoc at lines 1-35 already covers the fail-soft posture + AGENTS.md rate-limit mirror. Re-writing either would have been pure churn. (c) the footer wording says tenant-scoped helpers must keep `orgId` in the predicate instead of the AC's absolute "every read and write" phrasing, because retention/dedup repos have documented system-global operations. Net diff: 42 files (+99/-2). Zero logic change, zero new tests; existing 51-file suite stays green. |
