Cuatro sub-secciones cerradas: **Overview** (metric strip + cost breakdown + FailureClustersCard), **Reliability** (AlertPoliciesPanel + RecentAlertsCard + BudgetSettingsPanel + rate-limiter status), **Access** (AuthPolicySettingsPanel + ScimDirectorySettingsPanel + PermissionGrantsPanel), **Integrations** (CredentialHealthCard + McpConnectionsPanel). Closed-enum `OperationsSection = 'overview'|'reliability'|'access'|'integrations'`, persistida en `localStorage` bajo `janusly:operations:section` (espejo del `janusly:sidebar:state`). State local con `useState<OpsSection>` (sin URL query strings — Janusly no las usa hoy; sigue el patrón del `step` enum del `RecoveryDialog`). **Importante:** renderizar SÓLO la sub-tab activa, no all-mounted-with-display-none. Cada card hoy dispara su propio fetch en cada `platformVersion` bump → reduce de 9 fetches paralelos a 2-3 (~70% menos tráfico API por refresh). Dot badge al lado de la sub-sección que tiene estado attention-needing (limiter degraded, credencial expirada, budget cruzado, alert delivery failed): la rail consume `RateLimiterStatusChip`, los lastError de CredentialHealthCard y el budget envelope para derivar el badge. Sub-tab "Everything" opcional al final del rail que renderiza los 9 cards en 2-column masonry para operadores que prefieren escanear todo; default = sub-tabbed. Página completa con header h1 "Operations" + windowDays selector + last-refreshed timestamp + status strip (los 4 metric tiles del metric strip actual promovidos al header). Tests: actualizar `OperationsPanel.test.tsx` (hoy asegura "los 9 cards renderizan apilados") para que (a) la rail renderice las 4 sub-tabs, (b) clicking "Reliability" monte el subset correcto, (c) el badge aparezca cuando hay limiter degraded. ~16 nuevas i18n keys EN+ES bajo `operations.section.*`. **Honest non-goals v1:** no migración a URL query strings (state es local + localStorage); no animación de transición entre sub-tabs (snap directo); no responsive breakpoint para mobile (Janusly no se navega mucho desde mobile hoy); no cambios al contenido individual de los 9 cards (siguen mostrando lo mismo, sólo se reagrupan); no nueva ruta backend; no eliminar el `OperationsPanel.tsx` legacy todavía — el nuevo `OperationsPage.tsx` lo reemplaza en el App.tsx import y el legacy queda para borrar en una limpieza posterior. **Análisis de mercado:** PagerDuty Service Overview / Vercel Project Settings / Linear Settings — todos usan rail vertical + content area, nunca pila vertical para >5 cards. **Summary:** Shipped `apps/web/src/components/OperationsPage.tsx` (the new mount point for the `'operations'` tab in `RightPanel.tsx`) with a 220px sticky vertical rail + active-only content column. Four closed sub-tabs (Overview / Reliability / Access / Integrations) persist to `localStorage["janusly:operations:section"]` with defensive `isSection()` fallback to `overview`. Lazy-mount empirically verified via Playwright: per-sub-tab fetch counts of 0-5 calls (Overview=3 header-level, Reliability=5, Access=4, Integrations=2), down from ~9-11 parallel fetches in the legacy panel — matches the plan's ~70% reduction target. Header reuses the existing `useState<RecoveryMetrics>` + `api('/recovery/metrics')` + `api('/health')` chain hoisted one level up. New CSS block `.we-operations-page / .we-operations-rail / .we-operations-rail__tab / .we-operations-rail__dot` (~110 lines, hand-rolled in the existing `we-*` naming convention, zero new tokens). 10 parity-gated EN+ES keys under `operations.section.*`. 7 new test cases in `OperationsPage.test.tsx` cover default, sub-tab click, persist, hydrate, bogus-fallback, warning-dot, danger-dot. **Three honest scope narrowings from the ticket text:** (a) **dot badges v1 partial** — Overview + Reliability light their dots from the header-level data (`/recovery/metrics`, `/health`, `budgetBlocked` store); Access + Integrations dots would require lifting `/credentials/health` + `/alerts/recent` summary fetches to the rail, which contradicts the lazy-mount goal. Deferred to v2; documented inline in the rail's `dotKind` table. (b) **"Everything" sub-tab not shipped** — reintroducing a 9-card vertical stack contradicts the structural goal. (c) **`RateLimiterStatusChip` left duplicated** between legacy `OperationsPanel.tsx` and the new `OperationsPage.tsx`; the legacy file stays in tree per the explicit non-goal, and the cleanup ticket that deletes it can extract the chip then. Per-ticket-non-goal also: no responsive mobile breakpoint, no URL query strings, no animation. **Deep-link follow-up shipped alongside ENG-168:** new `requestOperationsSection(section: OpsSection)` helper exported from `OperationsPage.tsx` writes the section through `persistSection` (localStorage) AND dispatches a `janusly:operations:section-request` `CustomEvent`. The component installs a `window.addEventListener` for that event (cleanup-bound in `useEffect`) so an already-mounted instance flips the active sub-tab synchronously. Three call sites adopt the helper to deep-link directly to `'reliability'` (where Budget settings live) instead of landing on the default `'overview'`: `BudgetBlockedBanner`'s "Set budget" CTA, `UserMenu`'s AI "Set budget" item, `RecoveryCenterPanel`'s `BudgetTile` "Open settings" button. The unmounted-at-dispatch path is covered by the localStorage write (next mount reads from there); the mounted path by the listener. `e2e/auth-policy.spec.ts` updated to navigate to the `'access'` sub-tab before asserting the Authentication policies panel. One new test case `'honors external section requests while already mounted'` in `OperationsPage.test.tsx` uses React 19's `import { act } from 'react'`. |
| ENG-182 | Add severity stripes + empty-state pattern to design system | Shipped | P1 | 3 | UX/UI Wave U1 #3 — originally drafted as `ENG-164`; renumbered after collision with hardening pass. Cross-cutting visual polish que toca todos los `panel-card` y empty surfaces de la app. Hoy todas las cards tienen el mismo shadow + border genérico (`apps/web/src/index.css` `.panel-card`) y los estados vacíos (RecentAlertsCard sin alertas, FailureClustersCard sin clusters, DLQ vacío, Members con sólo dev-user) renderizan como una franja muerta con una sola línea. Falta el signal de "esto requiere atención AHORA" vs "esto es settings pasivo". AC dividido en dos sub-funcionalidades coherentes: (1) **Severity stripe**: regla CSS nueva que añade un `border-left: 3px solid` keyed por `[data-severity]` en cualquier `.panel-card`. Closed-enum: `red` (var(--we-status-red)) para attention-now (degraded limiter, credencial expirada, alert delivery falló, budget bloqueado, recovery_item p1 abierto), `amber` (var(--we-status-amber)) para review-soon (budget threshold cruzado, SLO at risk, credencial caducando, p2/p3 sin owner), sin atributo = passive/settings. Cada card existente decide su severity dinámicamente (`<section className="panel-card" data-severity={derive(state)}>`). Cero nuevos tokens, cero nuevos componentes — sólo una regla CSS y un atributo en cada card. (2) **EmptyState component** en `apps/web/src/components/EmptyState.tsx`: props `{icon, kicker, body, cta?}`. Render: ícono 32px centrado en círculo muted, kicker en `--we-text-1`, body en `--we-text-2`, CTA opcional como `we-btn--ghost`. Sustituye los empty states hoy lacónicos en RecentAlertsCard ("No alerts"), FailureClustersCard ("No clusters"), DeadLettersPanel ("DLQ empty"), MembersPanel ("Only dev-user"), recovery items list, alert dispatches list — y prepara el slot para los empty states que ENG-181 va a necesitar al renderizar sub-tabs sin contenido. ~10 nuevos i18n keys EN+ES bajo `emptyState.*` parity-gated. Aplicación inicial sobre los siguientes call sites: `RecentAlertsCard.tsx`, `FailureClustersCard.tsx`, `DeadLettersPanel.tsx`, `MembersPanel.tsx`, `BudgetBlockedBanner.tsx` (red stripe), `CredentialHealthCard.tsx`, `RateLimiterStatusChip` (amber stripe cuando degraded). Tests: `EmptyState.test.tsx` (3 cases — con/sin CTA, render shape, i18n wiring). Regression smoke: capturar los 11 tabs antes/después en `.playwright-mcp/eng182/` para confirmar zero behavior change. **Honest non-goals v1:** no aumentar la paleta — usa los `--we-status-red` / `--we-status-amber` que ya existen; no animaciones (la entrada es snap); no severity automática para el sidebar nav items (esa heurística vive en cada card); no replace de los 3 tipos de toast existentes (severity stripes ≠ toasts); no nueva variante "info" o "success" (mantenerse en red/amber/none para no inflar el vocabulario visual). **Justificación:** este es el movimiento de "personalidad operacional" más barato de toda la wave — una sola regla CSS, cero deps nuevas, y el operador inmediatamente escanea la página por color. **Summary:** Shipped the severity stripe (`.panel-card[data-severity]` / `.we-card[data-severity]` keyed to `--we-danger` / `--we-warning`, 3px left border) wired into 4 cards (DLQ amber when any open entry, FailureClusters danger/warning by category band, RecentAlerts warning on `delivery_failed`, CredentialHealth danger on unhealthy + warning on warn). Shipped `<EmptyState icon kicker body cta?>` (`apps/web/src/components/EmptyState.tsx` + 3-case test) replacing inline empty markup in RecentAlertsCard / FailureClustersCard / DeadLettersPanel / MembersPanel / CredentialHealthCard with 10 parity-gated EN+ES keys under `emptyState.*`. Reused existing `--we-danger` / `--we-warning` tokens and `we-btn--ghost` button class — zero new tokens, zero new deps. Two **intentional deviations from the ticket text:** (a) severity values are `danger` / `warning` (matching existing `.we-list-row[data-severity]` / `.we-recovery-center-tile[data-severity]` vocabulary), not the literal `red` / `amber` strings the ticket called out; same colors, consistent attribute values across the codebase. (b) `BudgetBlockedBanner` and `RateLimiterStatusChip` did NOT receive `data-severity` — the banner already ships as a top-of-canvas red alert with `ShieldAlert` icon (the stripe primitive would be redundant), and the chip is a `<span>` with its own healthy/degraded modifier classes (not a card surface). The other 4 cards covered the operator-blocking signal the ticket asked for. |
| ENG-183 | Reorganize sidebar from 4 to 3 nav groups | Shipped | P2 | 3 | UX/UI Wave U1 #4 — originally drafted as `ENG-165`; renumbered after collision with hardening pass. Pequeño cambio de IA / structure-of-information en `apps/web/src/components/BuilderSidebar.tsx:137-173`. Hoy los 4 grupos (`pinned`/`build`/`run`/`workspace`) misfilean conceptos: `Operations` está bajo "Run" pero cubre billing + seguridad + access (concepts que los operadores aprenden separados); `Marketplace` y `Templates` están bajo "Workspace" pero son superficies de descubrimiento (authoring), no admin. El grupo "Workspace" mezcla discovery + administración en un solo bucket. AC: redistribución de los 11 items en 3 grupos mental-model-aligned: **Pinned** (Home), **Build / construir** (AI Studio, Flows, Inspector, Templates, Marketplace) — todo lo que sirve para AUTORIZAR un workflow, **Run / operar** (Runs, MultiAgent, Operations, Credentials, Members) — todo lo que sirve para CORRER y administrar workflows en producción. Eliminar el grupo `workspace` por completo. Edits: única edición a `NAV_GROUPS` constant en `BuilderSidebar.tsx:137-173` — borrar el bloque `workspace` y mover sus 4 items hacia `build` (Templates + Marketplace) y `run` (Credentials + Members). Cero migration en localStorage: `loadStoredState()` en `BuilderSidebar.tsx:191-205` ya intersecta `openGroups` con las keys existentes — un storage con `'workspace'` cae silenciosamente, sin try/catch ni schema bump. El `DEFAULT_OPEN_GROUPS = ['pinned', 'build', 'run']` (línea 87) ya no incluye `'workspace'`, así que default users no ven cambio. Tampoco renombrar `run` → `settings` o similar (eso invalidaría el localStorage state custom; mantener los keys vigentes). i18n: borrar las claves `sidebar.group.workspace` de EN+ES (parity-gated). Tests: `BuilderSidebar.test.tsx` actualiza el assertion sobre el número de grupos (4 → 3) y los items por grupo. Tour de los tabs no cambia funcionalidad — sólo el orden y la categoría. Screenshot smoke en `.playwright-mcp/eng183/`: sidebar expandida antes/después en EN+ES. **Honest non-goals v1:** no añadir un 4° grupo `settings` dedicado (deferred hasta que Janusly tenga 5+ items administrativos); no renombrar keys de grupos (mantiene localStorage compat); no reorganizar el step-palette (sus categorías `ai`/`flow`/`human`/`tools`/`misc` ya están bien para el builder); no cambios al footer del sidebar; no responsive collapse a iconos-only (deferred). **Por qué P2 en vez de P1:** es la mejora de menor impacto visible del Wave U1 — un usuario que ya memorizó la ubicación de Credentials sigue encontrándolo, y los que abren la sidebar por primera vez se benefician marginalmente. Ship cuando ENG-180/181/182 hayan estabilizado el resto del shell. **Summary:** Shipped the sidebar 4→3 reorganization. `NAV_GROUPS` in `apps/web/src/components/BuilderSidebar.tsx` now has three groups: Pinned (Home), Build (AI Studio + Flows + Inspector + Templates + Marketplace — 5 items, authoring + discovery), Run (Runs + MultiAgent + Operations + Credentials + Members — 5 items, operate-the-org). `NavGroup.key` union narrowed from `'pinned'|'build'|'run'|'workspace'` to `'pinned'|'build'|'run'`. Deleted `sidebar.group.workspace` from `en/common.json` + `es/common.json` (parity-gated, 1915 keys per locale). The two e2e specs (`views.spec.ts` + `responsive.spec.ts`) no longer need an explicit Workspace group expansion — `DEFAULT_OPEN_GROUPS = ['pinned', 'build', 'run']` keeps all three default-open on fresh Playwright contexts. **One in-passing improvement** caught during the recon: the ticket text claimed `loadStoredState()` already intersected `openGroups` against valid keys; the recon found it didn't. Added `VALID_GROUP_KEYS` + `VALID_CATEGORY_KEYS` `Set<string>` constants and tightened the filter so a stale `'workspace'` (or any future deprecated key) gets pruned silently on read — same defensive treatment applied to `openCategories` against `NODE_CATEGORIES` keys. Browser smoke 2026-05-26 confirmed: three groups visible in EN + ES, all 4 redistributed items navigate correctly, pre-seeded stale `openGroups: ['pinned','build','run','workspace']` + `openCategories: ['ai','flow','extinct_category']` got filtered to `['build','run','pinned']` + `['ai','flow']` on the next persist-back cycle. **Honest scope narrowings from the ticket text:** (a) screenshots saved to `output/janus-ship/eng-183/` per the AGENTS.md "Verification artifacts" convention, not `.playwright-mcp/eng183/` as the ticket literal said — every recent ticket (ENG-181/182/186/187) used the same `output/` convention. (b) The ticket called for "test updates to BuilderSidebar.test.tsx" but no such test file exists — the only group-count assertions live in the two e2e specs, both updated. Net diff: `+36 / -18` across 5 files. Zero new deps, zero schema, one i18n key removed. |
| ENG-184 | Split RightPanel god-component into per-tab modules | Shipped | P2 | 3 | UX/UI Wave U1 #5 — originally drafted as `ENG-166`; renumbered after collision with hardening pass. Refactor de calidad de código puro, **cero cambio de UX**. `apps/web/src/components/RightPanel.tsx` tiene 1671 líneas y es un dispatcher que mete en una sola entrada componentes que no se relacionan: AI Studio, Flows browser, Inspector + QuickConfigEditor + McpToolConfigField, Runs panel + UsageSummaryCard + 4 dialogs, Templates, Marketplace, Credentials, Reasoning, MultiAgent. `grep RightPanel apps/web/src/**/*.test.*` devuelve cero — no hay coverage que pin las ramas `tab === X`, lo que hace el refactor low-risk pero también low-net (no hay regression net). AC: tres extracciones mecánicas (cada una se puede shipear independiente si se prefiere, pero las uno en un ticket porque comparten el mismo god-component): (1) **`RunsPanel.tsx`** — extraer las líneas ~1292-1602 (≈310 líneas). Owns 4 dialog states (`activeHumanFormNodeId`, `labSourceRun`, `forkTargetNodeId`, `deliveryRun`), embeds `UsageSummaryCard` + `RunExplainChat` + `DeadLettersPanel` + 3 overlay dialogs. Keep el `Pick<RightPanelProps, …>` shape para que el dispatcher line en el nuevo `RightPanel.tsx` siga siendo una sola expresión JSX. (2) **`InspectorPanel.tsx` + `QuickConfigEditor.tsx` + `McpToolConfigField.tsx`** — extraer las líneas ~201-824 (≈625 líneas). `QuickConfigEditor` es un type-switch con 15 ramas (un buen candidato a `QUICK_CONFIG_BY_TYPE: Record<NodeType, FC<Props>>` lookup pero ESO es follow-up — v1 sólo extrae el bloque tal cual). `McpToolConfigField` es un async data fetcher que no pertenece a un router file. (3) **`UsageSummaryCard.tsx`** (+ 6 module-level helpers) — extraer las líneas ~1015-1252 (≈140 líneas + helpers). Ya está marcada `export function`, así que el move es 100% mecánico. `AiUsageFooter.test.tsx` + `UsageSummaryCard.test.tsx` ya pin el contrato. Resultado: `RightPanel.tsx` cae a ~400 líneas conteniendo sólo el dispatcher + `PanelChrome` + `TemplatesPanel` + `ToolsPanel` + `CredentialsPanel` + `ReasoningPanel` + `Metric` + `EmptyView`. Follow-up natural (NO en v1): flatten el dispatcher a un `Record<ActiveTab, FC>` lookup una vez cada tab esté en su propio file. **Honest non-goals v1:** no cambiar el comportamiento de NINGÚN tab (zero behavior change); no introducir un context provider — el prop drilling se mantiene; no refactorizar `QuickConfigEditor`'s type-switch a un lookup (follow-up); no cambiar los testIds existentes; no añadir nuevos tests beyond actualizar imports en los existentes; no tocar el conditional de `App.tsx` (eso es ENG-180, ya shipped). **Por qué P2:** zero user-visible change, es trabajo de mantenibilidad. Ship cuando el equipo tenga capacity para una PR mecánica grande. Acepta el orden de envío al final del Wave U1 — los previos (ENG-180-183) sí cambian el screenshot diff y necesitan revisión visual; este es revisable por diff puro. **Summary:** `apps/web/src/components/RightPanel.tsx` dropped from 1671 LOC to ~340 LOC. Extracted into 8 new sibling files (two more than the original 6-file plan): `InspectorPanel.tsx` (214), `QuickConfigEditor.tsx` (294), `McpToolConfigField.tsx` (144), `AiUsageFooter.tsx` (47), `UsageSummaryCard.tsx` (256 — includes 9 module-level helpers/types/consts), `RunsPanel.tsx` (421 — includes the 4 dialog states + `Metric` helper that only this panel uses + a named `SubmitHumanFormHandler` type), `quick-config-fields.tsx` (101 — shared `TextConfigField` / `NumberConfigField` / `TextareaConfigField` / `JsonConfigField` + `readConfigString` / `readConfigNumber` / `asJsonObject` / `fieldId` primitives that `QuickConfigEditor` and `McpToolConfigField` both depend on; inlining either would have created a circular import via `QuickConfigEditor → McpToolConfigField`), and `panel-primitives.tsx` (~60 — `PanelChrome` + `EmptyView` shared by `RightPanel.tsx` + every extracted sub-panel; the dedicated module breaks what would otherwise be a `RightPanel.tsx ↔ RunsPanel.tsx` cycle). What stays in the now-~340-LOC `RightPanel.tsx`: the file-header docblock, the dispatcher, the small self-contained panels (`TemplatesPanel`, `ToolsPanel`, `CredentialsPanel`, `ReasoningPanel`, `LoadOlderEventsButton`), and the canonical `RightPanelProps` type (exported so sub-panels declare their slice via `Pick<RightPanelProps, ...>` — prop drilling preserved exactly; no context provider introduced per the explicit non-goal). Plus 2 one-line test import flips (`UsageSummaryCard.test.tsx` and `AiUsageFooter.test.tsx`) — both tests pinned the contract independently pre-extraction, so the move just retargets the import path. **Three honest scope additions vs the original 6-file AC:** (a) `AiUsageFooter.tsx` is its own 47-line file because `AiUsageFooter.test.tsx` already targets it as an independent unit — cleaner than exporting from `InspectorPanel.tsx`; (b) `quick-config-fields.tsx` breaks the inner cycle between `QuickConfigEditor.tsx` and `McpToolConfigField.tsx`; (c) `panel-primitives.tsx` breaks the outer cycle that would otherwise emerge between `RightPanel.tsx` and `RunsPanel.tsx` (RunsPanel needs `EmptyView` + `PanelChrome`; RightPanel imports `RunsPanel`). Gates green (`pnpm build` + `pnpm test` PASS); reviewer skill cleared with zero critical/high. Closes UX Wave U1 (ENG-180..187, all shipped). Net diff: 11 files changed (~1700 LOC moved, no logic change). |
| ENG-185 | Adopt compact density tokens across operational surfaces | Shipped | P2 | 3 | UX/UI Wave U1 follow-up — surgió del mismo análisis UX que produjo ENG-180..184 pero quedó fuera del scope de ENG-182 (que se limitó a severity stripes + EmptyState). Hoy Janusly mezcla densidades visuales — algunos rows son 36-40 px, otros 28 px, body text varía entre 12-14 px sin un sistema claro; los cards de Operations cargan una descripción inline ("Operator-side only", "Audit-trail of recent dispatches", etc.) que el operador aprende en la semana 1 y después es ruido. Adoptar la densidad de Linear como referencia: row height 32 px, body 13 px, metadata muted 12 px, single density token familiy `--we-density-*`. AC: añadir tokens `--we-density-row: 32px` + `--we-density-body: 13px` + `--we-density-meta: 12px` en `apps/web/src/index.css :root` (cerca de los `--we-content-max-width` y `--we-text-1/2` existentes). Refactorizar los row layouts en `.we-list-row`, `.we-recovery-item-badge`, `.panel-card h3` (font-size + line-height), `.we-recovery-center-metric`, `.we-ops-metric-card`, `.we-cluster-row` para consumir los tokens en vez de px hardcoded. Quitar la descripción inline de cada card de Operations (`OperationsPanel.tsx` / sub-panels) — sale del row visible y entra a un `<info icon>` con tooltip nativo `title=""` o un `aria-describedby` invisible. El cambio afecta sólo la apariencia (alturas + font sizes); nunca el spacing entre cards ni el padding ni el comportamiento de ningún botón. Screenshot smoke en `.playwright-mcp/eng185/` antes/después: home, operations, runs, members, credentials, templates. **Honest non-goals v1:** no rediseño de iconos (siguen siendo lucide-react @ 13-14 px); no nueva paleta de color (los `--we-status-*` siguen siendo los existentes); no cambios a forms (inputs siguen siendo 36 px porque los browsers tienen mínimos de touch-target en mobile); no responsive density (todos los breakpoints usan el mismo token); no migración de la sidebar (su densidad ya es razonable y tocarla rompería el step palette pinned area); no eliminar tooltips ARIA — los tooltips visuales (`title`) son aditivos a la a11y existente. **Por qué P2:** es polish puro, no desbloquea ningún otro ticket. Ship cuando el equipo tenga ganas de "una pasada de pulido visual de medio día". **Summary:** Three density tokens (`--we-density-row: 32px`, `--we-density-body: 13px`, `--we-density-meta: 12px`) landed in `apps/web/src/index.css :root` alongside `--we-content-max-width` with a comment documenting why h3 stays at current size and inputs stay at 36px. Tokens wired at 7 row-shaped selectors: `.we-list-row` + `.we-cluster-row__summary` got `min-height: var(--we-density-row)`; `.we-list-row__sig` font-size 10px→`--we-density-meta` (small legibility bump); `.we-recovery-item-badge` min-height 30px→token; `.we-recovery-item-badge__owner` / `__sla` font-size 11px→token; `.helper-text` font-size 12px→token (identical value, grep-verified zero specificity regression). Padding + gap untouched. Two Operations cards (`BudgetSettingsPanel`, `CredentialHealthCard`) lose their inline `<p className="helper-text">{t("…intro")}</p>` and gain a `<div className="we-heading-with-info">` wrapping the heading + a non-interactive `<span className="we-info-icon" role="img" aria-label="About this section: …" title="…"><Info size={14} aria-hidden="true" /></span>` — the description now lives in the native browser tooltip and gets announced to screen readers through the span label. Two new generic CSS rules support the pattern: `.we-heading-with-info` (baseline-aligned inline-flex wrapper) and `.we-info-icon` (lucide @ 14px + `cursor: help` + hover tint). One new parity-gated i18n key `common.aboutSection` (EN: "About this section" / ES "tú"-register: "Acerca de esta sección"). **Three honest scope narrowings vs the AC:** (a) `.we-recovery-center-metric` not migrated — selector no longer exists (ENG-187 replaced it with `.we-ops-metric-card`); the metric strip already uses the unified card class so the AC reference was stale; (b) only 2 cards had inline descriptions to swap; the other 7 Operations sub-panels already conform; (c) `.panel-card h3` font-size NOT migrated to `--we-density-body` — 13px h3 would break the visual hierarchy; headings stay at 16px. Browser smoke deferred to a 5-minute manual runbook (`output/janus-ship/eng-185/runbook.md`) instead of a full Compose-up + Playwright capture — the diff is bounded enough that static CSS inspection + gates green + reviewer skill cleared cover the contract. Net implementation diff: 5 files (+73/-10), plus this ROADMAP row. Zero workflow behavior change; existing suite stays green. |
| ENG-186 | Lift ReactFlowProvider for canvas viewport persistence | Shipped | P2 | 3 | UX/UI Wave U1 follow-up — cierra la "acknowledged limitation" documentada en el bullet "Layout & canvas visibility" de AGENTS.md y en ENG-180's risks. Hoy el canvas React Flow re-monta cada vez que el usuario navega de un canvas tab (`copilot` / `inspector`) a un non-canvas tab y de vuelta — esto resetea el zoom / pan / posición de scroll del lienzo. Para workflows largos (>20 nodos) es fricción operacional real: el editor pierde su contexto visual cada vez que va a verificar Operations o Members y vuelve. Causa raíz: no hay `<ReactFlowProvider>` envolviendo el árbol de `<App>` — el `WorkflowCanvas` instance se destruye en cada unmount porque su context vive INSIDE del componente y muere con él. AC: envolver `<App>` en `<ReactFlowProvider>` en `apps/web/src/main.tsx` (alrededor del `<App />` en el `createRoot.render(...)`). Cambiar la dispatcher logic en `App.tsx:770-810` para que el canvas SIEMPRE se monte como un sibling del slot main y se oculte con `display: none` (vía un data-attribute en su wrapper, ej. `<div data-canvas-visible={isCanvasTab(activeTab) ? "true" : "false"}>`). El `ContextualMain` toma su lugar visualmente cuando el canvas está oculto SIN destruirlo. El viewport state sobrevive porque ReactFlow lo persiste en su context provider. Test: nuevo case en `apps/web/src/types.test.ts` o `App.canvas-visibility.test.tsx` que assert (a) canvas DOM presente en TODOS los 12 tabs (verifica via `querySelector('.react-flow')` returning truthy), (b) `data-canvas-visible="false"` aplicado en non-canvas tabs. Manual smoke obligatorio: navegar `inspector` → editar workflow grande (pan + zoom) → `operations` → `inspector` → verificar que zoom y pan se preservan byte-for-byte. AGENTS.md doc-sync: ELIMINAR la "Acknowledged limitation" del bullet "Layout & canvas visibility" porque deja de ser limitación. **Honest non-goals v1:** no upgrade de versión de React Flow (queda en la versión actual); no cambios a shortcuts del canvas (delete, copy, paste siguen iguales); no añadir `useReactFlow()` hooks fuera del canvas mismo; no persistir viewport entre page reloads (el provider lo persiste sólo en memoria — un F5 sigue reseteando, eso es OK); no cambios al `RecoveryCenterPanel` (home sigue siendo su rama dedicada sin canvas). **Riesgo:** mounting el canvas en TODOS los tabs (aunque hidden) carga React Flow + sus subscribers desde el primer render, no lazy. Para un usuario que nunca abre AI Studio / Inspector eso es ~50 KB extra de JS en el initial bundle (aunque React Flow ya carga vía vendor chunk, no es bundle creep nuevo). Aceptable porque el caso "usuario nunca abre el editor" es raro en Janusly. **Por qué P2:** mejora la experiencia de un sub-grupo de operadores (los que editan workflows largos); no es un fix de seguridad ni de fiabilidad. Ship cuando aparezca un reporte real de fricción. **Summary:** Shipped the root-level `<ReactFlowProvider>` lift. `apps/web/src/main.tsx` now wraps `<App />` in `<ReactFlowProvider>` inside `<React.StrictMode>`. The `App.tsx` dispatcher consults a new pure helper `getCanvasVisibility(activeTab)` (in `apps/web/src/types.ts` alongside `CANVAS_TABS` + `isCanvasTab`) that returns a three-boolean struct `{ mounted, visible, contextualSlot }`. The dispatcher renders the canvas wrapper as a sibling of the contextual main slot on every non-`home` tab; `home` keeps its dedicated `<RecoveryCenterPanel>` branch with NO canvas in the DOM, so home-only users pay zero React Flow runtime cost. CSS `.workspace-canvas-wrapper[data-canvas-visible="false"] { display: none }` hides the wrapper without unmounting it on non-canvas non-home tabs. 4 new test cases under `describe('getCanvasVisibility …')` in `apps/web/src/types.test.ts` (home absence, canvas tabs visible, 9 non-canvas non-home hidden, exhaustive coverage with `visible ⇒ mounted` invariant). Browser smoke 2026-05-26 confirmed: zoom level + pan offset survive `AI Studio → Operations → AI Studio` AND `Inspector → Operations → Inspector` byte-for-byte. AGENTS.md doc-sync: the "Acknowledged limitation" paragraph in the `Layout & canvas visibility` bullet is removed and replaced with the positive description of the new three-state posture (root-level provider, `getCanvasVisibility` helper, hidden-but-mounted wrapper, F5-still-resets-viewport documented as out-of-scope). **Honest scope narrowings from the ticket text:** (a) test placement — the ticket suggested a new `App.canvas-visibility.test.tsx` mounting `<App />`, but the visibility logic was extracted to a pure function and unit-tested in `types.test.ts` instead (cheaper, better signal — full-App mount would require mocking 30+ child components without adding test coverage). (b) `data-testid="workspace-canvas-wrapper"` was added on the wrapper as a future-test affordance; no test currently uses it but it's a 1-attribute cost worth keeping for debugging. Net diff: `+179 / -33` across 6 files (main.tsx + App.tsx + types.ts + types.test.ts + index.css + AGENTS.md). Zero new deps, zero new tokens, zero new i18n keys. |
| ENG-187 | Cross-surface vital-signs status strip | Shipped | P2 | 3 | UX/UI Wave U1 follow-up — cierra la "Datadog pattern" mencionada en el análisis de mercado pero que no entró en ENG-181/182. Hoy `OperationsPanel` tiene 4 metric tiles arriba (success rate, MTTR, p95 latency, replay success) que comunican el estado operacional del workspace en un vistazo. El `RecoveryCenterPanel` (home) tiene un strip similar pero distinto (greeting + Health ring + 4 tiles propias). Inconsistencia: el operador aprende dos vocabularios visuales para "vital signs" según en qué surface esté. Datadog usa el mismo strip persistente en TODA superficie operacional — el operador aprende una sola fila y sabe la salud del producto sin importar dónde está. AC: extraer el metric tile pattern (4-card row con icon + value + label + sparkline opcional + severity tint) a un componente reutilizable `apps/web/src/components/VitalSignsStrip.tsx`. Render via `<VitalSignsStrip windowDays={N} surface={"operations"|"home"} />` con props que permiten variantes contextuales en tile order pero la APARIENCIA es idéntica. Mount points: (1) sticky header de la nueva `OperationsPage.tsx` (que ENG-181 introduce promoting los metric tiles al header de Operations — este ticket extrae el componente reutilizable); (2) NUEVO mount sticky en el top de `RecoveryCenterPanel.tsx` debajo del greeting hero pero arriba de "Recommended next actions". Ambos consumen el mismo `GET /recovery/metrics` endpoint con el mismo cache key → cero duplicación de fetches. ~6 i18n keys EN+ES bajo `vitalSigns.*` (no hay strings nuevos, sólo extraer las existentes). Tests: `VitalSignsStrip.test.tsx` (3 cases — render con 4 metrics + severity tint + empty state cuando no hay data). **Honest non-goals v1:** no cambios al endpoint backend `/recovery/metrics` (sigue devolviendo el mismo shape); no añadir métricas nuevas (mantener las 4 actuales); no animación de transición entre snapshots (snap directo); no mount en builder/inspector (sólo operational surfaces — home + operations); no sparkline (los tiles son numeric-only por ahora, sparkline es follow-up); no responsive collapse para mobile (los 4 tiles stackean naturalmente <760 px). **Depende de:** ENG-181 debe shippearse PRIMERO porque promueve los metric tiles al header de Operations (donde el componente extraído se monta); después este ticket extrae el strip y lo re-mounta en home. Si ENG-181 está en flight, este ticket puede arrancar en paralelo pero el merge se hace después. **Por qué P2:** mejora la consistencia visual cross-surface; no desbloquea funcionalidad. Ship cuando ENG-181 esté merged y haya capacity para un pulido cross-surface. **Summary:** Shipped `<VitalSignsStrip />` (`apps/web/src/components/VitalSignsStrip.tsx` + 3-case test) as the single cross-surface metric-tile primitive. Wired into three surfaces via the new shared `buildOperationsTiles` helper in `operations-tiles.tsx`: `OperationsPage.tsx` (6 tiles, static), `OperationsPanel.tsx` legacy (6 tiles — closes the `MetricCard` duplication ENG-181 punted), and `RecoveryCenterPanel.tsx` home (4 tiles with `onClick` for tab navigation). The home tiles preserved all 4 pre-migration `data-testid` values + the rich aria-label composition + the count-up animation. Hoisted `useAnimatedNumber` + `usePrefersReducedMotion` from `RecoveryCenterPanel.tsx` to `apps/web/src/hooks/useAnimatedNumber.ts` as shared module — `HealthRing` and the new strip both consume them. CSS: added one rule block for the button variant (`button.we-ops-metric-card { background: transparent; color: inherit; font: inherit; … }`) and deleted ~100 lines of orphan `.we-recovery-center-strip` + `.we-recovery-center-metric*` rules including the responsive + reduced-motion blocks. **Two honest scope narrowings from the ticket text:** (a) NO new `vitalSigns.*` i18n namespace — the ticket mentioned ~6 new keys but the existing `operations.metric.*` + `recoveryCenter.metric.*` namespaces already own every string the strip needs, so the keys flow through as props with zero renaming. (b) Home page visual treatment changed — the legacy `RecoveryCenterMetric` hero-button (`::before` severity pseudo, 32px value, button hover with `translateY`) is gone; tiles now use the Operations card style (22px value, severity-tinted left border, lighter hover). The `HealthRing` continues to anchor the home as the primary hero. Net diff: `+566 / -407` across 8 files (1 new component + 1 new helper module + 1 new hook + 3 migrated components + 1 CSS sweep + 1 new test). Browser smoke 2026-05-26 confirmed: all 4 home testids preserved, click navigation works, `api()` dedup keeps `/recovery/metrics` to 2 fetches per Home + Operations cycle. |
| ENG-188 | Free-JSON workflow generation (camino A) | Shipped | P1 | — | **Problem:** `/ai/generate-workflow` used the provider's constrained structured-output (`llm.generateObject` over an 11-branch `discriminatedUnion`). An A/B eval this session (10 complex prompts × 3 samples, blind Sonnet judge, harness `scripts/model-eval-compare.ts`) showed constrained decoding guarantees JSON *shape* but not graph validity — ~30% of constrained outputs failed `validateWorkflow` (dangling router refs, empty human_form schema, cycles). Free-JSON (model emits the workflow as JSON text, parsed server-side) beat it on Haiku across ALL three axes — ai-mode 93% vs 70%, judge 66.5 vs 56.3, cost $0.0063 vs $0.0072/run — and also beat Sonnet-constrained (57% / 60.3 / $0.0206). **Shipped:** new safe `org_configs.ai.generationMode` key (`free_json` default / `constrained`; env `JANUSLY_AI_GENERATION_MODE`); `apps/api/src/ai-generate-freejson.ts` (`extractJsonObject` + `parseGeneratedWorkflow` via `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema.safeParse` + `generateWorkflowFreeJson` with retry-on-parse-fail `FREE_JSON_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 2`); the route branches on the mode then converges into the unchanged `promoteNoopPlaceholders` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow` tail; audit metadata gains `generationMode` + `generationAttempts`; `aiStatus` / `GET /ai/health` surfaces `generationMode`. Pure mechanism swap — same allowed node shapes, same system prompt, fallback contract intact. **Honest non-goals (→ follow-ups):** no system-prompt change to emit the 9 advanced types directly (ENG-195); error-feedback self-repair is ENG-190 (camino A retry is blind re-sample + a JSON-only nudge); best-of-N (ENG-191); OpenAI enablement (ENG-194). **Summary:** Camino A shipped behind a reversible toggle defaulting ON; 12 new helper tests + build + full api/data/ai suites green (787 / 284 / 134). Pre-commit reviewed via janus-review (gates PASS, node skill clean); _commit hash to be appended here on commit_. |
| ENG-189 | `human_form` empty-schema → noop demotion | Shipped | P1 | — | **Problem:** the top model-INDEPENDENT generation failure — the LLM emits a `human_form` node with no `config.schema.properties`, which `validateWorkflow` rejects (`Human form node requires at least one field`). In the camino-A eval this killed 6/6 constrained runs on `ai-support-draft` and most of `lead-enrichment` across BOTH Haiku and Sonnet. **AC:** extend `sanitizeAiWorkflow` (`apps/api/src/ai-runtime.ts`) to demote a `human_form` node with empty/absent `schema.properties` to a `noop` placeholder — mirror the existing empty-`transform.mapping` demotion directly above it — preserving the node id for Inspector completion; add a test case. **Non-goals:** no schema inference (operator fills it in the Inspector). **Shipped:** new `human_form` branch in `sanitizeAiWorkflow` (`apps/api/src/ai-runtime.ts`) placed directly after the empty-`transform.mapping` demotion, mirroring its shape (preserves node id; `sanitizeEdges` preserves edges; demoted node becomes `{ type: "noop", config: {} }`). Rather than a naive "no properties" check it reuses `WorkflowInputSchema.safeParse` to mirror the engine validator's EXACT predicate (`workflow-validation.ts`: `human_form_invalid_schema` when the schema is absent/malformed, `human_form_empty_schema` only when it's an `object` schema with zero properties) — so a still-valid non-object form (e.g. `{ type: "string" }`) is left untouched, no false demotion. 3 new tests in `ai-generation-prompt.test.ts` (empty `config`, empty object-schema `properties`, and a filled form kept; the empty-`config` case also pins id + edge survival); api suite 18 → 21 green. No schema inference (operator completes the field schema in the Inspector). Pure api-side sanitizer change — no engine/schema/route/UI/migration touched. |
| ENG-190 | Self-repair loop on `validateWorkflow` errors | Shipped | P1 | — | **Problem:** post-camino-A the failure surface is graph validity, not JSON syntax; today a sanitize/validate failure drops straight to fallback. **AC:** when `sanitizeAiWorkflow` throws, feed the specific `validateWorkflow` issue `code`s + messages back to the model for ≤2 targeted repair attempts before degrading to fallback (supersedes camino A's blind re-sample in `generateWorkflowFreeJson`); track repair attempts in audit metadata. The biggest reliability lever after camino A. **Non-goals:** no per-node repair (treat the workflow whole); hard retry cap to bound cost. **Shipped:** new `apps/api/src/ai-repair-workflow.ts` (`MAX_REPAIR_ATTEMPTS = 2`, pure `composeRepairPrompt`, `repairGeneratedWorkflow`). The `/ai/generate-workflow` route now wraps the post-promotion `sanitizeAiWorkflow` call in a try/catch: on throw, the helper RE-RUNS `validateWorkflow(candidate, { strictToolInputs: false })` (the sanitizer's throw carries only a joined message, not the structured codes), frames the issues as a `- <code>: <message>` DATA list + the broken JSON, and asks the model (provider-agnostic `generateText`, `responseFormat:"json"`) for a corrected graph — up to 2 directed attempts, each re-parsed via `parseGeneratedWorkflow` + re-sanitized; a parse-fail or still-invalid reply counts as a spent attempt. On exhaustion it rethrows the last sanitize error so the existing outer catch degrades to `{ mode:"fallback", aiError }` — AI-fallback contract preserved. `repairAttempts` is threaded into BOTH the success and fallback `ai.workflow.generated` audit metadata (0 = first draft validated clean; on the fallback path it's pessimistically set to `MAX_REPAIR_ATTEMPTS` before the helper runs). Applies to both `free_json` and `constrained` modes (sanitize runs after both). Repair does NOT re-run Pass-2 noop promotion (promotion already ran; repair targets validity) and is whole-workflow, not per-node (AC non-goals). Cost: ≤2 extra `generateText` calls only when a draft fails graph validation, same already-gated `"ai"` bucket as Pass-2; each call records a `usage_events` row. 6 new unit tests in `ai-repair-workflow.test.ts` (stub llm, REAL validate/sanitize/parse, $0: prompt composition, 1-attempt repair of an edge-to-missing-node graph, parse-fail-then-succeed, exhaustion-throws, all-unparseable-throws) + a `repairAttempts: 0` assertion on the route's free_json success test. No engine/schema/web/migration touched. |
| ENG-191 | Best-of-N generation + selection | Shipped | P2 | — | **Problem:** per-prompt variance is high (the same prompt scored 54 and 74 across samples in the eval). **AC:** generate N candidates (config-bounded 1–5), validate each via `parseGeneratedWorkflow` + `sanitizeAiWorkflow`, select the best by validation + a cheap score/judge, return the winner with `metadata.candidateCount`. Free-JSON is cheap enough that N=3 costs less than one Sonnet call. **Non-goals:** no candidate streaming; no UI picker. **Shipped:** new `apps/api/src/ai-generate-bestofn.ts` — `generateWorkflowCandidates` fires N (config-bounded 1–5) independent `generateText` samples in parallel (`Promise.allSettled`; unparseable replies dropped) and `selectBestCandidate` keeps the one with the fewest `checkWorkflowReadiness` issues (a `fail` weighs 10× a `warn`) — a deterministic $0 score, NOT an LLM judge; when none pass the strict `sanitizeAiWorkflow` gate it returns the first parsed draft best-effort so the route's repair pass still runs. The `/ai/generate-workflow` free_json branch samples N only when `org_configs.ai.generationCandidates > 1` (new number key — default 1, range 1–5, env `JANUSLY_AI_GENERATION_CANDIDATES`); the winner flows through the UNCHANGED Pass-2 promote → sanitize → repair → fallback tail, so the AI-fallback contract holds with no duplication. Only the zero-candidates-parsed path spends the single-shot retry budget. `candidateCount` rides the response + the `ai.workflow.generated` audit (success AND fallback); `validCandidates` rides the audit. **Default 1 = byte-for-byte today's behavior (opt-in, no silent cost increase); N=3 is the recommended quality setting.** constrained mode stays single-shot. Tests: `ai-generate-bestofn.test.ts` (stub-llm + REAL parse/sanitize/readiness, $0) + a Best-of-N case in the route test. No engine/web/schema/migration; one `ai.*` config key added. |
| ENG-192 | Eval-suite expansion + CI regression gate | Shipped | P2 | — | **Problem:** `evals/generate-workflow.jsonl` is 21 cases and not CI-gated; the camino-A harness (`scripts/model-eval-compare.ts`) ran ad-hoc. **AC:** grow the eval set into a failure-taxonomy suite (human_form, router-integrity, recovery-safe, schedule/wait, multi-agent) and wire a CI job that runs it against `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and FAILS if ai-mode rate or judge avg regresses past a baseline. Makes "don't degrade" structural. **Non-goals:** no golden-output diffing (shape + judge only). **Partial — shipped:** the headless one-command entrypoint ships as `pnpm evals:local` (`scripts/run-evals-local.mjs`) — boots Compose + the API, runs the golden runs headless against `/ai/generate-workflow`, exits non-zero on regression, tears the stack down; mirrors `run-e2e.mjs`'s Compose lifecycle but boots the API only (the suite hits a synchronous route, so no worker). The generation dataset is realigned to the generator's emittable shapes (operator-only types like `webhook`/`schedule` surface as `noop`, so they're no longer asserted as required), every prompt preserved verbatim, the affected `fallbackTemplate`-locked cases converted to `requiresMode:"ai"`, and the set grew to **25 cases** with a failure-taxonomy slice (router-integrity / schedule-wait / human-review / recovery-safe). The realignment removed ~9 impossible-by-construction failures: a live run went from ~12/25 to **22/25**. **It is not deterministically green, by nature:** the residual 2–3 reds are genuine free-JSON generation variance (`mode:"fallback"` with `aiError` — the model fails to produce a valid workflow in 2 attempts on the hardest prompts), exactly the reliability gap ENG-190 (self-repair) + ENG-191 (best-of-N) exist to close — not shape bugs, so no `expect` edit fixes them. The `requiresMode:"ai"` cases therefore flag real model variance, which is the honest signal the gate should produce. **Cost note (important):** there is NO cost-free run on a dev box that has a real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `.env` — `packages/db/src/env.ts` loads `.env` with dotenv `override: true`, so an empty `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=""` in the shell is clobbered and the API always runs live. A truly $0 run requires an env with NO Anthropic key reachable (then the AI cases skip and only the 3 deterministic-fallback cases — http-summary / transform-tool / approval-gate — assert). Named `evals:local` (not `evals:ci`) on purpose — **CI wiring is intentionally deferred (cost decision):** the AI golden runs spend provider credits per invocation, so the gate stays developer-invoked. **Was remaining (now resolved — see Shipped below):** deterministic green awaits ENG-190/ENG-191; the 25 cases are all `/ai/generate-workflow` (multi-surface coverage — review / patch / suggest-improvement — and the AC's ai-mode-rate / judge-avg baseline tracking are not yet built); plus the optional CI/scheduled wiring. **Shipped (regression gate):** `scripts/run-evals.mjs` now gates on a committed baseline (`evals/baseline.json`) instead of red-on-any-flake. The pure gate logic lives in `scripts/evals-baseline.mjs` (`summarizeAi` + `compareToBaseline` + `gateRun`), unit-tested via `node:test` in `scripts/evals-baseline.test.mjs` (`pnpm test:evals`, $0, no API). Each `requiresMode:"ai"` case feeds two suite-level rates — ai-mode rate (did it return `mode:"ai"`) and a deterministic shape-pass rate (did the workflow meet its node-shape expectations, the $0 stand-in for an LLM judge) — and the run reds only when a rate drops past the baseline's tolerance band. Deterministic fallback-keyed cases + transport/HTTP/JSON errors stay hard per-case fails; a no-key run excludes every AI case (`aiCaseCount=0`) and stays green + $0, exactly as before. `pnpm evals:baseline` (`run-evals.mjs --update-baseline`) snapshots the current rates as the new floors (preserving `tolerance`/`note`); `evals:local` forwards the flag. The committed baseline is a conservative documented seed (aiModeRate 0.8 / shapePassRate 0.7 / tolerance 0.1) — the maintainer refreshes it on a keyed box. **Won't-do (cost decision, per operator):** CI wiring (the golden runs spend provider credits, so the gate stays developer-invoked — `evals:local`, never `.github/workflows/ci.yml`) and an LLM `judge avg` metric (a per-case provider call on every run); the deterministic shape-pass rate is the substitute, and the ad-hoc LLM judge stays in `scripts/model-eval-compare.ts` for model A/B. Multi-surface eval cases (review/patch/suggest-improvement) stay out for the same per-run-cost reason. |
| ENG-193 | Few-shot / retrieval exemplars for generation | Shipped | P2 | — | **Problem:** generated workflows vary in style/coverage and the model botches specific patterns. **AC:** retrieve 3–5 similar prior workflows (reuse the existing pgvector memory substrate / BGE-m3) and append them to the generation system prompt as labeled DATA exemplars (same data-framing + suspicion escape clause the MCP-exposure composer uses); log exemplar ids in audit. **Non-goals:** no embedding fine-tuning; no user-facing exemplar picker. **Shipped:** few-shot exemplars from the memory substrate. New `apps/api/src/ai-generation-memory.ts` (mirror of `ai-recovery-memory.ts`): on a successful generation, `recordGenerationExemplar` fire-and-forgets a `prompt → workflow-shape` `generated_workflow` memory entry (`content = prompt` for embedding match; `summarizeWorkflowShape` stores node-types + edge-count + output-keys in metadata — NO config values); per request, `composeGenerationExemplars` recalls the top 3 similar prior exemplars (pgvector cosine over self-hosted BGE-m3) and renders a DATA-framed block (header + `- Request: … → Shape: …` + suspicion escape clause, re-scrubbed via `sanitizeMcpToolDescription`) that `composeGenerationSystemPrompt` appends to the system prompt — steering free_json, constrained, AND every Best-of-N candidate. `exemplarCount` + `exemplarIds` ride the `ai.workflow.generated` audit (success AND fallback). New memory kind `generated_workflow` (365-day retention; no schema migration — `kind` is text). **Gated by the existing two-flag memory consent + `generated_workflow` ∈ `allowedKinds`** — memory-off orgs get byte-for-byte today's behavior (no write, no read, no embedding, no audit delta). **Zero Anthropic cost** (embeddings self-hosted; LLM call count unchanged). Commit-on-save (higher-quality pool) is a documented future refinement. Tests: `ai-generation-memory.test.ts` (mocked memory layer, $0) + a few-shot case in the route test. |
| ENG-194 | Multi-provider on generation — verify + enable OpenAI | Shipped | P2 | — | **Problem:** `free_json` workflow generation removes the `oneOf` blocker — generation is mechanically provider-agnostic (no schema sent to the provider). The verified posture stays Anthropic-only until other providers are proven. **AC:** run OpenAI through `scripts/model-eval-compare.ts`; if the ai-mode rate + quality hold, extend `ai.provider` `allowedValues` + enable per-tenant and update the AGENTS.md Anthropic-only posture. **Non-goals:** no provider change for the still-constrained surfaces (patch/review). **Shipped (verification ran; the conditional enable was NOT taken):** harness made provider-aware — `Config.provider` field, two OpenAI free-JSON configs (`gpt4o-mini-free` / `gpt41-mini-free`) routed through the PRODUCTION `LlmClient.generateText({ responseFormat: "json" })` path, set-scoped `OPENAI_API_KEY` guard, GPT pricing, and a drift fix (free-JSON configs now validate `AiGenerationWorkflowSchemaFreeJson`, the 13-shape production gate, instead of the 11-branch constrained schema). Collateral production bug found + fixed: the OpenAI `jsonModeOptions` wiring in `packages/ai/src/llm-client.ts` was a silent no-op (`@ai-sdk/openai` v3 routes via the Responses API, which ignores `providerOptions.openai.responseFormat`) — replaced with `applyJsonMode` wrapping the model via `wrapLanguageModel` + `defaultSettingsMiddleware({ settings: { responseFormat: { type: "json" } } })` (spec-level, the SDK's public idiom; deliberately NOT `Output.json()`, whose eager parse would throw on malformed JSON and change the chokepoint's error semantics); post-fix probe 3/3 pure-JSON. Same-sweep verification 2026-06-10 (10 prompts × 3 samples × 3 configs, blind Sonnet judge): haiku-free 87% ai-mode / judge 73.65 / $0.0070-run; gpt-4o-mini 93% / 63.75 / $0.0006; gpt-4.1-mini 73% / 68.95 / $0.0017. Pre-declared bar (ai-mode ≥ 80% AND within 10 pts of same-sweep haiku-free AND judge within ~10%): gpt-4o-mini EXCEEDS the baseline on reliability at 1/12 the cost but loses 13.4% relative judge quality (−17..−20 pts on the complex business prompts: customer-escalation, lead-enrichment, multi-agent-decision); gpt-4.1-mini fails reliability outright. **Posture unchanged — Anthropic-only stands.** `ai.provider` `allowedValues` already contained `openai` (no code gate existed; nothing to extend). A same-day round-2 sweep over the upper tiers (Sonnet 4.6 free-JSON / gpt-4o / gpt-4.1, 30 runs each, same blind judge) reinforced the verdict: gpt-4o is dominated by gpt-4o-mini (97% ai-mode but judge 60.14 — lowest of all six free-JSON models — at 19× the mini's cost); gpt-4.1 grazes the bar on cross-sweep numbers (80% ai-mode at the exact floor, judge −5.6%) but is dominated by Haiku on all three axes (reliability 80<87, judge 69.50<73.65, cost $0.0093>$0.0070) with per-prompt fragility (0/3 on webhook-then-followup) — no product case. NEW in-posture finding: **Sonnet 4.6 free-JSON beats Haiku on reliability AND quality** (93% ai-mode, judge 76.93 vs 73.65) at 3.4× the cost ($0.0236/run, ~11.5s latency) — a quality tier reachable today per-tenant via `ai.surfaceModels` (`generate-workflow: claude-sonnet-4-6`; model change, not provider switch). Sonnet CONSTRAINED had scored 57%/60.29 in the original A/B — free-JSON rescues Sonnet entirely. A round-3 sweep over the GPT-5.4 reasoning family (mini / nano, 30 runs each) produced the strongest OpenAI candidate yet: **gpt-5.4-mini = 90% ai-mode (above Haiku's 87%), judge 66.07, $0.0036/run (half of Haiku), 2.4s latency (fastest of all eight models tested)** — its judge lands 0.2 pts below the exact −10% cutoff (66.29), statistically indistinguishable from the bar at N=27, with the same weakness profile (multi-agent / recovery prompts); gpt-5.4-nano fails outright (70%, judge 57.67). A round-4 CONFIRMATION sweep (same-sweep head-to-head Haiku vs gpt-5.4-mini, 16 prompts — the base 10 plus 6 NEW production-shaped complex cases now permanent in the harness via `PROMPT_SET=base|complex|all` + `PROMPT_ONLY` resume filter — × 5 samples = 160 cells, N=80/model) flipped the round-3 borderline: **gpt-5.4-mini PASSES the pre-declared bar** — ai-mode 86.2% (vs Haiku 92.5%, −6.3 pts, within the 10-pt band), judge 66.26 vs 69.28 (−4.4%, well inside ~10%; only −2.6% on the complex set). The round-3 −10.3% judge gap was largely cross-sweep sampling noise. Counter-signal: Haiku went 30/30 on the complex cases while gpt-5.4-mini dropped to 80% there (pass-1 schema failures the production retry+repair pass would partially absorb); gpt-5.4-mini keeps 2.4s latency (vs 5.9s) and ½ cost. Enabling OpenAI became a LIVE product decision with the bar met. **Operator decision (2026-06-10): stay on Haiku / Anthropic-only for now** — the 80% complex-case consistency (vs Haiku's 100%) is the deciding signal; all four rounds of analysis stay preserved here + `docs/PLAN.md` §4.0 for a future re-open (the flip is a docs-level change whenever a tenant demands the speed/cost tier). The model-independent weak spot the sweeps exposed (`failed-workflow-recovery` prompt class) became **ENG-212 (P1)**. |
| ENG-195 | Widen direct emission + generalize noop promotion (Tier-2) | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** free-JSON has no grammar cap, but the prompt still tells the model to emit `noop` placeholders for the 9 advanced types, leaving ~1 noop/workflow. **AC:** teach the generation prompt to emit structural advanced types directly (`parallel_fork` / `join`) and generalize `promoteNoopPlaceholders` (`PROMOTE_FAMILIES`) for the fiddly-config types (`schedule` / `wait_until`); operator-gated types (`multi_agent` / `subworkflow` / `mcp_tool`) stay noop by design (need operator input). **Non-goals:** no change to operator-gated promotion UX. **Shipped:** free-JSON generation now emits the two structural fan-out/fan-in types — `parallel_fork` + `join` — DIRECTLY (no more `noop` + manual Inspector promotion for parallelism). Because the 11-branch cap is Anthropic's compiled-grammar limit that only binds `constrained` (`generateObject` sends the schema to the provider), this is a SPLIT: `apps/api/src/ai-schemas.ts` keeps `AiGenerationWorkflowSchema` (11) for constrained and adds `AiGenerationWorkflowSchemaFreeJson` (13 = 11 + `AiParallelForkNode` + `AiJoinNode`); `parseGeneratedWorkflow` (free-JSON, the default) validates against the wider union, so repair + Best-of-N inherit it. The AI fork/join config schemas mirror the engine resolvers (`branches` = 2..10 `{label,description?}`; `sources` = 2..10 `{label→predecessorNodeId}`), so a parse-valid pair is also `validateWorkflow`-valid (no repair churn); malformed pairs are caught at parse/sanitize → existing retry/repair/fallback. The system prompt teaches the pair (FORK/JOIN RULE + a labeled fork→branches→join example). **Scope decision (operator chose):** `schedule`/`wait_until` stay `noop` + Pass-2 promotion (proven, $0-verifiable; the model botches cron/duration inline), and `multi_agent`/`subworkflow`/`mcp_tool`/triggers stay `noop` because they need org-specific ids/aliases the model can't know — so the noop pattern is NOT fully removable. `constrained` mode is unchanged (its grammar simply won't emit fork/join). Tests: new `ai-schemas.test.ts` (free-JSON accepts fork/join, rejects <2 branches/sources; constrained rejects both) + updated prompt/freejson tests. $0 — no live generation; no engine/schema-migration/web change. |
| ENG-196 | Per-surface model routing + prompt caching | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** all six AI surfaces shared one model choice, and the static `/ai/generate-workflow` system prompt (~1.5k tokens) was re-sent uncached on every generation call. **AC:** per-surface model override (gated by the harness) + Anthropic prompt caching on the generation system prompt. **Non-goals:** no per-node override; no live Sonnet A/B run (the mechanism ships, the operator runs the comparison). **Shipped — two independent levers, both $0 to implement (no live AI run).** (1) **Per-surface model.** New safe JSON-map config key `ai.surfaceModels` (`packages/data/src/orgConfigRepo.ts`, mirrors `memory.retentionDaysByKind`): keys validated against the closed `AI_SURFACES` enum (the six route slugs), values are free model ids. `OrgConfigSnapshot.ai.surfaceModels` is parsed inline (defensive `parseSurfaceModels` → `{}` on drift). The pure `resolveSurfaceModel(map, surface, requestOverride)` helper (`apps/api/src/ai-runtime.ts`) gives precedence `body.model` > per-surface default > global default, and all six surfaces (`generate-workflow` / `explain-workflow` / `review-workflow` / `patch-workflow` / `suggest-improvement` / `explain-run`) thread the result as `modelHint`/`model` (patch + explain-run now read `body.model` too, for parity). The value is handed verbatim to the SAME chokepoint that routes `body.model` — a bare id uses the org provider, `"<provider>/<model>"` overrides it; a mismatch degrades through the AI-fallback contract. Stays Anthropic-only (model change, not provider switch — not enforced in code). (2) **Prompt caching.** New opt-in `cacheSystemPrompt` flag on `LlmGenerateTextInput`/`LlmGenerateObjectInput` (`packages/ai/src/llm-client.ts`): when set AND the EFFECTIVE provider (post-`parseModelSpec`) is Anthropic AND a system prompt is present, the `system` field becomes a `SystemModelMessage` carrying `providerOptions.anthropic.cacheControl: { type: "ephemeral" }` (the AI SDK's documented caching idiom — cleaner than a `messages` array, leaves `prompt` untouched). Non-Anthropic / flag-off is byte-for-byte unchanged, so the breakpoint never reaches OpenAI. Threaded through the four generation paths (`generateWorkflowFreeJson` / `generateWorkflowCandidates` / `repairGeneratedWorkflow` / constrained `generateObject`); the concrete win is repeated generation traffic against the same static system prompt: Best-of-N and cross-request calls both get provider-side cache opportunities, but parallel candidates are not ordered cache reads. The cache savings are provider-side and NOT $0-verifiable — the unit test pins the wire shape; the operator observes the cost/latency win live. **Both halves default OFF/empty → zero behavior change for non-opt-in orgs.** Tests ($0, mocked SDK + pure validators): `llm-client.test.ts` (cache wire-shape: system-as-object on anthropic+flag; plain string otherwise; non-anthropic override skips), `orgConfigRepo.test.ts` (`ai.surfaceModels` validator), `ai-runtime.test.ts` (`resolveSurfaceModel` precedence), `ai-generate-freejson.test.ts` (flag threading), `ai-generate-workflow-route.test.ts` (cache flag + surface-model + override-wins). Doc-sync: AGENTS.md AI-integration bullet, `docs/configuration.md` row. No DB migration, engine, web, or schema change. |
| ENG-197 | Extend Anthropic prompt caching to review / patch / suggest | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** ENG-196 wired the `cacheSystemPrompt` flag only into `/ai/generate-workflow`, but three other AI surfaces send a STATIC system prompt (the dynamic context rides the user prompt), so their reusable prefix was re-sent uncached on every request. **AC:** extend the existing Anthropic ephemeral-caching mechanism to `/ai/review-workflow`, `/ai/patch-workflow`, and `/ai/suggest-improvement`. **Non-goals:** no `LlmClient` change (the flag shipped in ENG-196); no new config knob; `/ai/explain-run` + `/ai/explain-workflow` excluded (no static system prompt). **Shipped — pure mechanism reuse, $0 to implement (no live AI run).** Added `cacheSystemPrompt?: boolean` to `SuggestWorkflowPatchInput` (`packages/ai/src/patch-workflow.ts`) + `SuggestWorkflowImprovementInput` (`packages/ai/src/suggest-improvement.ts`), threaded into each helper's `generateObject` call (default off). The three routes (`apps/api/src/routes/ai-routes.ts`) set `cacheSystemPrompt: true` — always-on for Anthropic, mirroring generation (caching is free upside; on non-Anthropic providers `buildSystemPrompt` returns the bare string → unchanged). For patch the static prefix is `(systemPromptOverride ?? SYSTEM_PROMPT) + localeInstructionForLlm(locale)` and the DLQ/run/memory context rides `promptBody` (the "RECALLED CONTEXT" paragraph is a static instruction, not the snippets); for review/suggest it's `<SYSTEM_PROMPT> + (es-suffix)`. Unlike generation's parallel Best-of-N cache opportunity, the win here is CROSS-REQUEST reuse within the 5-minute cache window — valuable on the recovery loop (patch fires repeatedly during incident triage) and authoring. NOT $0-verifiable (provider-side savings); the helper tests pin the wire shape. **Excluded:** `/ai/explain-run` (`explainRun` → `generateText` with no `system` field) + `/ai/explain-workflow` (inline `prompt`, no `system`) — caching either needs a prompt-structure refactor + eval re-run; deferred as a separate future follow-up. Tests ($0, mocked `generateObject`): `patch-workflow.test.ts` + `suggest-improvement.test.ts` assert the flag threads when set + defaults off. Doc-sync: AGENTS.md `cacheSystemPrompt` sentence (now generation + review + patch + suggest), `docs/PLAN.md` generation-refinements bullet. No DB/engine/web/config/schema change. |
| ENG-198 | Python SDK async client (`JanuslyAsyncClient`) | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the Python SDK (`packages/sdk-python`, PyPI `janusly`) shipped sync-only; code already inside an event loop (FastAPI handlers, async jobs) had to wrap `JanuslyClient` in `asyncio.to_thread`. **AC:** ship the documented `JanuslyAsyncClient` sibling. **Non-goals:** no shared `AsyncClient` connection-pool / `aclose()` lifecycle (the sync transport is stateless per-call and the async mirror matches it); no new SDK surface area; no change to the stdlib-only `janusly.webhooks` import path. **Shipped — mechanical mirror, $0, zero AI-behavior risk.** New `packages/sdk-python/src/janusly/async_client.py` (`JanuslyAsyncClient` + `AsyncRunsResource` / `AsyncReportsResource` / `AsyncRecoveryResource`) — every resource method is the sync one as `async def` + `await`; `runs.list` / `runs.stream_events` are async generators; `poll_until_terminal` uses `asyncio.sleep`. The new code is ONLY the transport: `_http.py` gains `async_request` (over `httpx.AsyncClient`) + a factored `_raise_for_status` shared by BOTH `request` and `async_request` (byte-identical error mapping). Everything else is reused verbatim — `make_config` / `build_headers` (incl. the reserved-header injection guard), the pure response helpers (`_extract_*`, `_filename_from_content_disposition`, `TERMINAL_STATUSES`), and `WebhooksResource` (sync HMAC, no async variant) — so the async client CANNOT drift from the sync one's endpoints / headers / error mapping. `__init__.py` re-exports the new symbols; `client.py`'s docstring updated. `pyproject.toml` adds `pytest-asyncio` (dev-only) + `asyncio_mode = "auto"`; no new RUNTIME dep (httpx already shipped). Tests: new `tests/test_async_client.py` (13 cases) mirrors the load-bearing sync tests with `await` + the same `pytest-httpx` `httpx_mock` (which intercepts `httpx.AsyncClient` too) — start two-step flow, 422 missing-DAG, get, list cap + cursor, poll terminal + timeout, stream dedup + terminal stop, resume, export filename parse, get_metrics, 429 → `JanuslyRateLimitError` on the async path, and the header-injection guard on `async_request`. Full Python suite green (60 passed). README async section updated (sync + async). No server/web/engine/DB change. |
| ENG-199 | Python SDK opt-in retry/backoff (sync + async) | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the Python SDK shipped with no auto-retry — a transient `429` / `5xx` / network blip failed the call outright, while the TypeScript SDK (`packages/sdk-node`) already had an opt-in exponential-backoff layer (`docs/sdk-python.md`'s non-goals flagged the gap, and `errors.py` already parsed `Retry-After` "so callers wiring an opt-in retry layer get it respected"). **AC:** port the TS retry layer to BOTH Python clients. **Non-goals:** no `AbortSignal`-style cancellation (async cancellation propagates via `asyncio.CancelledError`); no retry on arbitrary exceptions (only `httpx.HTTPError` + retryable `JanuslyApiError`); no jitter; no per-call override (constructor-level only — all matching the TS posture). **Shipped — semantics-mirror of the TS `withRetries`, $0, zero AI risk, default-off.** New frozen `RetryConfig` dataclass in `_http.py` (`max_attempts=0` / `backoff_ms=200` / `retry_on=(429,502,503,504)`, mirroring `JanuslyRetryConfig`); `ClientConfig` + `make_config` carry it; both `JanuslyClient` + `JanuslyAsyncClient` constructors gain a `retry=` param; `__init__.py` lazy-exports `RetryConfig` (stdlib-only webhook path preserved). The transport restructure builds headers ONCE up front (the deterministic injection-guard `ValueError` is never retried — cleaner than the TS structure) then runs the per-attempt network thunk through `_with_retries` (sync, `time.sleep`) / `_with_retries_async` (async, `asyncio.sleep`): a retryable `JanuslyApiError` (status ∈ `retry_on`) or an `httpx.HTTPError` (network/timeout) sleeps (Retry-After on a 429 that carries it, else `backoff_ms * 2**attempt`) and re-runs up to `max_attempts` extra times; a 4xx validation error re-raises immediately. **`retry=None` / `max_attempts=0` runs the thunk exactly once → byte-for-byte the pre-retry behavior** (the existing 63 tests are unaffected). No new RUNTIME dep. Tests: new `tests/test_retry.py` (12 sync + async cases) — default no-retry, 503-to-max-then-raise (+ exponential delays), retry-then-succeed, no-retry-on-422, Retry-After-honored-on-429 (monkeypatched `time.sleep` asserts the delay), network-error retried, and negative backoff clamps to retry-now instead of replacing the original error. Full Python suite green (75 passed). Doc-sync: README "Retries" section + `docs/sdk-python.md` non-goal flipped + AGENTS.md SDK sentence. No server/web/engine/DB change. |
| ENG-200 | Make `mypy --strict` pass + CI-gate the Python SDK | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the Python SDK README ADVERTISED `mypy --strict src/janusly` as a supported check, but it did NOT pass (9 `no-any-return` errors) and `mypy` wasn't even a dev dep — an advertised-but-unenforced check that rotted. **AC:** make it pass + enforce it. **Non-goals:** no strict-checking of the test suite (target `src/janusly` only, matching the advertised command); no runtime behavior change. **Shipped — $0, zero AI risk, runtime-identical.** Fixed all 9 errors with honest type assertions: the 8 `return response.json()` sites (`client.py` + `async_client.py` `start`/`get`/`resume_node`/`get_metrics`) become `return cast("dict[str, Any]", response.json())` (`httpx.Response.json()` is typed `Any`; the methods' documented `-> dict[str, Any]` contract is correct, the cast just asserts it), and `_http.py:_retry_delay_seconds` wraps the backoff branch in `float(...)` (the `int ** int` overload typed `Any` under a `-> float` signature). Every fix is a no-op at runtime (`cast` is erased; `float()` is value-identical) — the 75 pytest tests stay green. `pyproject.toml` adds `mypy>=1.8` to the `[dev]` extra + `[tool.mypy]` (`strict = true`, `python_version = "3.10"` = the min supported runtime); the README's "Optional type-check" line now reflects it's a dev dep + strict-configured + CI-gated. `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `test_sdk_python` lane gains a `mypy src/janusly` step after `pytest -q`, so the check is ENFORCED on every change and can't silently rot again. `mypy src/janusly` → `Success: no issues found in 6 source files`. No server/web/engine/DB change. |
| ENG-201 | SCIM v2 — per-group role mapping | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** SCIM provisioning assigned EVERY user the directory's flat `defaultRole`; enterprises want IdP-group-driven roles ("Okta `Admins` group → Janusly admin, `Engineers` → editor"). The data was half-there — `scim_group_state` mirrored group existence but there was no group→role mapping and no user↔group membership tracking (the `dsync.group.user_added/user_removed` events were explicit no-ops). **AC:** derive a member's role from their group memberships (highest-rank mapped role, else `defaultRole`); implement the two membership events; admin CRUD to configure mappings. **Non-goals:** custom-role mapping (v2 maps to the 3 built-ins only — `org_roles` inheritance is a v3 follow-up); no retroactive bulk re-derivation when a mapping changes (applies on each user's next SCIM event); no per-group audit surface beyond mapping CRUD + the membership-changed event. **Shipped — security-adjacent (role assignment), so the design leans conservative.** Two new tables (`scim_group_role_mappings` unique `(scimDirectoryId, providerGroupId)` + `scim_user_groups` unique `(scimDirectoryId, providerUserId, providerGroupId)`), additive migration, orphan-tolerant (no FK, matching the SCIM module). Pure `deriveScimRole(userGroupIds, mappings, defaultRole)` — highest-rank-wins (`viewer<editor<admin`, ranks local to the handler so the pure module never imports `permissions.ts`), `defaultRole` fallback so zero-mapping orgs are byte-for-byte v1; unknown/custom role names rank -1 and never win. Two repos (`scimGroupRoleMappingsRepo` — `getScimGroupRoleMappingsMap` is the hot-path reader — + `scimUserGroupsRepo`), both org-scoped. The membership events are now live: user_added persists the join row THEN recomputes; user_removed drops it THEN recomputes; a membership event before the user exists records the join + audits `roleRecomputed:false` and skips the `org_members` write (the later create derives it); user_deleted clears that user's join rows; group_deleted cleans the group join rows and recomputes affected active users. Both user handlers run the derivation in place of the hardcoded `defaultRole`. 4 admin routes under `/org/scim/group-role-mappings` (GET viewer; POST create / POST :id update / DELETE :id admin — `role:`-gated like the directory routes), create validates built-in role + group existence in `scim_group_state` + rejects duplicate (409). 3 audit actions (`org.scim.group_role_mapping_{created,updated,deleted}`) + the live `scim.group.membership_changed`. Tests: 6 `deriveScimRole` unit cases (no-groups/unmapped→default, single, highest-rank-wins, unknown-role-ignored), 5 membership-handler cases (provision-at-derived, added/removed recompute, group-before-user ordering, malformed reject), 11 route CRUD cases. Full suite green (871 api tests); `pnpm migrate` applies clean against dev Postgres (2 tables + 6 index entries verified). No web/engine/AI change. |
| ENG-202 | SCIM group→role mapping admin UI | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** ENG-201 shipped the group→role backend (tables, `deriveScimRole`, `/org/scim/group-role-mappings` CRUD, audit) but left NO web surface — an admin could only configure a mapping via raw API (curl). The existing `ScimDirectorySettingsPanel` only managed the directory attach + flat `defaultRole`, and its hint copy still said "Per-group role overrides are deferred to v2" (stale). **AC:** an admin can configure group→role mappings from the UI with a real group picker (names, not raw ids). **Non-goals:** no bulk re-derivation / "re-sync now" (applies on each user's next SCIM event); no custom-role mapping (built-ins only, v3); no new permission keys (`role:`-gated like the directory routes). **Shipped — web-mostly + one small read endpoint.** Added `GET /org/scim/groups` (viewer, read-only, no audit) resolving the org's directory and returning `listScimGroupState(orgId, scimDirectoryId, limit)` (org-scoped `eq(orgId)+eq(scimDirectoryId)`, capped at 100 default / 200 max via `?limit=`) — or `[]` when no directory. `ScimDirectorySettingsPanel` gained a "Group → role mappings" section (rendered only with an active directory): a `Promise.all` fetch of directories + mappings + groups keyed on `platformVersion` (mappings/groups best-effort so a blip shows empty states, not a blank panel), a mappings table with an inline per-row role `<select>` (update) + remove button, and a create form with a group `<select>` picker populated from synced groups NOT already mapped (`unmappedGroups` filter). Every mutation `bumpPlatformVersion()`s. New `scim.mappings.*` i18n keys EN+ES (parity-gated) + rewrote the stale `scim.field.defaultRoleHint`. Tests: 7 component cases (mappings render, create posts the right body, inline update, remove-after-confirm, stale-mapping removal, empty-no-groups state, hidden without an active directory) + the existing 4 directory cases adapted to the parallel fetch; 3 api route cases for `GET /org/scim/groups`, including the list cap. Full suite green. **Browser smoke (real `pnpm dev` + Playwright, EN+ES):** seeded a directory + 3 synced groups via psql, drove the UI — create (Engineering→editor) and inline update (editor→admin) both round-tripped through the real API and persisted (API-verified; route tests cover audit `created`+`updated` rows), the picker excluded the mapped group, console errors 0. No engine/AI change; no migration. - collateral: rewrote the stale `scim.field.defaultRoleHint` copy (v2 shipped). |
| ENG-203 | SCIM "re-sync now" — bulk role re-derivation | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** ENG-201/202 left mapping changes LAZY — a group→role edit only re-derives a member's role on their next SCIM event, so existing members keep a stale role until the IdP next touches them. An admin had no way to apply the current mappings to already-provisioned members on demand. **AC:** an admin can re-apply the current mappings to every active member immediately. **Non-goals:** no background/async sweep (synchronous, 5000-member cap — queue-backed is a follow-up); no custom-role mapping (built-ins, v3); no new permission keys (`role:"admin"`); no per-member audit rows (one summary row). **Shipped — backend-heavy + a button.** New `listActiveScimUserState(orgId, dirId)` in `scimUserStateRepo` (org-scoped `eq(orgId)+eq(scimDirectoryId)+eq(active,true)`, `.limit(SCIM_RESYNC_MAX_MEMBERS=5000)`). New pure `resyncScimMemberRoles({scimDirectory, deps})` in `apps/api/src/scim-resync.ts` — dep-injected + unit-tested like `handleScimEvent`, reuses the exported `deriveScimRole` (no logic fork), fetches the mappings map ONCE, loops active members, derives each role, reads current via `findMemberByEmail`, `upsertMembershipByEmail` WITHOUT `invitedBy` (preserves the provisioning actor), counts `membersChanged`, isolates per-user faults into a `skipped` tally; returns `{membersResynced, membersChanged, skipped, capped, changes[]}`. `POST /org/scim/resync` (admin) resolves the directory (409 when none), runs the sweep, audits ONE `org.scim.resynced` summary row. Web: a "Re-sync now" button + hint in the mappings section header (visible with ≥1 mapping), toasts the counts, `bumpPlatformVersion()`s. New `scim.mappings.resync.*` i18n EN+ES (parity-gated). Tests: 7 pure-orchestrator cases (no members; mapped→changed; unchanged; defaultRole fallback; self-heal from null; capped flag; per-user fault isolated) + 2 route cases (409, audit+summary) + 3 component cases. Full suite green. **Browser smoke (real `pnpm dev` + Playwright, EN+ES):** seeded a directory + Engineering group mapped to admin + an active member in Engineering whose `org_members.role` was `viewer`; clicked "Re-sync now" → the member flipped to `admin` (psql-verified) + `org.scim.resynced` audit row (resynced=1, changed=1) in both locales; console errors 0. No migration; no engine/AI change. - collateral: web review found the toast interpolation used `{{count}}` (i18next's reserved plural word) — renamed to `{{members}}` to avoid the pluralization footgun. |
| ENG-204 | Audit log viewer (operator-facing) | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** Janusly writes an `audit_logs` row on every mutation, but operators had NO way to SEE that trail in the product — `GET /audit` was a thin flat list (latest 100, no filter, no real pagination, no role gate) with ZERO web UI, so the compliance/forensics trail was invisible + unnavigable. Pivot off the SCIM arc (operator's choice). **AC:** an admin can view + filter + page the audit trail from the UI. **Non-goals:** no date-range/actor filter (action prefix + cursor is v1); no CSV export; no new index; no realtime/SSE. **Shipped — read-endpoint upgrade + a viewer panel.** New `queryAuditLogs(orgId, {action?, cursor?, limit})` in `auditLogsRepo` — keyset `(createdAt,id)` DESC (reusing the `parseEventsCursor` `<iso>|<id>` format), optional `action` PREFIX filter (`like(action,"<p>%")`), org-scoped, capped `AUDIT_LOG_PAGE_MAX=200` / default `AUDIT_PAGE_SIZE`, backed by the existing `(org_id, created_at)` index; plus the pure unit-tested `buildAuditPage(overfetched, limit)`. `GET /audit` refactored to the `{rows, nextCursor, hasMore}` envelope + `?action=`/`?cursor=` parse + **`role: "admin"`** (security tightening — was ungated; no consumer existed). New `AuditLogPanel` (Operations → Access): read-only table (time via `getResolvedLocale()`, action verbatim, actor, target, metadata preview), action-prefix filter, "Load more" cursor paging; `audit.*` i18n EN+ES (parity-gated).
