Full suite green. **Browser smoke (real `pnpm dev` + Playwright, EN+ES):** seeded 120 varied audit rows (60 `org.scim*`); the panel rendered 100 newest-first with "Load more", filtering `org.scim` narrowed to exactly 60 (hiding "Load more"), and "Load more" appended page 2 (100→120, oldest seq 120 last) — all psql/API-cross-checked; console errors 0. No migration; no engine/AI change. |
| ENG-205 | SCIM email-change re-key collision handling | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** in `handleScimEvent`'s `dsync.user.updated` path, when a user's SCIM email changes, the handler `deleteMembership(oldEmail)` then `upsertMembershipByEmail(newEmail)`. If an `org_members` row ALREADY exists for the new email (a manually-invited admin at that address, or a stale prior-lifecycle SCIM row), the upsert takes the UPDATE branch and overwrites that pre-existing row's role with the SCIM-derived role — clobbering a DIFFERENT principal's membership. WorkOS normally guarantees unique emails per directory so the window is narrow, but the handler does no `providerUserId`/`userId` reconciliation to detect the target email belongs to someone else. **AC:** on an email-change re-key, detect when the new email's existing membership belongs to a different principal and handle it safely (audit + skip, or operator-visible conflict) instead of silently overwriting the role. **Shipped:** fail-safe collision guard wired into `scim-event-handler.ts` via a new `findMemberByEmail` dep (read-before-write). Two predicates, two paths: **re-key** (`handleUserUpdated`) blocks on ANY pre-existing row at the new email (it should be empty; also catches A-clobbers-B's-SCIM-row) → audit `scim.user.rekey_collision` + skip (no delete/overwrite/state-advance); **create** (`handleUserCreated`, included on decision — the more common founder-downgrade footgun) blocks ONLY a human-invited row (`invited_by != scim:webhook`) via the pure `isScimOwnedMembership` helper → audit `scim.user.provision_collision` + skip, while a SCIM-owned row is absorbed so re-attach/idempotency/reactivation are preserved. Both guards return BEFORE any write (mirrors the resurrection/out-of-order early-return pattern); the refused event keeps its dedup row so it can't re-audit, and the operator resolves via the audit log (visible in the ENG-204 panel, prefix-filter `scim.user.`). Backend-only, no migration. 43 handler tests green (+3 collision cases) + a psql-backed integration verify (real repos drive both collisions; pre-existing rows unchanged, audit rows present). |
| ENG-206 | SCIM mapping role select — optimistic update | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the per-row role `<select>` in `ScimDirectorySettingsPanel`'s group→role mappings table is fully controlled by `row.role` (server state) with no optimistic update — on a slow connection the dropdown visibly snaps back to the old role for the duration of the POST + `bumpPlatformVersion` refetch, and there's no in-flight/disabled state, so a second change can race the stale value. **AC:** optimistically reflect the selected role (or disable the row while the update is in flight) so the dropdown doesn't flicker back; reconcile against the refetch. Polish — surfaced by the ENG-201..204 quality sweep. **Shipped:** `updateMappingRole` now captures `previousRole`, optimistically writes the new role into local `mappings` state via a functional `setMappings` update BEFORE the POST (so the controlled select holds the new value through the in-flight request + refetch), and reverts to `previousRole` on failure followed by the error toast; success still `bumpPlatformVersion()`s to reconcile against server truth. Browser-smoked EN+ES: happy-path change persists with no snap-back and zero console errors; forced-failure path reverts the select to the prior role with the DB row unchanged. |

| ENG-209 | Recovery-metrics p50 percentile uses an inconsistent index formula | Shipped | P4 | — | **Problem:** in `computeMttr` (`packages/engine/src/recovery-metrics.ts`), `p50 = sorted[Math.floor(sorted.length / 2)]` while the sibling `p95 = sorted[Math.max(0, Math.ceil(sorted.length * 0.95) - 1)]` uses the nearest-rank convention. For even-length windows the two formulas disagree by one index (e.g. `[10,20]` → p50=20 vs nearest-rank 10). Display-only (the MTTR card's severity is driven by `avg`, not p50; p50 only appears in the rationale string), so impact is cosmetic — but the inconsistency is a latent correctness smell. Surfaced by the post-ENG-207 data/engine/SDK quality sweep. **AC:** make `p50` use the same nearest-rank index formula as `p95` (`Math.max(0, Math.ceil(len * 0.5) - 1)`), add a unit case pinning an even-length window, and confirm no severity-band drift. **Shipped:** extracted ONE internal pure helper `nearestRankIndex(length, fraction)` = `Math.min(Math.max(0, Math.ceil(length*fraction)-1), length-1)` and routed BOTH p50 and p95 through it — rather than copy the p95 literal a second time (which would leave two copies that can still drift, the exact smell the ticket is about). `nearestRankIndex(len, 0.95)` expands to EXACTLY the old p95 expression, so p95 is byte-for-byte unchanged (same `Math.ceil(len*0.95)` → identical float semantics); p50 moves from `Math.floor(len/2)` (upper-middle) to nearest-rank, changing ONLY even-length windows (odd-length + len=1 are unchanged). Severity still reads `avg`, so no band drift — structurally guaranteed. One engine test pins it: an even-length distinct-value window `[10s,20s]` asserts `rationaleMeta` `{ p50:"10.0s", p95:"20.0s" }` (fails under the old floor formula) plus unchanged `severity:"healthy"` / `display:"15.0s"`. Pure-logic engine change — no UI / DB / live AI / migration; `node`-reviewed (helper preserves p95 exactly; the non-empty precondition holds because both call sites are after the `length===0` early return). |
| ENG-210 | SDK reserved-header parity: TS allows overrides Python rejects | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the first-party SDKs diverge on reserved-header handling. `packages/sdk-node/src/request.ts` `buildHeaders` only skips `authorization` / `x-org-id` / `x-user-id` from caller overrides (silently `continue`s), so a caller can override `content-type` / `accept` / `user-agent` (e.g. set `Content-Type: text/plain` while the SDK JSON-encodes the body → a broken request). `packages/sdk-python/src/janusly/_http.py` reserves a broader set (`_RESERVED_HEADERS` incl. Content-Type/Accept/User-Agent) and **raises `ValueError`** on any reserved-header override. Two divergences: (a) which headers are reserved, and (b) silent-skip vs throw. Violates the AGENTS.md SDK 1:1 invariant. **Needs a decision** (harmonize to which behavior). **Decision (operator):** TS matches Python — reserve the broad 6-header set + throw on collision (Python is the louder, safer reference; only TS changes; SDK is pre-release 0.0.1 so the minor breaking change is acceptable). **AC:** the two SDKs agree on the reserved-header set AND the collision behavior, with mirrored tests in both. **Shipped:** TS `buildHeaders` now reserves the same 6 headers via a `RESERVED_HEADERS` set and throws `TypeError` (the JS-idiomatic mirror of Python's `ValueError`) on a caller collision, replacing the silent 3-header skip. CRITICAL detail caught in review: TS composed headers INSIDE the retry thunk (vs Python's compose-once-before-loop), so the throw would have been retried — fixed by hoisting `buildHeaders` into `sendApiRequest` BEFORE `withRetries` (also stops re-composing identical headers each retry), passing the composed headers into `performSingleRequest`. Python UNCHANGED (the reference; its 75 tests incl. the reserved-header throw still pass). sdk-node tests rewritten: `it.each` over all 6 reserved names asserts reject + fetch-never-fired (proves pre-loop) + a non-reserved passthrough + a not-retried-with-retry-config case (24/24). No live AI / no UI / no migration. |

| ENG-212 | Generation system prompt fails recovery-shaped requests (model-independent) | Shipped | P1 | — | **Problem:** the eval prompt `failed-workflow-recovery` was the consistently WORST generation case across EVERY model measured in the 2026-06-10 ENG-194 sweeps (Haiku 0/3→2/5·47; all GPTs judge ≤52) — model-independent ⇒ the cause was `GENERATE_WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, which actively FORBADE emitting retry config ("do not include them") while the review prompt and the `external_node_missing_retry` readiness rule require it. **AC:** ≥4/5 ai-mode with judge ≥60 on Haiku free-JSON, no base-set regression, golden-suite case added. **Shipped:** seven surgical prompt edits in `apps/api/src/ai-prompts.ts` — (1) `retry?: { maxAttempts }` declared in the http shape (ban inverted); (2) new RECOVERY RULE teaching the recoverable-write pattern: ALWAYS retry config on external calls (primary recoverability signal), NEVER loop-back edges (acyclic; exhausted retries land in the recovery queue), failure-branching via the `http.request` TOOL (its envelope never fails the node; branch on `context.<id>.output.result.ok` — tool results live under `output.result`), failure branch ends in a structured-failure `transform` (statusCode/body mapping) or a named notification tool — never a bare noop, and `approval` upstream of writes; (3) explicit acyclic rule; (4) condition-is-a-JSON-string clarification (kills Haiku's recurring `condition: true` schema fail); (5) full worked recovery EXAMPLE; (6) retry declared in the `tool` shape line too (fixed Haiku omitting it when emitting tools); (7) PLACEHOLDER RULE de-ambiguated — APIs described WITHOUT a URL now emit `tool http.request` with url omitted from input (operator wires it in the Inspector) instead of an invalid url-less `http` node (this was a pre-existing failure mode the verification surfaced on employee-onboarding/churn-rescue). Collateral (harness): the blind judge in `scripts/model-eval-compare.ts` now knows the platform semantics (retry-in-config IS the retry mechanism, approval-before-write is required practice, named trigger-noops are deliberate) — judge-v2 scores are NOT comparable with the 2026-06-10 round-1..4 absolutes. Verified: target case **5/5 ai-mode · judge 67.6–71.4** (was 2/5·47); full 16-prompt regression sweep **98% ai-mode (47/48), base set 100%** (pre-change reference 92.5%/88%); golden suite via `pnpm evals:local` **ai-mode 1.00 / shape-pass 0.96** vs floors 0.80/0.70 with both recovery cases passing (`recovery-safe-retry` expectations updated for the new legitimate tool+edge-branching shape; new `recovery-billing-charge` case pins approval+call). ~$1.5 provider spend across 5 verification runs. |

Filtering (ENG-214/216) narrows which rows show; sorting decides what order to work them. The payoff the ENG-218 hook extraction set up. **AC:** a Newest/Oldest/Severity/SLA sort control reorders the visible rows; default "Newest first" is byte-for-byte the current order (server already returns `createdAt desc`); the sort key persists with the filters; frontend-only. **Shipped:** `useRecoveryQueueFilters` gains `SORT_KEYS` (`newest`/`oldest`/`severity`/`sla`) + a `SortKey` type + `SORT_KEY_LABELS` + a `toSortKey` guard; `sortKey` joins the persisted `janusly:recoveryQueueFilters` blob (missing → `newest`, so pre-ENG-219 blobs degrade cleanly) + the persist effect; `filtered` now ends with `[...rows].sort(compareRows(sortKey, overlay))` — a pure `compareRows` helper: `newest`=`createdAt` desc (server-order no-op), `oldest`=asc, `severity`=`SEVERITY_RANK` (p1<p2<p3<p4< no-item) with newest tie-break, `sla`=`slaTargetAtIso` asc (SLA-less rows last) with newest tie-break; copy-not-mutate + stable `Array.sort` so ties keep server order. `DeadLettersPanel` renders a "Sort" `<select>` (mirrors the status/severity selects) + adds `sortKey` to the virtual-list `resetScrollKey`. New i18n keys `dlq.sort.{label,newest,oldest,severity,sla}` in EN/ES (parity-gated). Tests: 5 new hook cases (default newest, oldest reverse, severity P1-first via overlay, SLA soonest-first with no-item-last fallback, sortKey persists) + 2 panel cases (sort select default, severity-sort row reorder via `getAllByTestId` DOM order); existing cases unchanged (default newest is stable on equal/absent `createdAt`). Verified: `pnpm build`+`pnpm test` green; i18n scan clean; `typescript-react-reviewer`-approved; real-browser smoke (seeded mixed severity/SLA/age items) confirmed Severity sort lifts the P1 row to the top, SLA sort leads with the soonest-breaching, and the sort persists across reload, console clean. No backend/schema/API/migration; the default order is unchanged. |
| ENG-220 | Schedule-history anomaly overlay | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the cron-observability heatmap (`GET /workflows/:id/schedule-history` → `ScheduleHistoryPanel`) shipped with a forward-compat seam — every cell carried an `anomaly: boolean` (hard-coded `false`) and the web `<rect>` render had a comment marking exactly where a red-dot overlay would drop in — but the seam was never wired. An operator scanning the heatmap saw fire volume + success/fail tint, but nothing flagged a slot that *reliably* fails (e.g. "the 02:00-Sunday run fails 80%"); they had to hover every red-ish cell and do the math. **AC:** a deterministic anomaly signal fills the existing `anomaly` field and the heatmap renders a red dot on anomalous cells (tooltip note + legend entry); no API contract change (field already exists); healthy schedules unchanged (nothing lights up); EN/ES parity. **Shipped:** a pure **baseline-divergence** detector in `packages/engine/src/schedule-history.ts` — exported `isAnomalousCell(cell, baselineFailRate)` + `SCHEDULE_ANOMALY_MIN_SAMPLES` (3) / `_MIN_FAIL_RATE` (0.5) / `_BASELINE_MARGIN` (0.25); `bucketScheduleFires` computes `baselineFailRate = totalFail/totalFires` once and sets each `cell.anomaly`. A cell flags only when it has ≥3 fires AND fails ≥50% AND exceeds the window baseline by ≥25 pts — so a healthy schedule (baseline 0) and a uniformly-failing one (no slot diverges) both light up nothing; only a slot that fails markedly more than the schedule's norm flags. **Route unchanged** (`workflows-routes.ts` already passes the heatmap straight through — the field just starts being non-false). Web `ScheduleHistoryPanel.tsx` renders a `<circle class="we-schedule-heatmap__anomaly-dot">` on the cell's corner when `cell.anomaly` (`pointer-events:none` so the rect's tooltip still wins), appends an anomaly line to the cell `<title>`, and adds a legend entry; two CSS classes in `index.css` (danger fill + `--we-surface` ring so the dot reads on both a light empty cell and a solid `--we-danger` fail-3 cell). New i18n keys `scheduleHistory.cell.anomaly` + `scheduleHistory.legend.anomaly` (EN/ES, parity-gated). Tests: 10 new engine cases (4 `bucketScheduleFires` anomaly scenarios — healthy/one-bad-slot/uniformly-bad/below-min-samples — + 6 `isAnomalousCell` unit cases pinning both thresholds + the baseline margin at the boundary) + 2 web cases (anomalous cell renders the dot + tooltip note + legend; all-healthy renders no in-grid dot). Verified: `pnpm build`+`pnpm test` green; i18n scan clean; both `node` + `typescript-react-reviewer` reviews; real-browser smoke (seeded scheduled runs with a failure-clustered slot) confirmed the red dot lands only on the anomalous cell, tooltip + legend + es copy render, console clean. **Collateral (found via the smoke):** the heatmap day-row labels now format with `timeZone: 'UTC'` (`ScheduleHistoryPanel` `dayLabels`) to match the UTC bucketing + UTC hour labels — they previously formatted a UTC-midnight instant in the browser's local zone, shifting every row label back a day on negative-offset browsers (jsdom runs in UTC, so unit tests never caught it; the real-browser smoke surfaced "Sat" on a UTC-Sunday cell). No route/DB/migration/AGENTS change — the `anomaly` field already existed on the wire. |
| ENG-221 | Alert on schedule anomalies (`workflow.schedule_anomaly` trigger) | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** ENG-220 shipped the schedule-anomaly *signal* (red dots on the heatmap), but it's pull-only — an operator only sees it if they open that workflow's Schedule panel. A schedule that reliably fails at 02:00-Sunday keeps failing silently. **AC:** a new state-driven alert trigger in the ENG-122 framework fires when an actively-scheduled workflow has an anomalous slot, dispatching to the org's configured channels; configured in the same `AlertPoliciesPanel`; deduped per workflow; no new table/migration; EN/ES parity. **Shipped:** added `workflow.schedule_anomaly` to the closed `ALERT_TRIGGERS` enum + an `AlertParamsWorkflowScheduleAnomalySchema` (`.strict()`, single optional `workflowIds` allowlist — thresholds/window are the engine's fixed contract in v1) + its `ALERT_PARAMS_SCHEMAS` / `AlertParamsByTrigger` entries (`packages/shared/src/alert-policy.ts`). Engine: a `matchesPolicyFilter` case (workflowIds filter) + a `buildDedupeKey` case (`schedule:<workflowId>` — per-workflow dedup so one alert per cooldown summarises all a workflow's slots). API: new `scanScheduleAnomalies(orgId)` in `apps/api/src/alerts/scanner.ts` (5th `runOrgScan` call) mirroring `scanWorkflowSloBreaches` — early-returns with no policies; `listScheduledWorkflowIds` (distinct `workflowId` from enabled `schedule_entries`, explicit `workflowIds` allowlists prioritized before the 200-workflow general cap) → per workflow `queryScheduleFires(orgId, wf, MAX_HISTORY_DAYS, MAX_FIRE_ROWS)` → `bucketScheduleFires` (the SAME aggregator the heatmap uses, so an alert fires for exactly the slots the panel flags) → `dispatchAlert` with the workflow + anomalous-slot summary when any cell is `anomaly`. `composeAlertMessage` / dispatcher audit / dedupe / delivery all reused unchanged (the dispatcher already writes `alert.policy.triggered` / `_suppressed`). Web `AlertPoliciesPanel`: the trigger auto-appears in the `<select>` (maps over `ALERT_TRIGGERS`); added a `workflowIds` params block + `buildParameters` case (reusing the existing field + `alerts.form.workflowIds` keys). | ENG-222 | Cap-correct server-side recovery-queue filtering + sorting | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem (the documented ENG-216 limitation):** the recovery queue filtered by status/owner/severity (ENG-214/216) and sorted by newest/oldest/severity/SLA (ENG-219) ALL client-side over an already-capped fetch — `App.tsx` fetched `/dlq` (the existing newest-first capped `dead_letters` page) + the hook fetched `/recovery/items?limit=200` as a separate overlay, and the hook narrowed/sorted in the browser. Severity/owner/SLA live on `recovery_items`, and the 200-cap is applied by `createdAt` BEFORE any of those filters — so a P1 older than the 200th row could never surface, no matter the filter. **AC:** push filter + sort server-side so the page is the cap-correct top-200 for the chosen filter/sort; default (no filter) behavior-compatible for existing callers; no migration. **Shipped:** new `listRecoveryQueue(orgId, { status, owner, severity, sort, limit })` in `apps/api/src/dlq.ts` — a `dead_letters` LEFT JOIN `recovery_items` (on `(orgId, deadLetterId)`) that applies the WHERE (status on `dead_letters`; owner/severity on `recovery_items`, which excludes no-recovery-item rows) + ORDER BY (`severity`/`sla` use `NULLS LAST` + a `createdAt desc` tie-break; severity text `p1<p2<p3<p4` = rank order) BEFORE the `DLQ_LIST_MAX_LIMIT` (200) cap, folding the recovery overlay INLINE onto each row. `GET /dlq` gains `owner` (`me`→`auth.userId`), `severity`, `sort` params (validated via the new closed `RECOVERY_QUEUE_SORTS` + `isRecoveryQueueSort` guard; 400 on bad severity/sort); response stays a bare array (the inline `recovery` field is additive → `/dlq` with no params keeps the existing newest-first default page, so the home preview remains behavior-compatible). `listDeadLetters` removed (subsumed). Web: `useRecoveryQueueFilters` now OWNS the `/dlq?…` fetch (drops the `deadLetters` param, the separate `/recovery/items` overlay fetch, the `recoveryItemsScope` race-guard, and `compareRows`/client filter+sort); builds the overlay from each row's inline `recovery`; `DeadLettersPanel` drops the `deadLetters` prop (mini-grid stats + `hasOpenEntry` now read the fetched page — no-filter view unchanged); the App→`RightPanel`→`RunsPanel`→panel `deadLetters` thread is unwound (App keeps `/dlq` only for the home preview). Tests: backend `isRecoveryQueueSort` guard + the route's param-wiring/validation (owner=me→userId, 400s); the hook + panel tests reworked to the server-driven model (assert `/dlq?…` query-param wiring + render-from-server + inline overlay + persistence), with a `dlqMock` that simulates the server. `listRecoveryQueue`'s DB correctness is proven by the integration smoke (no apps/api DB-query harness exists). Verified: `pnpm build`+`pnpm test`+`pnpm test:e2e` green; both `node` + `typescript-react-reviewer` reviews; integration smoke (seeded >200 newest dead letters + one older P1 beyond the cap → `/dlq?severity=p1&sort=severity` surfaces the old P1; `/dlq` no-params remains behavior-compatible for legacy callers) + a real-browser smoke. Behavior change (intentional): the mini-grid Total/Open/Retried/Resolved + the warning stripe now reflect the fetched (filtered) page; the no-filter view is unchanged (the org-wide mini-grid summary was restored via `GET /dlq/counts` as ENG-224). Still 200-capped, no load-more (keyset "load more" shipped as ENG-223). No migration. |
| ENG-223 | Recovery queue "load more" (keyset pagination) | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** ENG-222 made the recovery queue cap-correct (the right 200 rows for the chosen filter/sort) but it was still a SINGLE 200-row page — an operator triaging a busy queue couldn't see incident #201. ENG-222 explicitly deferred load-more as "a separate UX ticket"; this closes the last documented limitation. **AC:** keyset ("load more") pagination over the same cap-correct join, in any filter/sort; the bare `/dlq` array stays back-compatible for its home-preview + MCP `dlq.list` consumers; no migration. **Shipped:** backend `apps/api/src/dlq.ts` — every `recoveryQueueOrderBy` branch gains a `deadLetters.id` tie-break (stable total order for keyset; ties were arbitrary before, so behavior-compatible); a pure opaque cursor codec `encode/decodeRecoveryQueueCursor` (`base64url(JSON)` carrying `{ sort, createdAtIso, id, leadKey|null }` — `leadKey` is the severity text / slaTargetAt ISO / `null` for newest/oldest or the NULLS-LAST tail; decode is defensive → `null` on malformed / sort-mismatch / corrupt sla key, mirroring `parseEventsCursor`); `recoveryQueueKeysetPredicate(sort, cursor)` — a plain `(createdAt,id)` keyset for newest/oldest and a COMPOSITE `NULLS LAST` keyset for severity/sla (a row follows when its lead value is `>`, or `IS NULL`, or ties the lead and advances the `(createdAt desc, id desc)` tie-break; a null-key cursor in the tail only admits other null-lead rows); `queryRecoveryQueuePage(orgId, query, pageSize)` over-fetches `pageSize+1` through `listRecoveryQueue` (one JOIN/WHERE/ORDER source) and folds via the pure `buildRecoveryQueuePage`, returning `{ items, nextCursor, hasMore }` (pageSize clamped `[1, RECOVERY_QUEUE_PAGE_MAX=100]`, default 50). New `GET /dlq/queue` route (registered BEFORE the `/dlq` wildcard, first-match-wins; `role: viewer`; same params + `cursor` + page-size `limit`; reuses the severity/sort 400s) returns the envelope; the cursor is unsigned but safe (org-scope is re-applied independently of it). Bare `/dlq` is UNTOUCHED (still an array — the home preview + MCP `dlq.list` back-compat). Web: `useRecoveryQueueFilters` gains `cursor`/`hasMore`/`loadingMore` + a `loadMore()` that appends the next page (with a `requestEpoch` guard that drops a stale append when a filter/sort/refresh resets mid-fetch); the first-page effect hits `/dlq/queue` and resets pagination on any filter/sort/refresh change; `DeadLettersPanel` renders a "Load more" button (mirrors `AuditLogPanel`) below the virtual list, `resetScrollKey` unchanged so appends don't jump scroll. i18n `dlq.loadMore`/`dlq.loadingMore` (EN/ES). Tests: cursor-codec round-trips (incl. null lead key + the no-recovery-item tail) + malformed/sort-mismatch/corrupt-sla decode + `buildRecoveryQueuePage` trim/hasMore/cursor (unit); `/dlq/queue` param-wiring (cursor decode→thread, pageSize, owner=me, severity/sort 400s, first-match-wins ordering) with bare `/dlq` tests unchanged as the back-compat proof; the hook's loadMore-append/no-op/reset-on-sort/epoch-guard cases; the panel's "Load more" render+append+disabled cases. Keyset SQL correctness proven by the integration smoke (no apps/api DB-query harness). Verified: `pnpm build`+`pnpm test`+`pnpm test:e2e` green; both `node` + `typescript-react-reviewer` reviews; integration smoke (seeded ~120 dead letters across severities/SLA/createdAt + a no-recovery-item tail, paged through `/dlq/queue` per sort → every row exactly once, cross-page order holds, NULLS-LAST tail last) + a real-browser both-locale smoke (Load more appends, no scroll jump, hides at end, sort change resets to page 1). No migration. |

| ENG-225 | Frontend maintainability and bundle-budget pass | Shipped | P2 | — | The ultra-audit scan found several critical UI surfaces above the local maintainability threshold (`RecoveryDialog.tsx` ~1300 LOC, `App.tsx` ~1100 LOC, multiple 400–700 LOC panels) and the production build's largest chunks are now concentrated in `App`, React vendor, React Flow vendor, Supabase vendor, and the global CSS bundle. add a cheap bundle-report script or CI note that records top JS/CSS chunk sizes after `pnpm build`; keep `pnpm build`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm test:browser`, and `pnpm test:e2e` green; include one desktop + one mobile UI smoke for the touched surface. **Shipped:** behavior-preserving refactor in 3 slices — zero feature/copy/dependency/migration change. **Slice 1 (bundle visibility):** `scripts/bundle-report.mjs` (pure `kib` + `formatBundleReport` helpers, `node --test` coverage in `bundle-report.test.mjs` auto-run by `pnpm test:evals`) reads `apps/web/dist/assets/*.{js,css}` and prints a raw+gzip Markdown table of the heaviest chunks; root `package.json` `bundle-report` script; advisory only (prints a note + exits 0 when `dist/assets` is absent, never a gate). **Slice 2 (`App.tsx` 1121→1020):** carved the cohesive bootstrap/effects into 3 named hooks under `apps/web/src/hooks/` — `useBootstrapData` (the 9-way `Promise.allSettled` platform fetch + `refreshPlatform`, fired on `authReady`), `useRunPolling` (the 1500ms `/status` poll + `loadStatus` + terminal cascade; the SSE-skip-while-`streamTransport==='sse'` and `addEvents`-merge invariants preserved verbatim), `useKeyboardShortcuts` (the global keydown listener); `App` consumes all three with every passed callback `useCallback`-memoized (so the poll interval / keydown listener don't rebind per render) and hook order `useBootstrapData`→`onRunTerminal`→`useRunPolling`. **Slice 3 (`RecoveryDialog.tsx` 1304→706):** split into `apps/web/src/components/recovery-dialog/` — `types.ts`, pure `helpers.ts` (+ `helpers.test.ts`, 10 cases), and prop-driven `EvidencePanel`/`ReviewBody`/`CancellingBody`/`ValidationFailedBody`/`AppliedBody` (no shared closures); the parent keeps the `Step` state machine, the 3 effects (focus / ESC / validation-poll incl. the double-apply `cancelled`-before-yield guard), and the apply-flow callbacks; public props unchanged → `RecoveryDialog.test.tsx` (23 cases) stays green unedited. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` (gate, 34s) + `pnpm test:browser` (2 files / 10 cases) all green; both `typescript-react-reviewer` + `node` reviews (zero real bugs); a focused real-browser smoke of the Recovery dialog (idle + review states; desktop 1280 + mobile 390 — the AC's two viewports; overlays hidden; DOM-probed; 0 render-time console errors) confirming the split renders identically; web-deps lockdown + i18n coverage held (no new copy). A standalone full-project `tsc --noEmit` could not complete in the dev env (pre-existing: `apps/web` does not declare `@types/node` though its tsconfig requests it, so raw `tsc` needs a workaround under which it hangs) — type-correctness is covered by the Rolldown build, the test suites, and line-by-line review. No migration. |
| ENG-226 | Bundle-size reduction — lazy-load per-tab panels + on-demand modals | Shipped | P2 | — | **Problem:** ENG-225 shipped `pnpm bundle-report`, which showed the eager App chunk at 298.6 KB — bloated because `apps/web/src/components/RightPanel.tsx` eagerly imported ~11 tab panels (only `OperationsPage` was `lazy()`), so every tab's panel shipped in the initial download though each is only seen on its own tab (never on the Recovery Center landing or the default authoring tab); `App.tsx` likewise eagerly imported the on-demand overlays. **AC:** defer the tab-specific panels + on-demand modals to on-demand chunks via the proven `lazy()`+`<Suspense>` pattern; measurable via the bundle-report; behavior-preserving; no migration. **Shipped:** `RightPanel.tsx` — converted 6 imported tab panels to `lazy()` (`MultiAgentTimeline` / `WorkflowsDashboard` / `MembersPanel` / `SolutionPacksPanel` / `RunsPanel` / `CredentialRotateModal`; `OperationsPage` was already lazy) behind ONE shared `<Suspense fallback>` (the existing `common.working` panel-list fallback), with the parent split into a thin `RightPanel` wrapper + the verbatim `RightPanelRouter` if-chain; the lazy `CredentialRotateModal` gets its own `<Suspense fallback={null}>` so the credentials list doesn't blank. Kept eager: `AiCopilotPanel` / `InspectorPanel` + the inspector sub-panels (default authoring path). `App.tsx` — converted the 4 on-demand overlays to `lazy()` (`CommandPalette` / `ShortcutsModal` / `SnippetInsertMenu` / `RunInputDialog`), wrapped the overlay in `<Suspense fallback={null}>`, and GATED the 3 always-mounted modals on their open flags so their chunks load on first open (each `return null`s when closed → gating is user-invisible). `vite.config.ts` untouched (`manualChunks` stays the 4-vendor function; Rollup auto-splits each dynamic `import()`). **Result:** current `pnpm bundle-report` shows App chunk **298.6 KB → 249.8 KB** (−48.8 KB raw / 65.5→45.6 KB gzip, ~16% smaller); the deferred panels became on-demand chunks (RunsPanel 114.8 KB, a shared schemas 67.4 KB, +21 more in the current build), total raw +7.8 KB (per-chunk overhead) but the eager landing-page path is 48.8 KB lighter. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` + `pnpm test:e2e` green (one load-induced 1/506 unit flake under concurrent gate load; 5 isolated web-suite runs all 506-green; e2e green); `typescript-react-reviewer` (zero real bugs); a focused real-browser smoke (Members + Packs tabs + the ⌘K command palette each fetch their module ON-DEMAND — zero lazy modules in the 105-resource boot — and render correctly; 0 console errors). Out of scope → ENG-227 (defer `flow-vendor`). No migration. |
| ENG-227 | Defer `flow-vendor` (React Flow) off the boot path | Shipped | P2 | — | **Problem:** ENG-226 code-split the per-tab panels but the single biggest JS chunk, `flow-vendor` (`@xyflow/react`, 182.3 KB raw / 58.4 KB gzip + a 15.1 KB CSS), still loaded eagerly on every page — including the Recovery Center home landing — because `main.tsx` imported `<ReactFlowProvider>` at the ROOT and `App.tsx` statically imported `WorkflowCanvas`, so React Flow sat in the boot graph; the "Layout & canvas visibility" invariant wanted home users to pay zero React Flow cost yet they still downloaded it. **Key finding:** the canvas viewport is UNCONTROLLED (`<ReactFlow fitView>` — no `viewport`/`onViewportChange`/`defaultViewport`, no persistence), so the root provider never actually preserved the viewport across `inspector→home→inspector` (the wrapper unmounts on home → `fitView` re-runs); it survives ONLY `inspector→operations→inspector` (wrapper stays mounted via `display:none`). So relocating the provider to the non-home boundary is behaviorally identical for viewport — the feared "deliberate-decision" tradeoff dissolved. **Shipped:** new lazy `apps/web/src/components/CanvasWorkspace.tsx` owns the (relocated) `<ReactFlowProvider>` + renders `<WorkflowCanvas>`; `App.tsx` renders it via `lazy()` + `<Suspense>` (reusing the `common.working` fallback) INSIDE the unchanged `workspace-canvas-wrapper`; `main.tsx` drops the root provider. To actually defer the chunk, ALL boot-path `@xyflow` VALUE imports were removed: `store.ts` no longer statically imports `applyNodeChanges`/`applyEdgeChanges`/`addEdge` — they're registered lazily from `CanvasWorkspace` via a new `registerFlowOps` seam (the three change reducers guard on a `flowOps` ref; the canvas always mounts before any node-drag/connect can fire) — and `canvas-projections.ts` uses the marker string literal `'arrowclosed'` (type-only `EdgeMarker` import) instead of the `MarkerType` enum value. `vite.config.ts` `manualChunks` early-returns `undefined` for `@xyflow/` so the loose `/react/` matcher can't sweep `@xyflow/react` into the eager `react-vendor` chunk; Rolldown then keeps the renderer in the on-demand `CanvasWorkspace` chunk. **Result:** `pnpm bundle-report` — the React Flow renderer leaves the boot graph entirely into a deferred `CanvasWorkspace-*.js` (174.1 KB / 55.6 KB gzip + its 15.1 KB CSS); verified `index.html` no longer preloads any flow chunk, the entry/store no longer import it, and `App` imports `CanvasWorkspace` via `import()` only. Boot/home-landing drops ~193 KB raw / ~60 KB gzip; React Flow now loads on the operator's first NON-HOME navigation (canvas tab OR a hidden-canvas tab like operations — both mount the wrapper to preserve the operations-cycle viewport), reconciling the AC's "not at boot" with "viewport invariant holds across `inspector→operations→inspector`"; a home round-trip resets the viewport (unchanged — uncontrolled `fitView`, now documented). | ENG-242 | Bulk folder assignment: select-all (global + per-folder) | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** ENG-241 shipped bulk folder assignment via multi-select, but selection was still **one row at a time** — organizing a flat list of 20 flows meant 20 ticks before the single bulk move, undercutting the "bulk" part. | ENG-243 | Drag a Flows row onto "+ New folder" to create a folder | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the folder thread lets you drag a row onto an *existing* section (ENG-238), but filing it into a *new* folder meant leaving the drag interaction (Inspector field, or typing a name in the bulk-assign datalist). Drag-to-create was deferred in **four** out-of-scope notes (ENG-238/240/241/242). (Born from the empty §3b pool; operator-picked, mirroring ENG-238→242.) **Key reuse — no backend:** folders are *derived* from each row's `folder` value (no folders table), so "creating" one = assigning a row to a name that doesn't exist yet; the existing `moveToFolder(id, name)` already does exactly that, so this is **web-only** — no new route/repo/schema/audit/migration. **AC:** drag a row onto a "+ New folder" zone, name it inline, the row is assigned to the new folder; applies in the foldered view (where drag exists). **Shipped (web-only):** `WorkflowsDashboard` gains 3 transient state fields (`newFolderHover` bool, `newFolderDropFor` `string\|null`, `newFolderDraft`) + 2 callbacks (`commitNewFolder` — trim → `moveToFolder(id, name)`; empty/whitespace name cancels silently; deps `[newFolderDropFor, newFolderDraft, moveToFolder]` so each keystroke rebinds it; `cancelNewFolder`). A new zone is the last child of `.we-list-folders` with three render states: **naming** (inline `<input>` autoFocus/maxLength 60 + Create/Cancel; Enter commits, Esc cancels), **drag-active** (dashed `FolderPlus` drop target mirroring the `<details>` drop handler but, instead of moving immediately, reads `dataTransfer 'text/plain' \|\| draggingId`, clears `draggingId`/`dropTarget`/`newFolderHover`, and opens the input via `setNewFolderDropFor`), **idle** (`null`). The drop→input transition relies on React batching `setDraggingId(null)` + `setNewFolderDropFor(id)` into one render so the input shows regardless of the cleared drag. New `.we-list-folder-new*` CSS (dashed zone + `data-drop-target` highlight mirroring `.we-list-folder`, tokens only). 4 EN+ES i18n keys (`newFolderDropzone`/`newFolderPlaceholder`/`newFolderLabel`/`saveNewFolder`; reuse `cancelAction`; ES "tú", parity-gated). Testids `workflows-newfolder-dropzone`/`-input`/`-save`. Scope: foldered view only (the drag handle is gated on `hasFolders`); first-folder creation keeps its existing paths (bulk-assign datalist, Inspector). **Tests:** `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` — dragging reveals the zone; drop → name → Enter → POST `/workflows/wf1/folder {folder:'Q3 Launch'}`; Esc cancels (no POST, row unchanged); blank name → no-op. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green (incl. i18n parity); `typescript-react-reviewer` (zero real bugs); focused both-locale real-browser smoke against a live stack — seeded 4 flows (Billing×2/Onboarding/Ungrouped), each state DOM-probed via **synthesized HTML5 DnD** (Playwright's mouse-drag can't fire React's drag handlers; dispatched `DragEvent`s with a shared `DataTransfer`): idle (no zone), dragging (zone `New folder`/`Nueva carpeta`), dropped (autoFocused inline input `Folder name`/`Create` · `Nombre de la carpeta`/`Crear`), created (dragged the Ungrouped row → new `Q3 Launch` section, "1 flow"; **persisted server-side** — `GET /workflows` shows `wf-adhoc-1 \| Q3 Launch`), cancelled via Esc (input gone, row stayed in Billing); 0 console errors from the surface (only pre-existing health-badge 404s from version-less seed rows). No backend, no migration, no new dep, no new route/permission/audit-action/error-code. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` drag note extended. Out of scope: flat-view (no-folder) first-folder-via-drag (no drag handles there), nested hierarchy, drag-from-bulk-multi-select, duplicate-name warnings (typing an existing name merges, same as rename), cross-device sync. |
| ENG-244 | Bulk tag assignment: add / remove a tag across selected Flows | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the Flows-list **folder** thread is complete, but **tags** — the parallel organizing dimension — only supported filtering (`GET /workflows?tag=`) + pill display; you couldn't organize many at once. ENG-241 gave folders a multi-select bulk bar; this gives tags the same. (Born from the empty §3b pool; operator-picked, mirroring ENG-241.) **What's new vs ENG-241:** folder is a SCALAR (one literal `set: { folder }`); tags are a multi-value `jsonb` array, so the write is a per-row set operation — `add` = atomic union (append if absent, deduped, capped at `WORKFLOW_METADATA_TAGS_MAX`), `remove` = filter. **AC:** select N rows → add OR remove ONE tag in a single request; reuse the selection infra; no migration / no new permission. **Shipped (full-stack):** **shared** — `WORKFLOW_METADATA_TAG_MAX_LENGTH=40` extracted (now shared by the metadata tags schema + the new one) + `AssignTagToWorkflowsBodySchema` `{ workflowIds: string[](1..500), tag: string(1..40), op: 'add'\|'remove' }` `.strict()`. **data** — `assignTagToWorkflows({orgId,workflowIds,tag,op,actorUserId})` in `workflowMetadataRepo.ts`: ownership-gate (drop cross-tenant) → `add` inserts `[tag]` candidates and uses an atomic conflict `setWhere` (`tag` absent and below cap) to append to the current jsonb array without clobbering concurrent different-tag adds; `remove` is one org-scoped `UPDATE` filtering the current jsonb array where the tag is present. Existing rows touch ONLY tags+updatedAt, a tagless workflow gets a defaults row carrying `[tag]` (add only), already-present / absent / at-cap no-ops are skipped from the count. Returns changed ids. **api** — `POST /workflows/tags/assign` (`editor`/`workflows.write`, exact matcher beside the folder collection routes, BEFORE the per-id ones); 422 `workflow_metadata_invalid`; audit `workflow.tags.bulk_assigned` `{tag,op,count,workflowIds}` (1 new `AuditAction`); org-scoped in the repo. **web** — `WorkflowsDashboard` bulk bar gains a divider + a tag `<input list=datalist>` (org `tagOptions`) + **Add tag** / **Remove tag** buttons; `bulkAssignTag(op)` optimistic (union/filter the selected rows' tags, prior-tags captured for an exact rollback) → POST → `bumpPlatformVersion()` + toast (server `count`) → clear; rollback on failure. New `.we-list-bulk-bar__divider` CSS (tokens only). 8 EN+ES i18n keys (`bulkTagPlaceholder`/`bulkTagAddCta`/`bulkTagRemoveCta`/`bulkTagAdded`+`bulkTagRemoved` plural/`bulkTagFailed`; ES "tú", no Spanglish — "etiqueta"/"agregar"/"quitar"). Testids `workflows-bulk-tag-input`/`-tag-add`/`-tag-remove`. **Tests:** `workflow-metadata.test.ts` (schema: op enum / id cap / tag length / strict), `workflowMetadataRepo.test.ts` (add union+dedupe+no-op-skip, creates row for tagless wf, at-`TAGS_MAX` skip, remove filter, remove-absent no-op, cross-org subset, empty), `workflow-metadata-routes.test.ts` (gate, 422 bad op, audit + metadata, matcher isolation vs `/:id/folder` + `/:id/metadata`), `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` (bulk add → POST `op:add`, remove → `op:remove`, optimistic + rollback). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green (incl. i18n parity); `typescript-react-reviewer` + `node` reviews (zero real bugs); i18n coverage clean; curl matrix (add 4 → 3 metadata rows CREATED; add-again → count 0 no dup; remove → count 1; cross-org id dropped → subset; bad op → 422); **psql** confirms the jsonb arrays — add appended+deduped, remove filtered ONLY the named tag (others intact), rows created for tagless workflows, another org untouched (foreign id dropped); focused both-locale real-browser smoke (Select → bulk bar tag group → type a tag + Add → pills on all rows, persisted → Remove → pills gone, other tags intact; ES `Agregar/Quitar etiqueta`, EN `Add/Remove tag`; 0 console errors from the surface). No migration, no new dep, no new permission/error-code. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` tag note extended. Out of scope: per-row inline tag edit, tag rename/delete-everywhere, autocomplete beyond the datalist, tag colors, whole-set replacement, nested tags. |
| ENG-245 | Tag rename / delete-everywhere from the Flows list | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** tags had filter + bulk add/remove (ENG-244) but, unlike **folders** (ENG-240), couldn't be MANAGED as a whole — no rename-across-all or delete-from-all; you'd edit each workflow by hand. ENG-245 completes the tag-management parity. (Born from the empty §3b pool; operator-picked, mirroring ENG-240 + ENG-244.) **What's new:** the write is org-wide via the Postgres **`jsonb - text`** operator — removes ALL matching array elements and yields `[]` for an emptied array (never NULL, **no coalesce** — cleaner than ENG-244's `jsonb_agg`). No migration, no new permission. **Shipped (full-stack):** **shared** — `RenameWorkflowTagBodySchema` `{from,to: string(1..40)}` + `DeleteWorkflowTagBodySchema` `{tag}` `.strict()`. **data** — `renameWorkflowTag({orgId,from,to})`: ONE org-scoped `UPDATE SET tags = CASE WHEN (tags - from) @> [to] THEN tags - from ELSE (tags - from) || [to] END WHERE orgId AND tags @> [from]` — drops `from`, appends `to` only when absent so a row carrying BOTH **merges/dedupes** to a single `to`; can't exceed `WORKFLOW_METADATA_TAGS_MAX` (one out, ≤one in). `deleteWorkflowTag({orgId,tag})`: `UPDATE SET tags = tags - tag WHERE orgId AND tags @> [tag]`. Both return affected ids; the `@> [tag]` WHERE makes count = only changed rows. **api** — `POST /workflows/tags/rename` + `/tags/delete` (`editor`/`workflows.write`, exact matchers beside the assign/folder collection routes, BEFORE the per-id ones); 422 `workflow_metadata_invalid`; audit `workflow.tag.renamed` `{from,to,count,workflowIds}` / `workflow.tag.deleted` `{tag,count,workflowIds}` (2 new `AuditAction`, singular like `workflow.folder.*`). **web** — `WorkflowsDashboard`: when a tag is selected in the filter (`tagFilter !== ''`), an inline manage group next to the tag `<select>` — Rename (`Pencil` → input + Save/Cancel, Enter/Esc) + Delete (`Trash2` → inline confirm), mirroring the folder-summary controls. `renameTag`/`deleteTag` optimistic (re-map / strip the loaded rows' tags + the `tagOptions` dropdown, set/clear `tagFilter`) → POST → `bumpPlatformVersion()` + toast → exact rollback on failure. New `.we-list-tag-manage` CSS (tokens only). 8 EN+ES i18n keys (`renameTag`/`renameTagLabel`/`deleteTag`/`deleteTagConfirm`/`tagRenamed`/`tagDeleted`/`renameTagFailed`/`deleteTagFailed`; reuse `saveRename`/`cancelAction`/`confirmDeleteCta`; ES "tú", no Spanglish). Testids `workflows-tag-rename`/`-rename-input`/`-delete`/`-delete-confirm`. **Tests:** `workflow-metadata.test.ts` (both schemas), `workflowMetadataRepo.test.ts` (rename/delete: one UPDATE setting ONLY tags+updatedAt, returns ids, none-match → []), `workflow-metadata-routes.test.ts` (gate, 422, audit action + metadata, matcher isolation vs `/:id/folder` + the other collection routes), `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` (rename via inline input → POST `{from,to}` + no-op when unchanged; delete via confirm → POST `{tag}`). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green (incl. i18n parity); `typescript-react-reviewer` + `node` reviews (zero real bugs); i18n coverage clean; curl + **psql** matrix — rename `billing`→`finance` org-wide (a row carrying BOTH ends with a single `finance` → **merge/dedup**; another tag preserved); delete `gone` (a row whose only tag it was becomes `[]` **not NULL**); **cross-org** `wf-x` (otherorg) with the same tag **untouched**; bad body → 422; focused both-locale real-browser smoke against the live stack — filter a tag → Rename (inline input → renamed org-wide, filter follows; psql `["finance","priority"]`) / Delete (confirm → stripped from all, filter clears; psql `[]`); ES copy (`Renombrar`/`Eliminar etiqueta`, `¿Eliminar "…" de todos los flujos?`); 0 console errors from the surface. No migration, no new dep, no new permission/error-code. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` tag-management note extended. Out of scope: per-row inline tag edit, multi-tag filter, tag merge UI beyond rename-into-existing, tag colors, bulk-rename of several tags, undo beyond the optimistic rollback. |
| ENG-246 | Per-row inline tag add / remove on the Flows list | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the LAST folder↔tag parity gap — you can reassign a single workflow's **folder** inline from its row (the per-row `<select>`, ENG-239), but adding/removing a single workflow's **tag** still needed the Inspector. (Born from the empty §3b pool; operator-picked, mirroring ENG-239 + reusing ENG-244's tag SQL.) **Key reuse:** the write reuses ENG-244's `assignTagToWorkflows` with a single-id array behind a NARROW per-id `POST /workflows/:id/tags` (modeled on `/workflows/:id/folder`) — **no new data fn, no migration, no new permission.** **Scope boundary:** the per-row "+ tag" picker adds an **existing** org tag (lists `tagOptions`), exactly like the per-row folder `<select>` only moves to existing folders; brand-new tags stay in the bulk datalist / Inspector. **Shipped (full-stack):** **shared** — `SetWorkflowTagBodySchema` `{tag: string(1..40), op: 'add'\|'remove'}` `.strict()` (no `workflowIds` — that's the URL id). **api** — `matchTagsPath` (`/workflows/<id>/tags`, 2-segment `<id>/tags`, can't collide with `/tags/{assign,rename,delete}` whose 2nd segment is assign/rename/delete) + `workflowIdFromTagsUrl`; `POST /workflows/:id/tags` (`editor`/`workflows.write`, in the per-id block beside `/:id/folder`): `assertWorkflowBelongsToOrg`→404, 422 on bad body, `assignTagToWorkflows({orgId, workflowIds:[id], tag, op, actorUserId})`, `changed = workflowIds.length>0`, audit `workflow.tag.set` `{workflowId,tag,op,changed}`, return `{workflowId,tag,op,changed}`. 1 new `AuditAction` (`workflow.tag.set`). NO data-layer change. **web** — `setRowTag(id,tag,op)` (optimistic re-tag of the row, prior-tags rollback, mirror `moveToFolder`); each `we-list-row__tags` pill gains a ✕ button (lucide `X`, `stopPropagation`) → remove; a controlled `<select value="">` "+ tag" (org `tagOptions` minus the row's tags, rendered only when addable tags exist) → add. New `.we-list-row__tag`/`__tag-remove`/`__tag-add` CSS (tokens only). 6 EN+ES i18n keys (`addTagPlaceholder`/`addTagAria`/`removeTagAria`/`rowTagAdded`/`rowTagRemoved`/`rowTagFailed`; ES "tú").
