No migration, no new dep, no new permission/error-code. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` per-row tag note extended. Out of scope: per-row creation of a brand-new tag (existing-only picker), multi-tag filter, tag reordering, drag tags between rows, tag colors. |
| ENG-247 | Multi-tag AND filter on the Flows list | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the Flows list filtered by exactly ONE tag (a single `<select>`); you couldn't see the workflows carrying `billing` **AND** `urgent` at once — picking one dropped the other. With the per-row tag edit (ENG-246) and tag rename/delete (ENG-245) done, multi-tag AND is the completion of the tag-filtering story. (Born from the empty §3b pool; operator-picked, continuing the Flows-list thread.) **Key shape change:** the filter state goes from a scalar (`tag: string`) to a set (`tags: string[]`) across the list query, the API, the web filter UI, and the persisted view — **no new route, no schema, no audit action, no migration.** **Shipped (full-stack):** **data** — `WorkflowListFilters.tag?: string` → `tags?: string[]`; the containment is ONE jsonb check over the whole set: `tags @> '["billing","urgent"]'::jsonb` is true only when the row's array contains **ALL** listed tags, so multiple tags AND together in a single predicate (the exact `${col} @> ${JSON.stringify(tags)}::jsonb` parameter-bound pattern already used 5× in `workflowMetadataRepo`). Folder stays scalar (a workflow has one folder). **api** — `GET /workflows` parses the repeatable `?tag=` param via `getAll("tag")` → trim, drop empty / >40, **dedupe** (`new Set`), cap at `WORKFLOW_METADATA_TAGS_MAX` (10 — a row holds ≤10 tags, so a longer AND can never match; bounding keeps the containment small and the query adversary-safe). Single `?tag=x` still works (`getAll` → `['x']`); empty / absent → no predicate. **web (persistence)** — `flows-filters.ts` `FlowsFilters.tag` → `tags`; `readFlowsFilters` migrates a legacy scalar `tag` → `[tag]` (non-empty) else `[]`, a present `tags` array wins, `isFlowsFilters` requires only `{query,sort}` (lenient like `folder`/`collapsedFolders`). **web (UI)** — `WorkflowsDashboard`: `tagFilter:string` → `tagFilters:string[]`; the single `<select>` becomes selected-tag **chips** (each `we-pill we-pill--ghost we-list-row__tag` + ✕ → drop) plus a `"+ tag"` add-picker (`<select value="">`, org `tagOptions` minus the already-filtered, gated on at least one addable). `load` appends one `?tag=` per filter tag; the stale-reconcile drops filter tags the org no longer offers; persists `tags`. The ENG-245 rename/delete manage group now renders only when **exactly one** tag is filtered, via a render-body `soleTagFilter = tagFilters.length===1 ? tagFilters[0] : undefined` (derived, no effect). New `.we-list-filter-tags` CSS (tokens only). i18n: new `removeTagFromFilterAria` (EN+ES), `noTagMatches` switched to the joined `{{tags}}`, `tagFilterAria` reworded, orphaned `allTags` removed; ES "tú", no Spanglish. Testids `workflows-tag-filter` (wrapper) / `-filter-add` / `-filter-remove-<tag>`. **Tests:** `workflowsListRepo.test.ts` (multi-tag `{tags:['a','b']}` builds one AND containment; empty `tags:[]` adds no predicate), `flows-filters.test.ts` (legacy scalar `tag`→`[tag]` migration, array round-trip, array-wins precedence, corrupt `tags`→`[]` lenient not null), `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` (add 2 tags → two `?tag=` params + both chips; remove a chip widens; legacy-scalar restore → chip; multi-tag restore; stale reconcile drops the chip; persists `tags`; rename/delete still gated to exactly one). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green (incl. i18n parity); `typescript-react-reviewer` + `node` reviews (zero real bugs; the >10-tag cap loosening an already-impossible query noted as the intentional size/correctness tradeoff); curl + **psql** matrix against a seeded one-of-each truth table — `?tag=billing&tag=urgent` → only the row with BOTH, single `?tag=billing` → both billing rows, 3-tag AND with no common row → `[]`, no/empty filter → all (each cross-checked against `tags @> …` in psql); focused both-locale real-browser smoke against the live stack — default → add `billing` (narrows, manage affordance appears) → add `urgent` (AND-narrows to the single both-tagged row, affordance gone) → remove a chip (widens, affordance returns) → no-match empty-state (`No flows have every selected tag: billing, onboarding.`); ES copy (`+ etiqueta`, `Quitar billing del filtro`, `Renombrar/Eliminar etiqueta`, `Ningún flujo tiene todas las etiquetas seleccionadas: …`); 0 console errors from the surface. No migration, no new dep, no new route/permission/error-code. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` tag-filter note extended. Out of scope: tag OR / a mode toggle (AND only), NOT/exclude filters, multi-folder filter (scalar), saved filter views, tag count badges, fuzzy tag search. |
| ENG-248 | Server-side name search on the Flows list | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the Flows list's three organizing pillars are "folders + tags + **search**". Folders and tags became SERVER-side filters applied BEFORE the 100/200 cap (a match surfaces even when older than the newest page), but **name search lagged** — it was purely CLIENT-side over the loaded page (`w.name.toLowerCase().includes(q)`). So a workflow named "Acme onboarding" beyond the newest 100 rows **never appeared** when you typed "acme", even though its tag/folder filter WOULD surface it. This closes that asymmetry and completes the search pillar. (Born from the empty §3b pool — ENG-093 is a field experiment, the rest Deferred/Gated; operator-picked, continuing the Flows-list thread.) **Shipped (full-stack, mirrors the tag/folder server filter):** **data** — `WorkflowListFilters` gains `search?: string`; ONE predicate before the cap: `or(ilike(workflows.name, '%term%'), ilike(workflows.id, '%term%'))` (name OR id, mirroring the client filter so the two never disagree). A module-local `escapeLikePattern` escapes `\ % _` (Postgres's default LIKE escape is `\`) so a literal `%` matches literally — the term is parameter-bound by drizzle, so this is match-correctness, not injection-safety (injection is impossible). Imports `ilike, or` from `drizzle-orm`. **api** — `GET /workflows` parses `?q=` (trim, drop empty / >100) beside `tag`/`folder`, passes `{ tags, folder, search }`; no new route (the list matcher is unchanged). **web** — `WorkflowsDashboard`: a `debouncedQuery` mirror of `query` (300ms inline `setTimeout`/cleanup useEffect — the repo had no debounce util) drives a server `?q=` refetch (added to `load`'s params + deps); the instant client-side `visible` name filter (on `query`) STAYS, so feedback is immediate while the debounced fetch back-fills beyond-cap matches (hybrid: instant + complete). The four empty-state blocks fold into ONE precedence chain over `visible.length === 0 && !loading` (search → folder → tag → all-clear), so a server `?q=` returning `[]` shows `workflows-no-matches` ("No flows match your search."), never the all-clear `workflows-empty`. **No new i18n key** (reuses `noMatches`/`searchPlaceholder`/the folder+tag empty keys), no CSS, no new dep. **Tests:** `workflowsListRepo.test.ts` (`{search:'acme 100%'}` exercises the `or(ilike,ilike)` + escape branch; empty `search` adds no predicate), `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` (typing sends a debounced `?q=`; a row returned ONLY by the `?q=` fetch back-fills after the debounce = beyond-cap; an active search whose server result is `[]` shows `workflows-no-matches` not `workflows-empty`; the existing instant client-narrow tests stay green). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` (+ `--e2e`) green; `typescript-react-reviewer` + `node` reviews (zero real bugs); i18n parity green (no new keys); curl + **psql** — `?q=<term>&limit=1` returns a match that is NOT the newest row (proves before-the-cap), `?q=` empty → all, `?q=&tag=` narrows by both, a `%`-term matched literally (escape works), psql cross-checks the `name ILIKE`/`id ILIKE` set; focused both-locale browser smoke — type → instant client narrow → debounced beyond-cap back-fill → clear restores → no-match shows the search empty-state; ES copy; 0 console errors from the surface. No migration, no new route/permission/error-code/dep/i18n key. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` search note updated. Out of scope: fuzzy/typo-tolerant search, full-text `tsvector` ranking, searching description/owners text, result highlighting, regex/saved searches. |
| ENG-249 | "Clear filters" affordance on the Flows list | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the Flows-list filters became rich and STACKABLE — a server-side name search, several tag chips that AND together, and a folder filter can all be live at once (ENG-244…248). Clearing them meant resetting each by hand (delete every tag chip, empty the search, set the folder back to "All folders") — tedious with three filters up. This adds a one-click bulk reset; the ergonomic capstone to the filtering UX. (Born from the empty §3b pool — folders+tags+search thread complete; operator-picked, user-selected this direction over pagination / a new surface.) **Shipped (web-only):** `WorkflowsDashboard` — a render-body `hasActiveFilters = query.trim() !== '' || tagFilters.length > 0 || folderFilter !== ''` gates a **"Clear filters"** `small-command` button (lucide `FilterX`) placed after the folder `<select>` in the toolbar. Its `clearAllFilters` useCallback resets `query` + `debouncedQuery` (the latter so the unfiltered refetch fires immediately, not after the 300ms search debounce) + `tagFilters` + `folderFilter` — React batches the four setStates so `load` refires exactly ONCE (unfiltered); the persist effect writes the cleared view. Deliberately leaves `sort` / `collapsedFolders` / `selectionMode` untouched (not filters). ONE new i18n key `clearFilters` (EN "Clear filters" / ES "Limpiar filtros"; reused as label + aria-label; ES "tú", no Spanglish); `.small-command` is already `inline-flex` + `gap` so the icon+label need NO CSS. Testid `workflows-clear-filters`. **Tests:** `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` — stack a tag chip + a search term → the button shows → click clears the search box + drops the chip + fires ONE unfiltered `/workflows` fetch (no `?tag=`/`?q=`/`?folder=`) + the button disappears; the button is absent when no filter is active. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` (+ `--e2e`) green; `typescript-react-reviewer` review (web-only diff — zero real bugs); i18n parity green (the new key has its ES sibling); focused both-locale browser smoke against the live stack — stack search + a tag chip → "Clear filters" appears → click → all reset + full list returns + button vanishes; EN/ES copy (`Clear filters` / `Limpiar filtros`); 0 console errors from the surface. No backend / route / schema / migration / permission / CSS / dep. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` filter note extended. Out of scope: a per-filter count badge, a confirm dialog, resetting sort/collapse/selection, keyboard shortcut, Flows-list pagination (a separate future ticket when org sizes grow past ~200 workflows). |
| ENG-250 | "Load more" keyset pagination on the Flows list | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the Flows list capped at 100 (default) / 200 (max `?limit=`) rows with **no way to browse past the cap** — workflow #101 was unreachable except by filtering to it. Folders/tags/search surface beyond-cap *matches*, but you couldn't *browse* the full list. This adds keyset **"Load more"** so the list extends past the cap, composing with every filter. (Born from the empty §3b pool; operator-picked — the user chose this Flows-list scalability capstone over a new surface.) **Response-shape decision:** `GET /workflows` returns a BARE ARRAY with three consumers (`WorkflowsDashboard`, `useBootstrapData`, `BudgetSettingsPanel`); an envelope would break all three, so the array is KEPT and a `?before=` REQUEST param is added — the web infers `hasMore` from page fullness (non-breaking). **Shipped (full-stack, mirrors the events keyset `run-pagination.ts`/`runs-routes.ts`):** **data** — `WorkflowListFilters` gains `before?: { createdAt: Date; id: string }`; ORDER BY becomes `(createdAt DESC, id DESC)` (id = the unique PK tiebreaker, so the order is total and the keyset never skips/repeats); predicate `or(lt(createdAt, c), and(eq(createdAt, c), lt(id, ci)))` applied BEFORE the cap, composing with the tag/folder/search conditions. `createdAt` is always non-null at runtime (Postgres `defaultNow()`), so no NULLS-LAST handling. Imports `lt`. The existing `(org_id, created_at DESC)` index serves the range scan (id tiebreak in-memory on rare equal-createdAt ties) — NO migration. **api** — `GET /workflows` parses `?before=<iso>|<id>` via a module-local `parseBeforeCursor` (split on the LAST `|`, reject NaN date / empty id → first page), passes `{ tags, folder, search, before }`; still `sendJson(res, rows)` (bare array). **web** — `WorkflowsDashboard`: `PAGE_SIZE = 100` (matches the server default), new `hasMore`/`loadingMore` state, a shared `buildListParams()` (tag/folder/q) used by both load + loadMore; `load` (page 1) replaces + `setHasMore(page.length === PAGE_SIZE)`; `loadMore` (mirror `AuditLogPanel.loadMore`) fetches `buildListParams()` + `before=${oldest.createdAt}|${oldest.id}` (oldest = `workflows[length-1]`, the global oldest since server order is createdAt DESC), APPENDS, updates hasMore. A "Load more" `small-command we-load-more` button below both the flat + folder-grouped views, gated on `hasMore`, disabled while `loadingMore` (text reuses `loading`). Select-all already keys off the accumulated `visible`, so appended rows are included. ONE new i18n key `loadMore` (EN "Load more" / ES "Cargar más"; ES "tú"). Testid `workflows-load-more`. **Tests:** `workflowsListRepo.test.ts` (`{ before }` exercises the `or(lt, and(eq, lt))` branch), `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` (a full PAGE_SIZE page → button shows → click sends `?before=<oldest cursor ending |wf99>` + APPENDS → 100→130 rows → short page hides the button; a short first page → no button). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` (+ `--e2e`) green; `typescript-react-reviewer` + `node` reviews (zero real bugs); i18n parity green; curl + **psql** against `generate_series`-seeded >100 workflows — page 1 `?limit=100` = newest 100; `?before=<row100>&limit=100` = the NEXT rows with NO overlap / NO skip vs `ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC OFFSET 100`; `?before=` composes with `?q=`/`?tag=`; focused both-locale browser smoke — seed ~130 → page 1 shows 100 + "Load more" → click → 130 loaded + button gone; ES copy (`Cargar más`); 0 console errors from the surface. No migration / new route / new dep / response-shape change / new permission. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` pagination note added. Out of scope: infinite-scroll auto-load, a total-count header, an envelope response, an `(org_id, created_at DESC, id DESC)` index migration, bidirectional paging, persisting the loaded-page count. |
| ENG-251 | Bulk-resolve (dismiss) the Recovery queue via multi-select | Shipped | P3 | — | **Problem:** the Recovery queue / DLQ (`DeadLettersPanel`) only acts on ONE dead letter at a time (click a row → Retry / Resolve / Suggest-fix). Bulk *replay* exists by error-cluster signature (`POST /dlq/cluster-apply`), but there was **no bulk resolve at all** — dismissing a batch of non-replayable failures ("upstream's fixed — clear these 30 open entries") meant resolving them one-by-one. Triaging a noisy queue is the core Recovery-Center workflow. (First ticket on a new surface — the user picked Recovery Center / DLQ after the Flows-list thread closed; §3b has no pickable code ticket.) **Shipped (full-stack, mirrors `/dlq/cluster-apply` + the Flows-list multi-select):** **api** — new `POST /dlq/bulk-resolve` (`role: editor`, in `dlqRoutes`, no new permission): a loop over `{ deadLetterIds: string[] }` (non-empty, ≤ `CLUSTER_MEMBERS_MAX_LIMIT` = 100, non-string → 400) that `getDeadLetter`-gates each id (org-scoped), `markDeadLetterResolved` + audits the EXISTING `dlq.resolved` action with `metadata.bulk=true` (each entry IS a genuine resolve, so no new audit action) + `autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay({ resolutionReason: "accepted_loss", via: "dlq_resolve" })`, returning a partial-success envelope `{ resolved, failed, errors }` (a not-found / cross-org id lands in `errors` without aborting the batch). **web** — `DeadLettersPanel` gains the proven `WorkflowsDashboard` selection idiom: a **Select / Done** toggle (`dlq-select-toggle`), per-row checkboxes (`dlq-select-row-<id>`, `we-list-row__select`, `stopPropagation`), a **Select all (N) / Deselect all** over the loaded rows (`dlq-select-all`), and a bulk bar (reusing `.we-list-bulk-bar`, `data-testid="dlq-bulk-bar"`) with the count + a **"Resolve selected"** button (`dlq-bulk-resolve`) → `bulkResolve()` POSTs the ids, then `bumpPlatformVersion()` + the hook's `refresh()` + a `dlq.bulkResolveSuccess` toast + exits selection. Row click is gated (toggle vs. open detail); the single-row detail box is hidden in selection mode. No CSS (nested `.split-row` groups the header toggle; bulk-bar styles reused). 10 new `dlq.*` i18n keys (incl. `bulkResolveSuccess_one/_other` plural; EN+ES "tú", no Spanglish). **Tests:** `dlq-routes.test.ts` (editor gate; resolves N + audits each `dlq.resolved` bulk; partial success with a not-found id; empty / over-cap → 400), `DeadLettersPanel.test.tsx` (select mode → tick 2 → bulk bar "2 selected" → Resolve → POST `/dlq/bulk-resolve {deadLetterIds}` → selection exits; Resolve hidden at 0 ticked; select-all ticks all loaded). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` (+ `--e2e`) green; `typescript-react-reviewer` + `node` reviews (zero real bugs); i18n parity green; curl + **psql** — a mixed batch (valid + cross-org + bogus) → `{ resolved, failed, errors }`; psql confirms only the org's listed rows flipped `status='resolved'`, the linked `recovery_items` closed with `resolution_reason='accepted_loss'`, and `audit_logs` has N `dlq.resolved` rows with `metadata.bulk=true` (cross-org untouched); focused both-locale browser smoke — Select → tick → "Resolve selected" → entries leave the `open` view; ES copy (`Resolver seleccionados`); 0 console errors from the surface. No new permission / audit action / schema / migration / dep / CSS. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` bulk-resolve note added. Out of scope: bulk **replay** of an arbitrary (non-cluster) selection (a follow-up reusing this multi-select), a confirm dialog, filter-driven bulk, bulk escalate / assign-owner, a "snoozed" DLQ status. |
| ENG-252 | Bulk replay across a multi-select in the Recovery queue | Shipped | P1 | 4 | **Problem:** ENG-251 gave the Recovery queue (`DeadLettersPanel`) bulk *dismiss* (multi-select → `POST /dlq/bulk-resolve`), but the inverse — bulk *replay* — still only worked by error-cluster signature (`POST /dlq/cluster-apply`), which requires every selected failure to share one normalized signature. An operator who multi-selected 30 arbitrary open failures with mixed causes ("upstream is back — retry all of these") could dismiss them as a batch but had to replay them one-by-one. That asymmetry (dismiss-many ✓, retry-many ✗) is a direct MTTR cost — the commercial wedge. (Follow-up picked when §3b had no implementable code ticket — ENG-093 is a non-code beta experiment; the rest Deferred/Gated.) **Shipped (full-stack, recombines existing machinery — mirrors `/dlq/bulk-resolve`'s envelope + `/dlq/cluster-apply`'s per-row replay, MINUS the signature recheck):** **api** — new `POST /dlq/bulk-replay` (`role: editor`, **`permission: dlq.replay`** to match single `/dlq/replay`, in `dlqRoutes`): a loop over `{ deadLetterIds: string[] }` (non-empty, ≤ `CLUSTER_MEMBERS_MAX_LIMIT` = 100, non-string → 400) that `getDeadLetter`-gates each id (org-scoped → cross-org returns null → reported in `errors`), **status-guards `open`** (a non-`open` row is reported in `errors`, never replayed — prevents double-enqueue across a batch, mirroring cluster-apply), then `dlqReplay.replayDeadLetter` (shared adapter) + `markDeadLetterReplayed` + audits the EXISTING `dlq.replayed` action with `metadata.bulk=true` + `autoResolveRecoveryItemFromReplay` (just auto-close, like single replay — no cluster-apply ensure-then-close), returning a partial-success envelope `{ replayed, failed, errors }`. No rate-limit (matches single replay; bounded by the 100-cap; no LLM cost). **web** — `DeadLettersPanel` bulk bar gains a **"Replay selected"** primary button (`dlq-bulk-replay`) beside a demoted-to-secondary "Resolve selected" (replay = recover is the encouraged path, resolve = accept-loss the dismiss) → `bulkReplay()` POSTs the same ticked `selectedIds`, on `replayed>0` `bumpPlatformVersion()` + the hook `refreshQueue()` + `onRefresh()`, keeps failed ids ticked on partial + warns, exits selection on full success. New `BulkReplayResult` type + `isBulkReplayResult` guard. 5 new `dlq.bulkReplay*` i18n keys (incl. `_one/_other` plural; EN + ES "Reintentar seleccionados", no Spanglish). **Tests:** `dlq-routes.test.ts` (editor + `dlq.replay` gate; replays N + audits each `dlq.replayed` bulk; partial success on a not-found/cross-org id; **status-guard** — an already-`replayed` entry reported in `errors`, never reaches the adapter; empty / over-cap → 400), `DeadLettersPanel.test.tsx` (Replay shown beside Resolve once ticked; tick 2 → Replay → POST `/dlq/bulk-replay {deadLetterIds}` → selection exits; partial keeps the failed row ticked + "1 replayed, 1 failed" toast). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `typescript-react-reviewer` + `node` reviews; i18n parity green; **psql** — a real bulk replay flips `dead_letters.status open→replayed` (+ `replayed_at`) for the listed org rows and writes one `audit_logs` `dlq.replayed` per id with `metadata.bulk=true` (cross-org untouched); focused both-locale browser smoke — Select → tick ≥2 open → "Replay selected" → rows leave the `open` view; a partial case keeps the failed row ticked; 0 console errors. No new permission / audit action / schema / migration / dep / CSS. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` bulk-replay note added. **Observation (out of scope):** `/dlq/cluster-apply` replays but requires only `role: editor` (no `dlq.replay`) while single + bulk replay require it — a pre-existing authz asymmetry; tightening it is a behavior change for existing roles, flagged as a possible follow-up. Out of scope: a confirm dialog, filter-driven "select all matching", select-all-across-pages. |
| ENG-253 | Free-text search on the Recovery queue | Shipped | P1 | 4 | **Problem:** the Recovery queue (`DeadLettersPanel`) filtered by status / owner / severity and sorted, but had NO free-text search — on a noisy queue an operator couldn't jump to "the failures for run `abc123`", "everything on node `slack_post`", or "all the `timeout` errors" without scrolling / paging. The Flows list already solved the equivalent gap with a server-side debounced `?q=` name search; this brings the same affordance to Recovery. (Follow-up picked while §3b had no implementable code ticket — ENG-093 is a non-code beta experiment, the rest Deferred/Gated.) **Shipped (full-stack, cap-correct, mirrors the Flows `?q=` pattern):** **data** — `listRecoveryQueue` (`apps/api/src/dlq.ts`) gains a `search` filter: `or(ilike(nodeId), ilike(runId), ilike(error_json->>'message'))` with the term LIKE-escaped via the shared `escapeLikePattern` (promoted from a private helper in `workflowsListRepo.ts` to a `@janusly/data` export — single source, no forked escape) and applied with the other filters BEFORE the cap, ANDed into the org-scoped WHERE; `RecoveryQueueQuery` gains a typed `search?` field. **api** — `GET /dlq/queue` + bare `GET /dlq` parse `?search=` (trimmed, non-empty, ≤100 → else undefined; the bound mirrors the Flows guard) and thread it through `queryRecoveryQueuePage` / `listRecoveryQueue`. **web** — `useRecoveryQueueFilters` gains `searchInput` (raw, controlled) + a 300ms-debounced `search` that drives the fetch (so typing doesn't fire a request per keystroke), threaded into `buildQueuePath` (`?search=`), added to the first-page + loadMore deps (a search change resets to page 1), and persisted with the other filters (coerced ≤100). `DeadLettersPanel` renders a `type="search"` input (`dlq-search`) above the status select + a search-specific empty state (`dlq-empty-search`). 4 new i18n keys (`dlq.search.label`/`.placeholder` + `emptyState.dlq.search.kicker`/`.body`; EN + ES "Buscar"/"Busca por nodo, id de ejecución o error…", no Spanglish). **Tests:** `dlq-routes.test.ts` (queue + bare `/dlq` thread `search`; trims; >100 → undefined), `useRecoveryQueueFilters.test.tsx` (debounce → `search=` param + page-1 reset; clear refetches without it; persist + restore), `DeadLettersPanel.test.tsx` (search narrows rows; no-match → `dlq-empty-search`). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `typescript-react-reviewer` + `node` reviews; i18n parity green; **psql / curl** — `GET /dlq/queue?search=…` returns only matching rows, a `%`-containing term matches literally (escape proven), org-scoped; focused both-locale browser smoke — default → type a node/run/error substring → list narrows → no-match term → `dlq-empty-search` → clear → full list returns; 0 console errors. No new audit action / permission / schema / migration / dep / CSS. `docs/architecture/recovery.md` filter sentence updated. Out of scope: full `error_json::text` (nested-field) search, a clear-all-filters affordance. |
| ENG-254 | `vector.search` / `vector.upsert` workflow tools (carved from ENG-025 Phase 3) | Shipped | P2 | 3 | **Problem:** Janusly already has a tenant-scoped pgvector memory substrate (`memory_entries` + BGE-m3 embeddings via Ollama + HNSW cosine index), but only the recovery-recall path uses it — a workflow author had no way to store or semantically recall their own data. `docs/PLAN.md` §7.1 still listed `vector_search` / `vector_upsert` as "still future". (Carved from the Deferred ENG-025 Phase 3 roll-up — the one self-contained sub-feature whose dependency was already met; most of the rest of Phase 3 already shipped under other tickets.) **AC:** two registered tools with Zod input/output; org-scoped; never-throw envelopes; respect the existing two-flag memory consent; a real embed→store→similarity round-trip works. **Shipped (thin wrappers over the existing substrate — mirrors the ENG-035/ENG-038 tool pattern, NO new infra/migration/dependency):** new `packages/engine/src/vector-tools.ts` registers **`vector.search`** (`{ query, topK? }` → `recallMemory` cosine top-k, post-sliced to `topK`) and **`vector.upsert`** (`{ content, metadata? }` → `commitMemory`; `writeSide:true` so validation/dry-run skips it). Both require `executionContext.orgId` (missing → `{ ok:false }`), degrade to envelopes (consent-off → `memory_disabled` / empty entries; embedding failure → `embedding_failed`) and never throw; an unexpected substrate throw is scrubbed (`scrubSecretShapes` + postgres-URL redaction). Operator vectors use a new dedicated `workflow_vector` memory kind (added to `MEMORY_KINDS` + `DEFAULT_RETENTION_DAYS`, 180d) so they stay isolated from the internal recovery kinds — `kind` is a `text` column so **no migration**. Telemetry rides on the substrate's existing `memory.commit` / `memory.recall` usage rows (the tools add none of their own — no fake credential). `.search`/`.upsert` are deliberately NOT in `SENSITIVE_TOOL_SUFFIXES` (an internal consent-gated store write is not an external mutation → no approval-ancestor gate). Registered in `tool-registry.ts`; advertised in the AI generate-workflow grammar (`ai-prompts.ts`) + EN/ES tool-description copy (`tools.vector-{search,upsert}.description`). No Connections/credential kind (the store is the internal substrate, configured via `org_configs.memory.*` / env). **Tests:** `vector-tools.test.ts` (search shaping + topK + workflow_vector scoping; upsert threads run/workflow ids; consent-off + embedding-fail envelopes; missing-org guard; never-throw + URL redaction), `tool-registry.test.ts` (listTools + isToolWriteSide matrix + required/optional), `node-registry.dry-run.test.ts` (upsert skipped in dryRun), `ai-generation-prompt.test.ts` (grammar advertises both + sanitize round-trips). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `node` + `typescript-react-reviewer` reviews; i18n parity green; **live smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-254/`) against Compose Postgres+Ollama — memory enabled + `workflow_vector` allowed, `vector.upsert` ×N → `vector.search` returns them ranked by `similarity`, `psql` confirms the `memory_entries` rows (kind=`workflow_vector`, embedding populated); consent-off → empty/`{ ok:false }`. **Carve-out / follow-ups:** single `workflow_vector` namespace per org in v1 (finer per-use namespacing later); `topK` was a post-slice over the tenant's `memory.recallMaxEntries` cap — **plumbing a true `limit` into `recallMemory` shipped as ENG-259** (the SQL `LIMIT` now reflects the requested `topK`, clamped to the ceiling, while the tool keeps a defensive final slice to preserve its max-results contract). **Post-review hardening:** `workflow_vector` is also registered in the `org_configs` validator (`orgConfigRepo` allowed-kinds + retention-max maps) so operators can actually enable it via the admin API, and `vector.search` pre-checks the kind is in `memory.allowedKinds` so revoking it stops reads too; the kind enum — previously hand-duplicated across `memoryEntriesRepo` + `orgConfigRepo` — was consolidated into a single leaf `packages/data/src/memory-kinds.ts` both import (no cycle), so adding a kind now fails the build until both per-kind retention maps are updated. |
| ENG-255 | Episodic memory for the agent loop (carved from ENG-025 Phase 3 — last open thread) | Shipped | P1 | 3 | **Problem:** the `agent` / `multi_agent` loop (`runAgentLoop`, `packages/engine/src/node-registry.ts`) only read CURRENT-run events (`getRunMemory`) — it never learned across runs, even though the durable pgvector substrate is wired into the recovery/generation prompts and the `vector.*` tools (ENG-254). This closed ENG-025's last open thread. **AC:** new `agent_episode` kind; the LLM planner recalls similar prior episodes into its prompt; completion writes a compact episode; honors the two-flag consent; live cross-run round-trip works. **Shipped (reuses the substrate — NO new infra/migration/dependency):** new `packages/engine/src/agent-memory.ts` with `recallAgentEpisodes` (consent + `agent_episode ∈ allowedKinds` pre-check → `recallMemory(kind:"agent_episode", query:goal)` → same-workflow-first ranking → DATA-framed block with escape clause + `scrubSecretShapes` + per-line/byte caps) and `recordAgentEpisode` (→ `commitMemory`). `runAgentLoop` recalls **only on the `openai` planner** (the deterministic rules planner ignores memory → no embedding call) and injects the block via a new `recalledEpisodes` field on the planner prompt (`agent-planner.ts`); both completion paths (`done` + `maxSteps`) record one episode (goal + outcome, metadata `{success, stepCount}`) **gated on `!ctx.dryRun`** so validation/sandbox runs don't pollute memory. New `agent_episode` kind added to the single-source `memory-kinds.ts` leaf (compiler forced `DEFAULT_RETENTION_DAYS` 180d + `MEMORY_RETENTION_MAX_DAYS` 730d) + the `org_configs` allowed-kinds validator; no migration (`kind` is text). Both helpers NEVER throw — any consent/substrate failure degrades silently so the agent loop is never broken; memory-off (default) → empty recall + no write → byte-for-byte today's behavior. Telemetry rides on the substrate's `memory.commit`/`memory.recall` rows. **Tests:** `agent-memory.test.ts` (ranked DATA block; consent-off/kind-disallowed/blank-goal short-circuits without a recall call; never-throw; record shape + no-op/never-throw), `node-registry.dry-run.test.ts` (records on completion in prod + skips on dryRun + rules planner skips recall), `agent-planner.test.ts` (`recalledEpisodes` in/out of the prompt), `orgConfigRepo.test.ts` (`agent_episode` in both validators). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `node` review; **live smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-255/`) against Compose Postgres+Ollama — agent run → `agent_episode` row in `psql` → second agent recalls it; consent-off → no row + empty recall. **Follow-ups:** richer episode shaping (tool-sequence distillation, success-weighted recall) and recall on the rules planner are deferred. |
| ENG-256 | Workflow soft-delete + retention purge (carved from ENG-026 Phase 4) | Shipped | P2 | 4 | **Problem:** `DELETE /workflows/:id` was an irreversible hard delete (the `workflows` row + all `workflow_versions` + `workflow_metadata`, gone at once) — an accidental delete couldn't be undone, and the route is API/SDK-only (no UI delete button). Closes ENG-026's "soft delete + retention" thread. **AC:** DELETE becomes a recoverable soft delete; reads exclude soft-deleted; a restore path; a retention sweep hard-purges old tombstones. **Shipped (backend-only — no Trash UI to extend; that's a follow-up):** new nullable `workflows.deletedAt` (one-file migration; `kind` of change = plain `ADD COLUMN`). DELETE now sets `deletedAt` + unregisters schedulers + KEEPS versions/metadata (audit `workflow.deleted` gains `metadata.soft=true`); idempotent (absent/already-deleted → 404). New `POST /workflows/:id/restore` (`role: editor`) clears `deletedAt`, re-syncs schedules from the latest version via `syncWorkflowSchedules`, audits the new `workflow.restored` action. **Every workflow read excludes `deletedAt IS NOT NULL`** — the keyset list (`workflowsListRepo`), `/workflows/latest` + `/workflows/versions`, `/start` ownership gate + `getWorkflowStatus`, the four `workflows-routes` ownership gates, and the metadata tag/folder distincts + assign gates (`workflowMetadataRepo`). **Retention:** a new `RETENTION_TARGETS` entry (`deleteExpiredSoftDeletedWorkflowsForOrg` in `retentionRepo`) hard-purges workflows soft-deleted longer than the new `retention.deletedWorkflowsDays` org-config key (default 30, range 1..365) + their versions/metadata via one atomic deferred-cascade statement, through the existing daily `system:retention` cron; `listOrgIdsForRetention` gains a soft-deleted-workflows UNION arm so an org with only a tombstoned workflow is still swept. Scheduler-entry worker reads filter to active parent workflows, preventing stale `schedule_entries` from re-running tombstoned workflows after a teardown blip. **Tests:** soft-delete + restore route behavior plus `/workflows/latest`/`/workflows/versions` tombstone gates (`workflows-slo-routes.test.ts`), `/start` pause-gate `isNull` mock, retention per-table wiring (`retention-scheduler.test.ts`), atomic hard-purge SQL (`retentionRepo.test.ts`), stale-schedule-entry tombstone guard (`scheduleEntriesRepo.test.ts`), org-config key validate/clamp (`orgConfigRetention`/`orgConfigRepo` tests), metadata distinct JOIN mock. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `node` review; **live smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-256/`) against Compose Postgres — save → DELETE → `psql` shows `deleted_at` set + versions kept + list omits it + `/start` 404 → restore → reappears (`deleted_at NULL`) → aged tombstone purge → workflow+versions+metadata hard-gone. **Behavior change (documented):** DELETE is no longer immediate/irreversible — this EVOLVES the AGENTS "Cascade posture" (updated). **Follow-ups:** a Trash/restore web UI (shipped as **ENG-258**); the other ENG-026 threads stay gated. |
| ENG-257 | Reject version-writes on a soft-deleted workflow (finish the soft-delete contract) | Shipped | P2 | 4 | **Problem:** ENG-256 gated every workflow **read** so a tombstoned workflow behaves as "not found", but left two **version-write** paths ungated: `saveWorkflowVersion` (`apps/api/src/workflows-save.ts`) and `rollbackWorkflowToVersion` (`apps/api/src/workflows-rollback.ts`) looked up the workflow row WITHOUT a `deletedAt` check, so a direct API/SDK `POST /workflows/save` or `/workflows/rollback` against a soft-deleted id silently appended a new `workflow_versions` row to a hidden workflow (the UI can't reach it — `/latest` 404s — but the API can). An inconsistency: reads say "not found" while writes pass. (Carved from ENG-026 Phase 4 — the last soft-delete write-path gap.) **AC:** save + rollback against a soft-deleted workflow → `workflow_not_found` 404 (no version written); a brand-new id still inserts; an active workflow still saves/rolls back; enumeration-consistent envelope. **Shipped (backend-only — no migration/dep/UI):** chosen behavior is **reject, NOT auto-restore** — a save must never silently resurrect a deleted workflow; the operator restores explicitly via `POST /workflows/:id/restore` first. `SaveWorkflowResult` gains a `{ kind: "deleted" }` variant; `saveWorkflowVersion` adds a pre-check (`select { deletedAt } from workflows where id+orgId limit 1`) BEFORE the retry loop → returns `deleted` when the existing row is tombstoned (no row → falls through to the insert path for a new workflow; active row → the normal update path), keeping the conflict-retry loop untouched. `RollbackResult` gains `{ ok:false; code:"deleted" }`; the same pre-check runs as the FIRST select inside the existing rollback transaction. `workflows-routes` maps both to the standard `errorEnvelope("workflow_not_found")` 404 (same envelope the ownership/DELETE/restore gates use); `solution-packs-routes` (a third `saveWorkflowVersion` caller) handles the new variant to restore type narrowing. No new audit on the no-op reject (nothing mutated); the `workflow.saved` / `workflow.rolled_back` audits stay on the success paths. The pre-check carries `eq(orgId)` (multi-tenant); the delete-races-save TOCTOU is benign (the hidden version is retention-swept). **Tests:** `workflows-save.test.ts` (soft-deleted → `kind:"deleted"` + nothing written; active gate row → still saves), `workflows-rollback.test.ts` (soft-deleted → `{ ok:false, code:"deleted" }` + no insert); the route 404 mapping is covered by the live HTTP smoke. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `node` review (zero real bugs); **live smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-257/`) full HTTP round-trip against Compose Postgres — seed active workflow (v1, `psql` count 1) → soft-delete (`deleted_at` set) → `POST /workflows/save` → **404 `workflow_not_found`** → `POST /workflows/rollback` → **404 `workflow_not_found`** → `psql` version count **unchanged (1)**; controls: fresh-id save → 200, restore → 200, save-after-restore → 200 (v2, count 2). **Behavior change (documented):** writes now match reads — soft-deleted = `workflow_not_found` everywhere (AGENTS "Cascade posture" updated). **Follow-ups:** none for this edge; the broader ENG-026 threads stay gated. |
| ENG-258 | Web Trash/restore UI for soft-deleted workflows (close the soft-delete loop) | Shipped | P2 | 4 | **Problem:** ENG-256/257 shipped soft delete + the write-edge, but the whole feature was **API/SDK-only** — the web UI had no Delete button and no way to see or restore a deleted workflow. ENG-256's documented Trash-UI follow-up. **AC:** a Flows-list Delete affordance (soft); a Trash view listing soft-deleted workflows with Restore; org-scoped read endpoint; both locales; gates + UI smoke + psql round-trip. **Shipped (full delete → trash → restore loop; backend-only addition is one read route — no migration/dep/new mutation):** **Backend** — `listDeletedWorkflowsWithRunSummary(orgId, limit)` (`workflowsListRepo.ts`) mirrors the active list but `isNotNull(deletedAt)` + `deletedAt DESC`, reusing a newly-extracted shared `foldRunSummary` (run aggregate) helper; `deletedAt` added to `WorkflowListRow` (active rows → null). New `GET /workflows/trash` (`permission: workflows.read`, capped 100/200, no keyset — retention bounds the set) registered BEFORE the bare `/workflows` matcher; DELETE + restore routes reused unchanged. **Web** (`WorkflowsDashboard.tsx`, single component) — per-row Delete (`Trash2`) with an inline confirm (reuses `confirmDeleteCta`/`cancelAction`), optimistic removal + rollback, `bumpPlatformVersion()`; a header **Trash** toggle (`showTrashed`) that swaps the fetch to `/workflows/trash` and renders a flat, filter-free, Restore-only list (active-only folders/tags/bulk/drag hidden), each row showing "Deleted {date}"; Restore (`RotateCcw`) POSTs `/restore`, optimistic + rollback; distinct trash-empty state. `SavedWorkflow.deletedAt?` added. 16 EN+ES i18n keys (`deleteFlow`/`deleteConfirm`/`workflowDeleted`/`deleteFailed`/`trashToggle*`/`trashTitle`/`trashHelper`/`trashEmpty*`/`deletedAtLabel`/`restoreFlow`/`workflowRestored`/`restoreFailed`, parity-gated, ES "tú" — "trash"→"Papelera"). Decisions: **toggle not a new tab** (no `ActiveTab`/nav change); **no "expires in N days"** (per-org retention window unknown to the client — a hardcoded "30" would be wrong). **Tests:** `workflowsListRepo.test.ts` (deleted-list fold + tombstone date + `deletedAt` on active rows), `workflows-slo-routes.test.ts` (`GET /workflows/trash` repo call + limit clamp + `permission` + registration-before-bare), `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` (delete confirm→DELETE→removal, cancel = no request, DELETE-fail rollback, trash toggle→fetch→restore→removal, trash empty). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `node` + `typescript-react-reviewer` reviews; i18n parity + coverage green; **live smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-258/`) both-locale focused browser run against Compose Postgres+API+web — seed flows → Delete (confirm → row gone) → Trash (deleted flow listed w/ date) → Restore (returns to active); `psql` confirms `deleted_at` set then `NULL`, versions retained; state-by-state PNGs + 0 console errors. **Follow-ups:** "expires in N days" shipped as **ENG-261**, bulk restore as **ENG-262**, keyset pagination as **ENG-263** — the trash threads are now fully closed. |
| ENG-259 | True topK limit for `recallMemory` / `vector.search` (carved from ENG-254) | Shipped | P2 | 3 | **Problem:** ENG-254's `vector.search` accepts a `topK` (1..50, default 8), but `recallMemory` (`packages/data/src/memoryEntriesRepo.ts`) had no `limit` param — it always issued `LIMIT recallMaxEntries` (default 8) and the tool **post-sliced** to `topK`. So `topK < recallMaxEntries` over-fetched (pgvector computed cosine distance for the full ceiling, then the surplus was discarded) and `topK > ceiling` made the slice a no-op. ENG-254's documented follow-up. **AC:** `recallMemory` honors an optional `limit` in the SQL `LIMIT` (clamped to `recallMaxEntries`); `vector.search` threads `topK`; backward-compatible for the no-`limit` callers. **Shipped (backend-only — no migration/dep/API/UI):** `RecallMemoryInput` gains an optional `limit`; `recallMemory` computes `effectiveLimit = min(max(1, ⌊limit⌋), recallMaxEntries)` (sanitize to int ≥ 1, then clamp — `recallMaxEntries` stays the **hard ceiling**, so a large `topK` can never over-fetch) and uses it at the `.limit(...)`; omit → the ceiling (today's behavior). `vector.search` (`packages/engine/src/vector-tools.ts`) passes `limit: topK` and keeps only a defensive final `entries.slice(0, topK)` so the public tool contract still holds if the substrate ever over-returns. The four no-`limit` recall paths — recovery ×2, generation, agent episodic — are byte-for-byte unchanged (LIMIT = `recallMaxEntries`). **Tests:** `memoryEntriesRepo.test.ts` (the `.limit()` spy asserts `limit:3`→3, `limit:100`→8 clamp, `limit:0`→1 sanitize, `limit:3.9`→3 floor, no-limit→ceiling); `vector-tools.test.ts` (recall called with `limit: topK`; default `topK`=8 when omitted; shaping still ≤ topK). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `node` review; **live smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-259/`) against Compose Postgres+Ollama — `recallMaxEntries`=8, upsert ~12 vectors, `vector.search topK=3` → exactly 3, `topK=100` → ≤8 (clamped), confirmed the SQL `LIMIT` reflects the request (not a fixed 8); `psql` confirms the rows. **Follow-ups:** plumbing a per-call `limit` into the agent/recovery recall paths (they still use the ceiling) is deferred — no caller needs it yet. |
| ENG-260 | SDK `runs.cancel()` parity (wrap `POST /run/cancel`) | Shipped | P3 | 4 | **Problem:** the API has `POST /run/cancel` (`role: editor` + `permission: runs.cancel`; body `{ runId, reason? }` → `{ runId, status: "cancelled" }`; 400/403/404/409) but neither first-party SDK wrapped it — the Node `RunsResource` (`packages/sdk-node`) and the Python `RunsResource` (`packages/sdk-python`) both had start/get/list/poll/stream/resume but no `cancel`, so a consumer could start/poll/resume a run but not stop a misbehaving one programmatically. Violated the AGENTS SDK 1:1 parity intent + left ENG-112/ENG-113's lifecycle surface incomplete. **AC:** add `cancel` to BOTH SDKs mirroring `resume_node`/`resumeNode`; happy + 409 tests in each; gates green. **Shipped (thin wrappers, NO backend change):** Node `runs.cancel({ runId, reason? }, options?) → { runId, status: "cancelled" }` (POSTs `/run/cancel`, omits `reason` when undefined, routes through the existing `sendApiRequest` so 409 → `JanuslyApiError` statusCode 409); Python sync `runs.cancel(*, run_id, reason=None) → dict` via the shared `request(...)` and async `await client.runs.cancel(run_id=..., reason=None)` via `async_request(...)`. Each mirrors its SDK's existing idiom (object arg vs kwargs) + its `resume` sibling exactly; no new transport/error type/auth path. **Tests:** `runs.test.ts` (POST body shape with + without `reason`, `{runId,status}` return, a `409` Response → typed `JanuslyApiError` statusCode 409); `test_runs.py` + `test_async_client.py` (sync/async POST body shape with + without `reason`, `{runId,status}` return, and 409 → typed `JanuslyApiError` status_code 409). **Verified:** `@janusly/sdk` 62 vitest + `tsc --noEmit` clean; Python 81 pytest + `mypy src/janusly` clean; full `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `node` review; **live route check** (`output/janus-ship/eng-260/`) against Compose Postgres+API — `POST /run/cancel` bogus id → 404, a no-worker run left non-terminal/cancellable → 200 `{status:"cancelled"}` with `psql` status `cancelled` → second cancel → 409 (the SDK's mapped status codes). Both SDK READMEs gain a "Cancel a run" example; Python method-list/reference docs updated. **Follow-ups:** none — the SDK run-lifecycle surface (start/list/get/poll/stream/resume/cancel) is now complete. |
| ENG-261 | Trash "expires in N days" (retention countdown per row) | Shipped | P3 | 4 | **Problem:** ENG-258's Trash view showed "Deleted {date}" but not when the row would be auto-purged. The `system:retention` sweep hard-deletes a tombstone once it's older than the per-org `retention.deletedWorkflowsDays` (default 30). ENG-258 deferred a countdown — "the per-org window is unknown to the client; a hardcoded 30 would be wrong." That blocker is gone: `GET /org/config` (un-gated — any authenticated user) already returns the retention key, so the Trash view (viewer+) can read the real window. **AC:** per-row "Expires in N days" / "Expires soon" from the real window; no guessed number when unknown; both locales; gates + UI smoke. **Shipped (web-only — NO backend change):** new pure `apps/web/src/trash-expiry.ts` `daysUntilPurge(deletedAtIso, retentionDays, nowMs) = ceil((deletedAt + days − now)/1d)` (injectable `now` → deterministic). `WorkflowsDashboard` adds `retentionDays: number | null` + `trashNowMs: number | null` state and an effect (only when Trash is open) that captures `now` outside render, then reads `GET /org/config` (parsed like `BudgetSettingsPanel`): a finite `retention.deletedWorkflowsDays` → that, key unset → `DEFAULT_RETENTION_DAYS=30` (a const mirroring the backend default), fetch-fail → `null`. `renderTrashRow` shows a `we-pill we-pill--ghost` (existing class, no new CSS) only when `retentionDays != null && trashNowMs != null && deletedAt`: `daysLeft > 0` → `expiresInDays` (plural), else `expiresSoon`; **null window → no pill** (never a guessed number). 3 EN+ES i18n keys (`expiresInDays_one`/`_other`/`expiresSoon`, parity-gated, ES "tú"). **Tests:** `trash-expiry.test.ts` (5/30→25, 40/30→−10, exact boundary→0, sub-day ceil→1, custom window); `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` (window 30 → "Expires in 25 days" for a row deleted 5d ago + "Expires soon" for 40d ago; `/org/config`-fails → no pill). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `typescript-react-reviewer` review; i18n parity + coverage green; **live UI smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-261/`) both-locale focused Trash view showing the "Deleted {date}" + "Expires in N days" pill, DOM-probed, console 0. **Decisions:** display estimate only (the server purge is authoritative); the 30-day fallback applies solely when the org left the key unset. **Follow-ups:** the broader trash threads (keyset pagination, bulk restore) stay open. |
| ENG-262 | Trash bulk restore (multi-select + restore selected) | Shipped | P3 | 4 | **Problem:** ENG-258's Trash view restores one workflow at a time (per-row button); restoring a batch (accidental multi-delete) means N clicks. ENG-258's last open Trash follow-up. **AC:** multi-select + a "Restore selected" action; both locales; gates + UI smoke. **Shipped (web-only — NO backend change; reuses the existing per-id `POST /workflows/:id/restore`):** in the Trash view every row gets an always-on checkbox (selection is that view's purpose; the active-list `selectionMode` toggle stays active-only), feeding a `we-list-bulk-bar` action bar with "Select all (N)" / "Clear" + "Restore selected (N)" (disabled at 0). New `bulkRestore` (`WorkflowsDashboard.tsx`): optimistically drops the checked rows, fires the per-id restore for each via `Promise.allSettled` (the trash set is retention-bounded → small N; no batch endpoint), one success toast (`bulkRestored` plural) + `bumpPlatformVersion()`; a partial failure surfaces an error toast and the bump-driven refetch re-adds the failed rows (never leaves the list lying). Reuses `selectedIds`/`toggleSelected` + the `we-list-row__select`/`we-list-bulk-bar` CSS — no new selection machinery, no new CSS. The Trash toggle already clears `selectedIds` on entry/exit so trash selection never bleeds into the active list. 5 EN+ES i18n keys (`trashSelectAll`/`trashRestoreSelected`/`bulkRestored_one`/`_other`/`bulkRestoreFailed`, parity-gated, ES "tú"; reuses `clearSelection`/`selectRowAria`/`bulkSelectedCount`). **Tests:** `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` — check 2 rows → "Restore selected (2)" → two `POST /workflows/<id>/restore` + both rows gone (3rd stays); select-all ticks all then clears; a partial failure (one restore rejects) → the failed row reappears via the refetch. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `typescript-react-reviewer` review; i18n parity + coverage green; **live UI smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-262/`) both-locale focused Trash view (checkboxes + action bar; select 2 → Restore selected → both leave trash, third stays) + `psql` confirming the two `deleted_at` cleared, console 0. **Decisions:** client-loop over the tested per-id route (batch endpoint a noted follow-up if N grows); incremental value (carved past a "stop and consolidate" recommendation), scope kept tight on purpose. **Follow-ups:** trash keyset pagination is the only remaining trash thread. |
| ENG-263 | Trash keyset pagination (reach tombstones beyond the first page) | Shipped | P3 | 4 | **Problem:** the Trash view (ENG-258) loaded one capped page (100/200) with **no keyset cursor** — an org that soft-deletes >1 page of workflows within the retention window couldn't reach the older tombstones in the UI (still restorable by id via the API + purged by retention, but the list silently truncated). ENG-258's last open follow-up. **AC:** `?before=<deletedAtIso>\|<id>` returns the page strictly after that `(deletedAt, id)` in `deletedAt DESC, id DESC` order (no overlap/skip); the Trash view shows "Load more"; active keyset unchanged. **Shipped (mirrors the active list's keyset on `deletedAt` — no new pattern/dep/migration/copy):** `listDeletedWorkflowsWithRunSummary` gains an optional `before: { deletedAt, id }` + the `or(lt(deletedAt), and(eq(deletedAt), lt(id)))` predicate (the `deletedAt` analogue of the active `createdAt` keyset; `deletedAt` is non-null under the `isNotNull` filter). The `/workflows/trash` route parses `?before=` via the existing `parseBeforeCursor` (generic `<iso>\|<id>` → the instant carries `deletedAt`). `WorkflowsDashboard` `load()` drops the `!showTrashed` `hasMore` gate (a full trash page now means more may exist); `loadMore()` branches the base path + cursor field by `showTrashed` (trash → `/workflows/trash?before=<deletedAt>\|<id>`, filter-free; active → unchanged); the existing `hasMore`-gated "Load more" button renders under the trash list. **Tests:** `workflowsListRepo.test.ts` (the `before` keyset branch on `deletedAt`); `workflows-slo-routes.test.ts` (trash route threads the parsed `{deletedAt,id}` cursor; malformed → unpaged; existing limit assertions updated for the 3rd arg); `WorkflowsDashboard.test.tsx` (full first trash page → "Load more" → 2nd fetch is `/workflows/trash?before=<deletedAt>|<id>` using the oldest row's deletedAt NOT createdAt + rows append). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `node` + `typescript-react-reviewer` reviews; **live smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-263/`) Compose Postgres+API — seed ~5 tombstones, `GET /workflows/trash?limit=2` then `?limit=2&before=<page-1 oldest deletedAt>\|<id>` returns the next 2 with no overlap, third page exhausts, `psql` confirms `deletedAt DESC, id DESC` order (small `?limit` exercises the real keyset without seeding 100+). The web "Load more"-in-trash button needs >100 in-window tombstones to appear (impractical to seed for a screenshot) — its wiring is covered by the component test (UI-smoke unreachable-state allowance). **Behavior change (documented):** AGENTS "Cascade posture" trash clause updated ("no keyset cursor" → keyset-paginated on `(deletedAt, id)`). **Follow-ups:** none — the trash threads (UI, expiry, bulk restore, pagination) are now fully closed. |
| ENG-264 | Recovery SLA policies (configurable per-severity targets + attainment) | Shipped | P1 | — | Promotes deep-review proposal §3 #1. **Problem:** recovery SLA targets were hardcoded in `SLA_SECONDS_BY_SEVERITY` (`@janusly/shared`; p1=1h..p4=7d) — same for every org, un-tunable — and there was no metric showing how well an org met its SLAs. **Verify-before-implement:** breach→alert was ALREADY built (the `recovery_item.sla_breached` trigger, `scanRecoveryItemSlaBreaches` on the `*/2` alerts cron, `listOpenItemsWithBreachedSla`, dispatcher + `sla:<itemId>` dedup) and `recovery_items.slaTargetAt` already existed — so the ticket was only the two open gaps. **AC:** org-configurable per-severity targets feeding `slaTargetAt`; SLA-attainment % on the value dashboard; no migration. **Shipped (no migration):** new closed-catalog key `recovery.slaPolicies` (JSON object of MINUTES keyed by p1..p4; empty default = built-in defaults; partial map allowed; per-severity 1..43200min = 30d, matching the per-item override cap; `validateRecoverySlaPolicies` mirrors `ai.surfaceModels`). New scoped reader `getRecoverySlaSeconds(orgId)` (narrow row read, fail-open to defaults) merges config over `SLA_SECONDS_BY_SEVERITY`; `defaultSlaTargetAt(severity, from, secondsBySeverity?)` gains an optional map (stays pure) and `createRecoveryItem` / `acknowledgeRecoveryItem` / `escalateRecoveryItem` pass the tenant map (explicit `slaTargetAtOverrideIso` still wins). New `queryRecoverySlaAttainment(orgId, since)` (Postgres `count(*) filter (where resolvedAt <= slaTargetAt)` over items resolved in-window) + a `slaAttainment` `RecoveryMetric` in `composeRecoveryMetrics` with `SLA_ATTAINMENT_BANDS` (green ≥90, amber ≥75, red <75); surfaced as an SLA-attainment tile in the Recovery Center metric strip (reuses `VitalSignsTile` band coloring + `tRecoveryMetricRationale`; NOT a bespoke ValueDashboardSection card — the metric-strip is the sibling home of successRate/replayRate) and a row in the `/reports/value-dashboard` markdown export. Empty state (nothing resolved in window) → `null`/neutral, not 0%. **Tests:** `recovery.slaPolicies` validator (valid/partial/unknown-severity/non-integer/bounds/empty/array); `defaultSlaTargetAt` override-map + per-severity fallback; `computeSlaAttainment` bands + empty; RecoveryCenterPanel SLA tile renders; reports markdown SLA line. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green (data 353, engine 950, shared 362, api 1065, web 693); `node` + `typescript-react-reviewer` reviews; **live smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-264/`) Compose Postgres+API — set `recovery.slaPolicies={"p1":5}` via `POST /org/config`, seed a p1 incident, `psql` confirms `slaTargetAt ≈ createdAt + 5min`; resolve one within + one after target → `GET /recovery/metrics` `slaAttainment` = 50%. **Deferred follow-up (ENG-265 candidate):** a SEPARATE acknowledge-by (TTA) deadline needs an `acknowledgedAt` column + migration + dual breach detection — out of scope; this ticket makes the existing single resolve-by deadline configurable. |
| ENG-266 | Credential expiry watch | Shipped | P2 | — | Promotes deep-review proposal §3 #18. **Problem:** credentials point at an env `secret_ref` (tokens/webhooks/DSNs) that expire, but Janusly had no expiry awareness — you found out when a workflow failed at 2am with an auth error. **Verify-before-implement:** no expiry column existed; `credentials.health` is a *computed* concept (`credentialHealthRepo`), extensible; the alert infra (trigger enum + params + dispatcher filter + dedupe + the `system:alerts-scanner` `*/2` cron) was fully reusable via the mapped 7-point recipe. **AC:** optional operator-declared expiry; warn N days ahead via alert policies; no new cron. **Shipped:** one migration (`credentials.expires_at` nullable timestamptz — plain column add, no two-file pattern). Repo: `setCredentialExpiry` (CAS on `updatedAt`, mirrors rotation), `listCredentialsExpiringWithin(orgId, days)` (`isNotNull + lte`, capped 500). `CredentialHealthEntry` gains `expiresAt`. Routes: `POST /credentials` accepts+validates a future `expiresAt`; `GET /credentials` projects it; NEW `POST /credentials/:name/expiry` (admin, set/clear, optional `ifMatch` CAS, `withAuditTx`, audit `credential.expiry_set`, coded 400/404/409). New alert trigger `credential.expiring` (7-point recipe: `ALERT_TRIGGERS` + `AlertParamsCredentialExpiringSchema` (`warnDays` 1..365 + optional `credentialKinds`/`credentialNames`) + `matchesPolicyFilter` (`daysUntilExpiry <= warnDays`) + `buildDedupeKey` `credential:<id>` + `scanCredentialExpiry` in `runOrgScan`). Web: pure `credential-expiry.ts` (`expiryStatus`, mirrors ENG-261's `trash-expiry.ts`); `CredentialsPanel` expiry `<input type=date>` on create + expired(red)/soon(amber) badge from health; `AlertPoliciesPanel` `credential.expiring` params sub-form; `OperationsPage`/`RecoveryCenterPanel` type carries the field; sdk-node `RecoveryMetrics` unaffected. i18n EN/ES (parity). **Tests:** `expiryStatus` (expired/soon/ok/boundary), `setCredentialExpiry` CAS branches, `credential.expiring` params schema, `buildDedupeKey` branch, `POST /credentials` expiry validation + `POST /:name/expiry` set/clear/404/409/audit. `listCredentialsExpiringWithin` SQL + `scanCredentialExpiry` end-to-end + web components covered by the live + UI smokes. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; `node` + `typescript-react-reviewer` reviews; **live smoke** (`output/janus-ship/eng-266/`) Compose Postgres+API — a credential 3d out fires `credential.expiring`, a no-expiry cred does not; **UI smoke** both-locale expiry badge + policy form. **Deferred:** auto-detecting real provider expiry (v1 is operator-provided); auto-rotation on expiry (warn-only); editing expiry of existing creds via the rotate modal (the `POST /:name/expiry` route exists; a richer edit-existing UI is a follow-up). |
| ENG-267 | HTTP response compression (gzip in `sendJson`) | Shipped | P2 | — | Promotes world-class-audit proposal P-01. **Problem:** no `Content-Encoding`/gzip anywhere in `apps/api` — every JSON response (recovery metrics, run detail, clusters) shipped uncompressed. **AC:** gzip when the client sent `Accept-Encoding: gzip` AND the body clears ~1 KB; SSE untouched; env kill-switch; both branches tested. **Shipped (single chokepoint, no route changes):** `sendJson` (`apps/api/src/http.ts`) gzip-compresses via `node:zlib` `gzipSync` when `maybeGzip` clears three gates — compression enabled (`JANUSLY_HTTP_COMPRESSION !== "false"`), the request positively advertised gzip (`gzip;q=0` treated as an explicit refusal per RFC 9110), and the serialized body ≥ `GZIP_MIN_BYTES` (1024). Sets `Content-Encoding: gzip` + merges `Vary: Origin, Accept-Encoding` (preserving the CORS `Vary`). The dispatcher (`server.ts`) stashes `requestAcceptEncoding` on the response the same way it already stashes `requestOrigin`. SSE writers (`sendEventFrame`) bypass `sendJson` and stay uncompressed. Compression only fires when the client opted in, so any client that can't decode gzip (never sends the header) is byte-for-byte unaffected — HTTP-contract-safe for both SDKs. **Tests:** `http.test.ts` gzip block (round-trips large body identically via `gunzipSync`; preserves `Vary: Origin`; leaves <1 KB uncompressed; skips when gzip not advertised; honors `gzip;q=0` refusal; honors the kill-switch). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green (api 1084). **Follow-ups:** none. |
| ENG-268 | Org-config snapshot cache (hot-path read) | Shipped | P2 | — | Promotes world-class-audit proposal P-02. **Problem:** `listOrgConfig`/`getOrgConfigSnapshot` (`packages/data/src/orgConfigRepo.ts`) hit Postgres with no LIMIT and no cache on most authenticated hot paths (AI routes, recovery metrics, per-node engine config reads). **AC:** second read within TTL issues zero queries; write invalidates same-process immediately; `TTL=0` restores today's behavior; eviction cap tested. **Shipped:** process-local TTL cache keyed by `orgId` (`JANUSLY_ORG_CONFIG_CACHE_TTL_MS`, default 30_000, `0` disables); FIFO eviction at 1000 orgs; `upsertOrgConfig` calls `invalidateOrgConfigCache(orgId)` after the write so a same-process change is immediate (other replicas converge within one TTL — acceptable since the catalog is runtime *tuning*, not authz; `auth.*` keeps its own narrow un-cached reads). Only the default-env path is cached — a caller passing a custom `env` (tests) always reads fresh, so a test env never poisons the cache. Also added `.limit(200)` to the base scan as a defensive bound (catalog is ~50 keys). **Tests:** new `orgConfigCache.test.ts` (cache hit issues one query; explicit invalidation re-queries; TTL expiry re-queries via fake timers; `TTL=0` bypass; custom-env bypass; FIFO eviction past the cap). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green (data 364). **Follow-ups:** none. |
| ENG-269 | `audit_logs` action-prefix index | Shipped | P2 | — | Promotes world-class-audit proposal P-03. **Problem:** the audit viewer's action-PREFIX filter (`queryAuditLogs`: `action LIKE 'prefix%'`) had no supporting index — only `(org_id, created_at)` — so an org-scoped prefix query seq-scanned. **AC:** `EXPLAIN` for `org_id = $1 AND action LIKE 'org.scim%' ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 50` uses the new index; migration applies from zero; `production-rollout.sql` present. **Shipped (two-file hot-path pattern):** new index `audit_logs_org_action_created_idx` on `(org_id, action text_pattern_ops, created_at DESC)` declared in `schema.ts` and generated via `db:generate`, then hand-edited to add `IF NOT EXISTS` + the `text_pattern_ops` opclass (drizzle's builder can't emit an opclass — same precedent as the GIN index); sibling `production-rollout.sql` with the `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` variant. Migration `20260706142624_cool_martin_li`. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green; **live DB check** — brought up Compose Postgres, `pnpm migrate` applied cleanly from zero, seeded 2000 rows, `EXPLAIN` on the prefix+keyset query shows `Index Scan using audit_logs_org_action_created_idx` with `Index Cond: (org_id = ... AND action ~>=~ 'org.scim' AND action ~<~ 'org.scin')` (the `~>=~`/`~<~` pattern-ops operators confirm the opclass is live), Compose torn down. **Follow-ups:** none. |
| ENG-270 | Recovery metrics micro-cache | Shipped | P2 | — | Promotes world-class-audit proposal P-05. **Problem:** every `GET /recovery/metrics` poll re-ran `queryRecoveryMetricsSignals`'s ~8-query fan-out (each capped up to 10k rows); with multiple operators watching the Recovery Center, identical polls repeated the work. **AC:** two GETs within TTL run the fan-out once; DLQ replay/resolve clears the org's entry; `TTL=0` disables. **Shipped:** new `apps/api/src/metrics-cache.ts` — process-local cache of the composed envelope keyed by `(orgId, windowDays)` (`JANUSLY_RECOVERY_METRICS_CACHE_TTL_MS`, default 30_000, `0` disables; nested `Map<orgId, Map<windowDays,…>>` so per-org invalidation is a single `delete`; FIFO cap 500 orgs). `recovery-routes.ts` reads through the cache before the fan-out and stores after composing. Invalidation is wired at the single chokepoint `markDeadLetterReplayed`/`markDeadLetterResolved` (`apps/api/src/dlq.ts`), through which all five DLQ mutation paths (resolve, bulk-resolve, replay, bulk-replay, cluster-apply) flow — so a mutation drops the org's cached rollup immediately; the TTL is the backstop for signals it doesn't know about (e.g. an approval resuming). **Tests:** `metrics-cache.test.ts` (hit within TTL; window isolation; invalidate drops every window; TTL expiry via fake timers; `TTL=0` disabled). **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green (api 1084); dlq/recovery route tests unchanged-green. **Follow-ups:** the `recovery_metrics_daily` rollup table stays the documented scale path if the cache stops sufficing (≈500 orgs / 2M-run windows). |

`pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green with data still 31 files/364 (integration excluded). **Follow-ups (all shipped):** ~~grow the lane~~ → `workflows-keyset.integration.test.ts` (active-list + Trash keyset boundaries, soft-delete exclusion). ~~DLQ `day` filter SQL~~ → new **`apps/api` integration lane** mirroring the data one (`apps/api/vite.integration.config.ts`, `apps/api` `test:integration` script, both `run-integration.mjs` + `run-e2e.mjs` run it after the data lane, `apps/api/vite.config.ts` excludes it from the unit suite): `dlq-queue.integration.test.ts` proves `listRecoveryQueue`'s `created_at ∈ [day, day+1)` UTC filter (+ boundary rows at 23:59:59 / 00:00:01, malformed-day drop, org scope). ~~memory recall~~ → `memory-recall.integration.test.ts` stubs `generateEmbedding` (1024-dim unit vector via `vi.mock('@janusly/ai')`), enables two-flag consent through the real `upsertOrgConfig`, and proves the `commitMemory → recallMemory` round-trip against real pgvector + the fail-closed empty recall when consent is off. **Finding:** the stub first hit `persist_failed` because `EmbeddingResult.provider` is required (`embedding_provider` is `NOT NULL`) — the real-DB lane caught the incomplete stub the mocked suite would have accepted. Also migrated both `vite.integration.config.ts` files off the Vitest-4-removed `poolOptions` to top-level `singleFork`. **Verified:** `pnpm test:integration` green (data 3 files/10 + api 1 file/2) against Compose Postgres, torn down clean; unit suites unchanged (data 31/364, api 83/1094, integration excluded); `tsc --noEmit` clean on both. |
| ENG-280 | Recovery-loop e2e journey (batch 3 / P-29) | Shipped | P2 | — | Promotes world-class-audit proposal P-29. **Problem:** the product's core loop — fail → DLQ → validate a fix in the sandbox → replay — had no end-to-end test; the F3 demo spec stopped at "landed in DLQ". **Verify-before-implement (empirical):** probed the live recovery endpoints to learn the REAL mechanics before asserting anything: F3's `charge` (http) fails with `Missing secret: BILLING_API_KEY` — a template-resolution error the writes-skipped sandbox still hits (so the gate is exercisable with no AI key / no network); `/dlq/validate-fix` with the ORIGINAL workflow → validation run `failed` (rejects the non-fix), with a fix that edits the auth header off the missing secret → validation run `succeeded` (the template resolves and the write-side http call is skipped); `/dlq/replay {deadLetterId}` flips the DLQ row to `replayed`. **AC:** an e2e drives fail → DLQ → sandbox reject + accept → replay; deterministic + provider-free; green in `pnpm test:e2e`. **Shipped:** new `apps/web/e2e/recovery-loop.spec.ts` + helpers (`validateFix` / `replayDeadLetter` / `deadLetterStatus` in `_helpers/demo-helpers.ts`): F3 → `failed` → DLQ on `charge`; `validateFix(original)` → validation `failed` (+ charge node failed); `validateFix(auth-fixed)` → validation `succeeded` (+ charge succeeded); `replayDeadLetter` → DLQ `open` → `replayed`. **Verified:** the spec passes green (2.9s) driven against a live Compose API+worker; `pnpm build` clean; it runs inside `pnpm test:e2e` with the rest of the suite. **Finding spotted in passing (spawned as a background task, NOT fixed here):** `/dlq/replay {deadLetterId}` replays `item.workflowJson` (the ORIGINAL failed snapshot), so the RecoveryDialog's save-fixed-workflow + replay-by-id RE-RUNS the broken workflow (F3 fix + replay re-fails on the same missing-secret error) — the spec deliberately asserts only the sandbox gate + `replayed` status, not replay-to-green, pending that investigation. **Follow-ups:** ~~the replay-to-green assertion once the fix-persistence path is confirmed/fixed~~ → done in ENG-281 (the e2e now asserts replay-to-green). |
| ENG-281 | Recovery replay actually recovers the run (P0 bug from ENG-280 probe) | Shipped | P0 | — | **Bug (confirmed by empirical tracing, spawned from the ENG-280 e2e):** applying a fix and replaying NEVER recovered the run — a THREE-part failure in the manual replay path, so the RecoveryDialog showed "Recovered after Xh" while the run silently stayed failed. (a) `/dlq/replay {deadLetterId}` passed `item.workflowJson` (the ORIGINAL failed snapshot) to `replayDeadLetter`, not the operator's fix. (b) `replayDeadLetter` never reset the failed node to `queued`, so the worker's `markNodeRunning` (`queued → running`) claim couldn't win → the re-enqueued node was skipped. (c) it never un-terminated the run, so the runtime's guard (which drops queued jobs on a `failed`/`cancelled` run — a cancellation-safety check) skipped the node anyway. **auto-healing hit (b)+(c) too** — it passed the patched workflow but still never re-ran the node, so automated recovery was equally broken. **Fix (3 coordinated parts):** (a) `POST /dlq/replay` accepts an optional `suggestedWorkflow`, validated through the same schema+sanitize+failing-node gate as `/dlq/validate-fix`, and replays against it (falling back to the snapshot for a plain retry); the RecoveryDialog passes `selected.workflow`. (b) `replayDeadLetter` now `markNodeQueued(runId, node.id)` before enqueueing (mirrors start-run / retry / replay-lab). (c) new `resetRunForReplay(runId)` flips a `failed` run back to `running` — conditional on `status='failed'` so a `cancelled` run is never un-cancelled, and the runtime re-reads status at execution so a cancel racing the replay still wins. This fixes BOTH the manual dialog path AND auto-healing (shared `replayDeadLetter`). **Tests:** `dlq-replay.test.ts` updated (production replay now asserts `resetRunForReplay` + `markNodeQueued` are called); new `dlq-routes.test.ts` cases (replay against the fix → adapter gets the patched node; drop the failing node → 400); the ENG-280 e2e now uses a real fix (`charge → noop`) and asserts the replayed run reaches `succeeded`. **Verified:** empirically against a live stack — a real fix (`charge → noop`) → run recovers to `succeeded`; a sandbox-only "fix" (auth-header edit that still makes the real http call) → stays failed (proving the replay genuinely re-executes, not a false green); `pnpm build` + full `pnpm test` green (engine 956, api 1090); recovery-loop e2e green (4.2s). **Follow-ups:** `cluster-apply` / `bulk-replay` still replay each member's original snapshot (now they actually re-run it — good for transient failures — but don't apply a per-member fix); applying a fix across a cross-workflow cluster is a separate design. |
| ENG-282 | Cluster-apply applies the fix to same-workflow members (ENG-281 follow-up) | Shipped | P1 | — | Closes the `cluster-apply` half of ENG-281's follow-up. **Problem:** after ENG-281 the single `/dlq/replay` applied the operator's fix, but `/dlq/cluster-apply` still replayed each member's ORIGINAL snapshot — so a bulk cluster recovery re-ran the broken workflow for every member. **Design decision (cross-workflow clusters):** a cluster is grouped by failure SIGNATURE and can span workflows, so the one representative fix applies ONLY to members of the SAME workflow whose failing node survives the patch; every other member re-runs its own snapshot (which, post-ENG-281, now actually re-executes — good for transient failures). **Shipped:** `/dlq/cluster-apply` accepts an optional `suggestedWorkflow`, validated ONCE through the same schema+sanitize gate as `/dlq/validate-fix`; new pure `pickClusterReplayWorkflow(memberWorkflow, failingNodeId, sanitizedFix)` (`cluster-recovery.ts`) decides per-member — returns the fix + patched node when `sanitizedFix.id === memberWorkflow.id` and the failing node id survives, else the member's own snapshot. The RecoveryDialog cluster mode now passes `selected.workflow`. **Tests:** `cluster-recovery.test.ts` — `pickClusterReplayWorkflow` applies the fix to a same-workflow member, refuses a different-workflow fix, falls back when the fix drops the failing node, and plain-retries when no fix is supplied. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + api suite green (1094). **Follow-ups:** `bulk-replay` stays a plain retry by design (no fix concept in its UX); it already re-runs originals correctly post-ENG-281. |

Test: `<title>` renders when supplied. **(ENG-272) Downtime chip on the Operations recovery queue:** `DeadLettersPanel` open rows now show a `humanizeAge` "downtime so far" indicator colored by the SAME `downtimeSeverity` thresholds as the home wedge tile (neutral → amber ≥1h → red ≥4h), static (refreshes on the panel's `platformVersion` refetch — no per-row timer). i18n EN/ES. **(ENG-278) SSE in the contract test:** `routes-contract.test.ts` now also extracts SSE `fetch(`${API_URL}…`)` streaming paths (GET) that bypass `api()` — the run stream `/runs/:id/stream` is now asserted against the registry. **Verified:** `pnpm build` + `pnpm test` green (web 728, api 1094). **Follow-ups:** none. |
| ENG-284 | Run input reaches node context — trigger payloads dropped in production (fourth-wave B-01/B-04) | Shipped | P0 | — | Promotes fourth-wave-audit B-01 (confirmed correctness bug). **Problem:** the template scope was `{ context: getRunContext(runId), inputs: node.config }` and `getRunContext` reduces `run_nodes` rows keyed only by nodeId — `runs.inputJson.input` was never read, so `ctx.context.input` (the contract the triggers docstring promised) was undefined in every production run: `email_received`/`file_dropped`/`mcp_server_event` executors returned `event: {}` and the inbound payload never reached downstream nodes. Unit tests masked it (context passed directly); sandbox validation masked it (trigger outputs seeded from input) — validation showed the event flowing while production dropped it. **Shipped:** `getRunMetadata` also returns `input` (`runs.inputJson.input` normalised to `{}`) — it already read the runs row once per node execution, zero extra queries; `executeNode` merges it as `context.input`, guarded so a legacy node literally named `input` keeps its slot; new validation rule `node_id_reserved` rejects the id on new saves; B-04 companion rule `edge_condition_inputs_scope` rejects `inputs.*` paths in edge conditions (edges evaluate with `inputs: {}` — the branch silently never fired) with a pointer to `context.input.*`; triggers docstring corrected (`{{context.input.*}}` is the real path). **Verified:** engine 76 files/970 green — 4 new `execute-node.test.ts` regression tests (incl. `{{context.input.*}}` template resolution + legacy-id guard) + 4 new validation tests; `tsc --noEmit` clean. |
| ENG-285 | Router routes its decision — both branches executed on every run (fourth-wave B-02) | Shipped | P0 | — | Promotes fourth-wave-audit B-02 (confirmed correctness bug). **Problem:** the router branch recorded `decision.made` + succeeded, then `enqueueReadyNodes` queued every outgoing edge — nothing consumed `chosenNodeId`, so both candidate paths executed on every run (double cost + side effects); the AI prompt's canonical example wired candidates with no incoming edges, so the root scan queued them at t=0 regardless. **Shipped (4 layers):** runtime marks each non-chosen candidate that is a direct successor AND still `pending` as skipped before the readiness scan (mirrors the condition-edge skip; `skipped` satisfies join readiness — no deadlock; guarded by the already-loaded context snapshot, no extra queries); validation rules `router_candidate_unknown` + `router_candidate_not_successor` (candidates must exist and be wired `router → candidate`); `sanitizeAiWorkflow` auto-wires missing router→candidate edges on drafts (same repair posture as transform→noop) so old-shape generations don't 502; the prompt example now wires `pick→fast_path`/`pick→accurate_path` and states the successor rule. Two pre-existing validation tests had codified the unwired shape and were updated. **Verified:** engine 76/970 green (3 new routing tests: loser skipped with reason, unwired candidate untouched by the router skip, non-pending candidate never flipped), api 83/1095 green (matrix 50/50), tsc clean both. |
| ENG-286 | Inspector edge-condition corruption + stale JSON-error banner (fourth-wave B-03) | Shipped | P1 | — | Promotes fourth-wave-audit B-03 (confirmed UI data-corruption bug). **Problem:** the edge-condition textarea was uncontrolled (`defaultValue`) with no `key` — selecting edge A then edge B reused the DOM node, so B's editor showed A's condition and the blur-commit WROTE A's stale text onto B (silent edge corruption in the product's own builder); the advanced-JSON parse error also lingered across node selections. **Shipped:** `key={selectedEdge.id}` on the textarea + a `useEffect` clearing `jsonError` when `selectedNode?.id` changes (`InspectorPanel.tsx`). **Verified:** new `InspectorPanel.test.tsx` — 2 regression tests (the first fails without the key: edge B must show its own condition and blur must commit B's text, never A's; node B must not inherit node A's error banner); web 84 files/732 green. |
